Merged or removed then, each with its stored value carried once by `migrateRetiredPrefs` (`src/main/retired-prefs.ts`: `MERGES` decide the new value, `RETIRED_PREFS` delete the old keys; pinned by `retired-prefs.test.ts`): Appearance's "hide thinking" and "expand thinking by default" are one choice, Thinking: hidden / collapsed / expanded (`ThinkingDisplayRow.tsx`, over the two prefs it writes, `displayPreferences.hideThinkingBlock` (⌃T, follows omp until chosen) and `thinkingExpanded`; what is stored reads as hidden if hiding was on, else expanded if expanding was on, else collapsed, and hiding keeps the open state for ⌃T); the System light/dark pair shows only while the theme is System; the run state in the window title and progress on the app icon are always on (no `titleState`/`showProgress`); the sidebar's shortcut pins are set on the sidebar (Help › Customize sidebar… sheet `SidebarPinsSheet`, a pinned row's context menu Unpin), not in Settings; Agent behavior has no Goal/Autopilot/open-agents action rows (they are the Run chip's, the palette's and the menus'); the GitHub update token row shows only while the updater is `auth-required` or a token is stored; one "Keep tools up to date" (`toolsAutoUpdate`, on unless all three old switches were off) replaces the omp, tool-CLI and extension switches; one "Approve new devices on this computer" (`mobileWeb.confirmNewDevices`, on if either old switch was) covers phones and computers; the work board has no switch (it docks whenever a pane has room; its ✕ hides it for that window, the palette brings it back).
- Curated rows come first. Rare rows and raw stock rows (the auto panels, window placement, non-curated schema rows, launch profile, reset passes, account pools, model overrides, the proxy) fold under one closed `SettingsAdvanced` disclosure at the section's end; `SettingsPage` opens it before scrolling to a search hit or deep link inside, so every row stays reachable and searchable.
- Models & accounts reads connection, then models: Providers & accounts (accounts, live quota, rotation), Custom endpoints, then Model (setup assistant, Model roles, Defaults, Compare models). "This device" / "Selected host" scope lines (`SECTION_SCOPE`) show only while the focused tab runs on a paired host.
- No section intros: `settings.section.*.desc` only feeds nav search. App and Agent sections (except Memory) are tip-only (`SettingsTipOnly`, `isTerse` in `SettingsPage`): every string description sits behind its info glyph; rows read icon + label + control.
- Sidebar shortcuts (prefs `sidebar.railPins`, `stores/sidebar-prefs.ts`) are off by default, including the footer's Agents and Stats (reachable through the palette, the menu, ⌥A and Settings; Settings and the help menu always stay). Only the usage block is on until a saved `railPins` array (even empty) says otherwise; it lists one line per subscription account with usage windows and renders nothing for API-key-only users. They are set on the sidebar itself, never in Settings: Help › Customize sidebar… opens `SidebarPinsSheet` (one switch per pin, a bottom sheet on a phone), and a pinned row's context menu (right click, Shift+F10, long press) has Unpin and Customize sidebar…; `toggleRailPin` is the one writer.
- Compact rows (`settings/SettingsLayout.tsx`): `SettingsRow`/`SettingsSwitchRow` are one dense line (`--sc-control-md` high): optional decorative `icon` (lucide glyph, or `ProviderMark` for a provider, model or vendor CLI), label, muted `description`, control at the right. A string description up to `INLINE_DESCRIPTION_MAX` (56) characters reads inline; a longer one sits behind a `SettingInfo` glyph (tooltip on hover/focus, text kept as `sr-only`); a node description (a cost, a warning) stays a visible small muted note. Collections (accounts, CLIs, devices, schedules, MCP servers, shortcuts) are dense list rows with secondary actions behind an overflow `MenuButton`. New rows reuse these, never a boxed card or a full-width description paragraph.
- Outside Settings (Mode popover, dialogs, run settings) `SettingInfo` and `splitDescription` (`common/SettingInfo.tsx`) are shared with `common/Switch.tsx`'s `SwitchField`: the glyph sits outside the `<label>`, so the switch is named by the label alone. A boolean row never stacks a paragraph under its label.
- The composer's Mode popover (`layout/composer/ComposerModes.tsx`, `modes/ModeConfig.tsx`, `modes/AutopilotConfig.tsx`) is built from `SettingsRow`s (`compactControl`; fixed-width `Select`/`Input`/segmented `Tabs` at the right; explanation behind `SettingInfo` in the row's `badge`; `FocusChips compact` within two rows; a one-line `GrowArea` that opens on first focus and never collapses on blur; release-procedure check as a glyph with the sentence as tooltip and name; one short warning line beside Start; no footer line) and scrolls inside `max-h-[min(36rem,calc(100dvh-6rem))]`. Nothing in it may change layout on a blur: a press blurs the focused field at mousedown, a reflow moves the control from under the pointer and its click never fires (v0.35.0: typing in the guide or backlog box and clicking Start did nothing). Pinned by `ComposerModes.autopilot.test.tsx` and the guide test of `e2e/autopilot-cycles.e2e.ts`.
- Opt-in captures: `e2e/settings-captures.e2e.ts` (`OMPDESK_SETTINGS_SHOTS=<dir>`; `OMPDESK_SETTINGS_SECTIONS` / `OMPDESK_SETTINGS_SIZES` narrow it), `e2e/mode-captures.e2e.ts` (`OMPDESK_MODE_SHOTS=<dir>`).

### Figure headers
- A box with a title and actions (code block, mermaid/svg figure, a write's figure in its tool card, the Files viewer's mode switch; `chat/FigureHeader.tsx`, `chat/Figure.tsx`) wears ONE header row on its top edge, `--sc-control-sm` high: kind or file name left, lucide icon buttons right (Preview/Code as two `aria-pressed` buttons via `FigureModeToggle`, then wrap, copy, download, enlarge), each with `IconButton`'s tooltip + `aria-label`. Never a separate text-tab row above such a box.
- `Figure` (`data-figure="mermaid|svg"`) owns the box and the Preview/Code state (`mode`/`onModeChange`); `CodeBlock` takes `title`, `headerActions`, `endActions`, `bare` for the same header. Tests: `chat/Figure.test.tsx`, `e2e/figure-header.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_FIGURE_HEADER=<dir>` captures).

### Phone and tablet (web build only; an Electron window keeps the desktop layout byte for byte)
- `common/MasterDetail.tsx` is the one list-beside-detail primitive (`MasterDetail`, `useMasterDetail`, `MasterDetailBack`). Phone (`useShellMode() === "phone"`): one pane at a time, the list, then the row's detail under a back bar; the system back gesture takes a back layer while the detail is open. Tablet and desktop keep two columns. `SettingsPage` uses the hook (`data-stacked` on `.settings-page`: the section list is its own page, search results replace it, a deep link opens the detail); the GitHub center sections and the PR center use `MasterDetail` (the PR store's `deselect` is the back).
- On a phone `SettingsRow` stacks label and description above a full-width control unless `compactControl` (a switch row, or a one-button control such as push "Turn on" and install: stays at the right). Switches and `SettingInfo` glyphs are small with a 44 px hit area, except an info glyph in Settings' content on touch: it heads lines 28 px apart, so it declares `--hit-gap` 20 px (a 36 px target) and its line is 36 px tall, and it never takes the next line's taps (`mobile.css`, 34245bb5f); `ListRow` carries `omp-list-row` (40 px on touch). `e2e/mobile-pages.e2e.ts` covers it on the dev harness in Chromium and WebKit (`OMP_GUI_MOBILE_PAGES=<dir>` captures; `harness.html?rail=all` pins Agents and Stats).
- Scale (`styles/mobile.css`): a tablet and plain touch do NOT resize controls or type (`--sc-control-*` 28/32/36, `--sc-icon-*` 14/16/20, body and transcript 15 stay the desktop's). A **phone is denser**: the "Phone density" block (`html[data-shell="phone"]`) is the only place that overrides those tokens: `--phone-type-scale` 0.8667 (`applyFontSize` in `lib/theme.ts` multiplies it into `--gui-font-size` and the body size, so the type preference reads 13 px instead of 15 and still scales), `--sc-font-2xs/xs/sm` 12, `--sc-control-sm/md/lg` 24/28/32, `--sc-hit-min` 24, `--sc-icon-lg` 16, `--sc-row-touch` 36, `--sc-row-action` 44. A new phone rule goes in that block, gated on `html[data-shell="phone"]`, and never changes a ≥ 1200 px or tablet layout. Fields stay 16 px (iOS zooms into anything smaller); sheet items are `--sc-row-action`.
- A phone turned sideways (`html[data-shell="phone"]` under `(orientation: landscape)`, the last block of `mobile.css`) has the width portrait lacks: composer chips 10 px apart (each reach 5 px, the 26 px + a 36 px tap), the model chip's whole name, 12 px chip badges, a 36 px work strip, a 38 px suggestion row and a 36 px work-card "Steps" row. A work card's 24 px step chips sit 12 px apart under any finger (phone in either orientation, tablet; reach, never drawn bigger; portrait drew 26 × 28 targets until 22 Oct 2026). Portrait's 19.4 % chrome budget does not apply sideways. Pinned by the phone landscape test of `e2e/responsive-smoke.e2e.ts` (844×390, zero soft findings on the session surfaces) and the rotate step of `e2e/phone-day.e2e.ts`.
- Chrome budget at 390×844: gutters 12 px (`--omp-column-pad`), header bar 40 px, dock card head 28 px, always-open composer (see "Phone composer"), side-panel title row 36 px and tab bar 40 px; header + work chip + dock + composer stay ≤ 19.4 % of the height while idle (measured 18.4–18.5 % without suggestions, 22.6 % with them; the user prefers the always-open composer to a lower share).
- The 44 px rule is a hit area only. Under `html[data-input="touch"]` every `button`, `[role=button|switch|tab|checkbox]`, `summary`, `[data-setting-info]` and checkbox label gets an invisible `::after` reaching `--sc-touch-target` (`@layer base`, so an element's own `::after` or positioning utility wins; an element with `after:`/`before:` utilities is left alone). Never lift a switch, chip, ⋯ or icon button to 44 px visually; a field has no pseudo-element and is drawn 36 px.
- A hit area never covers a neighbour (the responsive audit's `stolen-tap`): a container of close controls sets `--hit-gap-x` / `--hit-gap-y` to the gap between them and the reach stops at half of it (title bar icons 14 px apart ≈ 40 px hit; composer toolbar, dock header, work chip, sidebar and panel top bar declare theirs); unset, the reach is the full 44 px. The bound counts from the control's own edge, so it must not be tighter than the room around it: a compact settings row resets it (`--hit-gap-y: initial`; a stacked row's own control reaches 7 px up and down on a phone, `--hit-gap-y: 14px`, under its label's 36 px info line; the stacked rows' 8 px bound left switches 32 px between rows that no tap reached), and a scroller clips every reach to its padding box (the terminal key bar's key scroller holds the bar's padding so its keys reach the panel tab bar's hit area).
- **36 px through spacing, not size** (20 Oct 2026): a 28 px control (26 inside its border) reaches 36 when its neighbours sit 10 px apart and the container declares `--hit-gap-x|y: 10px`, so on touch (`html[data-input="touch"]`, web build only) the clusters that sat 0–4 px apart are spaced 10 px: page top bars, the side panel's title row and its views' button clusters, the tablet composer row, Settings' button clusters (14 px on a phone, whose icon buttons are 24 px), Stats' section pills, the context popover's actions, a schedule's days (their rows 14 px apart, a 24 px day reaching 7 px up and down), a message's actions, a figure's header buttons (reaching 5 px out of its 28 px header; a phone's 24 px ones 14 px apart), a multi-question card's step tabs (outranking the tab bars' 4 px bound), a phone's message actions (14 px apart), the sidebar update card's Restart and dismiss (10 px apart). Rows that stack flush (tabs, sheet items, Changes rows, the Run sheet's Advanced and Model roles, a host sheet's actions, the children-at-work rows) are a row tall with `--hit-gap-y: 0` (a Changes row's stage box label and file stand as tall as the row, the label as wide; the discard icon keeps its size and reaches the row's height, 14 px from the file; the Changes view's own 24 px controls behind a 1 px border sit 14 px apart, bounded at 14, so each reaches 36); a segmented tab bar (`[data-segmented]`, its tabs 2 px apart) declares `--hit-gap-x: 2px`, and a wrapping button row or chip list gets room on touch as well as its bound, since a reach is its own box plus half the gap and a bound alone over 4-6 px left them 26-29 px (the Autopilot run's controls 10 px apart, the benchmark's models 12 px, each remote address 7 px above and below inside its row, the import dialog's search and refresh and the terminal's toolbar 10 px; a finished work card's findings and copy button 10 px apart (a wrapped line 14, the row no longer tucked under the narrative; the outcome's "Result" a touch row), the phone Run sheet's Autopilot buttons 14 px apart, the goal dock bar's Pause, Edit and Drop 10 px apart (`data-goal-dock`; an iPad held sideways handed a tap on Pause to Edit), an Autopilot run's board controls (Pause, End, Stop now) 14 px apart, a section head's own buttons (the run log's copy) 8 px in from its end, and a board row's own pin or ✕ reaching the touch row (`--hit-gap-y: 10px`) and only 6 px sideways, so no reach makes a docked board scroll sideways; on a phone, whose icons draw 24 px behind a 1 px border and reach from the 22 px inside, the side panel views' clusters sit 14 px apart, bounded at 14, and a view's lone buttons (the terminal's New terminal, the Files viewer's Back, an empty state's actions, `data-terminal-head`, `data-file-viewer-head`, `data-empty-actions`) get the room around them); tabs and sheet items are `--sc-row-touch` / `--sc-row-action` rows on touch. A docked tablet sidebar's device and folder headers, starter guide and project ⋯ are 36 px boxes; on touch the always-shown project ⋯ takes its own slot beside "+" instead of overlaying the title. The drawer's left-edge strip (`EdgeSwipeZone`) owns swipes only: a tap on it is handed to the element under it (`tapThrough`).
- Pinned by `styles/touch-targets.test.ts` and, for the hit area, `hitSizes()` in `e2e/harness.ts`. The harness page takes `?stress=1` (long URL, wide table, long code line, diff, mermaid, long tool paths) for the responsive audit (`e2e/responsive-audit.ts`).

### Themes
- Ten distinct themes, five light and five dark (18 Oct 2026; replaced eleven near-identical tints). Ids, groups, schemes and the retired-id table: dependency-free `src/shared/theme-names.ts`. Light: `light` (OMPdesk Light), `github-light`, `solarized-light`, `sepia`, `hc-light`. Dark: `dark` (OMPdesk Dark), `nord`, `tokyo-night`, `gruvbox-dark`, `hc-dark`. `LEGACY_THEME_NAMES` maps titanium/nord/latte/gruvbox/paper/solarized/dawn/frost/matcha to the nearest same-scheme successor (CIE Lab distance of page and accent).
- `light` and `dark` are written token by token in `lib/themes.ts` because `theme-light.css` / `theme-dark.css` must match them (`styles/tokens.test.ts`). Every other theme is one compact `ThemePalette` in `lib/theme-palettes.ts`; `lib/theme-builder.ts` derives all 119 `--omp-*` tokens plus the terminal's ANSI 16, six chart series and the focus ring (`themeExtrasFor` writes `--omp-ansi-*`, `--omp-chart-1…6`, `--sc-focus-ring` inline). Published palettes (Solarized, Nord, Tokyo Night, Gruvbox, GitHub Primer; MIT, credited in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` via `scripts/generate-third-party-notices.ts`) start from their own colors; a color failing AA as text is shaded toward the text color until it passes.
- Never add a theme that is a tint of an existing one: `themes.contrast.test.ts` fails a same-scheme pair whose page, accent and three loudest syntax colors are under mean ΔE 15 apart, or whose accents are under ΔE 15.
- Contrast is a test, not a review. `lib/theme-contrast.ts` lists every text, accent, status, syntax, diff, button/badge, selection, border, focus-ring, chart and ANSI-16 pair with its floor: 4.5:1 (7:1 High Contrast), 3:1 for graphics. `themes.contrast.test.ts` fails any theme under a floor; `styles/colorblind.test.ts` holds the color-blind palette (per scheme, `--cb-*` in `global.css`) to 4.5:1 on every theme. After touching a palette run `bunx vitest run src/renderer/lib/themes.contrast.test.ts`.
- **System** pairs one light and one dark theme (prefs `systemLightTheme` / `systemDarkTheme`, device-local like `themeName`; default `light` / `dark`; a wrong-scheme name falls back to the default). `applyThemeByName("system")` resolves the OS scheme to the pair's theme and writes its tokens inline (not stylesheet-driven); `data-theme` carries the scheme, `data-theme-name` the theme shown (Mermaid, e2e and captures read it); `setSystemThemePair` re-applies when System is selected; the watcher in `lib/theme.ts` re-applies on an OS flip. Picker: `settings/ThemePicker.tsx` (System card with both halves previewed, a light group, a dark group, and the two pair selects only while System is chosen); `ThemePreview` is shared with `dialogs/ThemePickerDialog.tsx`.
- Migration: `migrateThemeName` maps a retired id to its successor and an unknown value to null (the caller falls back to System, never a blank theme). `getPersistedThemeSelection` stores the successor on first read (desktop prefs and a paired device's uiPrefs alike); the main process rewrites `themeName` / `systemLightTheme` / `systemDarkTheme` once in `prefsStore()`; `nativeThemeSource` (`lib/theme.ts`, used by `main/window-chrome.ts`) takes the scheme from the theme id, so a saved window opens in the right scheme before any renderer runs. A paired host's reported `themeName` goes through the same migration (`hooks/use-host-appearance.ts`).
- Every surface follows the theme through tokens, never a literal: xterm (`TerminalPanel.terminalTheme`, `--omp-ansi-*`), highlight.js (`--omp-syntax-*`), diffs (`--omp-diff-*`), Mermaid (`MermaidBlock` builds `themeVariables` from `--sc-*`, re-renders on `data-theme` / `data-theme-name`), charts (`lib/chart.ts`, `--omp-chart-*`), the Windows title-bar overlay and the phone page's `theme-color` metas (`lib/window-chrome.ts` `syncWindowControls`).
- Pre-paint: `applyThemeByName` stores `omp.themeScheme` (dark, light or system) and `omp.themePaint` (`{light?, dark?}` page backgrounds: System stores both); `public/pre-paint.js` paints it and sets the `theme-color` metas before any module runs. Static `manifest.webmanifest` colors stay the OMPdesk dark. `?theme=<any id>` works on the dev harness.

### Profile and reply language
- Profile: Settings › General › Profile stores prefs `userProfile` (`{name, experience}`; contracts and prompt text in dependency-free `resources/gui-bridge/modes/user-profile-contracts.ts`; levels beginner, vibe-coder, junior, expert). It rides the reply-language path: `OMP_GUI_USER_PROFILE` (JSON) at spawn, `user_profile_set` on change and on bridge connect to every live local sidecar and hosted tab; `modes/user-profile.ts` appends one `<user-profile>` section through `before_agent_start` (nothing for an empty profile). Stock has no such setting.
- Reply language: Settings › General › Conversation language stores prefs `replyLanguage` (`auto` default | a curated English name | `off`; `src/shared/reply-language.ts`; missing or unknown reads as `auto`). `auto` is the device language's English name (first usable `app.getPreferredSystemLanguages()` entry, then `app.getLocale()`; not the UI language). Each spawn passes it as `OMP_GUI_REPLY_LANGUAGE`; a pref change (or bridge connect) pushes it to every live local sidecar and every tab this machine hosts via the bridge's `reply_language_set {language: string | null}`. A paired host's tabs follow the host's own pref.
- `modes/reply-language.ts` keeps the value as module state and appends one instruction through `before_agent_start` from the next turn (nothing when off, unset or English, so the prompt stays stock); `currentReplyLanguage()` / `onReplyLanguageChange()` feed the session titles (see Automatic session titles).
- A second line in the same section, naming the same resolved language (never hardcoded), covers the labels a person sees: todo items and phase names, and a `Title: <short sentence>` first line in each `task` assignment, and in an Autopilot run the cycle's `title`, `summary` and `followups` the agent gives the `autopilot` tool, which the run's timeline and board show as written (the user's report, 21 Oct 2026: a Korean user's timeline was English, because tool arguments stayed in the model's language). A subagent's stock title (`generateTaskLabel`, `task/label.ts`) is always English with no hook and stock's task tool has no `description` field, so the GUI shows that `Title:` line (`assignmentTitle` in `modes/subagent-title-contracts.ts`, applied in `subagentPrimaryLabel` for sidebar rows, the Subagent panel header and the work board, and in `stores/subagents.ts` / `work-run.ts` for the work map; no line → stock's label). Thinking, the rest of every assignment, tool arguments, code and file contents stay as the model writes them (Korean costs more tokens).
- Every session kind gets both lines: main, subagents (their rebind installs the hook), workers an agent opened, Autopilot runs; a paired host's tabs get the host's language (above). An instruction that would put todo items in another language yields to it: pstack's "copy the playbook's steps into `todo`" says to write them in the todo language this section sets (see Built-in pstack engineering mode).
- Pinned by `bridge-reply-language.test.ts`, `subagent-graph.test.ts` and the opt-in `e2e/real-subagent-titles.e2e.ts` (Korean and Japanese). Never inject GUI text with `--append-system-prompt`: it collides with the launch profile's flag and turns off omp's fork prompt-cache key.

### Sidebar header
- Under the Code/Chat switch each lane has ONE line, the same geometry on both (the user's rule, 19 Oct 2026): the lane's search field filling the width (`data-sidebar-filter`; Code "세션 찾기"/"Find sessions", Chat "채팅 찾기"/"Find chats"), then one icon button (Code: open or create a project folder, `data-sidebar-open-project`; Chat: new chat, `data-sidebar-new-chat`). The row carries `data-sidebar-header-row="code|chat"`. There is no New session button: a code session starts from a project row's +, ⌘N, the palette, the menu and the tray.
- **With device sections the project folder button moves onto this device's header** (the user's call, 21 Oct 2026): once a paired host is listed (or remembered, `knownHosts`), "This device" (`data-sidebar-device-header="local"`) ends in its own folder-plus button like every host header (`data-sidebar-device-add="local"`, label `remote.sidebar.addProject` with "This device"; the same open-or-create picker), its chevron after the name as on a host's, and the Code lane's search row drops `data-sidebar-open-project` so the field takes the whole line. With no host the search row keeps it. On touch the header is a 36 px row like a host's (`mobile.css`). The starter tour's project step falls back to the header's button.
- Code sessions are always grouped by project. The "list by recent activity" grouping was removed (the user's call, 19 Oct 2026): `sidebar-prefs.ts` rewrites a stored blob that still names `grouping` once without it (`RETIRED_KEYS`).
- Pinned by the header case of `Sidebar.test.tsx` and the retired-key case of `sidebar-prefs.test.ts`.

### Sidebar memory
- The left side keeps what was left open or closed across a relaunch, a reload and a reopened window. Device-local prefs `sidebarVisible`, `sidebarFolds`, `sidebarFamilies` (`DEVICE_LOCAL_PREF_KEYS`: a paired web page keeps them per device, the desktop per profile). Code: `lib/sidebar-memory.ts`, `stores/sidebar-folds.ts`, `stores/sidebar-subagents.ts`, `stores/ui.ts`. Each pref has a localStorage mirror (`omp.sidebarVisible`, `omp.sidebarFolds`, `omp.sidebarFamilies`) read synchronously by the first render; the pref is read again after mount and wins when it differs, unless the user already edited.
- **Shown/hidden**: `useUiStore.sidebarVisible` starts from the mirror and every change is saved by a `useUiStore` subscription, except while the shell is a drawer (phone, portrait tablet: `AppShell` `useDrawerSidebarSync` calls `setSidebarDrawerMode`; the flag is then the drawer's open state, never saved, and the drawer always starts closed; saving resumes when the window docks). `pre-paint.js` sets `data-boot-sidebar="hidden"` from `omp.sidebarVisible` and the boot shell (`scripts/boot-shell.ts`) drops its sidebar and clears the traffic lights, so the first frame never flashes an open sidebar.
- **Folds** (`sidebarFolds`: `groups` project / folder `cwd` → folded, `devices` `local` and each paired host's deviceId, `deviceGroups` a host's folders as `deviceId\ncwd`, `lane` Code/Chat, `archivedOpen`, `starterOpen` Get started, `usageCollapsed` the footer usage block, `deviceOrder` the device sections' order, `knownHosts` the last host list): the remembered lane beats the open session's lane at launch and the session wins from its next change on; a filter still shows every match.
- **Device order** (`layout/sidebar/SidebarDeviceSections.tsx`, rules `lib/device-order.ts`): once a host is paired, "This device" (`data-sidebar-local-device`) and each host's section can be reordered. The saved `deviceOrder` (ids, `local` = this computer) comes first; a device it does not name follows in the default order (this device, then the hosts as listed); `syncHosts` drops an unpaired host's id once the host list loaded. It lives in `sidebarFolds`, not the host's `sidebar` blob, so a paired web page keeps its own order. **Nothing moves after the first frame**: `knownHosts` (the hosts the last read named, `{id, name, offline?}`) draws each host's section before this launch's first read (`data-device-pending`, a still grey dot, the same body as the state it is likely to be: offline = header only, else connecting), so a host saved above This device does not push its rows down when the read lands; a host the read no longer lists leaves. While a host connects, its last-known rows (the session-list snapshot, namespace `device:<id>`) stand in, never read (`useSessionList(…, {read: false})`), so they are in place when it comes online; only with no snapshot does it show the connecting line. The list renders through the same component with or without hosts, so pairing never remounts this device's list. Only the section header is a handle (`data-device-drag-handle`; rows keep their drags): a mouse or pen drag arms after 6 px, a touch drag after a 400 ms long press (`TouchSensor`: a tap folds, a swipe scrolls, the long press's context menu is swallowed); nothing moves while dragging: an accent line (`data-device-drop-indicator`) marks the landing place and a chip with the device's name (`data-device-drag-chip`) follows the raw pointer (dnd-kit's delta includes the list's scroll), no drop animation. Keyboard: Alt+↑/↓ on a focused header (announced in a polite status region); right click or Shift+F10 opens Move up / Move down. Pinned by `device-order.test.ts`, `sidebar-folds.test.ts`, "Sidebar device sections › order" in `Sidebar.test.tsx`, `e2e/device-order.e2e.ts`.
- **Families** (`sidebarFamilies`: session paths whose family rows are open or hidden): a remembered open family keeps its entry until children have been seen and gone (`FamilyGroup`'s `hadChildren`), never at launch while they have not reported yet; a live tab's `tab:` key is never saved.
- **Bounded**: each record keeps its newest `FOLD_LIMIT` (50) entries and keys ≤ 200 characters (a paired device's whole `uiPrefs` record is capped at 64 KiB, `DEVICE_UI_PREFS_MAX_BYTES`: a write over it is refused for every pref in it). The sidebar prunes entries of folders, hosts and sessions that no longer exist (`pruneGroups`, `syncHosts`, `pruneFamilies`) only once a live read answered (`useSessionList().live`, never the last-known snapshot of the newest 150 rows) and the host list has loaded.
- Tests: `sidebar-memory.test.ts`, `sidebar-folds.test.ts`, `ui-sidebar-visible.test.ts`, "Sidebar remembered folds" in `Sidebar.test.tsx`, `device-prefs.test.ts`; `e2e/sidebar-memory.e2e.ts` (collapse, fold two projects, relaunch, read the boot shell's and the app's first frame). A test that mounts the sidebar resets `useSidebarFolds` in `afterEach`.

### Sidebar projects
- **A project is not its sessions** (the user's report, 21 Oct 2026: deleting a project's old sessions took the project off the sidebar). Each computer keeps a project list of its own, apart from its sessions, as Codex desktop keeps its saved workspace roots apart from its threads (its docs: hover a project › ⋯ › Remove, Add new project or ⌘O brings it back; an empty project's group reads "No chats"; issues openai/codex#18640 and #19615 name the stored keys). Claude desktop's Code tab (its `app.asar` read statically, never run; REA's analyzer timed out on its 6.7 MB chunk) and its docs group sessions by folder and keep folders only in saved environments; no separate project list was found there.
- Main owns it (`src/main/sidebar-projects.ts`, contract `src/shared/sidebar-projects.ts`, prefs key `sidebarProjects`: `{projects: [{path, addedAt, missing?}], removed, worktrees}`, never trimmed: a project leaves only by Remove and a removal is never forgotten). A folder enters when the person starts a new session there (`openTab` in `stores/tabs.ts`, local or a paired host's; a worktree names its main checkout) and by itself for every folder this computer's sessions ran in (`sync`, 1.5 s after the session index changes; its first pass lists the folders that had sessions before the list existed, each sorted by its newest session). Chats, the Work workspace (`~/.omp/work`), a file with no conversation yet and a folder that is gone never enter. It leaves only by the group menu's **Remove from sidebar** (`sidebar.menu.removeProject`), which deletes no file and no session: the folder goes to `removed`, its sessions list under no project (unless a tab holds one; the session picker still finds them), and only the person opening the folder again brings it back. The group menu's Delete deletes the project's sessions and the project stays, empty.
- An empty project shows its header with ⋯ and + and no row. A project whose folder is gone (`missing`, checked at every sync and read) stays, its name subtle with "Folder not found" in the tooltip and to screen readers, + disabled, Remove in its menu. Projects sort as before (pins, the dragged order, then recency), an empty one by `addedAt`.
- **A paired host's list is the host's own**: `MachineServices.sidebarProjects` (`host.projects.command {request}`, pushed as `host.projects.changed` → `SIDEBAR_PROJECTS_STATE` with the device) feeds the host's section (`SidebarDevices.tsx`, re-read each time its link comes up), whose group header has the same ⋯ › Remove. A device cannot ask the host's git, so the host's `sync` also sends `worktrees` (each session folder that is a linked worktree → its project) and the host section groups and removes by that project, never by the worktree's folder (an older host sends none: its sessions group by their own folder). The renderer store is `stores/sidebar-projects.ts` (`useProjectList(device, read)`, last read painted at the first frame through `lib/last-read.ts`).
- Pinned by `src/main/sidebar-projects.test.ts` (first pass lists folders with sessions and names worktree folders' projects, a deleted last session keeps the project, Remove sticks until the folder is opened, missing folders, 600 projects and removals kept across add, remove, sync and relaunch), the project-list case of `remote/host-services.test.ts`, the project cases of `Sidebar.test.tsx` (an empty project listed, delete keeps it, Remove local and on a host, a host worktree's session under its project and removed with it, a missing folder dimmed, a new session's folder added); captures `OMP_GUI_SIDEBAR_PROJECTS=<dir>` with `e2e/sidebar-projects-captures.e2e.ts` (dev harness `?projects=1`).

## Composer

User's rules (19 Oct 2026): **composer controls never move outside the composer box, and the phone composer never folds**; **the Run sheet is one line per row**. Never undo either.

### Run controls (Stop, Esc, queue)

Code: `lib/messages.ts` (`abortActiveTurn`, `stopFromEscape`, `restoreAbortedQueue`, `resumeRestoredQueue`), `stores/composer.ts`. Stock's semantics, nothing new on the wire.

- **One Stop per turn**: `composer.stopping` is set before `abort_and_restore_queue` goes out and held until the session's `isStreaming` goes false (10 s cap, `STOP_SETTLE_MS`), so a double click, a double Esc or Stop + Esc send one command.
- **Esc while a turn runs and the field holds text or a picture only arms** (`composer.stopArmedAt`, `DOUBLE_ESC_MS` 1.5 s; an edit of the draft disarms; the view says "Press Esc again to stop", `input.escAgainToStop`). A second Esc inside the window, or an empty field, stops. Popovers/menus/dialogs that consume the Esc never abort (`defaultPrevented`/`[role=dialog]` guards in `App.tsx`). The Stop button never asks twice.
- **Stop never releases the queue**: stock withdraws it. `restoreAbortedQueue` keeps each message its own `composer.restoredQueue` item (steers, then follow-ups, oldest first) while a view that shows them is mounted (`ComposerRestoredQueue` registers `restoredTrayMounted`): Resume sends the first as a `followUp`-behaviour prompt and the rest as follow-ups, "Put in the field" edits one, nothing is sent by itself. Without such a view they merge ahead of the draft with a blank line between and one toast.
- **Withdrawing a prompt that waits on the automatic mode choice returns at once** (`runAutoMode` `signal`, `composer.decidingMode` for the "deciding" cue); a decision the host makes afterwards is ended when it lands (`onLateDecision`).
- **⇧Tab is never ours** (keymap chords need ⌃/⌥/⌘; Shift+Tab is focus navigation). The thinking level cycles on `thinking.cycle` (⌥⇧T on both platforms, remappable in Settings › Keyboard and the palette).
- **Pictures sent as steers** are remembered by queue chip id in the queue store (`expectImages`, matched by text when the chip appears, dropped when an edit or a steer→follow-up move re-queues text only), so the pill and popover count them although stock's lanes are text only. A picture-only chip's "[Image]" is the UI language's word (`lib/queue-chip.ts`). `queue.moved` says where an edited message landed (the end of its lane).
- `lib/composer-blocked.ts` `composerBlockedBy` names what the cards above wait for (`ask` | `approval` | `plan`). Placeholder priority: blocked > editing queued > suggestion > follow-up > steer > idle.
- **A session activation hands the next key to the composer** (`lib/tab-focus.ts`, installed in `App.tsx`): after a tab or session change, focus on `<body>`, a sidebar button or a pane that just left moves to the active composer; a dialog, a text field, the terminal, the files editor and touch input are left alone. The move waits a frame for React's commit, and a printable key typed before that frame moves it at once so the key lands in the new composer, not the hidden pane's (`tab-focus.test.ts` "at the first typed key").
- **A shortcut or menu command given while the active tab changes waits for it** (`lib/tab-routing.ts` `waitForActiveTab`, `openingTab`; called first by `App.tsx`'s keymap dispatch and menu handler): while ⌘T's tab is not yet answered by main, or the selected tab's route has not landed, commands queue in order and run one at a time, each after the route the one before it started has landed; a command still waiting after `ROUTE_WAIT_MS` (2 s) is dropped. ⇧⌘]/⌥⌘] step from the tab the latest `switchTab` is on its way to (`steppingFromTabId`), since a switch to a tab that ran out of view first waits for its present. Before this a key in that gap was dropped or acted on the tab being left: ⌘W right after ⌘T closed the old tab (nightly 39525167897), and two quick ⇧⌘] under load landed on the same tab (runtime.e2e, 13 of 20 under `taskpolicy -c background`, 0 of 12 after). Pinned by `tab-routing.test.ts`, the stepping case of `tabs.test.tsx` and desktop.e2e's ⌘T-then-⌘W lifecycle.
- The queue is one pill in the send cluster (`ComposerQueuePill`, `[data-queue-pill]`) opening a popover (a bottom sheet on a phone): lane headings (`queue-lane.tsx`), the policy line from `steeringMode`/`interruptMode`, rows `Edit: <text>` with Steer now primary. Nothing is added to the box while messages wait (`e2e/composer-queue-view.e2e.ts` checks 0 px). Edit queued (`composer.editingQueued`, `ComposerQueueEdit.tsx`) takes the field over; Cancel/Esc restore the exact draft snapshot; Send becomes a save ✓ (`composerQueue.editSave`). Stop pauses: `ComposerRestoredQueue` is one 36 px row (chips, Resume, Discard). `ComposerQueueLive` is the single polite status region.
- Pinned by `messages.test.ts`, `auto-mode-client.test.ts`, `queue.test.ts`, `tab-focus.test.ts`, `ComposerRestoredQueue.test.tsx`, `e2e/composer-flows.e2e.ts`, `e2e/steer-send.e2e.ts`.

### Delegation strength

- The Run chip's Delegation section (`layout/composer/DelegationField.tsx`, mounted by `RunChip.tsx`; the phone's Run sheet shows it too; hidden when the omp cannot report a strength) sets Your settings / Solo / Assisted / Collaborative / Swarm per tab through `get_delegation`/`set_delegation` → bridge `modes/delegation.ts` (rules `delegation-core.ts`, presets `delegation-contracts.ts`).
- Each strength is stock `task.eager`, `task.maxConcurrency` and (Solo) `task.maxRecursionDepth = 0` held as runtime overrides, never written to config.yml; stock's own listeners rebuild the prompt and drop or restore the task tool.
- The last pick is prefs `delegationMode`, passed as `OMP_GUI_DELEGATION` on every spawn (`sidecar.ts`; omitted for "default") and applied inside the extension factory like the thinking default. A Settings write of one of those settings releases the strength.

### Working cue

- **Working state is a light over what is already on screen** (the user's call, 20 Oct 2026; supersedes the border comet and every border light before it: the left-edge bar's light, the masked ring, the two bars, the comet of pills). Prior art read first: Claude.app (`cds-shimmer-text-shine`, 3 s, a hold at each end), Claude Code and Codex CLI (a shimmer over the status word), ChatGPT and the Codex desktop app (`loading-shimmer`), Cursor (the agent's sentence animates); none uses a border light. One component and one stylesheet (`components/common/LiveShimmer.tsx`; `styles/components.css` `.omp-live-text`, `.omp-live-sweep`), one rule (`layout/live-work.ts` `liveWorkState`, `workingAgentCount`). **No new words, no new elements that take space**: a working session's **own title** shimmers (`LiveText` around the existing text: a sidebar session row through `SessionLiveTitle`, a paired host's row and a split pane's header title through `TabLiveTitle`/`useTabLive`, `layout/SessionLiveTitle.tsx`), and the **composer box's background** carries the same light (`LiveSweep`, `ComposerLiveSweep` mounted first in `.omp-composer-box`: an `aria-hidden` layer at `z-index: -1` under `isolation: isolate`, so typed text and every control sit above it; its band is accent at 11 %, low enough that a draft stays fully readable).
- States: `turn` (its own turn runs) the full light; `background` (the turn is idle, work it started still runs) the same animation and duration, lower contrast only (the title's band 62 % instead of 28 % of its colour, the box's 5 % instead of 11 %), so a switch never restarts it; `waiting` (a question or approval waits on the person; it wins) no sweep: the title turns `--sc-warning`, the box holds a still 7 % warning tint; `null` plain text, nothing drawn. Every sweep is 3 s (`--live-period`, `LIVE_PERIOD_MS`) with a hold at each end and starts on one shared phase (`--live-delay` = minus the document clock within the period, set when the work starts), so titles and the box sweep in step. Every light moves by `transform` alone, so the compositor draws each frame and nothing repaints: the box's is one band inside a clipped layer; a title's own glyphs turn transparent and a copy of its words (`LiveText` `text`, drawn by `.omp-live-shine-words::before` from `data-text`, so neither the DOM's text nor the accessible name doubles) sits in a window whose static mask holds the band, the window sliding across while the copy slides back by the same amount (the letters stay put). Both move in `steps(50)` over the 2.1 s crossing (`--live-steps`, about 24 a second): the masked window is drawn again only at a step and the compositor draws nothing in between (24.7 drawn frames a second instead of 47.7 on the harness). The earlier `background-position-x` under `background-clip: text` repainted every title every frame (+9.3 % of a GPU core with four panes streaming) and in Electron drew no title at all (a selected row in the background state showed only its chip). Nothing breathes by opacity, nothing changes layout, all rest while the window is hidden (`data-paused`). Reduced motion and **software compositing** (`html[data-gpu="software"]`, set by main after `app.getGPUInfo("complete")` reports `gpu_compositing` not enabled; `softwareCompositing()` in `gpu-fallback.ts`) run no animation: a working title is still text with an accent cast (55 %, 30 % for background work), the box a still 5 % (3 %) accent tint. Any new looping cue draws still under both. Between turns the Send button also gets a still ring with a hint tooltip (`ComposerSend`, `input.live.background`), stepped out of the way while the field holds a draft.
- Background work = main's `settling` (`IpcTabInfo.settling`, `IpcHostedSession.settling`; `runSettling` in `sidecar-run-state.ts`, `RunState.turn` tells a turn from the time after it: stock's `prompt_result.sessionSettled: false` keeps `status` `running` until `session_settled` for resume, Stop and the quit guard, and that covers its subagents, async shells and queued wakes for hidden and paired-host tabs alike, with no poll), plus the tab runtime's working subagents (read from that tab's roster store directly, no runtime provider around the card) and the sessions its agent opened that run (not waiting; its whole family, grandchildren and children on a paired device included, since the sidebar files every descendant under the root); a session no tab holds counts only its opened sessions. **Restraint**: only top-level session rows and pane headers wear the title light; a child row (subagent or opened session) stays a plain row whose status token is the working arc while it runs (below; waiting ⚠). The family chip shows its count once: while something runs, one filled still accent pill with the kind glyph(s) and the running count (`data-family-running`); with none running, the plain muted counts per kind (`data-family-agents`, `data-family-sessions`); the label keeps the totals. `data-pane-running` = the turn only; compacting and (re)starting keep the header's spinner.
- **The age slot** (the user's call, 21 Oct 2026): a session row's end shows the time since the session last did something, and while its work goes on (the same rule as the title's light: its own turn, stock's background work, its subagents, every session it opened, local or on a paired host) the **working arc** instead (`common/WorkingArc.tsx`, `SessionLiveAge`/`TabLiveAge` in `layout/SessionLiveTitle.tsx`, `data-session-working`). Waiting on the person is not working: the waiting token stays (`.omp-sidebar-approval`). The age stays in the slot, `visibility: hidden` (`.omp-live-age`), so the row keeps its width. A running family child's token is the same arc (`FamilyStatus`), its age back when it stops. The arc is two 140° arcs (head, tail) on opposite easings (head ease-out, tail ease-in: up to 133° apart, so one stroke of 140°–273°), advancing 180° more each 1.5 s turn so the stroke grows from a new place, over an 18 % track. The easing is sampled into generated keyframes (`omp-working-arc-head`/`-tail`: (turn + eased) × 360° + t × 180°, 12 samples a turn, two turns per 3 s `--arc-period`) played in `steps(3)` per sample: 24 positions a second on one grid every arc shares (`--arc-delay` from the document clock), so ten arcs are one 24 fps compositor stream. Two transforms, no dash animation (which repaints), at rest while the window is hidden (`data-paused`), a still 270° arc under reduced motion and `html[data-gpu="software"]`; `background` only fades its colour. Every age ticks on the shared clock (`useTimeAgo`; a family child's too, `TimeAgo`).
- **What the age reads**: `SessionInfo.modified` is the session's newest conversation message's own `timestamp` (stock helper `session-activity.ts`, read from the journal's end in a 64 KB window growing ×4 to 4 MB, cached per `mtime:size`), else the file's mtime. Stock lists the mtime, and every relaunch appends to sessions that did nothing (`session_exit`, an idle recap, `model_usage`, `title_change`, reports and restart notices, the interrupted-turn record whose message keeps the cut's time): on this Mac 11 of 23 sessions of the last three days had a mtime more than 2 min past their last message. Every reader of `modified` means recency (the sidebar's age and order, the session picker, project recency, the agent tools' listings), so the helper replaces it at the source. Pinned by `session-index-activity.test.ts` (a relaunch's appends leave the age; a reply past the first window; no message keeps the mtime).
- **No leading slot** (the user's call, 19 Oct 2026): a session row (local or a paired host's) leads with nothing, so its title starts at its group header's content edge (rows `padding-left: var(--sc-space-2)`, the header button's `pl-2`); its state is its title's light or its left-edge bar (waiting, error; an overlay, no reserved space), its words the tooltip and screen-reader text. One small mark sits at the row's end before the age (`data-sidebar-row-end`): an unseen finish as a ✓ (`data-sidebar-done`, a shape, not a colour alone), else a chat, pin, worktree (`data-sidebar-worktree`, see Worktree names) or fork glyph. A family child keeps the old relation to its parent's title (`SidebarSubagents.tsx` `INDENT`/`CONNECTOR`: connector 2 px left of it, glyph 6 px right, a sub-subagent one 16 px step more; an Autopilot run's child row one 16 px step in).
- Pinned by `ComposerLive.test.tsx` (states, no border light in the DOM or the stylesheet, every `omp-live-*` keyframe animating transform or opacity only, still forms), the working-title cases of `Sidebar.test.tsx`, `SidebarSubagents.test.tsx` and `SplitWorkspace.test.tsx`, `sidecar-pool.test.ts` ("tells a turn from background work"), `row-glow.e2e.ts` (fake omp: three running rows and a split's header, a roster subagent and stock's unsettled turn, two transform animations per working title, and every working title's words visible in five sampled frames of the sweep in turn, background, selected, split header, light, dark and reduced motion), `row-glow-harness.e2e.ts` (dev harness, desktop and an iPhone 15 drawer: the titles, the composer's sweep behind a draft, the row geometry; `OMP_GUI_WORKING_SHOTS=<dir>` writes every surface × theme × language with frame strips and the composer while typing in a turn), `composer-live.e2e.ts`, `background-tabs.e2e.ts` and the perf-gated `sidebar-edge-light.e2e.ts`. Cost: `e2e/flow-performance.e2e.ts` `grid` measures the cues while four panes stream, every animation running, the titles alone, none, then all again (`lightWindows` `all`/`titles`/`none`; an older build's comet is paused too). Measured 20 Oct 2026 (three runs each): three idle running rows cost the comet 0.3-0.4 % GPU / 0.3 % renderer, the background-clip title 0.6-0.7 / 0.6, the masked title 0.8-0.9 / 0.2 with 0 paints (170.7 paints/s before), 0.5-0.6 / 0.2 once stepped (grid, titles minus none: +20.2 → +9.0 % GPU); its GPU share is the masked layer drawn again whenever the words move inside the mask (0.3 % GPU with the mask turned off, a probe, not a usable look). Shimmering only one of a session's two titles (row or split header) measured no clear difference (grid titles minus none +8.9 vs +9.0, runs ±7).

### Modules

`layout/composer/`; `InputArea.tsx` only wires state and lays out; one concern per file.

- Views: `ComposerToolbar` (memoised: scalar props, stable handlers via `use-stable-callback.ts`, so typing does not re-render it), `RunChip` (+ `RunSettings.tsx`), `ComposerSheet`, `ComposerQueue.tsx`, `ComposerQueueEdit`, `queue-lane.tsx`, `ComposerRestoredQueue`, `ComposerUnderField`, `ComposerField`, `ComposerAttachments`, `CompletionList`, `PasteMenu`, `ComposerPickers`, `ComposerExpandedHeader`.
- Hooks `use-composer-*` (`field` owns the one `textareaRef`, `placeDraft`/`ownEdit`, `field-height.ts`; `keys`, `menu`, `commands`, `paste`, `attach`, `dictation`, `draft`, `history`, `events` for `omp:insert-mention`/`omp:fill-composer`, `suggestions`, `submit`).
- Helpers: `strip-roving.ts`, `paste-marker-edit.ts`, `keep-focus.ts`, `common/popover-focus.ts`, `lib/composer-actions.ts`, `lib/composer-blocked.ts`, `lib/tab-focus.ts`, `lib/hardware-keyboard.ts`, `lib/listbox-nav.ts` (the keyboard model of a listbox driven from a field: the composer's menus wrap at the ends, the model, session and branch pickers stop there).

### Toolbar (desktop/tablet)

- `RunChip.tsx` is the one trigger and popover for Mode (Plan/Goal/Autopilot/Auto mode, `ModesSection` of `ComposerModes.tsx`), Thinking, Tool approval, Delegation, Fast/Advanced/Model roles. `data-run-settings-trigger` is its hook; `data-active` paints it accent (never a utility-class tie, unlayered CSS: a `.bg-transparent` once beat the accent wash); the aria-label spells the whole state. Its trailing glyphs are the approval shield and fast/slow only: **no thinking or delegation icon on or beside it, on any shell** (the user's rule, 20 Oct 2026; both live in the popover and the aria-label). "Mode popover" elsewhere means the Mode section of this popover.
- Opened from the chip, the popover's Mode section shows the tab's first mode and follows it until the person picks a tab (`followsModes` in `RunChip.tsx`): on a run's new tab the Autopilot state can arrive after the popover opened (until then the tab shows no mode or the run's underlying Goal), and a section left on Plan or Goal had none of the run's controls (open the screen, End). Pinned by `RunChip.sheet.test.tsx`.
- The model chip reads `Model · Thinking` (`data-model-chip`); the model picker has the thinking row. Beside the provider's mark the chip drops the brand word the mark draws (`chipModelName` in `settings/providers/provider-marks.ts`: the company's first word or the mark's model family, `MARK_FAMILY` anthropic Claude / gemini Gemini / xai Grok; only when a word, not a number, follows: "Claude Opus 5.5" → "Opus 5.5", "Gemini 3 Pro", "Grok 5" and "GPT-6.1 Sol" stay; no mark or an OpenRouter name like "Anthropic: …" stays whole), and Auto thinking reads "· Auto" alone (a fixed level reads itself); the tooltip (`input.modelTooltipThinking`), the accessible name and the picker keep the full name and the effort Auto picked (the user's call, 20 Oct 2026). Pinned by `chip-model-name.test.ts`, the model chip cases of `ComposerToolbar.test.tsx` and `InputArea.queue-shorthand.test.tsx`.
- **The model picker opens on the composer and dims nothing** (the user's call, 22 Oct 2026): `ModelPicker` measures the focused pane's `.omp-composer-box` and opens with `Modal` `placement="above"` and that `anchor` (its bottom 8 px over the composer's top, its right edge on the composer's, no taller than the room above it: `--omp-dialog-room`) and `backdrop={false}` (no overlay colour, no blur; a click outside still closes). With no composer on screen (a page open) it opens at the top, still undimmed; a phone keeps its bottom sheet. Pinned by "the model picker sits on the composer and dims nothing" in `e2e/responsive-smoke.e2e.ts`.
- **Send and Stop are ONE button in one slot** (the user's report, 20 Oct 2026: a reserved Stop slot and lane-chip slot left an empty gap beside Send while idle). Rule `layout/composer/send-slot.ts` `sendSlot` (first match): dictation recording → stop-and-send arrow; editing a queued message → save ✓; a turn runs (or a prompt waits on the auto mode choice) with an empty draft → Stop (■, `Esc`, `abortActiveTurn`: resume and scheduled retry unchanged); phone dictation with an empty draft → mic; else Send (disabled with nothing to send; while a turn runs it steers or follows up as before). The stop/send/save states are one `IconButton` node (`data-send-state`), so a state change swaps icon and name in the same box and nothing moves; `ComposerSend`'s background ring and hint tooltip wrap it (off while it is Stop, `cue`). **Stopping while a draft is typed**: Esc still arms then stops (double Esc), and every non-Stop state while a turn runs carries `stopHint`: a right click, or a long press on touch (touch has no Esc; `lib/long-press-menu.ts` makes it `contextmenu`, as the suggestion chips' "Send now"), opens a one-item menu (a bottom sheet on touch, `keepFocus`) whose Stop ends the turn and leaves the draft in the field; a small ■ inside the button (`data-send-stop-hint`, decorative, absolutely placed) and its description (`aria-describedby`: `input.send.stopHintTouch` / `input.send.stopHintDesktop`, the desktop tooltip too) say so. Chosen over a second ■ beside Send, which brought back a box that appears as typing starts and moves the row. The Steer/Follow-up chip renders only while a turn runs, before the `flex-1` spacer, so it takes free room and moves nothing; nothing is reserved for it while idle. Labels fold by the toolbar's own `omp-toolbar` container (860/740/620/500 px), not the viewport. Pinned by `send-slot.test.ts`, `ComposerToolbar.test.tsx` (state table, one node, right click → Stop never sends), `ComposerToolbar.phone.test.tsx` (long press → sheet → Stop), `e2e/composer-toolbar.e2e.ts` (no shift, no idle gap), `e2e/mobile-composer.e2e.ts` (running + draft + long press → Stop, draft kept).
- Below a 440 px toolbar (a pane split in four, 200 % zoom) the bash/python and queue badges give their room back (`display: none`; `e2e/desktop.e2e.ts` "narrow windows and 200 percent zoom"). There the row (and the send cluster) may also wrap, outside the phone shell: at 200 % zoom in a minimum-size window beside the docked sidebar the toolbar has ~110 px, and a row that cannot wrap pushed Send past the window's edge (regressed when the row became `flex-nowrap`). A row that fits is unchanged.
- Popovers close on Tab (`common/popover-focus.ts`); the context ring opens on click/Enter only. The palette's History/Expand/Attach go through `omp:composer-action` (`lib/composer-actions.ts`).
- **Context ring** (`ContextUsagePopover.tsx`, the same control on desktop and phone; the user's rule, 20 Oct 2026): a small ring whose arc is the used share of the window, never percentage text. `contextTone` (`lib/context-usage.ts`) colours it: danger at stock's resolved auto-compaction point (`useCompactionPoints`, this model's `tokens`), warning from `NEAR_COMPACTION` (85 %) of it; with auto-compaction off, warning at 80 % and danger at 95 % of the window; `data-context-ring` carries the tone. The numbers are the aria-label and the tooltip ("컨텍스트 62% · 124k / 200k · 160k에서 압축", `contextUsage.ring*`); the tooltip stays off while the breakdown shows (`Tooltip hidden`), which also names the point. Arc and colour ease once on a change (`.omp-context-ring`), no loop, none under reduced motion or `html[data-gpu="software"]`. Phone: `variant="sheet"` (breakdown in a `ComposerSheet`, `keepFocus`). Pinned by `ContextUsagePopover.test.tsx`, `ComposerToolbar.phone.test.tsx`.

### Phone composer

- The box is always open: a full-width field row over ONE compact controls row inside the box, the tablet's toolbar at phone size: `+`, the Run chip (a glyph: the mode's icon, the mode's name while one is on, except a live Autopilot run, whose state the work strip already says (`data-mode="autopilot"`; the dock's Autopilot row gives way beside the strip too, `mobile.css`, pinned by `e2e/mobile-autopilot-status.e2e.ts`), a warning pip while tool approval is full access; its sheet is the Run sheet below, which holds Thinking and Delegation: no chips of their own), the mode extras, the lane chip while a turn runs, the model chip (`ProviderMark` and a name that ellipsizes inside its own 3.5 em box; the mark alone under 340 px of toolbar and while a turn runs), the context ring (`ContextUsagePopover variant="sheet"`, breakdown in a `ComposerSheet`), the one Send/Stop button | mic (while Stop holds the button, the mic moves into the row).
- Labels fold to icons by the toolbar's own width (`omp-toolbar`). Everything before the action button is a sideways scroller (`omp-composer-scroll`, phone-only wrapper in `ComposerToolbar`; 8 px of padding taken back by the margin keeps the 44 px reach; chips sit 4 px apart with `--hit-gap-x: 4px` so no chip's reach covers a neighbour). The button and the queue pill sit outside it at the row's end, never scrolling or moving. No ⋯ toggle, status row or settings sheet outside the box exists (a ⋯ fold toggle and a 44 px status chip row above the box were both rejected by the user).
- **Nothing runs under the action button** (a Galaxy, 390 px, ko once overflowed 67 px): idle at 390 and 412 the whole row fits with no scroll (the scroller ends 4 px before the button); running (an icon-only lane chip appears) it fits at 390 with the model as its mark; at 360 the model is its mark alone and a running row may scroll, clipped before the button. `e2e/phone-run-sheet.e2e.ts` "keeps clear of Stop and Send" (360/390/412, en/ko): scroller ends before the button, model chip ≤ 130 px with an ellipsis, every control keeps ≥ 90 % of its taps.
- The `+` sheet carries attach/photos/history/skills only, plus Dictation while `stt.enabled` is off; expanding the field is ⌃G and the palette, a goal is the Mode popover's.
- Controls keep the field focused with `onMouseDown` `preventDefault` only, never `pointerdown` (it swallows the click in WebKit; `keep-focus.ts`).
- The field spans the box's inner width at every width: a Korean sentence wraps only where it stops fitting the field (`e2e/mobile-composer.e2e.ts` "wraps a long Korean line only at the box's inner width" measures the sentence in the face the machine draws Korean with and expects one line exactly when it is no wider than the field: its 25 characters are 354 px in Malgun Gothic, a full em a syllable, so two lines in the 340 px field at 390 on Windows; the old one-row grid wrapped Korean at half the box on a Galaxy S25 Ultra). It is capped at `min(40 % of the pane, visible − 120 px)` (`field-height.ts`) and refits on pane/visualViewport height changes. Touch sets `--omp-scrollbar-size: 0`.
- **A bottom sheet is ONE scroller with the platform's scrollbar** (v0.40.0: a Galaxy's Run sheet showed two tracks side by side). The sheet (`.omp-action-sheet`) scrolls; the surface inside it and any `overflow-y-auto` list inside that (the queue's `max-h-72` list) neither cap nor scroll on a phone, and a phone tablist scrolls only sideways (`overflow-y: hidden`; a touch hit area made it scroll vertically). Every `::-webkit-scrollbar` rule is gated on `:where(html:not([data-input="touch"]))` (`global.css`, `components.css`): any matching rule turns Chrome's overlay bar into a drawn 9 px track, which Chrome on Android shows. Pinned by `e2e/phone-run-sheet.e2e.ts` "every composer sheet scrolls in one container" (Run, context, model, + and queue sheets; Pixel 8 and WebKit iPhone 15).
- Idle chrome budget: `e2e/responsive-smoke.e2e.ts` `CHROME_SHARE_MAX` 19.4 % (measured 18.4 % at 390x844, plus one; the user chose the open composer over a lower share).
- First-paint shell follows the phone composer: `COMPOSER_BOX_PX.phone` 77 px (`src/shared/boot-shell-geometry.ts`) and `--omp-scrollbar-size: 0` under `(pointer: coarse)` in `scripts/boot-shell.ts`; regenerate `web.html` with `bun run gen:icons`; `e2e/no-loading.e2e.ts` compares the boot box with the React box within 2 px.

### Run sheet (one line per row)

User's rule, from phone screenshots (descriptions under every row, a full-width select with a caption, loose paragraphs, 2-line radio rows). The desktop/tablet popover is the same content at `w-96`.

- Every row of the sheet, the Mode section and Advanced is a lead glyph and its control on ONE line; every explanation sits behind an (i) glyph (`SettingInfo`, text kept in the DOM): no `data-setting-desc`, no `<p>`, no footer line.
- Switches are **tiles** (`common/Switch.tsx` `SwitchTile`: lucide glyph, a short label that truncates, (i), the switch; the whole tile toggles), two to a row in a `data-switch-grid` (`grid-cols-2`): Fast | Slow, Ultrafast (`SpeedFields`), then in Advanced (`AdvancedAndRoles`, opens by itself while a value is not the default) Auto-compact | Extended, Auto-retry | Prewalk, Batch steers | Interrupt. Glyphs: Zap, Turtle, Gauge, Shrink, Maximize2, RotateCw, Rocket, Layers, OctagonX. Fast's "not on this model" is a dimmed glyph plus a sentence in the (i) text. Tile labels ("Auto-compact", "자동 압축") are the old sentences' short forms; the sentences are the (i) texts (`input.more.*Desc`).
- Choices are **segmented rows** (`ChoiceRow.tsx`: the group's glyph, a radio row of short labels, (i) holding the group's name and the held option's effect; the full name is each segment's accessible name and tooltip; arrows move focus, `selectOnArrow` also picks): thinking level (`ThinkingLevelField`, `input.thinking.short.*`, variant `row`; the model picker keeps `segmented`), tool approval (`ApprovalModeField`, shield glyphs, `input.approval.short.*`; full access still asks first through the host's dialog), delegation (`DelegationField`, `input.delegation.short.*`; its "applies from the next turn" note is in the (i)). Short labels abbreviate the long names (ko/en parity).
- The compaction point is one line with its value in the label ("압축 시점 · 850k", `ContextSizeField`, the only select left, compact at the right, its other-layer note in the (i)).
- `mobile.css` bounds the reaches (`[data-choice-row]`, `[data-switch-grid]`: `--hit-gap` 4 px) except a choice row's (i), which sits 12 px further from the segments and reaches 10 px each way (a 36 px target, as in Settings' content) and gives a segment and a tile the touch row height.
- Tests: `RunChip.sheet.test.tsx` (no visible description or paragraph, an (i) per explanation, tiles two to a row with glyphs, three segmented rows, one select), `ThinkingControl`/`ApprovalControl`/`DelegationField`/`ContextSizeField` tests, `ComposerToolbar.phone.test` (no thinking or delegation icon, the context ring), `e2e/phone-run-sheet.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_RUN_SHEET=<dir>` writes captures at 390 light/dark × en/ko; at 360/390/412 the sheet has no overflow, every tile label stays on one line, tiles pair up, a segmented row is ≤ 56 px, every switch and segment keeps ≥ 85 % of its own taps; the desktop popover keeps its width and cuts no label).

### Input mechanics

- The field is uncontrolled while typing (React 19 re-syncs a controlled textarea's `defaultValue` on every key, which cut ⌘Z to one character per press): the draft store is the source of truth and every composer write goes through `placeDraft`.
- A `[Paste #N, +L lines]` marker is atomic: Backspace/Delete/word/line deletes that touch it remove marker and blob as one step (`paste-marker-edit.ts`); a dangling `[Paste #N` never reaches the agent.
- `@` lists up to 20 000 files (`MENTION_FS_MAX_ENTRIES`) at depth 16 (`MENTION_FS_DEPTH`, `input-area-utils.ts`) ranked by `pathMatcher` (file name, word starts, CamelCase, Hangul initials); ←/→ keep the menu while inside the token (`arrowKeepsMenu`). The completion menu and the history overlay share `lib/listbox-nav.ts`; the field is `role=combobox` while a menu exists.
- Attachment strips (`strip-roving.ts`): one tab stop each, arrows, Delete/Backspace, Backspace on an empty field focuses the last attachment, "Remove all" from 2.
- The draft flushes on `visibilitychange`/`pagehide` and on unmount.
- A draft past 8 000 characters (`LONG_DRAFT_CHARS`, `ComposerField.tsx`) is set in the system face (`[data-long]`): Inter Variable was reshaped for the whole text on every key.
- `compositionend` followed within 40 ms by Enter is ignored (iOS Safari's order, `POST_COMPOSITION_MS`, `lib/ime.ts`).

### Dictation and steer box

- The take shows as a bar under the field (`ComposerUnderField`, `DictationOverlay.tsx`, `use-composer-dictation.ts`, `lib/voice.ts`), never over the draft. A failed take keeps a `failure` kind (denied, no-device, in-use, busy, empty, no-model, failed): one sentence plus a Details toggle (none for busy/empty); only no-model offers Open Settings.
- The + menu lists "Dictation" while `stt.enabled` is off and opens Settings (`agent:setting-stt.enabled`) without writing it.
- The subagent steer box (`SteerSubagentForm`) grows like the main field (`fitFieldHeight`) and is never `disabled` while sending (`aria-busy`).

### Enter on a touch input

- `useShellInput() === "touch"` (desktop byte for byte unchanged): a soft keyboard's return key adds a line and only the Send button (or ⌘↵ / the follow-up chord) sends; `enterkeyhint="enter"` on the field.
- A physical keyboard on a tablet gets the desktop's Enter back: `lib/hardware-keyboard.ts` latches, for the page's life, the first key only such a keyboard makes (Esc, Tab, arrows, Home/End/Page keys, F-keys, a chord with ⌘ ⌃ ⌥, Shift with a named key such as Shift+Enter; never a letter, Backspace or Enter, which some soft keyboards report with a `code`).
- Settings › Appearance & input › "Enter sends on touch keyboards" (`displayPreferences.mobileEnterSends`, per device, default off) makes touch Enter send.
- The IME guard runs first, so a composition's Enter is never a send or a newline rule. In `!`/`$` shell drafts the field turns autocorrect, autocapitalize and spellcheck off.
- Pinned by `lib/hardware-keyboard.test.ts`, `InputArea.touch-enter.test.tsx`, `e2e/mobile-enter.e2e.ts` (Chromium + WebKit, en/ko).

### Design record (19 Oct 2026)

Decided by prototype (three variants each, scored on taps, visible state, density, layout jumps, touch/a11y, ko + narrowest width, stock semantics kept):

- **Toolbar → one Run chip and a toolbar-owned container** (19/21 vs 14 and 16): 6 controls instead of 8, Thinking in 2 taps instead of 3, nothing moves when a turn starts (the old row wrapped to 70 px at 340 px), Plan mode moves nothing to its left. Its reserved Steer/Stop slots were replaced on 20 Oct 2026 by the one Send/Stop button and a lane chip in the free room (see Toolbar).
- **Queue → pill and popover** (15/21, the only variant winning the two heaviest criteria): the transcript moves 0 px when items queue (tray 75/83 px, in-box 40/44 px); price is 2-3 taps for lane switching and editing.
- The prototyped phone status chip row is **superseded by the user's rule** (controls stay in the box, the phone composer never folds); the status row and ⋯ sheet are gone.
- Not decided by prototype: undo granularity, atomic paste markers, `@` ranking, keyboard route to attachments, paste menu anchoring, strip cap, drop target, focus after a tab switch, draft flush on page hide.

### Tests

- E2E: `e2e/composer-toolbar.e2e.ts`, `composer-queue-view.e2e.ts`, `mobile-composer.e2e.ts`, `mobile-enter.e2e.ts`, `phone-run-sheet.e2e.ts`.
- Component: `InputArea.queue-run`, `InputArea.touch-enter`, `ComposerToolbar.test` / `ComposerToolbar.phone.test`, `RunChip.sheet.test`, `ComposerQueue.test`, `ComposerRestoredQueue.test`, `field-height.test`, `use-completion-menu.menu.test`.

## Smart features

All three bridge features (prompt suggestions, automatic titles, automatic mode) live in `resources/gui-bridge/modes/`, each as dependency-free `*-contracts.ts` + rules in `*-core.ts` + behaviour in `*-engine.ts`/`*.ts`, each with a capability flag. They never use the session model silently and never fall back to the main model; a missing model, credentials, refusal or offline state is a quiet `unavailable`/none, never a toast.

### Prompt suggestions
- Built in, Settings › Agent behavior. Prefs `nextPromptSuggestions` (default **on**) and sub-switch `nextPromptInline` (default on). Files: `next-prompt-contracts.ts`, `next-prompt-core.ts`, `next-prompt-engine.ts` behind `next-prompt.ts`.
- Plumbing = the reply language: `OMP_GUI_NEXT_PROMPT` JSON at spawn, `next_prompt_set` on change and on bridge connect to every live local sidecar and hosted tab; a paired host's tab follows the host's pref.
- Model: stock's `ComposerPredictionController` runs only in its TUI and `runEphemeralTurn` can only use the session model, so the engine calls stock `completeSimple` on the model stock's `smol` role resolves to (`resolveRoleChain` over `roleCandidatePool`, credentials via `modelRegistry.resolver`). Reasoning off, ≤ 512 output tokens, one retry without the cap when a transport refuses it. NEVER the main model. No model / no credentials / refused / offline → `unavailable` frames, shown as a muted `role=status` line under the composer plus a bridge debug log, never a toast.
- When: one request per finished turn (`agent_end`). Regenerated (debounced `SUGGEST_DEBOUNCE_MS` 800 ms, gated by `gateSuggestion`: enabled, idle, main agent, empty composer, a reply exists, state key moved) after a compaction, a finished user `!` command (renderer `refresh_next_prompt`), a session switch and an emptied draft. Typing (`set_next_prompt_draft`, from `use-idle-compaction-draft.ts`), a new run or the switch going off aborts the request; an answer whose moment moved meanwhile (new messages, work started/ended) is dropped and asked again.
- **Running work does NOT suppress suggestions** (a delegating session has jobs running at almost every turn end, so suppression would switch the feature off where it is used most); the model is told the work is underway and suggests waiting for, checking or steering it, or something unrelated.
- **Nothing to approve** (reported: after the assistant steered a session it opened and said so, the suggestion was "좋아 그 방향으로 가"; on a replay of that moment 2 of 7 runs showed such a line, 0 of 7 after the fix, `src/main/bridge-next-prompt-real.test.ts`, opt-in `OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1` with `NEXT_PROMPT_BEFORE_REF=<ref>`):
  - Sessions the main agent opened with the control tools run in their own tabs, so stock's jobs never list them. Main's `AgentControlRuntime` sends the opener's tab `set_next_prompt_children` (→ bridge `next_prompt_children`, a free command) with the running or waiting items of the work board's review list each time that list is pushed (only on a change, at most `MAX_NEXT_PROMPT_CHILDREN` 20); `<app-state>` shows them as `sessions-working-now` / `sessions-waiting-for-an-answer`.
  - `awaitsDecision` (in code, no model): an `ask` still open, or the reply's final paragraph ends in a question or puts a choice (`할까요`, `골라 주세요`, `should I`, `which do you prefer` …). `<app-state>` says `last-reply: asks the user to decide …` or `… reports work done or underway; it asks the user nothing`, and the rules allow approving only in the first case.
  - With no decision pending the cleaner drops a line made only of approval words (`isApprovalOnly`, `APPROVAL_WORDS`: "좋아 그 방향으로 가", "그렇게 해", "진행해", "ㅇㅋ 고", "go ahead", "sounds good, proceed"); any line naming something ("진행 상황 알려줘") stays.
  - A `write` to `xd://…` (a tool device) is not a changed file in `<app-state>`.
- Prompt content:
  - First `<user-style>` with ONE line only: `register: 반말 …` / `register: 존댓말 …` when most of the user's last 6 typed messages (`REGISTER_MESSAGES`) end in one Korean register (`userRegister` over the first lines of `sentTexts`, which leaves out stock's `synthetic` and `attribution: "agent"` user messages, slash commands and `<…>` blocks; `koreanRegister` majority, a tie names none; `koreanRegisterOf`: polite = ends in 요/니다/니까/죠 except nouns like 필요, casual = a listed 해체/해라체/음슴체 last syllable such as 줘, 해, 래, 지, 음, 임, anything else neutral).
  - **NEVER include excerpts of the user's messages**: given six excerpts the small model re-asked them as new requests (v0.32.2: 9 of 16 restated with six, 0 of 16 with none, register held).
  - Then the transcript tail (the reply being followed up clipped to 1800 chars, older ones to 700) plus a compact `<app-state>` read from the live session (last failed tool/command and exit, files changed this turn, open plan/todos, a question answered this turn, the main agent's running subagents and background jobs, a subagent's own included, as `running-now` via stock `getAsyncJobTree` — a subagent with the intent of the spawning `task` call —, goal, whether tests ran/failed, the user's `!` command, compaction).
  - Rules: stock `prompts/system/composer-prediction-user.md` voice/follow-up lines (write as the user), exactly 3 different next steps (asked for "up to 3" the cheap model often wrote one, so no chips showed), each a message the user sends to the assistant (never the assistant's voice, never addressed to the user, never offering/promising work), in the user's own voice, never repeat/reword/re-ask what they sent, use the `<user-style>` register, 3–8 words within the limits below, nothing already done, started, planned or handed to a subagent.
- **The register is enforced in code** (v0.32.1 voice rules alone still produced 존댓말 for a user who writes "배포해줘"). `cleanCandidateLine` / `cleanCandidates`, whatever the model writes:
  - a line in the assistant's voice (`isAssistantVoice`, conservative: ko `드릴게요/드릴까요/드리겠습니다`, `둘게요/두겠습니다`, `보신다면/하신다면/원하시면`, `알려 주시면`; en a leading `I'll`, `I will`, `Shall I`, `Would you like`, `Do you want me to`, `Let me` but not `Let me know`/`see`) is dropped whole;
  - `matchRegister`: for a casual user each sentence's polite end becomes its 반말 form by a fixed list (주세요 → 줘, 되나요 → 돼, 죠 → 지, and 요 after a 해체 syllable: 해요, 줄래요, 할까요, 있나요, 좋네요 …); any other polite end (니다, 세요, 예요) or a remaining honorific/humble word (주시, 하세, 드리, 제가, 저희 …) drops the line. For a polite user only 반말 ends a noun never has gain their polite form (줘 → 주세요, 돼 → 돼요, ㄹ래/ㄹ까 → +요, 지? → 죠?) and nothing is dropped: **a polite user is never pushed to 반말**. No register named → the line is left alone;
  - length: over `MAX_CANDIDATE_CHARS_CJK` 40 (any Hangul, Han or kana) / `MAX_CANDIDATE_CHARS` 60 keeps its first sentence when that fits (`fitCandidateLength`), else is dropped — never cut at a comma/word (a cut sends a different, unfinished request);
  - mojibake lines are dropped (see Mojibake from the model);
  - last, a line restating a message the user already sent is dropped (`SentMessages.isCopy` over every typed message of the session): F1 of a weighted longest common subsequence of units (each Hangul/Han/kana char, each Latin/digit run; a text's first 400), weighted by rarity across the sent messages (IDF), so shared rare words count, shared endings/everyday words hardly do, and Korean rewording still matches. `COPY_SIMILARITY` 0.6 against the latest message at any length, and against ANY sent message only from `COPY_MIN_UNITS` 14 units on (short generic requests recur and stay worth suggesting).
- Display: the best of up to 3 ranked one-line candidates is the placeholder (Tab/→); the rest are chips (↓ from an empty composer enters them, ←/→, Enter/click fills; Alt+1..3 fills directly; up to `min(100%, 44em)` wide so a 40-char Korean candidate shows whole, wrapping to a second row, full text as tooltip). Per-session memory in `stores/next-prompt.ts`: submit or a run clears it, a deleted draft brings it back.
- **Inline completion** (`complete_inline` → `next_prompt_inline`, same cheap model, ≤ 512 tokens, `InlineScheduler`): ghost text after the caret for a single-line draft of ≥ `INLINE_MIN_DRAFT_CHARS` 8 with the caret at its end, `INLINE_DEBOUNCE_MS` 600 ms after the last keystroke, at most one request start per `INLINE_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` 2 s (≤ 30 a minute, answers cached per draft; `e2e/next-prompt.e2e.ts` scripts 10 typing bursts in a minute and sees 10 requests). Aborted on every keystroke; never during IME composition, with a slash/`@` menu or paste choice open, for drafts starting `/`, `!`, `$`, `->`/`=>`, or a draft stock's `shouldSkipHistory` calls secret. Tab accepts, Esc dismisses.
  - Request carries the same register line (no excerpts); rules ask for the whole message, the draft restated character for character then continued on its own topic, one piece of the same sentence, never the assistant's words.
  - `cleanInlineReply` takes what follows the restated draft (`inlineDraftWindow`; trailing spaces of a draft ending in one are not doubled). A reply that does not start with the draft as typed shows nothing: **the user's text is never rewritten**. The ghost must be ≤ `INLINE_MAX_CHARS_CJK` 20 / `INLINE_MAX_CHARS` 50 (longer shows nothing, never cut), no role label, tag, fence or second sentence. Then the assembled message (draft + ghost) is judged — no assistant voice, no leaving the register (an opposite ending or, for a casual user, an honorific/humble word), no restatement of a sent message; each check counts only what the ghost brought. (Why the whole message is restated: asked for the continuation alone, 9 of 12 glued onto the last word.)
  - Debug log `next-prompt inline` records model, time, whether a ghost showed and the register, NEVER the text. `next_prompt_state.requests` counts both kinds.
- The user's own `next-prompt` extension is found through stock's loaded-extension list (`legacyExtension`); while the built-in is on the renderer ignores its widget; with the built-in off or unsupported the widget path works as before.
- Tests: `src/main/bridge-next-prompt-stock.test.ts` (smol resolution, legacy detection, reply cleaning vs stock's `parseComposerPrediction`), `bridge-next-prompt-core.test.ts`, `bridge-next-prompt.test.ts`; opt-in `OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1` `e2e/real-next-prompt.e2e.ts`. Loaders `config/model-roles`, `config/model-resolver`, `modes/controllers/input-controller`, `pi-ai` in `capabilities.ts` / `health.ts`.
- **Suggestions on touch** (`useShellInput() === "touch"`; desktop unchanged):
  - No Tab/→/↓/Alt+1..3/Esc, so `use-composer-suggestions.ts` builds the chips = best candidate FIRST, then the runner-ups (≤ `MAX_NEXT_PROMPT_CHIPS` 3), drawn by `NextPromptChips` as **one sideways-scrolling row** (`.omp-next-chips[data-touch]` in `mobile.css`: `nowrap`, `overflow-x: auto`, 32 px high, 28 px chips, scrollbar hidden, `--hit-gap-y: 4px` because an overflow container clips the global 44 px reach). The row sits just above the composer box (never inside). Nothing with no suggestion; hidden while typing, streaming or with a menu open.
  - The best candidate is **never also the placeholder** (`placeholderPrompt` undefined on touch; the placeholder stays the idle hint); no `Kbd` hints, `aria-keyshortcuts`, "to accept" chip or tooltip titles.
  - A **tap** fills the field, caret at the end, NOT sent (`pickChip` → `placeDraft`). A **long press** (the shell turns it into `contextmenu`) arms the chip and shows a "Send now" / "바로 보내기" button (`input.chips.sendNow`) that sends it through `send(text)`; any other pointer-down disarms.
