  - **Inline ghost completion is OFF on touch** (`useInlineCompletion` gets `enabled: … && !touchInput`; no `complete_inline` request is ever made): the phone keyboard has its own suggestion strip, Korean IME composition keeps the draft "composing" so a ghost would shift every keystroke, and no clean accept gesture exists. The `nextPromptInline` switch still governs desktop.
  - Pinned by `InputArea.next-prompt-touch.test.tsx` (chip tap fills without sending, long press → Send now, first chip not the placeholder, no kbd/Tab hint, no `complete_inline`) and `e2e/mobile-suggestions.e2e.ts` (Pixel 8 emulation on the dev harness, which exposes `window.__stores.nextPrompt`; `OMP_GUI_SUGGEST_TOUCH=<dir>` writes the en/ko light captures).

### Mojibake from the model
- v0.33.0 showed UTF-8 read as Windows-1252 ("ì•Œê² ì–´ …" for "알겠어 …") in the placeholder and a chip. Nothing in OMPdesk decodes that text (bridge socket, omp stdout and IPC are UTF-8 end to end); the model itself wrote those characters. So OMPdesk **refuses, never repairs** (a model rarely spells every byte: Windows-1252 has no character for 0x81, 0x8D, 0x8F, 0x90, 0x9D; a no-break space 0xA0 becomes a plain space once whitespace is folded).
- `isUtf8Mojibake` (`resources/gui-bridge/mojibake.ts`, dependency-free) flags text with two or more whole UTF-8 sequences spelled in Windows-1252 characters (three- or four-byte ones, or Latin-1 letters led by Â/Ã). Real Latin-script text never forms them ("„Straße“", "café…", "ação" stay clean; pinned by `src/main/bridge-mojibake.test.ts`).
- Where applied: `cleanCandidateLine` drops the line; `cleanInlineReply` shows no ghost; automatic titles keep the current name (`concise` in `auto-title-engine.ts`, the shorter retry too); the idle recap is neither shown nor journaled (`session-recap-core.ts`, checked before `deliver`).

### Automatic session titles
- Built in, Settings › Agent behavior › Session titles. Prefs `autoTitle` and sub-switch `autoTitleEvolve` ("keep the title up to date as work changes"), both default **on**. Files `auto-title-contracts.ts`, `auto-title-core.ts`, `auto-title-engine.ts` behind `auto-title.ts`; capability `autoTitle`. Plumbing as prompt suggestions: `OMP_GUI_AUTO_TITLE` JSON at spawn, `auto_title_set` on change and on bridge connect to every live local sidecar and hosted tab; a paired host's tab follows the host's pref.
- **Stock machinery only, never the session model for a first title**: `AgentSession.generateTitle` (stock's `generateSessionTitle`, `tiny` → `commit` → `smol` role chain; the session model only as stock's own retry fallback after those failed) names the operator's first message (`operatorTitleText`); `renameTitle(title)` adds the card per `title.icons` (OMPdesk holds `boring`: no card, no card request — see Held stock defaults). A message the title model declines (a greeting) is retried from the conversation after the turn with `renameTitle()`. Main agent only; subagents are excluded.
- A brief another session's agent sent (`<ompdesk-agent-message>`, an `ompdesk_session_open` or Autopilot child) is named from its `Title:` line, else its body (`titleInput` in `auto-title-core.ts` over `memory-prompt.ts` `unwrapPrompt`), because stock's `isLowSignalTitleInput` strips the whole tag block and declined it, which left such a session untitled until its one long turn ended (pinned by `bridge-auto-title*.test.ts`).
- **Origin is stock's own record**: `SessionManager.titleSource` and the journal's `title_change` entries (`source`, `trigger`). The one case stock cannot say, "a fresh automatic title over a typed one" (`setSessionName(…,"auto")` is refused over source `user`), is written as source `user` with `trigger: "ompdesk-auto"` (`TITLE_TRIGGER`) and read back by `titleIsAuto` (`auto-title-core.ts`). Nothing lives in a GUI-only file.
- **Language, length, form**: stock's `title-system.md` is English-only and asks for 3-6 imperative words, so the model answered English and long. The engine sets the session's TITLE_SYSTEM.md slot (`setTitleSystemPrompt`, read by `generateTitle`, `renameTitle` and stock's replan refresh; the card request passes its own prompt) to stock's own `title-system.md` (`ompPrompt` + `prompt.render`, never copied) plus one line (`titleLanguageLine`): the language the user writes in first (for a non-English reply language, `currentReplyLanguage()`, that language when unclear); a plain noun-phrase summary, 2–4 words (`TITLE_MAX_WORDS`), ≤ 12 chars in Korean/Chinese/Japanese (`TITLE_MAX_CJK_CHARS`) and 30 otherwise (`TITLE_MAX_CHARS`), spaces included; subject and head noun kept, modifiers and generic words dropped; two `COMPRESSIONS` examples ("로그인 비밀번호 에러 메시지 버그 수정" → "로그인 오류 수정", "CSV export empty with filters" → "Empty CSV export"); no punctuation, quotes or emoji; identifiers and names as written. Set at attach, on `reply_language_set` (`onReplyLanguageChange`) and again before each request (stock's headless `/move` re-resolves the slot). Reply language off/unset/English → same line without the language fallback (the title stays in the user's language).
- **Over the limits** (`titleFits`), while the line is in effect: ONE retry, never more, never on the first pass, on a stronger model than the cheap chain (the cheap chain answered its own retry with the same 13–16-char titles): stock's `default` role (`roleCandidatePool` + `resolveRoleChain`), else the session's model, the first with credentials (`modelRegistry.getApiKey`/`resolver`), via stock `completeSimple`, reasoning off, `RETRY_MAX_TOKENS` 256, temperature 0, `cacheRetention: "none"` (Anthropic's 1 h cache write doubled a one-off request's cost).
  - System prompt = the slot's prompt + `titleShorterLine` (the too-long answer named, ≤ 12/30 chars, subject and head noun kept, modifiers and generic tails such as 화면, 메시지, 로직, 기능, 관련, "with …" dropped, all four `COMPRESSIONS` as examples); message = the operator's first message, or the answer itself when stock built the context for `renameTitle()` (✨, evolving). The reply's `<title>` goes through stock `normalizeGeneratedTitle`; counted in `requests`; a failed retry keeps the first answer.
  - The retry wins unless longer than the first answer (a tie goes to the retry) and is kept whole up to the hard cap (`TITLE_HARD_CJK_CHARS` 14, `TITLE_HARD_CHARS` 32, any word count). Past it `conciseTitle` cuts whole words, NEVER an ellipsis: in Korean/Chinese/Japanese generic words go first (`CJK_FILLER`: 화면, 메시지, 로직, 기능, 관련, 부분), then the leading words (the head noun comes last: a cut from the end turned "작업 현황판 화면 디자인" into "작업 현황판 화면"); otherwise the trailing ones, never ending on an English function word such as "with" or "the".
  - Every generated title is logged in omp's own log (`auto-title: title {answer, retry, retryModel, retryUsage, title}`). Cost reference (16 Oct 2026): a retry ≈ $0.005 (≈ 1.2 k input, 15–18 output tokens).
- Cards: under `title.icons: boring` a fresh title drops a card (stock's retitle keeps the current title's card through `keepTitleCard`, so ✨ and evolving on an old carded title come back plain; `cfgTitleIcons`, stock `splitCardTitle`); under another value the card is kept (`formatCardTitle`). Stored titles are never rewritten on their own. A person's own TITLE_SYSTEM.md (any slot value the engine did not set) is their whole title prompt: stays as written, uncut, never retried (`planTitlePrompt`). Setting the slot turns off stock's `title.generator: fork` path, which a GUI session never reaches (`autoTitle` is TUI-only in `main.ts`).
- **Evolving** (sub-switch): after a terminal `agent_end` while the title is automatic, `EvolveGate` (no model call; `DEFAULT_GATE_RULES`: `minTurns` 2 terminal turns and `minIntervalMs` 60 s since the title was set or last considered, and the work moved — a file edited/written 1, the todo plan changed 2, the goal changed 3, `threshold` 3 — or `forceTurns` 6 regardless) asks stock `renameTitle()` once per window. The result is applied only when not a reword of the current title (`isRewordedTitle`: normalized word overlap ≥ 0.6 `SAME_WORDS` or character-trigram overlap ≥ 0.55 `SAME_TRIGRAMS`). Every write re-checks the session id, the name and `titleRevision` it started from, so **a typed name wins over any request in flight and ends the evolving**.
- Cost: 1 title-model request for the first title (+1 for its card when `title.icons` ≠ `boring`, +1 retry on `default` when over the limits), then at most 1 per two finished turns and minute (+ its retry), only after files/plan/goal moved or six quiet turns (counted in `auto_title_set`'s `requests`).
- ✨ auto button (title bar, rename dialog, sidebar row; `hooks/use-auto-title.ts`, `common/AutoTitleButton.tsx`) sends `regenerate_session_title` → local handler → bridge `regenerate_title` (`renameTitle()` on the live session, saved as an automatic title even over a typed name; failure is one inline `role="alert"` sentence, never a toast). A stored session no tab has open has no omp to ask (origin tracking lives in that omp), so its button is aria-disabled with a tooltip. A paired host's tab answers on the host (the command is in `quota-commands.ts`' model list). A changed title eases in (`common/SwapText.tsx`, `.omp-title-swap`; the global reduced-motion rule shortens it).
- Tests: `bridge-auto-title-stock.test.ts` (against stock), `bridge-auto-title-core.test.ts`, `bridge-auto-title.test.ts`, `use-auto-title.test.ts`; `e2e/auto-title.e2e.ts` on the fake omp (`OMP_GUI_TEST_AUTO_TITLE`); opt-in `OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1` `e2e/real-auto-title.e2e.ts` (raw answers, retry model and usage into `/tmp/real-auto-title.json`, read from the private profile's omp log; `realOmpProfile(…, { keepDefaultRole: true })` keeps the user's `default` role and the audit moves its session to `smol` with stock `set_model`, which does not persist) checks four Korean first messages (Korean reply language) and two English ones (reply language off) get short plain titles in their own language, no card; four are the prompt's own compression examples, two held out.

### Automatic mode choice
- Settings › Agent behavior › Smart automation › Auto mode, and the "Auto mode" switch in the composer's Mode popover. Pref `autoMode`, default **on**, only `false` turns it off. Files `auto-mode-contracts.ts`, `auto-mode-core.ts`, per-session memory `auto-mode-state.ts`, behaviour `modes/auto-mode.ts`; capability `autoMode`.
- Decides **only Plan or Goal, or none**, and **uses that mode for the one request it classified, never as the session's mode** (the user's call after it put a busy work session into Plan). Other manual modes are never enabled by it.
- Classifier: stock has none (`plan.defaultOnStartup` is unconditional; `auto-thinking/classifier.ts` picks an effort), so the bridge copies its approach: stock `judgment` (`resolveJudge` over the `judge` role chain: a native System One judge first when its key exists (since omp 18.12.2 TypeSafe's Jev, `openrouter/typesafe/jev-1.14`), then `@tiny` → `@smol` → `@default`; one choice question answered in a few tokens, `sharedJudgmentCache`, `journalJudgmentUsage` with purpose `auto-mode` so session totals count it) and `preprocessTinyMessage`. The judge's state carries `previousExchange` (last user message and reply, 400 chars each); its instructions say none for anything that continues, answers or corrects that work.
- No env or sync plumbing: the composer reads the pref at send time (`lib/auto-mode-client.ts` `runAutoMode`, called from `planComposerSubmit`'s `beforePrompt` for a plain prompt only, never steer/follow-up/slash) and calls `auto_mode_decide` (a model command in `remote/quota-commands.ts`; a paired host's tab runs it on the host, with the host's quota). The bridge method decides and changes nothing; main (`local/auto-mode.ts`) applies it through stock's own commands: bridge `modes.set_plan_mode` with `auto: true` for Plan (`auto-mode-state.ts` `autoPlan` marks it), stock `goal create` with the prompt text as objective for Goal.
- After a Goal decision `planComposerSubmit` sends the prompt with `streamingBehavior: "steer"`: on an idle session stock's `goal create` admits the goal's own turn at once (`rpc-goal.ts` `#scheduleContinuation`), ahead of the prompt, and stock refuses a plain prompt while a turn runs (`AgentBusyError`; sent plain it came back as "Message not sent"); steer joins that turn or starts one.
- **One request only**: an automatic plan ends at its approval, and **nobody clicks it** (the user's call, 21 Oct 2026: Plan under Auto mode means the agent plans and goes on by itself). When the agent proposes a plan while `autoPlan` holds, the bridge (`plan.ts` `onProposalWritten`) emits no `plan_proposal` frame (so no card in any window, nothing waits on the person, no needs-you notification), aborts the planning turn as for a card, and runs its own `approve` with `keep_context`: plan mode ends and stock's approved-plan prompt (the plan inlined, as stock's own render does) starts the execution turn in the same session. A paired host's tab does it on the host (the bridge runs there). A plan mode the user turned on, or took over from an automatic one, still shows the card. An automatic plan also ends when the user sends a plain prompt before the agent proposed it (`runAutoMode` first sends `auto_mode_end` → main sends `modes.set_plan_mode {enabled: false}`); the chip's undo leaves it as before. An automatic goal ends when it completes (`goal_updated`) or the user stops the turn (`abortActiveTurn` → `endAutoGoal` → `auto_mode_end` → stock `goal drop`). Neither ending is an undo: later prompts are still classified.
- **The Mode picker never shows it** (`ComposerModes` leaves `autoMode`'s plan/goal out of its label, badges and Plan switch); only the "This request: Plan/Goal" chip (`AutoModeChip`, store field `autoMode`, clears itself when the mode ends) does, with the undo. Turning Plan on in the picker over an automatic plan makes it the user's (`set_plan_mode` without `auto` clears `autoPlan`; the store's `autoMode` goes).
- **No hijacking busy sessions**: `autoModeSkip` answers `busy` without a request when the session ran anything within `AUTO_MODE_IDLE_MS` (10 min; the last message's timestamp), has open todos (pending/in progress) or running subagents/background jobs, a subagent's own included (`getAsyncJobTree`) — so Plan only happens on a session's first message or after 10 idle minutes. Plan needs a stated probability ≥ `MIN_PLAN_PROBABILITY` 0.7, Goal ≥ `MIN_PROBABILITY` 0.6.
- Bounds: `AUTO_MODE_TIMEOUT_MS` 2000 ms in the bridge (3000 ms in the renderer; raised from 1500 because a cold first request or loaded machine often exceeded it and decided none); a timeout, failure, low probability or unknown answer is none; a message under `MIN_CLASSIFIED_CHARS` 12 is none without a request.
- **Never on the critical path longer than it must be** (20 Oct 2026; the order judge → mode → prompt stays: Plan must be on before the model can edit, a Goal's prompt joins stock's goal turn as a steer). The first request to the judge's host after an omp starts took 1.5 s and more (DNS and TLS while stock's model discovery downloads from the same host; 0.4 s afterwards), so the 2 s deadline lost every first message. So: the bridge warms a native judge once per omp as its main session starts (`warmJudge`, `install`'s `session_start` hook; one unbilled judgment of `AUTO_MODE_WARM_REQUEST`, outside the answer cache and the ledger; a prompted judge is never warmed); every typing pause (`AUTO_MODE_PREFETCH_DEBOUNCE_MS` 250 ms, `components/layout/composer/use-auto-mode-prefetch.ts`, this computer's own tabs only) sends `auto_mode_prefetch {message}` with the text the send will carry, whose answer lands in stock's judgment cache (journaled once, purpose `auto-mode`), so the decision at Enter is answered from it; a decision joins a prefetch of its own text or a warm-up still under way instead of asking again (`PrefetchSlot` in `auto-mode-core.ts`). The warmed connection also serves stock's auto-thinking classifier (held `defaultThinkingLevel: auto`). Measured (one app at a time, a new session's first message, 1.5 s between typing and Enter, 3 runs each): Enter → prompt in the journal 0.88 s before, 0.65 s after, 0.54 s with auto mode off (no judge); an Enter the moment the tab is ready takes 1.3–1.8 s in all three (stock's auto-thinking judgment on a cold host). Pinned by `bridge-auto-mode-prefetch-stock.test.ts` (installed omp, loopback native judge: one request per distinct question, the decision at Enter from the cache, a joined question, billing), the `PrefetchSlot` cases of `bridge-auto-mode-core.test.ts`, the prefetch and order cases of `auto-mode-client.test.ts` and the two prefetch tests of `e2e/auto-mode.e2e.ts` (set_plan_mode before the prompt; goal create before the steer).
- Other guards: pref off, a turn running, `/` and `!` messages, any active mode the user picked (plan or goal: **a manual mode always wins**), and per session (`auto-mode-state.ts`, keyed by transcript id) a rejected plan (no plan again, goal still allowed) and an undone decision (`auto_mode_undo`, a free command: leaves the mode, stops all auto decisions for that session). Logged at debug level (`gui-bridge: auto mode`).
- Measured (19 Oct 2026, 44 labelled prompts (plan 8 Korean + 3 English, goal 6 + 3, none 20 + 4), 3 rounds plus cold starts, 139 calls per model, the bridge's own instructions): Jev 92.4 % right (Korean 90.2 %, English 100 %), p50 412 ms warm / 467 ms cold, p95 563 ms, no timeout, ≈ $0.000027 a call; Sonnet (the `smol` judge before 18.12.2) 74.2 % because 48 % of its calls passed the 2 s deadline (97.7 % had it waited), p50 1.8 s, ≈ $0.0002 a call. Neither ever chose plan or goal for a none-labelled prompt (Jev's plan probability there ≤ 0.04, Sonnet's 0) nor plan for a goal prompt, so `MIN_PLAN_PROBABILITY` went 0.75 → 0.7 (Jev's Korean plan requests came back at 0.52–1.0; 0.7 takes the 0.73–0.74 answers, Jev plan recall 24 → 26 of 35). Pinned with those answers in `bridge-auto-mode-core.test.ts`.
- **Plan is narrow** (21 Oct 2026, the user's report: every long first message ran in Plan). The plan criterion used to read "a new feature across several files where reviewing a plan helps", which every detailed feature request matches, and a long first message is usually one: on the user's real `judge` (Jev 1.14) 2 of 10 long, concrete first messages chose plan (one of them a real session's "read docs/…, write the ADR, then scaffold apps/homepage", plan 0.97) and 3 more had 0.51-0.68 of plan's probability against the 0.7 floor. Not the cause: `preprocessTinyMessage` (none of them reaches its 2000 characters) or the threshold. Now plan is only a message that asks for a plan, design or proposal before any change, or large work (a migration, a rewrite across the codebase) whose approach the message leaves open; a concrete request is none however long, detailed or multi-step (`AUTO_MODE_INSTRUCTIONS`, `autoModeCriteria`). After: 0 of 10 long concrete messages plan (the reported one 0.39), both plan-asking messages still plan (1.0), the six short audit prompts unchanged except "refactor the whole settings module into separate stores … update every caller", concrete and now 0.5 plan (none). The 19 Oct measurement above was made with the broader criterion; its plan recall is lower now by design.
- Tests: `bridge-auto-mode-core.test.ts` (guards incl. busy, plan threshold, memory), `bridge-auto-mode-stock.test.ts`, `bridge-auto-plan-stock.test.ts` (installed omp, scripted loopback model: an automatic plan is approved by itself with no `plan_proposal` frame and runs with the plan inlined in the same session; the user's own plan mode still waits), `auto-mode-client.test.ts` (plan ended by an unrelated message, kept for a reply, goal ended by Stop, manual plan untouched), `ComposerModes.auto-mode.test.tsx` (picker unchanged, manual takeover), `e2e/auto-mode.e2e.ts` (fake omp: `OMP_GUI_TEST_AUTO_MODE_IDLE_MS` sets its busy window, `OMP_GUI_TEST_AUTO_MODE_DELAY_MS` holds each decision like the real judge, `OMP_GUI_TEST_GOAL_TURN` `{holdMs, complete}` starts stock's goal turn on `goal create`, refuses a plain prompt during it and can complete the goal, `OMP_GUI_TEST_AUTO_PLAN_PROPOSES` makes an automatic plan propose and be approved as the bridge does; covers an automatic plan running with no card and no click, plan ended by an unrelated message, goal ended by completion and Stop, a busy session left alone, a Mode popover with only the Plan and Goal tabs), opt-in real-model audit `bridge-auto-mode-real.test.ts` (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`: six short prompts and ten long first messages, printing each decision with the judge's probabilities).

### Judge-model assists
- Small decisions asked of stock's judge, the way auto mode asks (`judgment` `resolveJudge` over the `judge` role chain, `sharedJudgmentCache()`, `journalJudgmentUsage(session.sessionManager)` so session totals count them, telemetry), but **only while stock's `hasNativeJudge(settings, registry)` is true** (the chain resolves first to a native decision model: TypeSafe's Jev 1.14 through OpenRouter on a stock install) **and** pref `judgeAssist` is on (default on; only a stored `false` is off; Settings › Agent behavior › Smart automation "Use the judge model for small decisions", `JudgeAssistRow.tsx`, deep link `agent:setting-gui-judgeAssist`; the `judge` row of Model roles links to it). The check runs on every call, because a login, key or role change moves the chain. Never a direct Jev client, never new provider code.
- **OMPdesk works fully without the judge and only gets better with it (the user's rule).** No key, a prompted fallback (Sonnet, a local model), the switch off, an omp without the feature, an error, a timeout (`JUDGE_ASSIST_TIMEOUT_MS` 2 s; the engine races its own timer, so a judge ignoring the abort never holds a caller) or an unreadable answer → the judge "did not decide" and every feature runs its previous code rule, byte-identical. Nothing in the UI mentions Jev beyond Model roles' existing one-line hint.
- Files: `resources/gui-bridge/modes/judge-assist-contracts.ts` (pref, env, thresholds, method names), `judge-assist-core.ts` (the measured questions, answer → decision), `judge-assist-engine.ts` (gate, ask, in-flight dedupe of one reply; `setJudgeAssistStockLoader` is the test seam, `src/main/test-fake-judge.ts` the shared fake), `judge-assist.ts` (guarded methods `judge_assist_set`, `judge_assist.needs_user`, `judge_assist.preview_check`, `judge_assist.available`; capability `judgeAssist`, which needs omp ≥ 18.12.2 for `@oh-my-pi/pi-ai` `nativeJudgmentImageBounds`, hence `MIN_OMP_VERSION`). The stock loader is registered at import and every stock call sits in the engine's `try`, so a missing internal means "did not decide". Main's RPC commands `judge_preview_check` (model) and `judge_available` (free: `hasNativeJudge` and the image check only) are in `src/main/local/judge-assist.ts`. Plumbing as automatic titles: `OMP_GUI_JUDGE_ASSIST` (`"1"`/`"0"`) at spawn, `judge_assist_set {enabled}` on change and on bridge connect to every live local sidecar and hosted tab; a paired host's tab follows the host's pref.
- Measured 19 Oct 2026 on real data with Jev 1.14 (labels set before the judge answered; p50 0.39–0.43 s, p95 ≤ 0.56 s, ≈ $0.00004 a text question, $0.0001 an image). Kept only what beat today's behaviour:
  - **Needs-you notifications (kept).** The renderer sends today's `completion` notify, unchanged. Main (`src/main/notify-triage.ts` `createNotifyHandler`, wired in `ipc.ts` `SYSTEM_NOTIFY`) runs the 1.5 s dedupe first (one classification per tab end across windows), then, for a completion of a LOCAL tab with the pref on, asks that sidecar's bridge directly (`bridgeRequest`, 2.5 s; no RPC dispatch, so no `PeerQuota` and no `noteDriver`); `true` → kind `ask` (the batcher's highest rank) titled with main's `notify.needsYou` ("Needs you · {title}" / "확인이 필요해요 · {title}"), same body; anything else, a remote tab, a hosted view, or a window focused meanwhile → exactly today's notification. The bridge judges the transcript's last reply; a follow-up or goal continuation that already started puts its user message last and answers `null` (not `isStreaming`, which stock holds past `agent_end` during auto-compaction). 60 real final replies, 17 waiting on the person: today 71.7 % (0/17 flagged), code rule `awaitsDecision` 80 % (7/17, 2 false), judge at `NEEDS_USER_MIN` 0.7 95 % (17/17, 3 false; 0.5 had 6 false).
  - **Suggestion pre-gate (dropped).** "Is a suggestion useful now?" said yes for 59 of 60: one smol call saved in 60. Instead the same needs-you question (same request, so it shares the notification's answer through the cache and the engine's dedupe) feeds `awaitsDecision(messages, judged)`: an open `ask` call still counts; `<app-state>`'s `last-reply` and the cleaner's `decisionPending` use it (80 % → 95 % on the set above). The state key stays the code rule's, so a judged answer never re-asks an unchanged moment; inline completion never asks the judge.
  - **Title evolve gate (replaced by a title check).** "Did the task change from <title>?" before `renameTitle()` cut churn 5 → 3 of 25 real evolving retitles but missed 6 of 20 real changes: dropped. Kept: after `renameTitle()` and `isRewordedTitle`, a choice "which title fits the last 4 exchanges, A (current), B (new) or same"; written only for B, re-checking `unchanged` after the await (a typed name still wins). Wrong retitles 9 → 2 of 25; 4 of 16 good ones wait for the next window. First titles, ✨ and typed names never ask.
  - **Memory distill filter (dropped).** Score "durable rule or decision?" over 20 real `pi/smol` proposals (3 not durable): score < 2 of 0–4 dropped 1 bad, 0 good (precision 85 % → 89.5 %): one page in 20, and main's upkeep has no session ledger to journal a judge call against.
  - **Preview check (kept).** After `gui_visualize open` shows a FILE or INLINE visual (`src/main/preview/preview-check.ts`; URL visuals are never captured: they live in the `web` session the person browses and keep `page.visual` after the person navigates): pref on → `judge_available` (≤ 1.5 s, `PREVIEW_CHECK_PROBE_MS`: a busy omp on a slow machine took past 500 ms; true only for a native judge whose model takes images per stock `nativeJudgmentImageBounds`, so stock's image chain can never fall to a chat model; otherwise nothing else happens, no wait, no capture) → `capturePage()` of that visual's own `omp-visual:` page only (`state.session === "visual"`, checked before and after the settle; never the app window, another window or a web tab), once shown and loaded (+300 ms settle; the whole `PREVIEW_CHECK_CAPTURE_MS` 2 s to appear, load and settle when the visual's tab is the one in front, because the renderer opens the Preview for that tab only and a busy window mounts its panel late: a renderer 8x slower laid the page out 450+ ms after the tool opened it and a flat 400 ms dropped the check, 6 of 6 e2e runs; a tab behind gives up after `PREVIEW_CHECK_BEHIND_MS` 400 ms, nothing will show it), longer side ≤ 1280 → `judge_preview_check` with the UI-QA bench's questions; the whole check ≤ `PREVIEW_CHECK_TOTAL_MS` 4 s, a late answer dropped. `error` ≥ 0.5 or `empty` ≥ 0.8 → a dismissable `PreviewCheckNotice` on that page and a hint line appended to the tool result ("Preview check: the page shows an error message. …"); otherwise the tool result is byte-identical. 22 agent-made pages (9 normal, 7 error, 6 blank): 13/13 broken found, 0 false alarms (UI bench before: error 100 %, empty 98 % at 0.8). Never gates or blocks anything.
- Tests: `bridge-judge-assist.test.ts` (gate both ways: unset loader, not native, off, throw, unreadable, timeout → undefined; thresholds; purpose, usage journal and cache passed to `resolveJudge`; dedupe; image-capable check; lazy title turns), `bridge-judge-assist-stock.test.ts` (installed omp, loopback `typesafe` judge, no spend: no native judge → `{needsUser: null}` and no request; native → answers through the real `resolveJudge` and a `model_usage` entry with purpose `ompdesk-needs-user` lands in the journal; image-capable vs text-only native model), `notify-triage.test.ts`, the judge cases of `bridge-next-prompt.test.ts` and `bridge-auto-title.test.ts` (each also proves the no-judge path equals the code rule), `preview-check.test.ts`, the "Preview check" cases of `preview/host.test.ts`, `PreviewCheckNotice.test.tsx`, `JudgeAssistRow.test.tsx`, `ModelRoles.test.tsx`, `shell-env.test.ts`, `host-services.test.ts`; `e2e/judge-preview-check.e2e.ts` on the built app (the fake judge sees an error: hint and notice; no native judge: the result carries no hint and no notice shows). The fake omp answers the methods with "did not decide" unless `OMP_GUI_TEST_JUDGE_ASSIST` (`{"available": true}`, `{"needsUser": true}`, `{"preview": "error"}`) says otherwise.

## Autopilot

### Overview
- UI **Autopilot** (ko "오토파일럿"; code id `autopilot`; Mode popover tab beside Plan and Goal): an endless development run in its own new git worktree tab, built on **stock Goal continuation** (`goal.continuationModes ∋ rpc`), no scheduler of its own. States `running | stopping | paused(reason) | waiting_quota | ended`; "End" = stock `goal pause` (turn in progress finishes) then drop; "Stop now" = `abort`.
- Contracts (journal entries, states, settings, `AutopilotStatus` carried by `get_autopilot` and the `autopilot_update` frame (whole on every update to every window and paired device, rebuilt whole on a device that took it as a delta over the link (Remote devices, `slimAutopilot`), so its cycles leave out `checkCommand` and `resolved`, which only main's cycle-end frame and `begin`'s journal read, and carry each summary as the Markdown log clips it, one line of at most 400 characters, `clipCycleSummary`), `set_autopilot`'s `AutopilotAction`, `AUTOPILOT_BRIDGE_METHODS`) live in the dependency-free `resources/gui-bridge/modes/autopilot-contracts.ts`.
- **Bridge** (`resources/gui-bridge/modes/autopilot*.ts`): cycle ledger in the session journal, the `autopilot` tool, a static system section, and a `tool_call` guard (destructive git always blocked, no push/tag/publish for the agent, path checks, `eval`/`debug`/`ask` off the active tools).
- **Main** (`src/main/autopilot*.ts`): start, end/abort, children, approver, post-cycle git audit (second defence, `autopilot-audit.ts`: symbolic refs such as `origin/HEAD` are not compared; a remote-tracking ref a fetch moved is not the run's, nor one another session pushed, nor a local branch checked out in another worktree (another session's, or the user's own checkout pulled forward): created or moved forward holding none of the run's own commits, i.e. its branch's first-parent commits the remote default branch did not hold at the cycle's start, so pushing the run's unchanged branch is still caught; git failing to say counts as the run's; a deleted or rewound ref always counts; pinned by `autopilot-audit.test.ts`), `ShipRunner` (push/PR/merge/revert/land), release detection, restart resume.
- **Renderer**: store `stores/autopilot.ts`; UI in `components/autopilot/`; pure rules in `autopilot-view.ts`; log Markdown in `src/shared/autopilot-log.ts` (the board's copy and the draft PR body). **The run's home is the work board, never the side panel** (the user's rule, 21 Oct 2026: opening a panel is harder than glancing at the board; there is no `autopilot` panel view, no View options entry and nothing opens by itself at a run's start). In an Autopilot tab (`isAutopilotTab`, an ended run included) the board changes shape (`AutopilotBoard.tsx`, a lazy chunk so the phone's first screen stays in budget; `use-autopilot-state-text.ts` is the one small piece the strip and chip read): the head is named "Autopilot" with the run's state, active time and totals (`AutopilotBoardHead`: `data-autopilot-state`, `data-autopilot-reason`, `data-autopilot-totals`; branch ↑↓ and pace in the tooltips), then the run's sections ahead of the board's own (after a waiting question and a failed check): `autopilot` (the cycle in flight: its item or "Picking an item…" with the step it is in, the cycle's child sessions, the red streak from two on, where green cycles go (`ShipLandRow`: the one PR a protected branch asked for, or the shipping level; no line for a run that simply lands, the user's call, 22 Oct 2026; for an Automatic run also a release under way or the last release), Pause/Resume, End, Stop now), `cycles` (`AutopilotCycles.tsx`: the newest five one line each, older ones behind "+N more"; a tap opens a cycle's commit, time, change, verify note, children and Revert in place, `data-board-expands`), `followups` ("Left for later" / "남은 후속 작업": what the recent cycles left and no later cycle did or resolved, `AutopilotStatus.followups` from `openFollowups`, one line each with the sentence as tooltip, the first three and "+N more"; a pin (`data-autopilot-followup-pin`, not on an ended run or for one already on the backlog) puts it on the backlog with `backlog_add`, so the next cycle takes it first, and ✕ (`data-autopilot-followup-dismiss`, action `followup_dismiss`) journals a backlog `dismiss` event that closes it for the board and `begin` alike (`openFollowups`' `dismissed`, counted against the cycle that left it once that cycle ended; a cycle that lists it again reopens it, even one already running at the dismissal); one a pending backlog item names is the backlog's and leaves the section and `begin`'s list, and a done one closes it until a cycle after the one that did it leaves it again; absent while there is none), `ship` (`AutopilotShip.tsx`: push, PR, merge, release, automatic release, the queue; **only for a run whose start named a level**: under Automatic shipping (`AutopilotShipPolicy.automatic`, set by main whether it then decided land, push of the run's branch without a known default branch, or nothing without a remote) nothing there is the person's to do, the user's call, 21 Oct 2026) and `backlog` (pinned items, a field to pin one, the focus areas row that opens `FocusChips` in place). **No field for the next cycle's instruction and no auto-push switch** (the user's call, 21 Oct 2026): what the next cycle should do is typed into the tab's composer, as in any session (a steer while a turn runs), and the push level is the start's; the `note` action stays for agents (`ompdesk_app autopilot.*`) and main's own notes. No row of the board may push it sideways (a `-mx-2` switch row once made the docked board 2 px wider than its box and scroll). An ended run keeps its head, reason and cycles, no controls. The composer chip's button beside the Run chip, the dock row's and the Mode tab's "show the run" (`data-autopilot-chip-open`, `data-autopilot-open-screen`; `autopilot.chip.open`) call `revealWorkBoard(tabId)`: the pane's docked board unfolds the run's section and scrolls to it, else its strip expands (a narrow window, a split pane, the side panel open, a phone). **A run is reachable in every layout**: `workBoardForm`'s `run` keeps the strip in an Autopilot tab after the board's ✕ (`workBoard` off). Pinned by `WorkBoard.autopilot.test.tsx` (the head and sections, every control, an ended run, the four layouts the board cannot dock in, a reveal for another tab), `AutopilotBoard.test.tsx` (no shipping section for an Automatic run, landing or not; no next-cycle field; the follow-ups' rows and cap), `bridge-autopilot-core.test.ts` (which follow-ups the status names), `autopilot.test.ts` ("the ship level nobody chose": `automatic` on the armed policy), `panel-auto-open.test.ts` (a run's start opens no panel), `ComposerModes.autopilot.test.tsx`, `GoalDockBar.autopilot.test.tsx`, `e2e/autopilot-cycles.e2e.ts`; captures `OMP_GUI_AUTOPILOT_BOARD_SHOTS=<dir>` with `e2e/autopilot-board-captures.e2e.ts` (dev harness; wide, narrow, split, side panel open, dismissed, phone × light/dark × en/ko, each before and after "show the run").
- An Autopilot tab's approval card is held back 3 s (`APPROVAL_GRACE_MS`; main's approver answers it). A usage-limit stop waits in main, never in a window (`#waitForQuota` in `src/main/autopilot.ts`, 20 Oct 2026: a renderer booking never woke a run with no window open): the reset is the provider's first account to reset (`quotaResetAt` in `src/shared/quota-reset.ts`, the one rule the turn error card's booking uses too; `QUOTA_FALLBACK_MS` 15 min when no report names one), journaled `waiting_quota` with `quotaResetAt` so every window, a paired device and a relaunch read it; the wake (`#armQuotaWake`) runs stock `retry`, or the restart resume after a relaunch, only if the run is still waiting. Windows are a view: the chip reads the state, `use-quota-resume.ts` never retries a live run's session and drops a stray booking, and the error card offers no booking on a run.
- Prefs: none (`autopilotBranch`, the choice remembered per repository, retired 21 Oct 2026 with the branch picker). Shipping and notifications have no setting (removed 20 Oct 2026, deleted once from prefs by `src/main/retired-prefs.ts`): a start nobody chose a level for ships Automatic (see Start sheet), and a run notifies its stops, its end and release outcomes, never each cycle: this computer's banner under its own notifications switch (General › Notifications), the paired device that started the run (its charge, `chargeRun`, even after it closed the run's view) and every device with a tab on the run's session once each (`host.autopilotNotice`, `LocalHostServices.autopilotNotice`; the device shows it on the tab of that session, else as a plain banner, under its own switch), and paired phones through Web Push (`WebPushNotifier.notice`, the categories each phone chose). There is no `autopilotLimits` pref.
- **Ownership: a run belongs to the computer whose folder it works in, as a tab of that computer's window** (the user's decision, 19 Oct 2026). A run a paired device starts on a host folder (`ompdesk_autopilot` with `device`, or the start sheet in a session of the device's that runs on the host: the host answers with the run's session file and the device opens it, `use-autopilot-actions.ts`) is a tab of the host's front window, never a device tab or a hosted session (`hostedSessions` does not list it): it outlives the device closing its tab or going away, resumes from the host's own layout, and the person at the host sees it as an ordinary tab. The device shows it as a viewer tab of the host's run session (`viewForDevice`): closing it only leaves; status and stop go through it to the host's coordinator. Its replies and its children's count against that device's daily tokens until it ends (`LocalHostServices.chargeRun`/`runEvents`/`endRun`, persisted by worktree root in `<userData>/autopilot-device-runs.json`; `Autopilot.runRootOf`, `runEnded`); a device tab viewing any session of a charged run, the run or a child (`attachRunLookup`: the session's owner tab → `runRootOf`), counts none of its replies, so nothing counts twice. The sheet refuses only a folder that is no git repository: Plan mode or a Goal in the tab it starts from never blocks, the run is its own new tab. Details under OMPdesk control tools › Schedules and Autopilot.

### Tests
- Unit: `src/main/bridge-autopilot-*.test.ts`, `src/main/autopilot*.test.ts`, renderer `autopilot*` tests. The coordinator's tests are split by group so they run side by side (one file took 203 s on a Windows runner): `autopilot.test.ts` (start), `autopilot-branch-choice.test.ts` (the branch a run is on), `autopilot-branch.test.ts` (stop, failed turn), `autopilot-cycle-end.test.ts` (a cycle's end), `autopilot-cycle-end-land.test.ts` (Automatic shipping landing on the default branch), `autopilot-actions.test.ts` (ship actions, release, children, restart) on the shared rig `autopilot-test-rig.ts`; a pin below that names `autopilot.test.ts` may sit in a sibling. Fake-omp e2e: `e2e/autopilot-{cycles,ship,restart}.e2e.ts` on `e2e/autopilot-harness.ts` (en/ko × light/dark captures, Stop versus ■, approvals, quota wait, guide, shipping against a temp repo with a local bare remote and a fake `gh`, SIGKILL relaunch and normal quit). Opt-in real omp: `e2e/real-autopilot.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`: two cycles in a new worktree, the guard blocking a banned command, Stop, remote unchanged while local only, one push-level cycle; the yolo/tool-set test; the guide-draft test).
- Fake omp `e2e/fixture/autopilot.ts` (hooked into `rpc-ui.ts`; env `OMP_GUI_TEST_AUTOPILOT`, JSON keys documented at its top) emulates `modes.autopilot_*` and the frames by **importing** the bridge's own pure rules (`autopilot-core.ts`, `autopilot-guard-core.ts`, `restart-core.ts` `goalActiveAtExit`) over a journal written to the fake session file, and drives stock Goal continuation (create/pause/resume/drop as `mode_change` entries, abort pauses, `retry` re-arms, stdin EOF journals the turn aborted, a load pauses an active goal). Only the agent is scripted (tool calls through the real guard, real commits, real host tools for children, `end`); the yolo override is only recorded (`fixture_autopilot_session`), active tools are reported in `get_state.dumpTools`; it mirrors `pick` and first-end-stands (`endTwice`).

### Branch choice and target
- **The start sheet shows only the folder's own branch** (the user's rule, 21 Oct 2026, from a report that the list held names that were not the project's): the Branch row is one plain line, a branch glyph and the branch checked out in the folder (`AutopilotBranchInfo.current`, `readBranchInfo` in `src/main/autopilot-branches.ts`, re-read each time the sheet opens; no row while detached). No list, no other local or remote branch, no remembered or earlier run's name, no name field; the explanation is its (i). The sheet sends no `branch`, so every run from it works on a new `autopilot/<date>` cut from that branch (made free of clashes). `AutopilotBranchInfo.defaultBranch` is kept for an agent's start (`plan` in `agent-control/host.ts` asks before committing on it) and never shown.
- An agent's `ompdesk_autopilot` may still name a branch (`AutopilotStartOptions.branch`): `new` (`worktree_create` takes `branch: {name, mode: new|local|remote}` and never renames it) or `existing` (local, or only on the ship remote, checked out tracking `<remote>/<name>`), checked at the start against git's own branches (`readBranchEntries`).
- **A branch another worktree holds is never refused**: it is the run's **TARGET**. The run works on its own `autopilot/<date>` cut from the target (`branch: {name, mode: "new", from}`, `viaWorkBranch` in `src/shared/autopilot-branch.ts`), journaled as `target` (arm params, state entry, `AutopilotStatus.target`, survives restart); `ShipRunner` pushes `<sha>:refs/heads/<target>` (`ShipTarget.pushTo`), fast-forward only. The user's folder and working tree are never touched.
- **The default branch only when an agent names it**, and its start asks first whatever the switches say (see Schedules and Autopilot). A run committing on it is its own base (`baseBranch` = it, so a release's `HEAD:<default>` stays the one push the guard allows); Merge/PR refuse (`autopilot_on_base`).
- **A PR follows the push**: with a target, `ShipRunner.#pr` pushes the sha to the target (the only branch Autopilot ever pushes; the work branch never reaches the remote) and opens the PR from it. When that head is the base there is nothing to compare: Create PR is disabled with `autopilot.ship.prOnBase` and `set_autopilot ship pr` refuses with `autopilot_on_base` (Merge refuses only when the run's own branch is the base).
- Unchanged on any branch: the agent's pushes stay blocked (`ShipRunner` pushes `<sha>:refs/heads/<branch>`, never `+`); destructive git (force push, `reset`, branch delete/move, rewriting) stays banned in the guard; a non-fast-forward push is refused by git, never forced, and is a note to the agent (`#tellAgent` → bridge `note`, handed over by the next `begin`: fetch, merge, never force; text `<remote>/<branch> has commits this run lacks (not a fast-forward); Autopilot never forces a push.`), not a stop. The post-cycle audit judges the chosen branch (`AuditScope.branch`; the `autopilot/` prefix earns nothing). Run id = part after `autopilot/`, else the date.
- The choice persists in the journal as the arm's `branch` (`#adopt` reads `status.branch`). Rules are pure in `src/shared/autopilot-branch.ts`.
- Pinned by `src/shared/autopilot-branch.test.ts`, `autopilot-branches.test.ts` (the sheet gets the folder's branch and no other local, remote or earlier run's name), `autopilot.test.ts` ("the branch a run is on", restart; a retired `autopilotBranch` value is never read), `autopilot-audit.test.ts`, `autopilot-ship.test.ts` ("pr"), `bridge-autopilot-guard.test.ts`, `src/main/local/git/worktree.test.ts`, the Branch row tests in `ComposerModes.autopilot.test.tsx`, `AutopilotBoard.test.tsx`, and `e2e/autopilot-ship.e2e.ts` (an existing branch an agent names moves on the remote, main does not; the sheet shows only `main` with other local, remote and earlier run's branches present, and the run pushes its own branch, never main).

### No soft stops
- **The run does not stop for soft reasons.** Red cycles, a failed check, a missing report, compaction churn, cycles without a commit, a rejected/unreachable push, a failed release run, plan mode, all todos blocked, a quiet turn, a model failing after TurnResume's retries are repaired by the next cycle. `decideAfterCycle` only reports ceilings a start set; `classifyStall` returns `nudge` (frame `autopilot_nudge`: main leaves plan mode and sends one follow-up "call `autopilot begin`", at most every 20 s, 60 s after a failed turn). A nudge inside that window is never dropped: it is asked again when the window passes (a failed turn reports its failure once; a dropped nudge left the run silent while it read "running"). Every nudge runs from the run's cycle-end queue and checks right before it prompts (`#quietAndRunning`, again after leaving plan mode) that the run is still running and no turn, retry or connection wait holds it, so one queued behind a slow push never resumes a run the user paused or stopped meanwhile. `AutopilotStatus.redStreak` is the counter: from two red in a row the timeline shows one muted line "연속 빨강 N"/"N red in a row" (`data-autopilot-red-streak`).
- Pauses left: the user's Pause/Stop/End and ■, `ban_violation` (destructive git, always), ceilings an agent's start set (`budget`/`time`/`cycles`/`cost`; none by default and none in the UI), `app_closed` (Continue interrupted work off), `repo_gone` (worktree or git dir vanished, confirmed: `probeRepo` says `gone` twice 1.5 s apart, a missing folder or git's own "not a git repository"; a git that does not answer, `unknown`, skips that cycle's audit and shipping and the next cycle end checks again). `agent_done` ends the run; a quota wait resumes itself at the reset.
- Defaults: fan-out `maxParallel: null` (the agent decides, no cap; the protocol says optional, `begin` hints: fan out only for multi-minute items), shipping Automatic (see Start sheet), release cadence = agent decides, notifications = stops, end and releases, focus = all, remote = origin or the only remote.

### Start sheet
- `components/modes/AutopilotConfig.tsx`: visible are the full-access warning line and Start. Everything else sits in ONE closed `SettingsAdvanced` ("고급"/"Advanced"): 내보내기/Shipping (Automatic / Commit / Push / Release; Automatic, the default, names what it will do: "lands every green cycle on the default branch of origin (a protected branch gets one pull request), and releases when the project releases" or "no remote: commits stay here"), 브랜치/Branch (one plain line: the folder's own branch, see Branch choice and target), focus areas and the backlog. **No guide box** (the user's decision, 21 Oct 2026): nobody sees or edits `AUTOPILOT.md` in the sheet, the app owns it (see Project guide). The sheet sends only what the user chose: a Start on the closed sheet sends `{action: "start"}` and main decides the rest. Nothing in the popover changes layout on blur, Advanced included.
- **Automatic shipping = land** (`#start` in `src/main/autopilot.ts`, when the start names no level; the user's decision, 21 Oct 2026): with a remote and a known default branch the policy is `land: true` (`AutopilotShipPolicy.land`, `autoPush` off; without a known default branch the run's own branch is pushed; without a remote nothing). Every **green** cycle **with a commit** (`#cycleEnd`: the existing verify rule, green only when the turn's own tool calls show the check passed after the last edit) queues a `land` item pinned to that commit, with the command that made it green (`AutopilotCycleRecord.checkCommand`, `judgeVerify`'s `check`: the check the report names (`end.check`) when it passed after the last edit, else the last check after the last edit, exactly as it ran under the guard; a narrower run after the repo's check, a few e2e specs, was what a land re-ran until 21 Oct 2026); a red cycle or one without a commit never lands. `ShipRunner.#land` (`src/main/autopilot-ship.ts`): fetch `<remote>/<default>`, and push `<sha>:refs/heads/<default>` when the commit contains the remote tip (fast-forward, journaled first, URL pinned, never `+`, never the agent). The user's checkout is never written. **Default branch moved** (21 Oct 2026: a real run had 6 green cycles and landed none, because main took ~12 commits an hour from other sessions and the agent's own merge always lost the race): `git merge-tree --write-tree` (no checkout touched) says whether a merge is clean. Clean, with a known check: the merge commit is built with `commit-tree` (the cycle's commit first parent, the fetched tip second), and **a clean textual merge is not a green one**, so the cycle's own check runs on exactly that commit before anything moves (`autopilot-land-check.ts` `checkLandCandidate`: a detached `git worktree add` of the candidate in a temp folder with no hooks, `.worktreeinclude` and the setup hook as for every new worktree, then HEAD `reset --soft` to the default branch's tip so the run's whole change is uncommitted (a check of what changed against HEAD, like `check:changed`, passes a clean checkout of the candidate in 1 s without checking anything), the command in bash with pipefail (Git Bash on Windows) under the setup supervisor, in the environment a session's omp gets (`shellSpawnEnv`: the login shell's PATH and exports under the app's; a Finder-launched app's own PATH is launchd's bare one, where a cycle's `bun run check:changed` was "command not found" and nothing landed over a moved main), the run's worktree path in it pointing at the temp checkout, 30 min cap; the folder is removed every time). Only that commit is pushed, as a fast-forward of the tip it merged; a default branch that moved again during the check or the push gets a new candidate, checked again, up to `LAND_ATTEMPTS` (3). Measured on this repo (21 Oct 2026, warm bun cache): worktree add 0 s, setup hook 2 s, `check:changed --no-e2e` over a 12-commit, 333-file change 57 s. Landed as a merge: the run's branch is fast-forwarded to it only while its worktree still sits clean on the cycle's commit, else the agent is told to merge; either way one note says it landed. A failed check (`LandOutcome` `red`), a conflict, a cycle without a known check, or a branch that kept moving lands nothing and the next `begin` gets a note (`#landed`: "Not landed: … Run git fetch && git merge …, fix, run the check, commit; the next green cycle lands it", what the check printed (`checkExcerpt`: its first error line and the 11 under it, then its end, within 2,000 characters; vitest lists every failing file after their shared error and the tail alone was only that list) or the conflicting files named): main never merges into the worktree, because the next turn may already work in it. A run on an existing branch ahead of the default branch lands its earlier commits with the cycle's (they are in the cycle's commit). Never a stop. Pinned by `autopilot-ship-land.test.ts` (moved main → checked candidate → landed; red candidate; moved again during the check and during the push; a diff-scoped check; a worktree that moved on; an existing branch ahead), `autopilot-land-check.test.ts` (a tool only on the login shell's PATH runs under the app's bare PATH; the first error of a long failing output is kept) and `autopilot.test.ts`. **Protected default branch** (the push refused for any reason other than moving on, the network or the account): the run's branch is pushed and ONE pull request opened into the default branch (`#landByPr`; an existing one, "already exists", is reused; once it is open a moved default branch goes to it too, unchecked, since the pull request is merged on the host); later lands update that branch. Said once: `AutopilotShipState.landing {via: "pr", url, reason}` (overlaid by main; ShipBox's `data-ship-land` line with an open button), one notification (`autopilot.land.prTitle`/`prBody`) and one note. **Release** (cadence `agent`: the agent judges when, see Project guide) when the repository has a remote and a release procedure exists in the run's own checkout after Start committed its guide (`#releaseFor(made.path)`: a rules file's, else the guide's Release section, see `release-detect.ts`); the procedure runs on top of the landed default branch. A project whose guide says `Releases: no` only lands. The agent's `begin.ship.land` says green cycles land. Main logs the guide's source and the decision (`autopilot: guide …; ship land on <branch>, release …`). Kept: the guard (destructive git banned, agent pushes blocked), the post-cycle audit, and the sheet's Advanced levels (Commit / Push / Release) for a person who picks otherwise; an agent's `ompdesk_autopilot` start always names a level, so it never lands.
- **Every run leaves a summary** (`#summary`, `#reportStop`; the user's report, 21 Oct 2026: a run's session was deleted and its stop reason went with it): when a run ends or pauses, one `gui-runtime.jsonl` line (source `autopilot`, message `ended` | `paused`; `details` {summary, run, root, branch, reason, detail, cycles, green, commits, landed + landTo + pr, or pushed}) and the same line as its notification body (`autopilot.summary`: "<branch>: N cycles (G green), C commits; L of C landed on main | pushed to … | kept on this computer. Stopped: <reason>"). Counts come from the journal and git (`merge-base --is-ancestor` of each cycle's commit against `<remote>/<default>`), so they hold after a relaunch. The user's own pause and stop (`SILENT_REASONS`) write the line but send no notification.
- **No caps or ceilings in the UI**: fan-out is `maxParallel: null` (no cap; a number still caps it and 0 turns fan-out off, which `ompdesk_autopilot`'s `advanced.parallel` can send), the release cadence is the agent's, the remote is detected, and ceilings exist only as `limits` an agent's start may set (`advanced.limits`).
- Settings has no Autopilot row.
- Pinned by `ComposerModes.autopilot.test.tsx` ("sends only what the user chose", "shows only Start with Advanced closed … and no guide box", "sends no guide", "changes no layout on blur", "starts from a paired host's tab", "holds Start back only outside a git repository"), `autopilot-ship-land.test.ts` ("land": a green commit lands on main; main moved: not landed with the ahead count, lands after the run merged it; a conflict named; a protected main gets one PR updated by later lands; an existing PR reused) and `autopilot.test.ts` ("the ship level nobody chose": land, release by a rules file's procedure or the committed guide's Release section, land only for `Releases: no`, nothing without a remote; "Automatic shipping lands on the default branch" over a local bare remote: green lands, red and no-commit never, main moved by someone else's clone then merged and landed, a conflict note and no pause, a protected main's PR said once and the summary's counts, the user's pause logged without a notification; "the app's notification switch off", "a cycle's end is no notification"); captures with `OMP_GUI_AUTOPILOT_CAPTURES`.

### Permissions
- Held only while the run lives. Arm takes `ask`/`eval`/`debug` off, adds `autopilot`, holds `tools.approvalMode = yolo` as a runtime override on the session's settings (never written to config.yml), and journals what it took off plus which were `xd://` devices (`excludedTools`, `mountedTools`). Disarm clears the override and restores with stock `setActiveToolPresentation(names, mounted)`: `getEnabledToolNames` also lists devices, and `setActiveToolsByName` would bring a device taken off at arm (`debug`) back top level.
- Pinned by `src/main/bridge-autopilot-stock.test.ts` (tool presentation before arm = after disarm) and the real-omp yolo/tool-set test in `e2e/real-autopilot.e2e.ts` (while armed, after Stop, and across a SIGKILL relaunch where the run continues by itself, armed before it runs).

### Restart
- **The user's rule: a restart continues the run, always** (crash, SIGKILL, normal quit, omp restart). Only Settings › General › Continue interrupted work off leaves it paused `app_closed` with Resume.
- `#onReady` sees the journal say `running` and asks `TurnResume.relaunchContinuation`: with a launch marker the run waits for TurnResume's restore of the cut-off turn (`resumeGoal`; `goalSettled` `none` continues anyway, `cancelled` or the resuming row's Stop pauses); without one (kill between turns) it continues at once. A run `#start` is still arming is no restart.
- Order: stock `goal pause`, arm with `resume`, `goal resume` (`#continueAfterRestart`). Why: stock's startup reconcile reattaches the goal paused and remembers the tool set of that moment (ask, eval, debug, no `autopilot`); its `goal resume` puts that set back and its `goal pause` restores and forgets it (`RpcGoalController` `#previousTools`). Without the pause the run lost its tool set until the next turn's `ensureTools` and every later pause gave the startup set back.
- On omp 18.11.3 a killed/quitting OMPdesk closes omp's stdin and stock journals the cut turn aborted, with a `session_exit` (real SIGKILL) or without (stock test), so `goalPausedByExit` must not need one (see Interrupted work, quit and diagnostics).
- Pinned by `autopilot.test.ts` (restart), `bridge-autopilot-stock.test.ts` (third test: stdin EOF mid-turn on the real omp), `e2e/autopilot-restart.e2e.ts` (kill mid-cycle, normal quit, kill between turns, setting off), and the real-omp yolo/tool-set test.

### Project guide (`AUTOPILOT.md`)
- At a repo's root (`AUTOPILOT_GUIDE_*`, `AutopilotGuideParams` and `AutopilotEnsuredGuide` in `autopilot-contracts.ts`; rules in `modes/autopilot-guide.ts`, `modes/autopilot-release-core.ts` (pure), `modes/autopilot-release-facts.ts` (reads) + `boundGuide` in `autopilot-core.ts`): project-wide direction shared across sessions and computers (roadmap, area priorities, no-touch paths, done criteria, taste, release, ideas). OMPdesk's own is `AUTOPILOT.md` at this repo's root. It is **not** the run's backlog or notes (those stay in the session journal and return through `begin`); nothing ever writes app backlog items into it.
- Every `autopilot begin` reads it from the worktree root (`state.root`) and returns `begin.guide` (≤ 8 KB, `AUTOPILOT_GUIDE_MAX_BYTES`, cut on a character boundary with a marker; absent/blank leaves the field out), so an edit applies from the next cycle. The static system section stays byte-identical (says the guide exists, ranks below the rules files, never lifts the HARD LIMITS): rules files (AGENTS.md etc.) win over the guide.
- **The app owns it** (the user's decision, 21 Oct 2026): no one sees or edits it in the start sheet. At Start, while the worktree is made, main asks the bridge `modes.autopilot_guide {root, procedure, text?}` (`#guideToCommit`, `GUIDE_MS` 45 s; `procedure` = the folder's rules-file procedure, `text` = an agent's `ompdesk_autopilot` guide) and `#commitGuide` writes the answer into the new worktree and commits it (`--no-verify`, no signing) as the branch's first commit when the file differs, so it never touches the user's current branch and reaches main only on merge. `ensureGuide`: an existing guide (the agent's `text`, else the checkout's file) is kept byte for byte and only gets the facts' `## Release` section when it has none (no model request); never rewritten otherwise; one past `AUTOPILOT_GUIDE_COMMIT_MAX_BYTES` (256 KB) is left alone. A repo without one gets one built: ONE `completeOnSmol` request (`next-prompt-engine.ts`: stock's `smol` role via `resolveRoleChain`, never the main model, usage in stock's ledger as `autopilot-guide-draft`) over a bounded prompt (rules-file excerpts, README, recent commit subjects, test/build commands) writes the other sections, then the facts' Release section goes in (replacing any the model wrote); on any failure the deterministic `templateGuide`. A bridge that cannot answer means no guide commit; the run starts anyway.
- **Release facts** (`collectReleaseFacts`, read-only, no model, every read bounded, git and gh with a 10 s timeout): release/publish/deploy workflows in `.github/workflows` (triggers: a push to the default branch and its `paths`, a pushed tag, a published GitHub release, manual dispatch; what they publish (npm, crates.io, PyPI, electron-builder, GitHub release, goreleaser, …) or deploy (GitHub Pages, Vercel, Netlify, Cloudflare, Fly.io, Firebase); the secrets they read, `GITHUB_TOKEN` left out), version manifests (`package.json`, `Cargo.toml`, `pyproject.toml`, `go.mod`), local publish scripts (`package.json` scripts, `Makefile`, goreleaser), the changelog's shape (keep-a-changelog, versioned, unversioned), version tags (count, last 90 days, median gap of the latest 10, the latest tag's commit subject), GitHub releases (`gh release list`, only for a github.com remote), hosting configs whose Git integration deploys a push (`vercel.json`, `netlify.toml`, …), conventional commits (a majority of the last 30 subjects).
- **Verdict** (`releaseVerdict`): a rules-file procedure first (the section points there and adds only the cadence); else a publishing workflow (packaging alone, an electron-builder CI build, publishes nothing); else a deploy workflow or hosting config (a release is a fast-forward push of the default branch); else tags, GitHub releases or a publish script (push the release commit and a version tag; a local publish command stays the maintainer's); else **no release**. The `## Release` section starts with one marker line, `Releases: yes` or `Releases: no`, and is **always `releaseSectionTemplate` over the facts, never a model's**: the agent runs it as written, and on TokenCat (21 Oct 2026) the smol model's section added pushing the version tag before a workflow whose first job skips the release when that tag already exists, so the run would never have published; the template had it right. A `yes` section has a numbered Procedure (version bump rule from the commit kinds, where the version lives (manifests with a version, and the files a publishing workflow's push `paths` must change, e.g. TokenCat's `build.sh`), changelog section in its own shape, checks first, one release commit touching only those files and the changelog in the last release's message style, the exact push/tag/workflow, watch the run; a failed run is reported and fixed in the next cycle, never forced) and a Cadence (the history line, the newest of local tags and GitHub releases, then: release when user-visible fixes or features have gathered at about that rhythm, a breaking defect at once), within what the guard lets a release do (push `HEAD:<default>` fast-forward and version tags, `gh workflow run`, watch runs; never a publish command or `gh release create`: CI publishes).
- **Release detection reads it**: `detectReleaseProcedure` (`src/main/release-detect.ts`) takes a rules file's (AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, CONTRIBUTING.md), then `RELEASING.md`/`docs/release*.md`, then AUTOPILOT.md's Release section (`guideRelease`: a heading named Release/Releasing/Release procedure/릴리스/배포, never "Release cadence"; a section without a marker is a person's own procedure and counts) unless it says `Releases: no`. So a run started with the app's guide can release under Automatic shipping, and the agent's `begin.ship.releaseProcedure` names `AUTOPILOT.md › Release`. WHEN to release is decided by the content, never the clock (`propose_release`, cadence `agent`; `releaseReadiness` in `autopilot-core.ts`): the bridge reads what landed on the release target, never the run's own HEAD (`unreleasedOnTarget` in `autopilot-release-facts.ts`: `<ship remote>/<base branch>` fetched with its tags, the commits after the newest `v*` tag reachable from it; an unlanded run has nothing to release there, and a release made from another checkout is a tag on the remote, so its commits do not count again; pinned on real repos by `bridge-autopilot-release.test.ts`) and answers due for `RELEASE_BATCH` (3) user-visible ones (`feat`/`fix`/`perf` or a breaking `!`; every commit counts in a repository without conventional commits), for one the agent calls `urgent` (with why), or for one that has waited `RELEASE_WAIT_CEILING_MS` (a day); docs, tests, refactors and CI alone never are. The answer lists the user-visible subjects (newest 20) and counts the rest; a release not due opens no release scope. Replaced a time-based cadence (the user's call, 22 Oct 2026). A user's own release request (`ship`) is never held.
- Pinned by `bridge-autopilot-guide.test.ts` (kept byte for byte, a missing Release section added with no model request, a model's Release section always replaced by the facts', the agent's text first, a too-large guide left alone), `bridge-autopilot-release.test.ts` (facts on real temp repos: an npm package with tags and a tag publish workflow, an Electron app publishing on a version push to main like this repo, a repo with no releases, a Pages and a Vercel site; trigger parsing from the `on:` block only, a CI build publishing nothing, a push's `paths` as the version file, the newest release in the cadence; the section's marker and replacement), `release-detect.test.ts` (AUTOPILOT.md's Release section, `Releases: no`, a rules file wins), `autopilot.test.ts` (Start's guide request and commit, no guide call from the sheet), the sheet tests in `ComposerModes.autopilot.test.tsx`, the guide test of `e2e/autopilot-cycles.e2e.ts` (no guide box; the template guide with `Releases: no` is the branch's first commit and `begin` reads it) and the opt-in guide test of `e2e/real-autopilot.e2e.ts`.

### Cycle reports are checked, not trusted
- Why (real omp, small model: 11 of 11 cycles told the truth about their check, but the commit hash was missing in 5 of 7 reports and a second `end` overwrote the first). Pure rules in `modes/autopilot-verify.ts` (pinned by `bridge-autopilot-verify.test.ts`), wiring in `autopilot.ts`.
- **The first `end` of a turn stands**: a repeat only adds follow-ups, children, ship, commit; `shouldSteerStop` sends no stop steer once reported. Only a refused report is replaced by a second `end`.
- `observeReport` takes the commit from git (a claimed hash not in the branch history is dropped; no repo, the claim stands) and measures `change` (`AutopilotChangeStat`: files, +/−, commits, `docOnly`).
- **`end` waits for the calls beside it** (`settledBeside`): stock runs a message's tool calls side by side, so an `end` sent with its commit or check read HEAD and the ledger before they finished (cycle 90 of this repository's run: a mistyped hash, no commit recorded, the green cycle never landed). It first waits for stock's event stream to report its own start (up to 2 s; the stream lags the call, and a call listed after `end` starts after it), then until every other call of its own assistant message (stock's message list) has ended and no other call of the cycle runs (up to 5 min, `OMP_GUI_TEST_AUTOPILOT_SIBLING_WAIT_MS` in tests); a call still running then records nothing and answers `recorded: false` with the running tools, so the agent waits and calls `end` again. Pinned by "takes the commit a call beside `end` made", "takes the commit of a call listed after `end`" and "records nothing while a call beside `end` still runs" in `bridge-autopilot-stock.test.ts` (installed omp, a commit and `end` in one message).
- **A green claim needs the turn's own tool calls to show it** (ledger of `tool_execution_*`: a check-like `bash` command, or the one named in `end.check`, that did not fail and ran after the last `edit`/`write`/`ast_edit`). Otherwise `verify` becomes `skipped` or `red` with `verifyNote` (`no_check` | `check_failed` | `edited_after`; timeline in en/ko and Markdown log); the `end` result tells the agent, which may run the check and `end` again. A cycle with a commit and no green counts red: neither pushed nor ignored. Release cycles are exempt.
- A `pick` op names the item after the audit (`now.title`; before it `now.area` is unset and the screen says "Picking an item…", never the rotation's guess) and refuses a repeat of a recent title before the work.
- `begin` carries `pick` and `hints` (a follow-up carried ≥ 3 cycles asks for a decision, a docs-only last cycle, fan-out cost); all dynamic guidance rides in `begin`, the static section is unchanged. Fan-out measured: two 25–30 s items took 62 s and cost about twice as much in two children, hence "fan out only for items of several minutes".
- **A follow-up closes when a cycle says it did it** (21 Oct 2026, this repository's own run: four finished follow-ups stayed open for the whole window and `begin`'s defect hint kept sending the agent back to them, once to re-investigate a guess an earlier cycle had disproved). `openFollowups` drops a follow-up a later cycle's title names exactly (rare: a title is a short name, a follow-up a sentence) or lists under `end.resolved` (begin's exact text, case, punctuation and spacing ignored; the tool schema and the defect hint say so; a repeated `end` merges it). A cycle that lists it again in its own `followups` reopens it. Pinned by `bridge-autopilot-core.test.ts` ("resolves a follow-up").
- **Bugs before polish** (19 Oct 2026; a real run spent 2 of 4 cycles on docs and wrote a found bug off as a "known limitation"): `BEGIN_PICK` ranks a known bug or failing behaviour first, docs and polish last, and says a fixable bug is never a limitation; `beginHints` names up to two open follow-ups whose words mark a defect (`DEFECT`); `pick` refuses a docs item right after a docs-only cycle unless it carries `reason` (`docsPickRefusal`); `end` answers a report that writes a problem off with no follow-up with `writeOff` (`writeOffNote`), and the repeated `end` merges the follow-up. Pure in `autopilot-core.ts`, pinned by `bridge-autopilot-verify.test.ts`. Real run after it: 3 bug cycles and 1 docs cycle of 4, both seeded bugs and a third one fixed, nothing written off.
- **The run's session title** (`runSessionTitle`, `#retitle` in `src/main/autopilot.ts`): stock `set_session_name` "오토파일럿 · <repo folder>" once the tab is ready, then "… · <cycle 1's item>" from `pick` (or cycle 1's record), only while the name is still the run's own (`get_state.sessionName`). Stock answers an RPC rename without `session_info_update`, so `SidecarPool.noteSessionName` tells the tab entry and the windows. Pinned by `autopilot.test.ts` ("titles the run's session").
- The run's board sections have no empty-state sentences: the run, the cycles and the backlog show only their rows, and the cycles section waits for the first cycle (`AutopilotBoard.test.tsx` "one-line rows").

## OMPdesk control tools

### Overview and safety
- Switch: Settings › Tools & extensions › OMPdesk control tools, pref `agentControl`, default **on**. Contract `src/shared/agent-control.ts`, rules `src/main/agent-control/service.ts`, schemas `tools.ts`, app seam `host.ts`, skill `resources/omp-builtin/skills/ompdesk/SKILL.md`.
- **Full control** (the user's decision, 19 Oct 2026): Settings › Tools & extensions, first row "Give agents full control of OMPdesk" / "에이전트에게 OMPdesk 모든 권한", pref `agentControlFull` (`AGENT_CONTROL_FULL_PREF`), default **on** for every install (`agentControlFullEnabled`: only a stored `false` is off). While on, **no control tool or app action raises an `[OMPdesk]` card**: the `always` set (deletes, MCP/plugin install, sign-in starts, proxy, mobile web on, pairing, update apply, quit), a foreign session delete, Autopilot release or main targets, every power pref; it implies `agentControlApprove` and `agentControlManage` (`host.ts` resolves both as `full || stored`; the Settings rows show them checked and disabled as covered), and the catalog action `agent_power.set` (`fullOnly`; `FULL_CONTROL_PREFS`/`FULL_CONTROL_SETTINGS` in `app-guard.ts`, the two memory switches through ai-memory's own channels) lets an agent change the agent-power switches, this one included. Every action is still journaled and toasted with undo where one exists. Kept whatever it says: an agent never answers an `[OMPdesk]` card (and with the switch on none is raised), secrets/tokens/passwords are never readable, git safety (no force push, Autopilot's guard bans destructive git), and OS security prompts (UAC, TCC). Its tooltip names the risk: prompt-injected content can then make an agent delete data or install code unasked. **Every card rule below applies only while full control is off.** Pinned by `shared/agent-control.test.ts`, `app-control.test.ts` (each `always` action runs card-free when on, `agent_power.set` refused when off), `service.test.ts`, `host.test.ts`, `AgentControlRow.test.tsx`, and the on/off cases of `e2e/agent-app-control.e2e.ts`, `e2e/agent-control.e2e.ts`, `e2e/agent-review.e2e.ts`.
- Host tools (like `gui_terminal_read`) are registered with stock `set_host_tools` on every ready local sidecar (`SidecarManager.syncHostTools`, re-sent when the switch changes) and answered in `ipc.ts` (`hostToolExecutor` → `AgentControlRuntime.run`). The skill's root `resources/omp-builtin` is a `-e` directory only while the switch is on, so skill and tools come and go with the pref.
- Tools (`AGENT_CONTROL_TOOLS`): `ompdesk_sessions`, `_session_open` (one or `sessions: […]`, optional `worktree: true | {branch, base: HEAD|default}`), `_session_send` (steer or follow-up), `_session_result` (several ids, `wait`, `until all|any`), `_session_read` (pages via `get_transcript_page`), `_session_answer`, `ompdesk_tab_focus` (renderer pick/split via `AGENT_CONTROL_VIEW`), `ompdesk_notify`, `_session_stop` (`close`, `delete`), `ompdesk_compare`, `ompdesk_merge`, `ompdesk_schedule`, `ompdesk_autopilot`, `ompdesk_app_list`, `ompdesk_app`, `ompdesk_report`.
- Everything runs through existing GUI paths: prompts/steers through `dispatchRpcCommand` (a paired host's tab goes over the link, so the host's grants and the per-device quota apply; `src/main/remote/quota-commands.ts` lists `agent_control_confirm`/`agent_control_note` as free), `spawnTabForWindow` to open, the renderer's own close, `deleteSession` for delete.
- **Subagents have no control tools** (stock registers host tools on the RPC session only; `host_tool_call` carries no caller id). `e2e/real-agent-control.e2e.ts` checks it: a subagent answers `NO_TOOL` and no tab opens.
- **No count or rate caps** (the user's rule, 19 Oct 2026; none exist in `src/main/agent-control`): any number of sessions opened, messages sent, schedules and Autopilot runs. Only a self-send is refused. Provenance ("opened by you") is main-owned state, never read from text or tool args; it survives a relaunch (`#openedBy` adds every tab whose `host.parentOf` is the caller: the pool's `openedBy`, else the tab holding `openedBySession`).
- Session tools touch no accounts, settings or permissions. **Deleting a saved session the caller did not open** (`ompdesk_session_stop delete:true`) always raises one confirmation card (stock extension-UI `confirm` via the calling tab's omp, bridge `agent_control.confirm`, title prefix `[OMPdesk]`, so it survives a reload and reaches a paired device), whatever "Let agents manage other sessions" says: a delete cannot be undone. Stopping or closing a session it did not open raises the same card only while that switch (`agentControlManage`, **default on**: `agentControlManageEnabled` in `shared/agent-control.ts` treats an unset pref as on and only a stored `false` as off) is off. Sessions it opened itself need none, delete included.
- Closing a resting session's tab (not busy, no open ask, not in the person's view, no draft or prompt of theirs since: `ControlTab.looked`/`userTouched`) raises no card whoever opened it; a session in view or typed into is never closed (re-checked right before the close). Each close is journaled as one quiet `ompdesk.mail` note in both sessions.
- `_answer` answers asks (select, confirm, input, ask); the answered session journals `ompdesk.agent-answer` and toasts "에이전트가 답함 (title)" (bridge `agent_control.note`). A **tool approval** ("Allow tool: …") is approved only when "Let agents approve for other sessions" (`agentControlApprove`, **default off** on purpose: an agent approving another session's tool bypasses the user's own approval policy, so it stays the user's explicit choice; `agentControlApproveEnabled` is true only for a stored `true`) is on, else "사용자 승인이 필요해요" (deny always works). Stock's "Approve <tool> for this session" option needs the same leave. A card titled `[OMPdesk] …` is never answerable.
- **Stock's tool approval shape** is one rule, `src/shared/tool-approval.ts` (`approvalChoices`, `isApprovalSelect`; the renderer's `isApprovalRequest` narrows it; `APPROVAL_MODES` lists stock's `tools.approvalMode` values for the composer, tray and agent catalog): an RPC host has no `approve` UI, so stock `requestApproval` sends a `select` titled "Allow tool: <name>" whose options are stock's `approvalChoiceLabels`: under "Ask and remember" (`always-ask-cached`, omp ≥ 18.12.3) the grants first ("Approve Directory: <folder>", "Approve File" or "Approve" for a target that is no file: remembered for this session), then the same with "Always " in front (every session), then this call only ("Approve", or "Approve Once" beside grants), "Approve <subject> for this session" when the approval mode raised it, and "Deny" last. `ApprovalDialog` shows every choice in stock's order with words that name what is remembered and for how long (`approval.grant.*`); an agent's `approve: true` and Autopilot's approver answer stock's this-call option only (`ApprovalChoices.once`), so every later call still meets its guard, and every approving answer needs the approve leave. Before this a "Ask and remember" prompt read as a plain select. Pinned to the installed omp by `src/main/tool-approval-stock.test.ts` (a command, a file, a folder and a web address, with and without a session subject), the card by `ApprovalDialog.test.tsx`.
- **A plan awaiting approval is a waiting state.** The bridge (`modes/plan.ts` `settlePending`) emits `plan_proposal_resolved {planFilePath}` whenever a shown proposal stops waiting: approved or rejected from any window, device or agent, a turn starting while it waits (a prompt replies to it as feedback, as the card's refine does), plan mode left, a session switch.
