Then `bun run build:memory`, `bun scripts/gen-ai-memory-notices.ts && bun run gen:licenses` (`LICENSES/ai-memory-LICENSE` is the upstream MIT text; `LICENSES/ai-memory-dependencies.txt` lists every Rust crate linked and records the SHA-256 of `native/memory/Cargo.lock`; `licenses:check` fails when they differ), the packaged e2e and a release dry run. The binary updates only with OMPdesk (no CLI-tools updater entry). The upstream copyright and licence text appear only in `LICENSES/`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` and `native/memory/LICENSE`.
  - The launchd label `com.github.akitaonrails.ai-memory`, the Windows logon value `ai-memory` and the data dir are the binary's own and stay, so an existing install keeps working: a new build replaces the macOS binary in its one staged folder (Windows: a new `<version>-<hash>` folder the logon entry is re-pointed at), `setup` restarts the server on the new bytes, the data dir is untouched, other folders are pruned.

#### The Memory page

- Settings › Memory (`settings/memory/`; Settings sub-target `memory:<tab>`, the tab is the route). `MemorySection.tsx` is the shell: tabs Overview, Search, Pages, Handoffs, Sessions, Profile, Maintenance, each one component; project selector `resolveProject` = the user's pick, else the project whose name ends with the active session's folder name, else the latest. Memory off/starting/failed shows only Overview.
  - **Overview**: switch, status, project, its counts, last capture (7-day activity and health in the tooltip), disk size, data folder, "Open web console" (`웹 화면 열기`, only while running) and its password, terminal omp, one privacy line (full text as tooltip).
  - **Search**: full text in the project or all projects (toggle); a hit shows title, path, kind, tier, age, snippet and opens in Pages (hits carry no tier or time on the wire; main fills them from the projects' page lists).
  - **Pages**: by kind (rules, decisions, gotchas, procedures, sessions folded, notes), markdown reader, Edit (a new version through the server's write path, keeping kind, tier, tags, pin), Pin, Helpful/Not helpful, Delete (confirm) and a **Deleted** group whose Restore reads the page from the checkpoint the delete returned (`AiMemoryDeleted.rev`, in `useMemoryPageStore.deleted` and the snapshot cache). Session pages are read-only.
  - **Handoffs**: open first; the detail reads without claiming; Cancel (confirm) discards an open one. Korean says "인계". **Sessions**: recent captured sessions with observation counts; one opens its raw observations read-only, 50 a page. **Profile**: the cross-project profile, read-only; Edit opens the entry's page in Pages. **Maintenance**: rule-based lint (writes no report), retention sweep (Preview is a dry run; Run sweep enabled only after a preview that found something, then confirms), Delete all memory (typed-word confirmation).
- UI rules: lists/detail are `MasterDetail` (detail on its own screen on a phone). Rows use `SettingsRow` `tipOnly` (every description is a tooltip, never inline; `data-setting-desc` must not appear). Row actions are quiet secondary buttons (danger only in the confirm dialog). Close rows declare `--hit-gap-x/-y` (tab bar 4 px, reader's action row 4/16 px) so no touch reach covers a neighbour.
- Data: reads go through `useMemoryRead(key, read)` (`use-memory-read.ts`: last known value at the first frame via `lib/last-read.ts`, re-read in place and when `useMemoryPageStore.version` bumps; never a spinner); every write through `useMemoryAction` (`memory-ui.tsx`: toast, then `bump()`). Strings: `locales/memory/<section>.ts` (en and ko side by side, aggregated by `locales/memory/index.ts`).
- Tests: one `*.test.tsx` per tab plus shell/component row/panel on `memory-test-harness.tsx` (fake `window.omp.aiMemory`); `e2e/memory-page.e2e.ts` on the stateful fake server (palette → Overview → search → open → edit/save → delete → restore → read/cancel a handoff → session paging → side panel), rig shared with `ai-memory.e2e.ts` in `e2e/fixture/ai-memory-rig.ts`. The responsive audit covers every tab and list → detail state (`settings-memory-<tab>`, `settings-memory-<tab>-detail` in `e2e/responsive-surfaces.ts`, answered by `dev-harness/fixture-memory.ts`); run at 390 with `OMP_GUI_RESPONSIVE_ONLY=settings-memory`.

#### Client, binary, lifecycle

- **Client** (`src/main/ai-memory/client.ts`, one `AiMemoryClient`; the token is read in main, never in the renderer): reads are `/api/v1` REST routes plus `/admin/status`, `/admin/checkpoints`, `/admin/profile/*`. Writes go through the server's own write path, never its files: `/admin/write-page`, `/admin/delete-page` (returns `pre_checkpoint`; the client falls back to the head checkpoint read before the call), `/admin/restore-page`, `/admin/lint` (always `dry_run` + `no_llm`), `/admin/forget-sweep`; `memory_feedback` and `memory_handoff_cancel` (no REST route) go as MCP `tools/call` on `/mcp` (stateless JSON, `Accept: application/json, text/event-stream`).
  - Twelve IPC channels (`AI_MEMORY_OVERVIEW|PAGES|SESSIONS|OBSERVATIONS|PROFILE|LINT|SWEEP|WRITE_PAGE|DELETE_PAGE|RESTORE_PAGE|FEEDBACK|CANCEL_HANDOFF`): reads are web-`workspace`, writes `host-admin` in `IPC_POLICY`. For the agent (`app-channels.ts`) `memory.delete_page` and `memory.sweep` are `sensitive` catalog actions beside `memory.purge`; everything else is `never` (the agent has its own memory tools). `ipc.ts` validates every argument (a page path is never absolute or `..`).
- **Which binary** (`binary.ts`): the shipped file is never run in place (its path lands in a launchd unit, a Windows logon entry and omp's MCP entry, and a Windows update replaces the install folder under a running server). `stageBinary` verifies it and copies it to `<tools>/ai-memory/<stageKey>/` (atomic rename; other folders pruned after wiring): on macOS always `current` (`MAC_STAGE_KEY`), on Windows `<version>-<first 8 hex of the recorded SHA-256>` (a running server locks its .exe).
  - **Why one macOS path** (the user's report, 20 Oct 2026: Desktop, Documents, Downloads prompts came back after updates): the server is a launchd job, so it is its own responsible process for TCC, and TCC keys an unbundled program's grants by its absolute path (tccd's `AUTHREQ_SUBJECT` is the binary's path), checked against its designated requirement. The requirement never changes (`AI_MEMORY_REQUIREMENT`: identifier plus the pinned certificate; 0.42.0 and 0.43.0 binaries have different cdhashes and the same requirement), but a path per build was a new TCC client after every update. OMPdesk's own bundle keeps its grants across updates (same bundle id and requirement). Never move the macOS staged path again without a reason worth one more round of prompts for every user.
  - The unit `setup` renders names the binary's SHA-256 (`binary_line` in `native/memory` `user_service.rs`, a comment line in the plist and the systemd unit), so a binary replaced in place changes the unit and `setup` restarts the server on the new bytes instead of answering `Unchanged`. Pinned by `a_binary_replaced_in_place_restarts_the_launch_agent`, `binary.test.ts` (one macOS path across builds, one folder per Windows build), and the requirement in release.yml's `Verify bundle` by `packaging-config.test.ts`.
  - Accepted when its bytes are the build record's `binarySha256` (`readBuiltAsset`; Windows ships it unsigned; a dev build's own copy) or, on macOS only, when its signature satisfies `AI_MEMORY_REQUIREMENT` = `identifier "ai-memory" and certificate root = H"<scripts/mac-signing.json certificateSha1>"` (`codesign --verify --strict -R`, `codeRequirementProblem`). Packaging re-signs the nested binary with OMPdesk's certificate (`mac-sign.cjs`) after `after-pack.cjs` checked the build record, so a release's bytes never equal the record and any other valid signature (system binary, ad-hoc re-signed copy) fails. A package signed ad-hoc therefore has no memory, by design. `release.yml`'s `Verify bundle` checks the same requirement.
  - The staged copy is trusted only by bytes: reused when it is the recorded one or byte-identical to the shipped file verified at that launch; otherwise replaced. (0.38.0/0.38.1 compared the signed bytes with the then-pinned upstream hash, failed every launch silently, so memory never started on a Mac; pinned by `binary.test.ts`, `service.test.ts`, `e2e/packaged-ai-memory.e2e.ts`.)
  - A rebuilt binary changes the unit (its SHA-256 line; on Windows also its path), so ai-memory's `setup` restarts the service on it. A user's own ai-memory (PATH, `~/.local/bin`, Homebrew, the Windows script's folders) is used only when NEWER than the shipped one (data schema migrates forward only).
- **Runtime log**: `AiMemoryService` writes `<userData>/logs/gui-runtime.jsonl` lines with source `ai-memory`: `launch` {enabled, automatic, platform, arch, bundledDir, override} once per start, `binary` {source, version, bin}, `phase` {phase, step, detail, from, fromStep} on every change, `failed` {step, detail, stack} per failure (polls too). `start()` never rejects: whatever it throws becomes the `failed` phase, shown in Settings with its reason and Try again.
- **Lifecycle** (`service.ts`, `AiMemoryService`; started after the first tab). Only a packaged app or `OMP_GUI_AI_MEMORY_BIN=<binary>` changes the machine; a dev build only reports. `OMP_GUI_AI_MEMORY_NO_SERVICE=1` passes `--no-service` for tests; `OMP_GUI_AI_MEMORY_UNIT_PIDFILE=<file>` stands in for the OS service in the fake-binary e2e.
  - The server is **ai-memory's own per-user service** (launchd LaunchAgent / Windows logon entry, installed by `ai-memory setup`), not an OMPdesk child. OMPdesk owns only its own wiring and **never writes ambient configuration**: nothing goes to `~/.omp/agent`, no other agent's config is touched, `uninstall --apply` is never run (it removes every agent's wiring).
  - On: `setup --plan --json --agent omp`; on a fresh data dir `init` plus the one config change below; then `setup --yes --agent omp` with `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` at a scratch folder (`<userData>/ai-memory/omp-ext.setup`, deleted afterwards): `setup` cannot install the service without wiring an agent, so its omp wiring lands in the scratch folder, and the shared skills it writes to `~/.agents/skills` are taken back when this run created them (`terminal.ts removeNewSharedSkills`). A lone `problem: self-check` is not fatal; the `/healthz` poll decides.
  - Every `setup --yes` runs **isolated** (`SpawnSpec.isolated`, `setup/supervisor.ts isolatedLaunch`), still under the setup supervisor. WHY (v0.38.0): the service host outlives OMPdesk by design and on Windows a child inherits every inheritable handle of its parent, so a plain spawn gave the host the app's pipes and those of whatever launched the app, open as long as the service runs (the packaged first-launch e2e's teardown timed out). Isolated, Bun starts the command (`bun -e` with `bun:ffi` calling kernel32 `CreateProcessW`, no PowerShell) with an explicit handle list (`PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST`: NUL stdin, a temp log file as stdout/stderr, `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`); the supervisor passes the log on once the command exited. On macOS launchd starts the server, so it inherits nothing.
  - Bun may still be installing on a fresh Windows machine, so the wiring depends on it (dep `bunPresent`, `findBun`): without Bun a pass runs no command and fails with "Waiting for Bun…"; the setup state reporting Bun present (`index.ts` calls `AiMemoryService.bunInstalled()`) re-runs the pass once, so memory sets itself up on first launch with no relaunch or Try again.
  - Then ai-memory's OWN generators fill the **app-owned extension root** `<userData>/ai-memory/omp-ext/` (`root.ts`): `install-hooks --agent omp --apply --config-file <root>/index.ts`, `install-mcp --client omp --apply --config-file <root>/.mcp.json`, `install-skills --target-dir <root>/skills`, produced next to the root and swapped in only when bytes differ (nothing hand-written, no `.bak`, nothing touched on an unchanged launch). omp reads a package root's `index.ts`, `.mcp.json`, `skills/` (stock `omp-extension-roots.ts`), like `resources/omp-builtin/rea`.
  - Service ownership (`unit.ts`: launchd plist `com.github.akitaonrails.ai-memory` / HKCU `Run\ai-memory` runs a binary under `<tools>/ai-memory` or the binary in use) is stored as pref `aiMemoryServiceOwned`. A service OMPdesk did not start (someone else's unit, or a server already answering) gets `setup --no-service` and is never stopped.
  - Off: the pref is stored first (`extensionArgs()` returns `[]` at once), the root is deleted, and the server stops only when OMPdesk started it and no other client uses it. `uninstall` WITHOUT `--apply` is a read-only listing; any line that is not a managed skill means another agent (or the terminal omp) is wired; an unreadable listing counts as in use. Stop = `launchctl bootout` + delete the plist (macOS), delete the `Run` value + `taskkill` of the PIDs in `<data>/service-host.json` (Windows; never by image name: every live omp has an `ai-memory mcp-bridge`). The data stays.
  - Delete all memory: the same stop (owned units only), then `wiki/ db/ raw/` are deleted after `/healthz` stops answering (`uninstall --purge-data` refuses while any `ai-memory` process runs), then on again (memory off with other clients using the server: `setup` brings it back).
  - A 60 s poll (`/healthz`, `/admin/status` for counts, the root's files) heals by re-running setup and the generators (twice down in a row, or the root gone; at most once per 10 minutes). One check runs at a time (`#check` joins a running one): on Windows a check's file reads outlasted the test's 10 ms interval, and an older check applying its answer after a newer one read a removed root back as present (ready, no heal).
  - **An update never leaves memory down or stale** (v0.41.0: the server stayed off and the extension root kept a two-versions-old build). ai-memory's `setup` restarting a changed LaunchAgent waits until the booted-out job has left the domain (`launchctl print` polled up to 30 s) and bootstraps up to 3 times (`user_service.rs` `start_launchd`): `bootout` returns while the old server drains, and a `bootstrap` sent then fails "5: Input/output error" with nothing loaded. A failed wiring pass still regenerates the extension root from the binary in use (the root follows the binary, not the service), then runs again by itself after 30 s, 2 min and 10 min (`RETRY_DELAYS_MS`, a `retry` log line each; e2e shortens them with `OMP_GUI_RETRY_DELAYS_MS`) before it stays failed for Try again. Pinned by `user_service.rs` tests, the "setup problems" cases of `service.test.ts` and the update-like relaunch in `e2e/ai-memory.e2e.ts` (`FAKE_AI_MEMORY_REFUSE_SERVICE`).
  - Defaults stay ai-memory's zero-LLM path (no provider, no dream) and its single-user cross-project profile. The one change: a **fresh** config gets `embedding_provider = "none"` (`config.ts`), since local embeddings fetch an 87 MB model from huggingface.co and hold ~355 MB resident; search stays full-text, entity and graph, and removing that line turns hybrid search on. An existing config is never touched.
- **Coverage**: every omp OMPdesk spawns gets `-e <root>` while memory is on and the root is complete (`AiMemoryService.extensionArgs()`, read at each spawn: `sidecar-factory.ts` `aiMemoryExtensionArgs` for local tabs and tabs a host runs for a paired device, `schedules.ts` `ompRunLauncher` for scheduled `omp -p` runs; subagents are children of those omps). The terminal `omp` CLI is separate unless the user asks. Short-lived internal omps that pass `--no-extensions` (rename, model setup) capture nothing, on purpose. The brief is the folder's open handoff, injected as a hidden `ai-memory-handoff` custom message at a session's first prompt. A paired host's own OMPdesk wires its own.
- **Terminal omp** (Settings › Memory › "Also use in the terminal omp", `AiMemoryService.setTerminal`, pref `aiMemoryTerminal`; needs memory on): runs ai-memory's `setup --yes --agent omp` against omp's real agent dir (MCP entry `mcp.json`, extension `extensions/ai-memory-omp.ts`; skills to the shared `~/.agents/skills`); each later launch re-runs it so the entry follows the new staged binary. ai-memory has no per-agent uninstall, so the reverse (`terminal.ts removeTerminalWiring`) removes exactly what `setup` added: the extension only when it carries ai-memory's generated header, the `mcpServers["ai-memory"]` entry only when it runs an `ai-memory` binary or the `/mcp` URL (other servers and keys stay; the file goes only when nothing is left). Shared skills stay (other agents read them). While omp's agent dir holds the generated extension, `extensionArgs()` leaves the root out so a session never captures twice.
- **Migration** (once, in the first `enable` pass with pref `aiMemoryTerminal` unset): an earlier build wrote ai-memory's extension and MCP entry into `~/.omp/agent` with the staged binary. When the `ai-memory` MCP entry runs a binary under `<tools>/ai-memory` or the binary in use (`legacyOwned`), exactly those two entries are removed and the pref is stored `false`; wiring that runs someone else's ai-memory stays (it counts as the terminal's). Shared skills the old `setup` left in `~/.agents/skills` stay (indistinguishable from another agent's).
- **Memory quality** (`resources/gui-bridge/memory-prompt.ts`, `src/main/ai-memory/{retitle,distill,distill-model,rules-file,supersede,upkeep}.ts`, started by `index.ts` when the server answers):
  - **Titles**: a session page used to be titled by its first prompt's first line, so an agent-opened session read `<ompdesk-agent-message from="…">` and a subagent "Complete assignment thoroughly:". `promptTitle` (the ai-memory build embeds this very file, and `resources/gui-bridge/redactor.ts` above it, into the omp extension it generates, `native/memory` `install_hooks.rs` `omp_shared_modules`, `include_str!`; its one import of the redactor is dropped there, and `omp_extension_declares_each_top_level_name_once` fails on a name clash in that shared scope) strips the wrapper and stock's prefix and takes the `Title:` line (same rule as `assignmentTitle`), else the omp session title (first prompt only, never a junk one), else the first meaningful line (a one-word heading such as `# Target` labels a brief's section and gives way to the line under it). The extension sends it as `promptTitle` on `user-prompt`; the server titles the observation by it.
  - **Conclusions**: the extension's `agent_end` sends the turn's last reply as ai-memory's opt-in Stop excerpt (`_ai_memory_assistant: {version: 1, excerpt}` plus `capture_assistant=1` on the URL). `conclusionExcerpt` (`memory-prompt.ts`) takes only the text parts of the last assistant message (no thinking, no tool calls), runs them through `redactDiagnosticsText` and cuts them to 2000 UTF-8 bytes on a character boundary. Children send theirs too: a subagent's or agent-opened session's final answer is the most useful conclusion there is. The server keeps omp's excerpt unless config.toml says `capture_assistant = false` (`assistant_capture.rs` `server_captures`: unset keeps omp's alone, `true` every capable agent's; OMPdesk never edits an existing config, so that default lives in our source), re-scrubs and re-caps it. The session page (`synth.rs`) leads with `## Outcome` (the last Stop excerpt), then metadata, prompts and tool counts; it lists no raw observations (their count stays in metadata), and `frontmatter.summary` is the outcome's first sentence (`outcome_summary`), else the counting summary.
  - **The server never looks into a project folder** (the user's report, 20 Oct 2026, after the stable staged path above: Desktop, Documents, Downloads prompts still came for projects there): the launchd server is its own TCC responsible process, and routing an event used to run git discovery and `canonicalize` on the event's cwd in the server (`resolve_project_ids_inner`). The extension now resolves the checkout inside omp (OMPdesk's child, under the access the user already gave OMPdesk): `checkoutParams` (`memory-prompt.ts`; one `git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --show-toplevel --git-common-dir` per cwd per process, roots spelled as the cwd spells them through `realpathSync.native`, the main root = the shared git dir's parent, as libgit2's `commondir` reads it) puts `repo_root` and `main_repo_root` on every `/hook` and `/handoff` URL, `repo_root=` (empty) when the cwd is in no checkout. The server's `project_from_checkout` (`router.rs`) derives the same project name and `repo_path` from those strings and touches no folder; a sender that sends nothing (older extensions, other agents, git missing) or an invalid root keeps the old disk probe on the blocking pool (`client_checkout_from_wire`, `payload.rs`). Pinned by `a_checkout_the_sender_resolved_routes_without_reading_the_disk`, `a_sender_that_found_no_checkout_is_not_second_guessed_from_disk` (`router.rs`), `omp_extension_sends_the_checkout_it_resolved` (`install_hooks.rs`, the generated extension under Node in a real checkout under a `/var` → `/private/var` temp folder) and the `checkoutParams` cases of `memory-prompt.test.ts`.
  - **Korean search**: the FTS tokenizer is `unicode61`, which keeps a particle glued to its noun (`메모리도` found 2 pages where `메모리` found 20). `prepare_fts5_token` (`ai-memory-store` `fts_query.rs`) adds the stem as a prefix term for a Hangul token ending in a listed particle or ending (`HANGUL_ENDINGS`, longest first, stem ≥ 2 syllables): `메모리도` → `(메모리도 OR 메모리*)`; the exact token stays, so nothing an exact match found is lost.
  - **Conclusion hits**: `memory_query {conclusions: true}` (`ai-memory-mcp` `server.rs`, store `search_conclusions_for_project_as`) also answers `conclusion_hits: [{session_id, title, excerpt, created_at, rank}]`: Stop observations with a body in the resolved project (each `scopes` entry when given), FTS-matched with the same prepared query, one per session (its best bm25), best first, at most `limit`; `title` is the session page's, else its first prompt's, else the session id; `excerpt` is an FTS snippet. Independent of page hits and the raw fallback; ignored (key absent) on `global` and `as_of` queries and when nothing matched. Pinned by `ai-memory-mcp/tests/suite/query_conclusions.rs`.
  - **Children never create or consume a handoff**: a session is a child when omp says `ctx.agent.kind === "sub"` or every prompt it received was agent-written (`nextAgentDriven`). The extension adds `subagentType`/`agent_id` (`childMarker`) to its payloads and skips `fetchHandoff`; the router treats an omp child's SessionEnd like OpenCode's (page and summary, no baton). A person typing into an agent-opened session makes it theirs.
  - **A handoff stays in its folder**: `GET /handoff` claims (and an accepted one supersedes) only an automatic handoff whose `cwd` is the request's own folder, normalized (`auto_handoff_matches_cwd` in `ai-memory-store` `reader.rs`; it used to be any parent folder, so a session in another worktree or subfolder of the project took a baton about other work). A handoff with no stored `cwd` and a manual one (`memory_handoff_begin`) stay project-wide.
  - **No cross-project profile**: `[profile] enabled` defaults to off in our source (`ai-memory-core` `profile.rs`), so the server neither harvests a profile nor injects its block at session start; its zero-LLM harvest had filed a test description as "your usual choice". `enabled = "auto"` in config.toml turns it back on.
  - **Repair, once per launch** (`MemoryUpkeep.repair`, idempotent): session pages with a junk title are rewritten through `memory_write_page` with the session (keeps `session_id`/`agent`; precondition = the page version just read; `/admin/write-page` would drop that frontmatter) and the open handoffs a child left are cancelled. Counts go to `gui-runtime.jsonl` (`upkeep-retitle`).
  - **Learning**: ai-memory's own consolidation needs an LLM provider on the server and OMPdesk keeps the server provider-less, so durable pages (decisions, `_rules`, gotchas, procedures, and the owner's preferences and corrections) come from a one-shot `omp -p --model pi/smol --no-tools --no-session --no-extensions` in an EMPTY temp folder (a repo folder would load its AGENTS.md: 157k tokens, $0.64 a call instead of $0.015). It reads only what a PERSON typed (agent-written prompts never) and answers JSON; `parseProposals` refuses unknown kinds, thin bodies, confidence under 0.75, uncited or already-used titles and a `quote` that is not an exact substring of a prompt as typed (no whitespace folding or normalization; the model reads the prompts with their own line breaks; omp `sharpshooter`'s evidence rule), and the app creates pages with `create_only` and the session as evidence. **Preferences and corrections** (how the owner wants any assistant to work) need a quote and go to the reserved `_global` project under `preferences/` (no session evidence: the server refuses a session of another project), which `memory_query` unions into every project's recall; they have their own cap of 2 per window beside the 3 project pages (`DISTILL_MAX_GLOBAL_PAGES`; in one cap a clear preference lost to decisions in the replay of 19 Oct 2026), and a correction (the owner had to have something redone) weighs more: confidence floor 0.65 and it is kept first among them. A refusal line names the kind (`title (kind): confidence 0.7`). The model is shown the project's 30 newest durable pages and 20 of `_global/preferences/` (path · title · the body's first 200 characters, `KNOWN_*`). The prompt also carries the project's rules-file outline (`rulesOutline`: every `#`/`##`/`###` heading, then the bold lead phrases of lines in file order, ≤ 6 KB, no extra model call) and tells the model not to restate a rule it already has: the lexical check below matched 0 of 15 Korean pages against the English AGENTS.md in that replay. As the code backstop, a page the project's rules file already says is skipped (`rules-file.ts`: AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md at the session folder's repository root, read whole up to 4 MB, read-only; one sentence holds ≥ 0.6 of the page's terms weighted by IDF and ≥ 3 of them; the model's English `gist_en` is compared besides title and body, since the owner writes Korean and the file English; it does not tell a rule from its negation, so a correction contradicting the rules file is skipped too). A superseded page (below) leaves recall through its expiry and ai-memory's daily forget sweep later deletes it, so only a checkpoint restore brings it back. Schedule: a conversation with ≥ 6 new person prompts (`DISTILL_MIN_PROMPTS`) and 10 minutes quiet (`UPKEEP_IDLE_MS`), one run per hour (`UPKEEP_MIN_GAP_MS`), six per day (`UPKEEP_DAILY_MAX`), state in `<userData>/ai-memory/upkeep.json`; Settings › Memory › Overview "Learn from conversations" (pref `aiMemoryLearn`, default on).
  - **Supersede** (Graphiti's rule: a newer fact invalidates the old one instead of overwriting it): a learned page replaces only the page the model names in `supersedes` (a path it was shown, in the page's own scope; `supersede.ts`); a pinned page is never a candidate. No title-similarity fallback: "Run the checks before pushing" vs "… before merging" scores 0.73 (character-trigram Jaccard) and a replaced page is later deleted by the sweep, while in the replay (four real decisions that changed, three runs) every correct replacement (2–3 of 4, none wrong) came from the model naming the page. Only after the new page was created, `AiMemoryClient.retirePage` writes the old one again through `memory_write_page` with `expires_at` = now, what the write path takes of its metadata (title, kind, abstract, entities, relations, tags; not `summary`), tag `superseded`, a closing line `_Superseded by [[new]]_` (the write path takes no free frontmatter key, so no `superseded_by:`), precondition = the version the model was shown (`KnownPage.id`); a page edited since is left alone. The new page says `_Replaces [[old]]_`. The server hides an expired page from `memory_query`, search, recent and the page list (an exact read still answers) until the sweep (`sweep.rs` TTL pass) deletes it.
  - Tests: `memory-prompt.test.ts`, `retitle.test.ts`, `distill*.test.ts`, `rules-file.test.ts`, `supersede.test.ts`, `upkeep.test.ts`, `client.test.ts` (`retirePage`), `e2e/memory-upkeep.e2e.ts` (fake server project `gamma`, `FAKE_AI_MEMORY_UPKEEP=1`); the template's `omp_extension_*` tests in `install_hooks.rs`.
- **Memory care** (`src/main/ai-memory/care.ts`, units in `care-units.ts`, started by `index.ts` with the upkeep while the server answers; no setting): memory tidies what it holds by itself, once after every OMPdesk update (`care.json` names the version that ran) and then on a schedule, one unit per 15-minute tick, the first 5 minutes after the server came up. Quiet: no card, no toast; one `gui-runtime.jsonl` line (source `ai-memory`) per page change (`care-curate`, `care-fresh`), one per backfill pass (`care-backfill`), `care-failed` (the unit tries again an hour later), `care-undo`. Settings › Memory › Maintenance shows one line of what changed in the last week ("Tidied by itself this week: 2 out-of-date pages hidden · 1190 conclusions filled in", the pages in its ⓘ) with **Undo**: each changed page comes back through the page's restore from the wiki checkpoint read just before the change (`AiMemoryClient.headCheckpoint`), and a page the person put back is never touched by a unit again (`kept`). Channels `AI_MEMORY_CARE`/`AI_MEMORY_CARE_UNDO` (`never` for agents). Every page change goes through `retirePage` (expired, restorable, synced as retired) or `memory_feedback stale`; nothing is deleted. Measured 20 Oct 2026 on APFS clones of the user's store and journals, never the live store:
  - **One unreadable project stops nothing else** (21 Oct 2026: this Mac's server held an older `seuput0705-ompdesk` project with no repository identity beside `ompdesk`, whose repository identity gives it the canonical key `seuput0705-ompdesk`; every name-based read of the first answered "project … is ambiguous", and learning, Curate, Fresh and Backfill failed for every project all day). Learning (`upkeep.ts` `#pick`, a `failed` line with step `learn-project`), Curate (`loadPages`) and Fresh (`care-skipped`) skip a project the server will not read and go on. Backfill and the sync stay fail-closed on purpose: Backfill files a journal into the one project known for its folder, so a missing listing could misfile it, and the sync's pull cursor would pass a skipped project's older pages for good.
  - **An ambiguous name is repaired at launch and again from the upkeep tick at most once an hour** (`ambiguous-names.ts`, first step of `MemoryUpkeep.repair`, `NAME_REPAIR_GAP_MS`: a paired computer's sync made `yuyu-mos` beside `seuput0705-yuyu-mos` while the app ran, and learning and care failed on it every 15 minutes until a relaunch; log `upkeep-renamed` {workspace, from, to}, and `upkeep-failed` {step: rename, project, detail} for an ambiguous name no rename took, which went unsaid before): the plain `seuput0705-ompdesk` above was made by the paired Windows host's sync on 19 Oct (its five durable pages carry that device as `origin_device`; the host names the repository path style), and this Mac's first own capture then split off `ompdesk`. A listed project with no repository identity whose name `/admin/project-coordinate` calls `ambiguous` is renamed by id to `<name>-unclaimed` (`-2`, … when taken) through `/admin/rename-unclaimed-project` (`native/memory` `ops::rename_unclaimed_project`), which refuses (409) a project with an identity, a repo path or any session in the rename's own transaction; it is its own route, never a flag on `rename-project`, so an older server answers 404 and renames nothing. Names only, no file moves, reversible; afterwards the old name reads as the repository's project through its canonical key. The listing names each project's `identity` (`/api/v1/projects`, `AiMemoryProject.identity`), which the sync uses to pair one repository two computers named differently (Shared between paired computers). Pinned by `ambiguous-names.test.ts`, `identity_resolution.rs` `renaming_an_unclaimed_project_refuses_one_a_checkout_captured_into` and `the_project_listing_names_each_projects_repository_identity`, `admin_rename.rs` `rename_unclaimed_project_refuses_a_captured_project`.
  - **Backfill** (`backfill.ts`, after an update until through, a budget of 300 journals / 1500 rows a pass): conclusions for turns before native capture began (19 Oct 21:49 UTC), from omp's own journals (`SessionIndex.readMessages`, stock's reader). A conclusion is an assistant message with `stopReason: "stop"` and text, cut by the shipped `conclusionExcerpt` (184 of the 188 native conclusions are such a message; "the text before the next user message" matched 17, since runs end before goal, mail or recap custom messages). Per session only turns before its first native conclusion, never one equal to a native one. A session an agent started (a subagent's assignment, another session's `<ompdesk-agent-message>`) is copied only when its brief names the task with a `Title:` line (`explicitTitle` in `memory-prompt.ts`); untitled briefs ("Read history://X §1", "Continue Shell3's work") are the orchestration's own plumbing and were most of the wrong injections (`BackfillReport.untitled`). Project = the one the server holds the session id under, else the one project every known session of that folder belongs to, else skipped (paged past the server's 100-session cap with `sessions(…, offset)`). Written through `writeConclusions` under THIS computer's device id with id `backfill:<session>:<message ms>`, so sync carries them to the paired computers once and never back (a constant origin was offered back every round). State in `care.json` per store id; a second pass writes 0. On the clone: 976 journals, 948 mapped, 532 conclusions written (526 untitled agent briefs skipped) in four passes of 2–7 s. Replay on the prompts a person typed (21 of the 26: the 5 task-brief prompts get no recall live, `shouldRecall`; each prompt's own session excluded as `freshCandidates` does; store cut at each prompt's time, the term corpus built per cut): answer present before → every journal → titled only, code rule 1/9 → 5/9 → 5/9 with false injections 0/12 → 3/12 → 1/12; Jev 1/9 → 5/9 → 6/9 with false injections 0/12 → 1/12 → 0/12.
  - **Curate** (`curate.ts`, `memory-judge.ts`, daily): durable pages (decisions, `_rules`, gotchas, procedures, preferences, profile) of a project and `_global` that repeat or contradict each other. Candidates are each page's 3 nearest by weighted term cosine (≥ 0.1, `CURATE_MAX_PAIRS` 30 a run); a judge says duplicate / A outdated / B outdated / unrelated: Jev through stock's judge chain in a one-shot `omp --mode rpc --no-session --no-extensions -e resources/gui-bridge/memory-judge-host.ts` in an empty folder (only while `hasNativeJudge` and the judge switch, `askJudge` with a 25 s per-call limit, purpose `ompdesk-memory-curate`, confidence ≥ 0.6), else the cheap model (`runSmol`, one batched JSON call with the rules-file outline, only while "Learn from conversations" is on: it sends stored pages to the provider), else the code rule (word Jaccard ≥ 0.8 over both WHOLE bodies, duplicates only). The judge reads each page cut at `PAGE_CHARS` (600), so a verdict other than unrelated on a pair where either page is longer retires nothing (two release procedures with the same opening and different tails stay); only the whole-body code rule may still call such a pair a duplicate. The older or worse page retires naming the kept one; a contradiction across a project and `_global` retires nothing (a project override); pinned pages never; pairs judged unrelated are remembered by both page versions. On a clone seeded with 34 pages (11 stale: pairing codes replaced by Tailscale, manual OMPdesk updates, the board's review buttons, 4 true duplicates; 8 look-alike traps): Jev 11/11 in 7 of 8 runs (10/11 once, cold) with 0 wrong, ≈ $0.001 cold; cheap model with the outline 11/11 in 5 of 5, 0 wrong, $0.007–0.054; code rule 1/11, 0 wrong. The user's real store: 1 candidate pair, unrelated, nothing retired.
  - **Fresh** (`freshness.ts`, daily): decisions, gotchas and procedures naming repository paths or backticked code symbols are checked read-only against the project's main checkout (the newest live session folder → `--git-common-dir`; `origin/HEAD`, else `main`/`master`/`HEAD`; `ls-tree`, `cat-file -e`, `grep -w`, `log --diff-filter=D`, `show -M`, ≤ 8 `log -S`; `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, no fetch, the working tree untouched). Every named thing gone and at least one of them existed in history → retired with the reason; some gone, renamed, or an unknown path beside them → flagged `stale` once per page version (`memo.flagged`); lines that say a thing was removed, `rm`/`git rm` lines in fences, URLs and `~/` paths name nothing. On 24 seeded pages (real deletions from this repo's history, live files, traps): retire 6/6, flag 6/6, leave 12/12, 0 wrong, 2–3 s a run. The user's real store: nothing to change.
  - **Not shipped: feedback from use.** After a recalled turn a judge question (or term overlap) said whether the reply used each note and sent `memory_feedback`; on a held-out half of the replay recall precision did not improve (code rule 0.63 → 0.57, Jev 0.50 → 0.50 with a salience floor; it dropped a relevant decision a status reply had not restated). `memory_feedback` moves only `pages.salience`, which feeds decay of episodic pages (session pages, already out of recall), not `memory_query` ranking or conclusions. The measured patch is kept outside the tree.
  - Tests: `care.test.ts` (one unit per tick, an unfinished one goes on, an update reruns all, a failed one waits an hour, the line, undo through restore and the kept pages, the memo across launches), `backfill.test.ts`, `curate.test.ts`, `memory-judge.test.ts`, `bridge-memory-judge-stock.test.ts` (installed omp, loopback judge), `freshness.test.ts` (real temp repos), `client.test.ts` (`retirePage` with a reason), `MemoryMaintenance.test.tsx`.
- **Recall** (bridge `resources/gui-bridge/modes/memory-recall{,-core,-contracts}.ts`, feature `memoryRecall`; main `src/main/ai-memory/recall-state.ts`): before a prompt a PERSON typed in a main session (`shouldRecall`: never a subagent, an `<ompdesk-agent-message>`, `/`, `!`, `$`; never an Autopilot run, read from its journal state), `before_agent_start` asks the local server `memory_query {query, limit: 8, conclusions: true}` over `POST {url}/mcp` (stateless JSON, the root token, `RECALL_TIMEOUT_MS` 600 ms) and returns at most 3 notes as one custom message `ompdesk-memory-recall` (`display: true`, `attribution: "agent"`): the model reads `recallText` (one `<memory-recall>` block, marked untrusted history, ≤ 600 estimated tokens, `<`/`>` in notes made inert), the transcript draws `MemoryRecallNote` (one muted line "메모리 N건 사용 · <title> · …", kind and date per item as the tooltip, `data-memory-recall`; details `{v: 1, items: [{title, kind, path?, sessionId?, at?}], judged}`, never the text). Any failure, an older server, or nothing relevant: no message, one debug line (`gui-bridge: memory recall`; counts and ms, never the prompt).
  - **Project, fail closed**: never derived by OMPdesk (markers, git-remote identity, home routes and aliases stay the server's). Both calls carry `x-memory-actor-session-id: <omp session id>`, the coordinate ai-memory's hooks publish their active project under (`[auto_scope]` `per_actor`, the default). First `memory_status`: only `scope.resolved_by: "session"` counts (`sessionScope`); then `memory_query` names that `workspace` + `project` (a single-project query still unions `_global`). WHY: a keyed pointer expires after an idle hour (`active_project.rs`) and the first hook may not have landed (the extension queues it), and a miss resolves to the server's default project (`scope.rs` `DefaultAfterMismatch`/`Default`), maybe another project. A miss uses the scope matched earlier in this omp for the same session id and cwd, else no recall. A server without C4's `conclusions` ignores the argument (`QueryArgs` takes unknown keys): page hits only.
  - **Address**: env `OMP_GUI_MEMORY` = the path of `<userData>/ai-memory/recall.json` (`{url, dataDir}`, 0600) on every spawn (`sidecar-factory.ts`, so tabs hosted for a paired device read the host's memory), rewritten by `index.ts` from `AiMemoryService.recallEnv()` on each memory state change and deleted when memory is off; an unknown data dir keeps the last launch's file. A file, not values, because the first tab spawns before memory's setup pass finds the data dir, and the switch can change under a running omp; the bridge reads it and the token (`<dataDir>/auth-tokens/default`) at each prompt.
  - **Choice** (`selectRecall(prompt, candidates, judge?)`, `recallText(items)`, `recallCandidates(answer)`: pure, imported by the parent session's replay harness): candidates are durable pages, `_global` preferences and conclusions, never a session page (its prompt list and tool counts matched nearly any prompt: in the replay most false injections came from one 32 KB orchestrator page). With a native judge (`judge-assist-engine.ts` `recallRelevance`: one judgment, one `noul` question per candidate, purpose `ompdesk-memory-recall`) keep p ≥ `RECALL_JUDGE_MIN` (0.5), most likely first; when it does not decide, the code rule `codeRuleSelect`: ≥ `MIN_SHARED_TERMS` 2 shared content terms (a fifth of a long prompt's, up to 4) between prompt and title+snippet, Korean words stemmed on both sides by one rule (`koreanStem`: up to two particles, a `하다`/`되다` tail after a two-syllable noun; `recallQuery` sends each word as typed plus its stem), durable kinds before conclusions before notes, more shared terms, newest, the server's order. **Conclusions pass an IDF coverage gate** (code rule only; `codeRuleSelect(prompt, candidates, stats)`): over the prompt's terms the conclusion corpus has seen, the shared ones must be at least 2, one of them distinctive (in at most a tenth of the conclusions, idf ≥ `MIN_DISTINCT_IDF` ln 10), and carry ≥ `MIN_CONCLUSION_COVERAGE` 0.2 of the prompt's IDF mass (idf = ln(n / (1 + df)), df summed over the corpus terms `termsMatch` accepts), so a conclusion matched only by common stems is dropped, however much of the prompt they make up, and a greeting or chatter (no term the corpus has seen) gets none. The corpus is main's `RecallTermsIndex` (`src/main/ai-memory/recall-terms.ts`): conclusion bodies of both streams read through `GET /admin/conclusions` after its cursors (sync.ts `readConclusions` reset rule), `addConclusionTerms`, written to `recall-terms.json` beside recall.json (`RECALL_TERMS_FILE`) at memory ready and every 10 min, removed with memory off; deletions are not subtracted until the store changes. The bridge reads it per prompt, cached by mtime and size; no file → today's rule. Everyday words `아니 그걸 맞아 맞지 하나 전부` are stopwords. Replay (20 Oct 2026, 11 answerable and 15 nothing-to-recall prompts, corpus built by `RecallTermsIndex` from each replay server, 254 conclusions): code rule false injections 5/15 → 2/15 on the Mac and on the host, answer present Mac 6/11 → 5/11 and host 5/11 → 4/11 (the one lost: "omp 기능 조사" shares only `omp` and `기능`, both common). Coverage alone gave 3/15 with no answer lost but let an all-common-terms match through; deduped stems changed nothing, coverage 0.3 lost the same answer and kept 3/15 on the host. The remaining two are near-topic hits of one orchestrator session. Earlier replay on real data (9 answerable typed prompts, 12 typed prompts with nothing to recall: greetings, status questions, new topics): answer present 5/9 with either path, false injections 0/12 with the judge and 2/12 with the code rule; raising the code rule's floor to 3 terms left 1/12 but only 1/9 answered. Task briefs other agents write are never recalled for (`shouldRecall`), so they are no control. A candidate is named by the shared session-title rule (`promptTitle`/`isJunkTitle` of `resources/gui-bridge/memory-prompt.ts`: a conclusion's title is its session's first prompt, so stock's subagent prefix and the `<ompdesk-agent-message>` wrapper are stripped), a junk title by the excerpt's first 60 characters. The judge is not warmed ahead of the first prompt: a warm-up would be a billed judgment.
  - **Nothing the model already has** (`freshCandidates`, before the choice): never this session's own conclusion (its turns are in the context; in the orchestrator session that matched almost every prompt, 14 of its 18 recall lines named the session itself, 20 Oct 2026), and never the same version of a note an earlier recall of this branch gave (a page by its version id, so an edited page comes back; a conclusion by its `Stop` time, so the session's later conclusion comes back), unless a compaction since replaced the turn that held it (read from the branch's `ompdesk-memory-recall` entries, so a relaunch keeps it).
  - Tests: `bridge-memory-recall-core.test.ts`, `bridge-memory-recall-judge.test.ts` (the shared fake judge; every undecided state equals the code rule), `bridge-memory-recall.test.ts` (the hook against a real local HTTP server: request shape, header, the scope matched to the session named in the query, every fallback `resolved_by` failing closed, the remembered scope after an expired pointer and never for another folder, quiet paths, the budget), `ai-memory/recall-state.test.ts`, the recall line in `MessageBubble.test.tsx`.
- **Shared between paired computers** (C8; `src/main/ai-memory/sync.ts`, no setting: on whenever memory is on at both ends and a remote link is up): a decision made on the Mac is in the Windows host's memory and the other way round, so a session the Mac opens on the host recalls it there ("remote works like local"). Only durable pages (`isDurablePath` in `remote/app-protocol.ts`: `decisions/ _rules/ gotchas/ procedures/ preferences/ profile/` of any project, `_global` too) and session conclusions (below) cross; `sessions/` pages, every other observation and deletes never do.
  - The device that connected drives both directions over its link (`MemorySync`, at link up, when memory turns ready, every 5 min `MEMORY_SYNC_INTERVAL_MS`; `OMP_GUI_TEST_MEMORY_SYNC_MS` in e2e): `host.memory.pull {since}` brings the host's pages after a cursor (its `updated_at`, then key), `host.memory.push {pages}` sends this computer's pages changed since it last offered them. The host serves both with `servePull`/`servePush` through its own server's write path (`AiMemoryClient.pageCopy`/`writePageCopy`), refuses a device that is no longer paired or whose node is blocked (`remote_blocked`, `RemoteDevices.allowsPeer`) and answers `{memory: false}` with nothing read or written while its memory is off. An older host answers `unsupported`: logged once, left alone until its link comes up again.
  - Identity `(workspace, project, path)`; content `pageHash` (title, kind, tier, body, tags, the write path's metadata; never pin, time or origin). Per host `<userData>/ai-memory/sync.json` keeps the cursors and, per page, the pair of hashes at which both copies last agreed (`base`): a side whose hash still equals its half has not changed, which keeps a sync idempotent when a server stores a page differently from what it was sent. A copy carries frontmatter `origin_device` (the server's admin write-page takes it, `native/memory` admin.rs) until someone edits it there; the host never serves a device its own unedited copy and the device never pushes the host's. A push names the host hash it last saw (`expect`): changed there meanwhile → `conflict` with the host's version, settled at once. Both changed: the later `updated_at` wins on both, logged `conflict` in `gui-runtime.jsonl` (source `memory-sync`, with `round` summaries and `failed`).
  - **One repository, one project on both computers** (21 Oct 2026: the paired Windows host names this repository `seuput0705-ompdesk`, path style, this Mac `ompdesk`; pairing by name made a stray plain project here and an ambiguous name, see Memory care). A page (`MemoryPageWire.identity`) and a conclusion (`MemoryConclusionWire.identity`) carry their project's repository identity (`AiMemoryProject.identity`, `/api/v1/projects`), and the receiver files them under its own project holding that identity (`ProjectNames.localize` in `sync.ts`) before it keys, compares or writes them, on both sides (`servePush`, `serveConclusionPush`, the device's pull, conflict replies and conclusion pull); the pull cursor stays in the host's own keys. An item whose name here belongs to a project of another repository (the same `owner/repo` on another forge) is never filed there: a pulled page or conclusion is skipped, a pushed page answers `failed`, a pushed conclusion `skipped`. An item without an identity, or whose name holds no repository here, keeps the name rule (an older peer drops the field). A computer that gets a repository's pages before it ever captured that repository writes them with the sender's repository (`MemoryPageCopy.repository`, `writeConclusions(…, repository)`, the wire's `identitySource` beside `identity`; `/api/v1/projects` lists `identity_source`): the server's `/admin/write-page` and `/admin/write-conclusions` then resolve the target with `resolve_project_by_identity` (path style; `sync_write_scope` in `admin.rs`), so its own first capture of the repository later joins that project instead of splitting off a second one. An older server ignores the fields and keeps the name rule. Memory synced by name before identities crossed (into a plain project, renamed aside by the repair) is brought back by one whole pass per host (`HostState.identityPass`): every page and conclusion cursor starts over once, and identities now file each item into its repository's project, what is there answering `same`; the pass is done once it saw an identity, else a host that sends none (an older OMPdesk) is passed again a week later (`IDENTITY_PASS_RETRY_MS`; log `identity-pass` {host, seen}). Pinned by the four identity cases of `sync.test.ts` (one where the receiver's server names a repository it did not know path style, `acme-api`, not as the sender did, `team-notes`: the sync reads the project list again after such a write and keys the copy where it landed, `ProjectNames.landed`) and `admin_write_page.rs` `write_page_with_the_senders_identity_lands_where_this_computers_capture_will` (an empty receiver, then its own capture: one project).
  - **Retired pages cross as retired** (with Supersede above): `retirePage` writes a superseded page again with frontmatter `expires_at` now, and every listing and `memory_query` then hide it. The sync lists pages with `include_expired=true` (`AiMemoryClient.allPages`; the server's `/api/v1/…/pages` takes it, default hidden as before), reads `expires_at` into the copy (`MemoryPageCopy.expiresAt`, the wire's `expiresAt`), counts it in `pageHash` (only when set, so a page without one hashes as before), and writes it with the copy (admin write-page takes `expires_at`, validated like `memory_write_page`'s). So a retire is a change that syncs, and a computer that never saw it (a fresh pairing, a lost `sync.json`) cannot re-create the page live: the host holds the expired version, the push is a conflict, and the later change (the retire) wins on both. Known gap: deletes do not cross, so a page the forget sweep removed on one computer before the retire reached the other stays live there.
  - **Every copy write is conditional** (no read-then-write race): `pageCopy` carries the version it read (`MemoryPageCopy.id`, the server's page id) and `writePageCopy(copy, origin, expected)` writes only over that version, or with `null` only where nothing is yet (admin write-page `expected_page_id` / `create_only`, `Wiki::write_page_if`; 409 `precondition failed` and nothing written, `false` to the caller). The host's push serving writes over the version it just judged; a page that moved on meanwhile (a retire) answers `conflict` with the new version, settled by the later change. Applying a pulled page here does the same; a page that changed here meanwhile is left as it is and goes out with this round's push.
  - **Session conclusions cross too** (the user's approval, 20 Oct 2026: each turn's final-answer excerpt, ≤ 2 KB, redacted). Recall reads conclusions from `conclusion_hits` (the server's `Stop` observations with a body), never from session pages, so durable pages alone answered 1 of 11 replayed host prompts. After the pages, the device runs `host.memory.conclusions.pull {since: {native, synced}}` then `…conclusions.push {conclusions}` (`serveConclusionPull`/`serveConclusionPush`, same `#memoryFor(peer)` gate, `remote_blocked`, `{memory: false}`). Each computer offers two streams through `GET /admin/conclusions?source=native|synced&after=` (`AiMemoryClient.conclusions`; `seq` = an AUTOINCREMENT log of the native conclusions, `native_conclusions` in `V85__native_conclusion_log.sql`, never `observations.rowid`, which SQLite reuses after the newest session is purged; the copies' own AUTOINCREMENT id; each page names its database, `store`, a random id made by the migration, and `last`, the stream's highest seq): its own conclusions and the copies it holds from other computers, never one whose origin is the asking computer. `MemoryConclusionWire` carries workspace, project, origin id (the capturing server's observation id), session id, title (session page title, else first prompt, else id), body, time and `origin`. Title and body go through `redactDiagnosticsText` and the 2000-byte cut before they leave and again on receipt (`cleanConclusion`). The receiver writes through ONE server write path, `POST /admin/write-conclusions` (`AiMemoryClient.writeConclusions`; native/memory `synced_conclusions.rs`, migration `V84__synced_conclusions.sql`): insert-if-absent per `(workspace, project, origin id)`, scrubbed again by the server's own sanitizer, `written|same|skipped` per row. The copies live in their own table `synced_conclusions` with an FTS twin on `observations_fts`' tokenizer: they feed no consolidation, session page, session count or raw-hit fallback. `memory_query {conclusions: true}` merges them with the local conclusions, one hit per session, best first. A copy is immutable, deletes do not cross, and the receiver's own observation retention (`prune_consolidated_observations`'s cutoff) and `purge_session` clear copies. `sync.json` keeps two cursors per host (`conclusions` for the host's streams, `conclusionsSent` for this computer's, stopping at the first row the host did not take), each with the `store` it was read from: a cursor from another database (Delete all memory makes a new one) or past `last` (a restored backup) restarts its stream from 0, and the re-offered rows answer `same`. A host without the conclusion methods (`unsupported`) or whose server lacks the routes costs only the conclusions step (`conclusions-unsupported` / `failed` step `conclusions`); pages still sync. Replay (a fresh host store after one sync round with a time-cut copy of the Mac's store; 11 answerable prompts, 15 with nothing to recall): answer present 1/11 with durable pages only, 5/11 with conclusions by the code rule (5 false injections; the Mac itself 6/11, 5) and 6/11 with the native judge (2 false; the Mac itself 6/11, 2).
  - Tests: `sync.test.ts` (two fake servers on one clock: both directions, sessions stay, no echo and no traffic once settled, a lossy server without origin marks settles once, an edited copy goes back, the later change wins, a host change between pull and push survives, a page whose read failed once on either side still crosses with nothing changed since (a failed host read fails the pull batch and keeps its cursor; a failed read here stays `pending` past the push time), a page retired on either side leaves the other's listing and stays retired, a computer with no sync history cannot bring a retired page back live, a retire landing between the check and the write on either side is never overwritten (the fake runs it at the write's start, before the precondition), state across a relaunch, memory off, an older host; conclusions: both directions, never back to their origin, idempotent rounds, a third computer's copy forwarded, a secret redacted on send and on receipt, the cursor across a relaunch, a failed row offered again, either computer's store replaced or rewound, an older host or server costing only conclusions), `client.test.ts` (expiry and version on the copy, `expected_page_id`/`create_only`, 409 → `false`, `allPages`, the two conclusion routes), the `host.memory.*` cases of `remote/host-services.test.ts`, the admin write-page `origin_device`, `expires_at` and `write_page_conditional_refuses_a_page_that_moved_on` tests, `api_pages_lists_expired_pages_only_when_asked` and `tests/suite/synced_conclusions.rs` (written copies answered as `conclusion_hits` beside local ones, repeat → `same`, invalid → 422 with nothing written, secrets scrubbed, both listing streams, other projects never mixed, a saved cursor still seeing a conclusion captured after the newest session was purged, `store` and `last`) in `native/memory`, `e2e/memory-sync.e2e.ts` (two apps, two fake servers that honor the preconditions: a decision written on either side readable on the other with its origin, never written again; the host retires it and it leaves the device's page list, never coming back live; a conclusion captured on either side is written once on the other under its origin, answered there as a `conclusion_hit`, never written back).
- **Cost and privacy** (3.1.1): +3.5k tokens of MCP schemas and +0.36k of skills per session (prompt-cached), +0.4k at the first prompt only with an open handoff; idle server 0.0% CPU, 39 MB resident without embeddings. Prompts and tool results are scrubbed of vendor keys (`sk-`, `ghp_`), bearer tokens, private keys, `*_KEY=…` env shapes and URL passwords before storage (tested); free text such as "my password is …" is stored as typed; the last reply of each turn is kept as a ≤ 2 KB conclusion, redacted by the diagnostics redactor before it leaves omp and scrubbed again by the server, on this computer and, through the sync above, the person's paired computers (no other reply text is recorded; prompts, tool activity and session pages never leave the computer); `[capture] ignore_paths` in `.ai-memory.toml` covers file and shell tools only.
- **Tests**: `src/main/ai-memory/*.test.ts` (staging, selection, client, service with a fake runner and fake unit: off never lists or applies an uninstall-all, the root is loaded only while on, ownership, other clients, purge, the terminal button, the migration; plus `terminal`, `unit`, `root`, IPC; on macOS the real codesign refuses `/usr/bin/true`, an ad-hoc copy named `ai-memory` and an ad-hoc re-signed copy of the synced release); `src/main/setup/runner.test.ts` (an isolated command's detached descendant holds none of the supervisor's pipes; argv quoting); `sidecar-factory.test.ts` and `schedules.test.ts` (the `-e` seams).
  - `e2e/ai-memory.e2e.ts` on the stateful fake binary `e2e/fixture/fake-ai-memory.ts` (a planted Claude Code wiring survives on/off, root, terminal button, delete-all, migration, Settings, panel). CI: `gh workflow run ci.yml -f win_e2e=true -f win_e2e_grep=ai-memory`; `-f installer_check=true` for the packaged setup.
  - `e2e/first-launch-window.e2e.ts` (`@smoke`, Windows: the packaged app sets the real service up; ⌘Q's quit and an update's quit must close the app's pipes within 30 s and leave no supervisor, `ai-memory` command or omp of OMPdesk's running, while the service may stay; "started by the app" is what descends from its main process (`descendantsIn`, `e2e/process-tree.ts`), never what runs its binary: an unpackaged run (no `dist/win-unpacked`, no `OMP_GUI_E2E_EXE`) is `node_modules/electron/dist/electron.exe` for every spec's app, and a killed worker leaves its app running; ci.yml's win-e2e packages `dist/win-unpacked` for it; `-f win_e2e_grep=first-launch`).
  - `e2e/packaged-ai-memory.e2e.ts` (`@smoke`, macOS: the packaged `dist/mac-arm64` app, or `OMP_GUI_E2E_APP`, in a private HOME and userData stages the re-signed binary, reaches `ready` with its real launchd service, creates the root and logs `launch`/`binary`/`phase: ready`; skipped for an ad-hoc package and while the user's own label or port 49374 is in use; ends with `uninstall --apply --yes` in the private HOME).
  - Opt-in `e2e/real-ai-memory.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`: real release in a temp HOME with its real service, real omp reads the root's MCP entry, two sessions in one folder, the second gets the first's handoff, off removes the launchd service).

### Sidebar families and subagent titles

- Files: `layout/sidebar/SidebarSubagents.tsx` (group, chip), `SidebarFamilyRow.tsx` (one-line row), `sidebar-family.ts` (pure rules), `stores/sidebar-subagents.ts` (open state).
- **Family** = everything derived from a session, shown together under its row, one indent level in: its subagents (the tab runtime's roster) and the sessions its agent opened with `ompdesk_session_open`, in a worktree or not, whatever their folder.
- **Provenance**: `AgentControlRuntime.#spawn` calls `SidecarPool.setTabOpener(tabId, callerTabId)` (not for an Autopilot child, which keeps its run's row). The tab entry carries `openedBy` (opener's tab id) and `openedBySession` (its session file) on `IpcTabInfo`; the saved tab layout keeps the file as `PersistedTabDescriptor.openedBy` (a restored tab's id is new; the file finds the opener; the opener's tab closing leaves the file). The renderer matches by tab id first, then by file (`openerOfSessions`): an opened session whose opener is listed leaves its project's rows (one without a live opener stays a marked row of its project, see Worktree names), one opened by an opened session goes under the first of them, a cycle nests nothing.
- **Row**: ONE line (`.omp-family-row`, 28 px; `--sc-row-touch` 36 px on a phone): 16 px kind glyph (subagent `Bot`, worktree session `GitBranch`, plain session `MessageSquare`), title, one status token (the working arc while running, waiting ⚠, done ✓, failed ✕, paused) and age (an opened session's ticks and steps aside while it runs; a subagent's is its elapsed time); status in words, folder, model and progress are the tooltip. A subagent's title follows "Subagent titles" below; an opened session's is `agentTaskTitle`/`sessionDisplayTitle` (the `Title:` line else its first line, via `assignmentHeadline`/`stripTitlePrefix` in `subagent-title-contracts.ts`). The parent row's chip reads "🤖5 · ⑂4" with the running count as a filled pill: counts only. **The sidebar shows no money** (the user's rule, 20 Oct 2026: a "⑂1 · $1060.19" chip squeezed the title to "병...", and on a subscription the figure is stock's API-price estimate, not a charge): no cost on the chip, the row or a child's tooltip, which names only the model and role (`childUsageText`: "anthropic/claude-x · @task"; a subagent's from stock's `AgentProgress` `resolvedModel`, `modelRole`; an opened session's from main's review push `AgentReviewEvent.usage`, else the closed record). Cost lives on the work board and in the Work tree (see Work board › Money). The sidebar is on the phone's first screen (`WEB_FIRST_SCREEN_JS_BYTES`): usage is read where it is shown, never passed through `openedChildRows`. A phone bounds hit areas with `--hit-gap` (`mobile.css`).
- **A family is folded by default and NEVER opens or folds by itself.** WHY (v0.38.1, phone): every follow-up message to a subagent is a new run, so the old open-on-start edge (`noteFamilyActive`) re-opened families over the person's fold; it was removed with fold-on-finish. Running, finishing or starting children change only the chip (running count = filled pill; warning tint `data-family-waiting`, `sidebar.family.waiting` in its summary while a child waits for the person).
  - The only thing a folded family shows unasked is a child that waits for an answer or approval (`waiting`: a stale registration, or an opened session `awaitingInput`): that row shows alone under the folded parent (`data-sidebar-surfaced`, flat, no connector for the others) and leaves when answered; the store's `expanded` is untouched.
  - The person's own toggle (chip, session row click, ←/→, "expand/collapse all", the dock's reveal) is the only writer of `expanded`, remembered per session in memory; with no child the entry is dropped.
- **Finished children are never listed** (the user's rule: a finished child is meaningless in the sidebar). A family shows only children that are live or need a look: running, waiting, failed or stale, or an opened session with work left to review (`problem`: failed ending, unmerged commits, uncommitted work). A finished subagent (completed/aborted/cancelled, `isFoldedSubagent`) or a finished opened session that landed clean (`status` `done`, `isShownOpened`) has NO row and NO "n done" fold (no `sidebar.family.done` string, no `data-family-done`). The chip counts only shown children (`familyCounts`) and is absent when none show; a family left with nothing is dropped from the store. Nothing is deleted: a finished subagent stays in its session's Subagent panel and the work board; a finished opened session stays in the session picker/search. "Hide this session's subagents" hides only the subagents.
- **Opened sessions are children with the same lifecycle and visibility as subagents.** One derived status per opened session (`openedChildStatus` in `sidebar-family.ts`, a rule table over `ChildFacts`: waiting, running, problem = finished with an error/abort ending or unmerged/uncommitted work, done = finished; `CHILD_TOKEN` maps it to the row token, a problem is ✕) feeds the row token, chip counts and fold. A `done` one is not listed (`FamilyOpened.status`); running, waiting, problem and not-yet-run ones stay rows.
  - A session main closed after its work landed has no tab, so provenance is pref `agentClosedChildren` (`ClosedChild`: file, opener's file, title, folder; read by `stores/closed-children.ts`, `openerOfSessions(…, recordedOpeners)`): not listed (done); the record still finds its opener, so a reopened one keeps nesting and a stored one opens from the session picker (in `cwd` = the checkout it came from when its worktree was removed).
  - A session no tab holds is never a row of its own. Its opener is found, in order, by the tab's `openedBySession` file (the opener needs no tab: a stored opener row carries the family too), the closed record or the review list (`recordedOpeners`, below), then the agent brief in its indexed first message (`agentMessageSender` reads `from` of `<ompdesk-agent-message from="…">`; `briefOpenerOf`: the newest session with that title created before the child; an Autopilot child keeps its run's row whatever its brief says).
  - **A window viewing the opener sees its family like the owner** (the user's phone and iPad reports on 0.42.0: "병렬세션이 안보임", no light on the opener). A phone, tablet or second desktop window viewing a session another window holds has no tab of the sessions its agent opened, so the brief (a title that has often changed since) and the index were all it had: the children were loose or hidden as finished and the idle opener had no light. Main's review list (`AGENT_CONTROL_REVIEW`, pushed to every window showing the opener; `AgentReviewItem.sessionPath`) is the source for such a child: it names the opener (`recordedOpeners` in `Sidebar.tsx`) and the child's status (`familyChildFor`'s `elsewhere`: running, waiting, failed, unmerged), so the row, its token, the chip and the opener's light read as on the owner. The sidebar asks once for each tab this window views (`shared`), since pushes come only when a child starts or ends. A tap views the owner's tab (one omp). Pinned by "lights the boss's row in a window viewing it" in `Sidebar.test.tsx` and `e2e/viewer-family.e2e.ts` (phone through the fake serve; `OMP_GUI_VIEWER_FAMILY=<dir>` captures).
  - One without an opener (none listed from record or brief, or only its `omp/agent/…` worktree says an agent opened it) is listed only while it needs a look (`SidebarLooseChildren`, `data-sidebar-loose-children`: plain family-style rows closing its project's main-checkout group; a project with nothing else to show has no group); a finished one is not listed; a live tab keeps its row.
  - **A session a paired device's agent opened on this computer is a plain row here**: its opener is on that device, so neither the brief nor the loose-children rule applies (`SessionInfo.promptOriginDevice`, the `deviceId` of the `ompdesk.prompt-origin` entry in front of the first prompt, read by the stock helper's `withFirstPrompts` for rows whose first prompt is an agent brief). Before this, the host's own sidebar hid every such session once its tab closed (reported on 0.41.0 Windows: the Mac listed this PC's sessions, the PC showed none). Pinned by `session-index-first-prompt.test.ts` and `Sidebar.test.tsx`.
  - **A session opened on a paired device is the opener's child like a local one** (`ompdesk_session_open {device}`; the user's remote-parity rule). Its tab carries the same `openedBy`/`openedBySession`; `remoteOpenedChildren` (`sidebar-family.ts`: a device tab whose opener is a tab of this computer, or whose `openedBySession` is one of this computer's sessions) nests it under the opener as a one-line family row built from the tab (`remoteFamilyChildFor` in `Sidebar.tsx`: same status rule, token, chip count and opener light), labelled with the device's name (`FamilyRow` `device`, `data-family-device`), and `SidebarDeviceSection` leaves it out of that device's list (`nested`). The board's row names the device too (`OpenedChildRow.device`, `data-board-device`, from `useRemoteStore` hosts). Pinned by `Sidebar.test.tsx` ("on a paired device under the opener"), `opened-children.test.ts` and the paired-device test of `e2e/agent-control-across.e2e.ts`.
  - The row title is `sessionDisplayTitle`, else the tab's `task` (`IpcTabInfo.task`, the task's `Title:` line), else the closed record's title, so an opened session never reads "(untitled)" before its index entry has a first message.
  - The same children are on the work board ("Child sessions" section, `opened-children.ts` + `hooks/use-opened-children.ts`: title, status token, current step and clock from the review item main pushes, branch and merge state, cost (`data-board-opened-cost`, `CostAmount` by kind: "≈ $0.25" on a subscription, a charge apart, model and the cost in words in the tooltip); waiting first with the warning tint; status only, no buttons, a click focuses the child), counted by the phone strip (⑂ count with running/waiting in the label; the expanded panel is the board), the composer's background cue (running ones), the dock island ("Sessions" segment) and the turn-resume log (`opened`). The board has no "Needs review" section and no Review/Approve/Discard/dismiss action (`needsReview` is gone): the opener's agent reviews and lands its children with `ompdesk_compare`/`_merge`/`_session_stop`, and a child that needs the person is a waiting row and a notification.
  - **Finished children fold into one "n done" row** (`OpenedSection` in `WorkBoardOpened.tsx`, `data-board-opened-done`, the plan's own `workBoard.doneFolded`: "3 done" / "3개 완료"; it replaced a "closed N" count beside the title that repeated the closed sessions also listed as rows): a done tab and a session main closed after its work landed are no rows of their own, the fold sits after the rows still at work or worth a look, and the section's count names only those. A tap on the fold lists the finished ones in place (`data-board-expands`, so the phone's strip stays open), each with its report and opening its session as before. Pinned by `WorkBoardReview.test.tsx`, `WorkBoardOpened.live.test.tsx` and the board steps of `e2e/opened-close-relaunch.e2e.ts`, `e2e/daily-flows.e2e.ts` and `e2e/agent-control-across.e2e.ts` (a paired device's child whose turn ends).
- **Work tree** (the `work` sheet, `panels/work-tree/`; palette `work-tree` and the work board's Child sessions row, never opened by itself): every session of this window with children as one tree each (`buildWorkTree`, pure, `work-tree.ts`): the session, its subagents, the sessions its agent opened on any computer (device name on the row), their own subagents and opened sessions, then each worktree's branch (base, merged/unmerged in the tooltip). Live rows and the ones that need a look are listed; what finished, at any depth, goes in the family's one closed "Finished N" fold (`data-work-tree-finished`), so this is the one place a finished child shows. One line per row with its own cost (`CostAmount`), model, role and the cost in words in the tooltip; the head shows the whole family's cost (`familySpend`: the session's own journal, which holds a finished subagent's spend with its task result and main's review push carries under the caller's own key with its model; plus its subagents still at work; plus every session it opened at any depth, closed ones included; nothing counts twice and the total never drops when a child ends), by kind, each kind its own figure and never one sum (`CostKinds`: charged, estimate, and unknown while no billing class is read; "$0.09 · ≈ $0.49"). A paired device's session running on this computer (`IpcHostedSession`) is a root of its own while at work. Reads the stores the sidebar and the board fill; every tab's roster is followed through `onSessionRuntimesChange` and each runtime's `subagents` store (`useRosters`), no poll.
  - **Unused worktrees** (`OrphanWorktrees.tsx`; explanation behind ⓘ): `get_orphan_worktrees` / `remove_orphan_worktree` (local handlers in `local/git.ts`, so a paired host's tab answers on the host; free in `quota-commands.ts`) over `src/main/worktree-orphans.ts`: the registered linked worktrees under stock's worktree root (`stockWorktreeLocation`) named `gui-…` that no open session uses (`setOpenFoldersSource`, set once in `index.ts` from `SidecarPool.workspaceDirs()` and `LocalHostServices.workspaceDirs()`; before that the commands refuse `not_ready`, never list everything), classified by `classifyOrphan` (dirty wins, then untouched, landed (`squashed` when by content), else unlanded with its commits). A landed or untouched one has a remove button (and "Remove N that landed"), behind one `ConfirmDialog`; main re-reads the worktree, refuses `not_orphan`/`not_removable` (`in_use` when an open session holds it), removes without `--force` and the branch only with `branch -d` (a squash-merged branch stays: `branch-kept`). Removal and opening a tab exclude each other per folder (`src/main/worktree-removals.ts`): right before git runs, `withRemovalClaim` reads the open sessions again and claims the worktree in one synchronous step, and while it holds it `SidecarPool.acquire` returns null and a paired device's `tab.open` is refused `worktree_removing`; a tab already open there makes the removal `in_use`. Paints its last read (`lib/last-read.ts`, key per folder and host).
  - **Swept by themselves** (the user's rule, 20 Oct 2026: finished agent worktrees never pile up; v0.42.0 left 73 worktrees, 105 GB, under `~/.omp/wt` while stock's `omp worktree clear` judged none orphaned): disk care (`src/main/disk/care.ts`, see Machine setup and updates › Disk care) runs `sweepLandedWorktrees` with its `prepare`, in a packaged app outside `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=1` only (a dev or test instance does not know the real app's open sessions, so their worktrees would look unused to it; `OMP_GUI_TEST_DISK_CARE_WORKTREES=1` lets a test that owns its worktrees). `orphanRepositories` finds the repositories by the `gui-` folders under stock's global worktree root (stock helper `git.worktree_location`, so no session needs to run); every `landed`/`untouched` orphan goes through `removeOrphanWorktree` with `keepBranch` (the removal claim, `worktree remove` without `--force`, the branch always kept); dirty, unlanded, locked, in use, outside the repository's own root and the primary checkout stay. It also takes a closed child whose work landed after the close. **`git worktree remove` deletes ignored files without asking**, so every removal (the sweep and the person's Remove alike) first runs `archiveBeforeRemove` (`disk/saved-worktrees.ts`): what no tool makes again (an edited seeded `.env`, a local database) goes to the private archive and the worktree is listed under Saved worktrees with Restore; the sweep also keeps one a known process works in. The former switch `worktreeAutoClean` (stock `omp worktree clear` on a timer, default off; it removed 0 of 73) is gone and deleted once from prefs; stock's clear stays Settings › Projects & launch › Worktrees' "Clean up now" button, because stock deletes those folders outright. A repository whose own `worktree.base` puts its worktrees elsewhere is found through this computer's project list (`OrphanDeps.projects`, the sidebar's projects and removed ones): stock's location for it names another root holding `gui-` folders. Pinned by `worktree-orphans-sweep.test.ts` and `disk/disk-care.test.ts` (real temp repos).
  - **Saved worktrees** (`SavedWorktrees.tsx`, below the unused list; hidden while empty): `get_saved_worktrees` / `restore_saved_worktree` (local handlers in `local/git.ts`, the calling tab's repository, free in `quota-commands.ts`) over `disk/saved-worktrees.ts`. Restore puts the folder back at its old path (on its branch when the branch still points at the saved HEAD and no worktree has it out, else detached; `not_saved`, `path_exists`), the saved commit's changes as uncommitted work and what was staged back in the index, then the repository's seeding and setup hook, then the archive: **the archived bytes win** over anything a hook or the seeding made at that path, which is moved aside to `<name>.ompdesk-before-restore`, never deleted (a tracked secret's edit goes over HEAD's copy, which git holds). Record and archive go only when every archived file came back (a folder where a file was keeps both).
  - **Squash-aware landing** (`branches.ts` `landing`; pattern after Orca's `branchMergesWithoutTreeChanges`, MIT, re-implemented): a tip that is no ancestor of the parent's HEAD or upstream still counts landed when `git merge-tree --write-tree <target> <tip>` yields the target's own tree or `git cherry` lists only `-` lines; then the diff base is the tip, so only uncommitted work is left. Review items, auto-close, `removeSafeWorktree` and the orphan list all read it.
  - Tests: `work-tree.test.ts`, `WorkTreePanel.test.tsx`, `worktree-orphans*.test.ts` and `branches*.test.ts` (real temp repos: fast-forward, squash merge, unlanded, dirty, untouched, in use, outside the root); captures `OMP_GUI_WORK_TREE_SHOTS=<dir>` (`e2e/work-tree-captures.e2e.ts`, dev harness, desktop/390 × light/dark × en/ko; harness answers from `dev-harness/fixture-work-tree.ts`).
- Tests: `SidebarSubagents.test.tsx` (no auto-open, the waiting child alone, remembered toggle, no done row, no money on the chip or a tooltip), `sidebar-family.test.ts` (child usage), `Sidebar.test.tsx` (nesting by opener, folded by default, finished children not listed), `sidecar-pool.test.ts` and `tab-layout.test.ts` (provenance persistence), `touch-targets.test.ts` (row sizes); `e2e/family-fold.e2e.ts` on the dev harness (phone and desktop, `OMP_GUI_FAMILY_FOLD=<dir>` writes captures).

#### Subagent titles
