- `subagentPrimaryLabel` (`panels/subagent-graph.ts`), store rules in `stores/subagents.ts`, `unwrapStockPrompt` in `resources/gui-bridge/modes/subagent-title-contracts.ts`. A subagent's row, panel header and board card read the `Title:` line of its ORIGINAL assignment, else stock's own label (`description`), else the first line of that assignment, else the role name; never a later message.
- Stock's frames differ per run (`task/executor.ts`, `modes/rpc/rpc-subagents.ts`). The first run's progress carries `task` = the assignment wrapped by `prompts/system/subagent-user-prompt.md` ("Complete assignment thoroughly:" + blank line) and `assignment` = the bare text. Every later run reports its own message as `task`, NO `assignment`, no label (stock's `handleProgress` then overwrites the roster's `assignment` with undefined): **(a)** a follow-up to an idle subagent (`attachIrcWakeTurnMonitor`; `task` is the plain follow-up text), **(b)** revival after relaunch (`restoreSession` → `runSubagentFollowUpTurn`; `task` is the wrapped original).
- So: `assignment` is sticky in the store (a frame without one keeps the row's); `unwrapStockPrompt(task)` recovers it from (b) (store and label); a plain `task` is never a label; a roster snapshot after a follow-up (no assignment, no label) reads the first user message of the subagent's transcript once (`recoverAssignment`, `get_subagent_messages` then `get_subagent_transcript`). Pinned by `stores/subagent-titles.test.ts` with stock-shaped frames.

### Work board and work card

- **Work board** (`layout/work-board/WorkBoard.tsx`, rows `board-parts.tsx`, rules in `work-board.ts`, look in `styles/work-board.css`, design record `docs/work-board-design.md`; mounted per session pane by `SplitWorkspace`): a pane with at least 300 px (`WORK_BOARD_MIN_SPARE`) beside its transcript column (`workBoardFits`, measured by ResizeObserver; constants pinned to components.css by `work-board.test.ts`) docks this session's live state in that margin, 264 px wide, 320 px from 420 px of margin (`workBoardWidth`, set on the pane as `--omp-board-width`; a 1920 px window beside the sidebar).
  - **A tablet held sideways docks it too** (the user's call, 20 Oct 2026; `workBoardDocks`, by the measured pane width, never a device check): in the web build's `tablet-landscape` shell the board also docks at its 264 px where the transcript column may narrow to keep at least 503 px (`WORK_BOARD_MIN_TRANSCRIPT`; `workBoardFitsNarrowed`: pane − board − gap − the column's paddings). The floor is what still reads, measured in 1 px steps on the harness (`?stress=1`, ko): the composer toolbar is the box less its 2 px border and at 500 px (`@container omp-toolbar (max-width: 500px)`) the model chip loses its name, so 503; at 504 the five-column table fits without sideways scroll (notes wrap to 3 lines; 5 at 466, 8 at 419), a diff's 61-column paths fit, the work card's head stays one line and the toolbar one row (`work-board.test.ts` pins the floor to that rule). With the sidebar open (248 px): iPad Pro 12.9" sideways (1366) keeps 763 px of text, iPad Pro 11" (1194) 601, iPad Air (1180) 588, iPad mini (1133) 544: all dock; a 1024 px tablet (776 px pane, 441 px) keeps the strip until its sidebar folds; every portrait tablet keeps the strip. A desktop window keeps the full rule (its transcript never narrows), so it docks from a 1137 px pane as before. Under touch the board's list declares `--hit-gap-y` (rows 1 px apart) so a docked row's reach never takes the next row's tap. Pinned by `work-board.test.ts`, `responsive-smoke.e2e.ts` (1366 and 1133 docked, 1024 strip), `mobile-shell.e2e.ts` (iPad Pro 11 sideways docked) and the responsive audit's 1133/1180/1366 landscape looks.
  - **One line per row, everywhere** (the user's rule, 20 Oct 2026): a lead mark, a label that truncates, one short figure; everything else (a subagent's step, tokens and model, a job's kind, an opened session's report, branch and cost in words, the goal's budget and time) is the row's tooltip. No row may widen the board: every row's text column clips (`min-w-0 overflow-hidden`), and the responsive audit's `overflow-sideways` (with `responsive-smoke`'s busy board, docked at 1600 and in the 390 strip) fails a board wider than its box. Sections cap their rows (`SECTION_CAP`: waiting 3, running 4, opened 3; the plan 5, `TODO_BOARD_ROWS`) and fold the rest into one "+N more" row that lists them on a tap (`CappedRows`).
  - Content, in order: the head (one line: pulse, name, the plan's bar and "1/3", ✕; a linked worktree's project; one meters line where the subscription accounts' gauges (`AccountLimit.tsx` with `UsageGauges compact`, the same chip as the sidebar's usage lines: the window's name muted, a `--sc-space-1-5` gap that holds however narrow the bar, the percent in tabular numerals; read as "5시간 한도 64%"), and each metered provider's charge, `SessionSpendLine.tsx`, sit side by side and wrap only when they do not fit; addresses, resets and today's charge are tooltips); waiting questions/approvals (warning wash; ⌥B `board.focus` jumps to them; the only live region); a failed check (`FailedCheckSection`, danger; a tap sends `requestRevealStep` (`chat/work/reveal-step.ts`) to the tab's transcript, which finds the work card holding the call, scrolls to it, opens it and reveals the call: a collapsed or unmounted card too); in an Autopilot tab the run's own sections (its head and the `autopilot`, `cycles`, `ship`, `backlog` sections, see Autopilot › Overview); "Tools used" ("사용 도구", section `turn`: the turn's last three calls, turn meter's tok/s beside elapsed time); running subagents and background jobs; "Child sessions" ("하위 세션", section `opened`; the one row with a second line, by the user's call of 21 Oct 2026: under the title, muted and truncated, what the child does (`childActivity` in `opened-children.ts`: a running one's step from main's live push, a waiting one's question, a finished one's report; no line when it said nothing), since in the board's width "title · step" left no room for the step); todos (every finished one folds into the one "n done" row wherever it sits, an agent marks steps done out of order; a finished list's too; the unfinished ones listed in order; `todoBoard`, harness `?todos=mixed`); the goal (the larger share of budget or time used, `goalMeta`); **Current work** ("현재 작업", section `current`; `WorkBoardSession.tsx`, rules `session-board.ts`; the user's naming, 20 Oct 2026: rows read without a turn or session qualifier, and the last turn's and the old "This session" sections are folded into it): what the turn's own edit tools changed (write, edit, ast_edit, apply_patch: the work card's `workOutcomes` file outcomes over the running turn's calls, the last finished turn's while idle, with its duration as the section's aside; no line at all when nothing changed), never git, so another session's work in a shared checkout never counts (the user's report: a git-based count since the session started read 1000 files, +149005 −42996 of everyone's commits; that code is gone); **plus the family's** (the user's report, 21 Oct 2026: an orchestrator whose four children edited many files read "바뀐 파일 없음"): main's `agent_control_family_edits` (answered in `dispatchRpcCommand` beside `agent_control_review`, `AgentControl.familyEdits`; contract `src/shared/turn-edits.ts`) lists every session the board's session opened (an Autopilot run's cycle children included, in any window or on any paired device, so a phone viewing the opener counts them too), each with what its worktree branch has not landed (`get_git_unlanded`) and its newest turn's file-changing calls (`get_turn_edits`, local handler in `local/sessions.ts`: read back from its own transcript to the prompt that started the turn, a steer joining it, however long the turn (a count cut short would read as exact), nothing else of the turn leaves; a session not started, from its saved file), and with the board's turn start as `since` each one main closed after its work landed since then (`agentClosedChildren`, which keeps the paired device a child ran on; its saved file read once, there). The renderer counts a member that runs or waits, or whose turn ended after the board's turn began: a worktree member's unlanded files, else its turn's edits through the same `workOutcomes`. A file counts once per computer and absolute path (`familyChanges`), its +/− lines summed over every session's edits of it; the row's tooltip names each session's share ("This session: …", "<child>: N files changed +a −r"). The family is read on mount and when its review rows (step, state) or the turn's start move, at most every 5 s. A session that only shares the checkout, or one hidden from agents, never counts; an older host that refuses `get_turn_edits` says nothing of its child; on a session's own branch or worktree, what it has not landed yet (`get_git_unlanded`, `src/main/local/git/unlanded.ts`: the branch's commits and its uncommitted and untracked work against the merge-base with `origin/HEAD`, else `origin/main`/`origin/master`, else a local `main`/`master`; "main에 아직 없음 · 파일 N개 +a −r"; never on the default branch itself, where sessions share the working tree, nor on a detached HEAD), read on mount and once per settled turn; the finished turn is kept as the tab's `useTodoStore.lastTurn`, so a board or strip mounted after it still counts it; a cut list (`truncated`, 1000 files) reads "1000+" and leaves its lines out; the newest test/build/lint/typecheck with the work card's own verdict (`backgroundJobVerdicts`; a backgrounded one without its verdict is running, never passed); each running dev server by `devServerUrl` (a tap opens Preview, nothing opens by itself); the sessions sharing the checkout (bridge frame `checkout_peers` + `get_checkout_peers`; never on a session alone; a row that expands in place into one line per peer, this worktree's and then the repository's other worktrees' (marked "other worktree"), each opening its session by `peerOpen`: a tab of this window is switched to, this computer's file goes to its owner (another window, a paired device's tab), a paired host's peer opens by the host's file; a session hidden from agents carries no file and does not open); the memory the turn's recall attached, a row that expands in place (`data-board-expands`: the strip stays open for it)). Section folds persist (pref `workBoardCollapsed`); rows leave through `usePresence`. The dev harness's `?board=busy&family=1` (`familyEdits` in `dev-harness/fixture-board.ts`) is that orchestrator, idle after its turn while its children work.
  - Docked, the pane carries `data-work-board`: transcript scroller and composer shift left by `--omp-board-reserve`, and `data-work-board-running` / `-todo` hide the dock strips (`[data-workspace-island]`, `[data-dock-card="todo"]`) that say the same. It reads the existing per-tab stores and `get_jobs`/`get_goal`, derives nothing, never docks beside the side panel, and has no setting: it docks whenever the pane has room; its own ✕ hides it for that window (`useUiStore.workBoard`, never stored) and the palette's "Work board" brings it back. The dev harness's `?board=busy` (`dev-harness/fixture-board.ts`) fills every section with long Korean content for the audit and captures.
  - Cost rules (`e2e/flow-performance.e2e.ts` `daily` flow): its only running animation is the head pulse (rows fade in/out once, then stay still; each animated row cost ~2 % of a GPU core while streaming); `useNowTick` stops while the window is hidden; `get_usage` and `get_jobs` run one poll per tab via `hooks/use-tab-poll.ts` (each joining surface reads once on mount): `use-usage-reports.ts` serves the board's account row, sidebar usage lines, Stats › Usage, Settings model tables and accounts and the usage-limit notice (every 5 minutes, stock's status-line cadence, plus a turn's end, focus and coming into view no sooner than a minute after the last read, `lib/usage-reports.ts` `watchUsage`: a read probes every account whose report expired, and Claude's `/usage` failure waits only a minute, so a 30 s poll kept three Anthropic accounts answering 429, 313 times on one Mac on 22 Oct 2026); `use-background-jobs.ts` serves the board, dock island and Background tasks view.
  - Tests: `WorkBoard.test.tsx`, `WorkBoardSession.test.tsx` (the family's Changed row: a same-checkout and a worktree child counted once per file with their lines, a child finished before the turn left out, a phone viewing the opener and a child closed after landing still counted, no line when nothing changed), `agent-control/service.test.ts` ("familyEdits": only the sessions it opened, never a peer in the checkout or a hidden one, a closed child's file read once), `local/sessions.test.ts` (`get_turn_edits`), `work-board.test.ts`, `use-usage-reports.test.tsx`, `now-tick.test.tsx`, e2e `work-board.e2e.ts` (fake omp's `fixture:board`).
  - **Money** (20 Oct 2026, the user's rule: a metered provider's real charge and a subscription's API-price estimate are never one sum). Bridge `session_spend` (`model-setup-facts.ts`, guard `modelSetupFacts`; RPC `get_session_spend`, free in `quota-commands.ts`, a paired host's tab answers on the host; contract `SessionSpend` in `model-setup-contracts.ts`) splits the live journal (`getEntries`, stock's own whole-journal tally rule) per provider in `session-spend-core.ts` `spendByProvider` (assistant replies by `message.provider`, `model_usage` rows by `provider`, a `task` result through its `results[].usage` and `resolvedModel`), with today's share since the host's local midnight, and classes every catalog provider by the model setup's billing rule (`providerBillings`: stock's auth data, unknown = metered). Measured on a real journal (20 Oct 2026): the judge's calls (Jev 1.14 via OpenRouter: purposes `auto-thinking`, `auto-mode`, `find`, `ompdesk-needs-user`, `ompdesk-title-check`, `ompdesk-memory-recall`) land in the session they served as `model_usage` (`journalJudgmentUsage`), each at OpenRouter's billed `usage.cost` (stock's decisions wire, `applyProviderReportedCost` for chat), and the per-provider sum equals stock's whole-journal tally to the cent. Not in any journal, so not shown: the stats server's standalone judge (`stats-host.ts` `openStandaloneJudge(cwd, "stats_frustration")`, Stats › Frustration's judge runs; stock's `judgment/standalone.ts` has no session ledger and passes no `onUsage`), unmeasured; `~/.omp/stats.db` is no source (its sync stopped on 15 Oct, before any OpenRouter row). The board's head shows, under the account rows, one row per metered provider with the session's charge and today's (`SessionSpendLine`: `ProviderMark` and stock's display name from `session_spend`'s `name`, `getProviderDefinition`, as Settings' provider rows; a lazy chunk loaded only once something metered was spent: the phone's first screen stays in budget; `data-board-spend-provider`, four decimals below $1); a subscription's estimate is only named apart in its tooltip. Elsewhere a cost is `CostAmount` over `CostKinds` (`lib/spend.ts`): the charge "$0.09" and the estimate "≈ $0.18" as separate figures (`data-cost-kind`), the words ("Charged … · Estimate at API prices … (subscription, not charged)") as tooltip. A session's kinds come from its own host: main's review push carries `AgentChildUsage.split` (`CostSplit`, `costSplitOf` over that tab's `get_session_spend`, also kept in `ClosedChild.split` at auto-close), so a child whose journal mixes a subscription model with the judge's metered calls splits right whatever it runs on now; without a split (an older host or record) `costKinds` classes the whole cost by the model's provider, and a subagent at work by its `resolvedModel`. `hooks/use-session-spend.ts` polls on the usage schedule, one read per tab and session (`useTabPoll`'s `scope` = the session id: a tab that switches session in place reads afresh and a reply for the old session is dropped; `use-session-spend.test.tsx`). Pinned by `bridge-session-spend.test.ts` (a real ledger row), `WorkBoard.test.tsx`, `WorkTreePanel.test.tsx`; captures with `OMP_GUI_WORK_TREE_SHOTS=<dir>` (dev harness `SESSION_SPEND`, a real journal's spend).
  - **Where it cannot dock** (`workBoardForm`: a phone, a tablet, a window or split pane too narrow, the side panel open; the user's rule, 20 Oct 2026: never nothing while the pref is on) the status strip under the header (`WorkBoardChip`; label priority: waiting, a failed check, plan, turn, running, goal) expands in place: a tap grows an inline panel (`data-work-strip-panel`, wrapper `.omp-work-strip`, absolutely placed under the strip) over the top of the transcript, which is neither pushed nor dimmed: no Sheet, no scrim or backdrop, so the transcript stays visible and takes taps below. The panel holds the board itself (waiting ask with its jump to the card first, a failed check, steps, running subagents, jobs, todos, goal, the session rows), capped at `50dvh`, scrolling inside (`.omp-work-strip-body`), inset like the strip for safe areas and scrollbar gutter. The panel opens upward (`data-opens="up"`, on the strip's button too, so its chevron points up while collapsed and down while open) when its pane has at least 160 px above the strip (`useStripRoom` in `WorkBoardChip.tsx`, `MIN_UP_PX`), else downward, and is never taller than that side's room in its pane, downward never past the composer's top (`--strip-room`, measured again when the pane or anything beside the strip on the way up to it resizes): a desktop window has no `data-shell`, so its strip stays in the dock just above the composer, where opening downward covered the composer and ran past the window's bottom at 1280×800 beside the sidebar, and a short pane or a dock filled by a question card cut a fixed 50dvh panel off at the pane's top. Beside the strip, on every shell, the dock's todo card and island step aside (`components.css`: the strip's line and panel hold the plan and the running work; a desktop window with the side panel open once showed the plan twice). Pinned by the 1280×800, 1280×480 and 1280×360 desktop work-strip tests of `e2e/responsive-smoke.e2e.ts` (at 360 a downward panel capped only by the pane once covered the composer). A phone's strip is drawn 28 px (the chrome budget) and reaches 10 px down, never up into the header buttons' reach (`.omp-work-chip::after`, pinned by "every tap reaches" in `e2e/responsive-smoke.e2e.ts`). Under touch every board row, section head, "+N more" row and head item is `--sc-row-touch` tall (the strip bounds every reach to its box).
    - Collapses on a second tap of the strip, a swipe up (≥ 32 px) on the strip or the 16 px grip at the panel's foot, the back gesture (`useBackLayer`), a pointerdown anywhere outside (document listener, so the tap still reaches what it hit), or a tap on a board row (`button.omp-work-board-row`; its action runs first). Section fold toggles and review's approve/discard keep it open. The strip stays and its chevron flips (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`). Nothing is remembered: every mount starts collapsed. Motion is height (`omp-strip-in`/`-out`, exit keeps the panel mounted 160 ms) and the chevron's transform only; reduced motion and `html[data-gpu="software"]` make both instant. Pinned by `WorkBoardChip.test.tsx` and the `mobile-shell` e2e (panel under the header, ≤ 50 % high, hit test below it lands on the transcript, second tap and outside tap collapse).
- **Children at work in the transcript** (`chat/work/ChildrenAtWork.tsx`, the user's call, 21 Oct 2026: the parent says what its children do without opening the board): the sessions this one opened that run or wait, one line each (title, step or question, clock; a tap focuses the child), in the live work card while a call of the parent's agent waits on them (`waitsOnChildren`: `ompdesk_session_result`, called directly or as stock's `xd://` device write), and between the parent's turns in the transcript's status row. Same rows as the board (`useOpenedChildren`); the live step ask (`hooks/use-live-review.ts`, `agent_control_review {live: true}` every 2 s while a running child shows and the window is in view) is one per opener tab however many surfaces show them. The rows (`ChildrenAtWorkList.tsx`) are a lazy chunk loaded once a child is at work, outside the phone's first-screen budget (they pushed it 440 B over on 21 Oct 2026). Harness `?board=busy&children=wait` (card) and `&family=1` (between turns). Pinned by `ChildrenAtWork.test.tsx` and `WorkBoardOpened.live.test.tsx`.
- **Work card head, findings and steps** (`chat/ExecutionGroup.tsx`, pure rules in `chat/work/work-checks.ts`, `work-findings.ts`, `work-steps.ts`; design record `docs/work-card-design.md`):
  - A finished card's fact line names what the run's shell commands checked instead of counting them. `checkKind` reads the command's own words (test runners, `tsc`/`check:types`, biome/eslint/ruff, `build`); the last run of each kind decides (`summarizeChecks`: "128 tests passed", "types OK", "build failed"); a non-zero `details.exitCode` fails even without stock's error flag; interrupted and unanswered runs are skipped and still count as commands. The test count is the runner's last printed summary (`testCounts`: vitest, jest, bun, pytest, cargo, go); other commands keep "N commands".
  - **A backgrounded check has no verdict of its own** (stock bash `async: true` or auto-background: its result is only the "Backgrounded as job bg_N" notice, `details.async.state: "running"`). `workOutcomes` takes the job's settled outcome instead, from `backgroundJobVerdicts` (`chat/completion-events.ts`, memoized per committed message list): stock's `async-result` delivery (`details.jobs[].status`, the job's text split by `asyncJobResults`) or, when the agent waited, its `wait` result (`details.jobs[]` snapshot: `status`, `exitCode`, `resultText`/`errorText`; stock does not deliver a waited result again). Until one arrives the command is `unknown`, never a passed check (it once read "tests passed" and Tests ✓ while the job still ran or had failed). Pinned by the "backgrounded checks" cases of `work-checks.test.ts`.
  - Under the head (LiveStepPeek's `findings` slot, finished cards only so streaming never reflows) one row of chips (class `omp-work-finding`; `omp-work-chip` is the composer's work chip), derived by `deriveFindings` from the ledger alone, no model call: **Error** (`data-work-chip="error"`, danger; the failing check's cause via `firstErrorLine` over `WorkCommandOutcome.text`, the last 8,000 characters kept only for checks and failed commands, ANSI and runner chrome skipped; else a failed non-check command's `firstFailure`; else the word "Error" for a failed call of any other tool), **Tests ✓ N** (`tests`, last test run passed), **Open Changes** (`changes`, `setPanelTab("diff")`), **Preview** (`preview`, a bash result `devServerUrl` (`lib/panel-auto-open.ts`), kept as `WorkCommandOutcome.previewUrl`; `openPreview`) and a copy icon (`CopyButton`, `workSummaryText`: head facts and time, cause, changed files with +/-, checks, preview address).
  - Error and Tests open the steps and show their call: `show(toolCallId)` sets a `StepReveal` read through `StepRevealContext`; `revealStep` (`work/reveal-step.ts`) finds the card by `data-tool-call-id` (`ToolCard`'s root; the ledger carries each main-agent command's key as `WorkCommandOutcome.toolCallId`, a subagent's none), opens it and scrolls to the middle, retrying for 2 s because a big card mounts in slices.
  - The opened finished card (`StepGroups.tsx`, `groupSteps`; a running card keeps `ProcessMessages`, one bubble per message, so the live list never rearranges; `transcriptDetail` full mode has no work card) lines consecutive calls of one stock intent (`ToolCallContent.intent`, then the arguments' `intent`/`i`; a generic "Running requested command" names nothing) under one closed row ("Checking the types · 3 steps"), folds consecutive collapsible reads into `ReadGroupCard`, and every call keeps its own `ToolCard`. A group (or read group, `forceOpen`) holding a failed call opens by itself and again when a finding reveals a call inside it; only thinking may sit between two calls of one intent.
  - Pinned by `work-checks.test.ts`, `work-findings.test.ts`, `work-steps.test.ts`, `work-replay.test.ts` (recorded `real-advisor-frames.json` and stock's bash envelope through ledger and rules), `ExecutionGroup.checks.test.tsx`, `ExecutionGroup.chips.test.tsx`, `StepGroups.test.tsx`, `e2e/event-replay.e2e.ts`. Captures: `OMP_GUI_WORK_CARD_SHOTS=<dir>` with `e2e/work-card-captures.e2e.ts` (pass and `OMP_GUI_TEST_WORK_TYPES_FAIL=1`, en/ko × light/dark, wide/narrow, card opened) and `e2e/work-card-phone.e2e.ts` (paired phone page at 390), both on the fake omp's `OMP_GUI_TEST_WORK_RICH=1` variant; `OMP_GUI_WORK_CARD_OUT`/`_LABEL` capture another build as the before.

### Split view and picking a session

- Files: `stores/tabs.ts`, rules in `shared/split-layout.ts`. Main owns tabs and the pool; the renderer owns what the window shows.
- A window shows one session alone or one split of 2–4 panes (`split`), and remembers the other splits it has shown (`rememberedSplits`: layouts only, no focus). A session is in at most one split, on screen or remembered.
- Three rules, all in `viewAfterPick`; every way of picking a session goes through `switchTab`: ① a pane of the split on screen only takes focus, the layout stays; ② a session outside the split leaves it and shows alone, full width, and the split on screen is remembered; it never replaces or opens into a pane; ③ a member of a remembered split brings that split back exactly as it was (sessions, positions, sizes) with the picked session focused, and the split that was on screen is remembered in turn.
- Picks: sidebar rows, ⌘1–⌘9, ⇧⌘[ ⇧⌘] / ⌘PageUp/PageDown when no split is on screen (with one they cycle its panes), tab overview, ⌃⇥ switcher, notification/prompt-card focus. The session picker (⇧⌘F) and the sidebar's stored-session rows open a session no tab holds as a new tab: a pick of an outside session (`switchSessionNow` never re-roots a pane's tab while a split shows; `activeTabUntouched` is false then).
- **A stored session of another folder opens as its own tab** (stock's RPC `switch_session` refuses a session recorded in another folder than the tab's, SESSION_CWD_CHANGE_REJECTED answered `cancelled`, so the session picker, `/resume` and deep links did nothing for one): `switchSessionNow` and `requestSessionSwitch` (`opensInOwnTab` in `hooks/use-session-switch.ts`, the tab's cwd against the session's, compared as stock does) open it with `openTab` in its own folder and abort no running turn; only the tab's own folder switches in place. "Open in new window" (`SESSION_OPEN_NEW_WINDOW`) starts that window in the session's recorded folder (`sessionFolderState`, refused `session_folder_missing` when gone), its tab's omp on the session file (`spawnWindow` hands it to the pool as the tab's `--session`; the boot `switch_session` it used to wait for never ran in a window whose tab list had not named its tab yet, so the window showed a new empty session; `SESSION_CONSUME_PENDING` answers null on the desktop, the web shim keeps its own). The fake omp refuses a cross-folder `switch_session` as stock does. Pinned by `use-session-switch.test.ts` and `e2e/session-picker-folder.e2e.ts`.
- The only edits of the layout: dropping a session on a pane edge (`splitTab`, drag-to-split, row menu's Split right/bottom) and the menu's Exit split view. A dropped session leaves the split it was remembered in; at four panes it replaces the pane under the pointer and that one leaves; Exit split view forgets the split. Closing a tab (`closeTab`, which a deleted session's tab goes through) and removing a pane drop the session from its split; a split left with one session dissolves.
- Sidebar: every row whose tab is a pane of the split on screen has `data-active`; the focused pane's row adds `data-pane-focus` (a quiet colorless edge, only while a split shows).
- Persistence: the renderer sends `rememberedSplits` with every `tabs.setView` (`tabs.setActive` only while neither side remembers any). Main keeps them per window (`#rememberedByWindow`, pruned on release, never overlapping what is on screen) and saves them as `splits` next to `split` in the tab layout (`PersistedTabLayout`, leaves are tab indices); `GET_TABS` returns each remembered split once, on its first tab (`rememberedSplit`).
- Tests: `shared/split-layout.test.ts`, `tabs.test.tsx`, `Sidebar.test.tsx`, `tab-layout.test.ts`, `sidecar-pool.test.ts`; e2e `split-select.e2e.ts` (3 panes: all selected, outside pick solo, member restores, relaunch), `split-drop`, `desktop-tabs`, `workbench-shortcuts`.

### Preview: agent browsing and agent visuals

- **Agent browsing** (Preview toolbar robot button, local tabs only, off until the user clicks it): the tab's bridge (`resources/gui-bridge/browser-relay.ts`) starts stock's own `omp browser-relay` server (`tools/browser/relay/server.ts`) on a free 127.0.0.1 port with a random extension token and holds `browser.relay`/`browser.relayUrl` as session runtime overrides, so stock's browser tool runs in relay mode. Main joins as the relay's "Chrome extension" (`src/main/preview/agent-relay.ts`, stock's `relay/protocol.ts`) over the preview page's `webContents.debugger`, offering that one page, never the app window (which keeps no remote-debugging port).
  - Electron's per-page debugger is not confined to its page (Electron 36: `Target.getTargets` lists the app window and `Target.attachToTarget` onto it answers `Runtime.evaluate`; only `attachToBrowserTarget` is "Not allowed"). So the relay forwards an **allowlist** only: the page domains stock's puppeteer uses (`Page`, `Runtime`, `DOM`, `Input`, `Network`, `Emulation`, … never `Browser`, `SystemInfo`, `Tethering`, `Tracing`, `Memory`), and from `Target` just `setAutoAttach` and `detachFromTarget`. `Tracing` is browser-wide (records the app's other windows), so stock's `tab.traceStart()` fails by design.
  - Also refused: `Page.navigate` off http(s)/about:blank, `Page.close`/`crash`, downloads, `DOM.setFileInputFiles`, `Page.setInterceptFileChooserDialog`, `Input.setInterceptDrags`, an `Input.dispatchDragEvent` whose `data` carries `files` or a `file:` URL (Electron 36's debugger turns `files` into a real dropped File from the local path), a `Network.loadNetworkResource` whose `url` is not http(s) (Electron 36 already says "Unsupported URL scheme" for `file:`; the rule keeps local files out whatever a later Electron does), and `Security.setIgnoreCertificateErrors`/`setOverrideCertificateErrors`/`handleCertificateError`.
  - `Page.navigateToHistoryEntry` is allowed only after the relay reads `Page.getNavigationHistory` and the target entry's URL passes the same http(s)/about:blank rule, so `tab.back()`/`tab.forward()` work. The relay addresses only sessions this page's own debugger announced with `Target.attachedToTarget` (its frames and workers, never a `page`/`tab`/`browser` target); the page keeps its partition, sandbox and no-preload rules.
  - A banner shows the agent's last CDP method; Take over (or a tab switch, closing the panel, the session ending) detaches the debugger, closes the socket and stops the relay. `e2e/preview-agent.e2e.ts` drives it with stock's relay server and puppeteer-core from the installed omp: back/forward work; `Target.getTargets`/`attachToTarget`, `Tracing.start`, a file drag and a `file:` resource load are refused.
- **Agent visuals** (`src/main/preview/visuals.ts`, `visual-navigation.ts`, `page-scripts.ts`; contract `src/shared/preview.ts`): the `gui_visualize` host tool (registered with `gui_terminal_read`, local tabs only) shows a project `.html`/`.svg`/image/`.txt` file, inline HTML the agent wrote or an http(s) page as a Preview tab of the calling tab, and keeps a version per change (`<userData>/visuals/<id>/v<N>.<ext>`, 20 per visual, 100 visuals in `history.json`, Page options › All visuals…). `gui_visualize open` with an existing `visual` id moves that record to the new target.
  - Sessions: each window's preview has two in-memory sessions reused by every preview host of that window, storage cleared when a host is disposed: `web` (`preview-<win>`) for every page the user, a popup, the agent's browser or a web visual browses, and `visual` (`preview-visual-<win>`) for file and inline visuals alone. Those load from `omp-visual://v<N>-<id>/…`, registered on the visual session only: the entry from its snapshot, other files from the entry file's own folder (the project folder when the entry sits at its root) by known asset type, never hidden paths or anything outside it. A page stays in the session it was made in (`PreviewPageState.session`): a visual opens in a visual-session page (an empty web tab is replaced, a web tab is never reused), an address typed on a visual's tab opens a new web tab, and the agent's browser never takes a visual's page.
  - Egress: every response (404 included) carries a CSP (`VISUAL_CSP`: `default-src 'none'` with each allowed kind named, `connect-src 'self' data: blob:`, `form-action 'none'`, no frames or objects) so the page cannot send what it reads off its origin by `fetch`, `sendBeacon`, `<a ping>`, WebSocket, image, CSS, prefetch, form or frame. What CSP does not cover the host refuses (Security #3): whatever a visual's page starts itself (link, `location`, form, meta refresh, frame load, redirect) goes only to another version of the same visual or `about:blank`/`about:srcdoc` (`visualMayNavigate`); popups never open; downloads are dropped silently; WebRTC runs with `disable_non_proxied_udp`; the visual session's proxy is a dead `http://127.0.0.1:1` with `<-loopback>`, so TURN over TCP and any other socket go nowhere.
  - DNS prefetch, which no CSP or proxy stops, is off app-wide: `index.ts` adds `dnsPrefetchingEnabled=false` to `--blink-settings` (merged with any given at launch) before ready; Blink applies it after each page's preferences in every frame and `x-dns-prefetch-control: on` cannot undo it.
  - A web address refused right after a real click or key on the page (the C143 gesture window) shows in full in a notice whose "Open in new tab" loads it in a web tab, only when the user picks it.
  - `e2e/preview-visual-egress.e2e.ts` pins all of it against a 127.0.0.1 sentinel (TCP and UDP) that must hear nothing and the visual session's net-log, which must hold no host resolution the page asked for.
  - The page keeps the Preview's sandbox (no preload, permission-less sessions). Comment on an element / region, pins, parent element and CSS variable tweaks run in an isolated world (`PAGE_WORLD`) on trusted events and go to the composer as one message; `choose` returns a `data-choice` click only when trusted: a real mouse/key release on that page within 1 s and neither the agent's relay nor any debugger holds the page.

### SSH tools on this computer

- `src/main/ssh/`, contract `src/shared/ssh-tools.ts`, Settings › SSH: port forwards for a saved host (`ssh -N -L 127.0.0.1:<local>:127.0.0.1:<remote>`, saved in `<userData>/ssh-forwards.json`, started again at launch), the server's listening ports every 5 s (`SSH_PORT_POLL_SECONDS`), ssh-agent/key state, and a reconnect with growing delay until the chosen grace time (1 min–7 days, default 1 h).
- Every such ssh runs under the setup supervisor with `-F <userData>/ssh/config`, whose first `Host *` block pins OMPdesk's own `known_hosts` with `StrictHostKeyChecking ask` and no connection sharing, then includes `~/.ssh/config`. OpenSSH hands `-F` to every ProxyJump hop, so each hop's key is checked too.
- Host-key, password, passphrase and code questions come through `SSH_ASKPASS` (`askpass.ts`: a Node helper run by the app binary, a per-process token over a local socket) into `SshPromptDialog`; answers are never stored and expire after 2 min. A changed host key stops the forward until the user forgets the saved key.
- The agent's own ssh tool stays stock's. SSH server windows (a headless OMPdesk on the server) are out of scope: OMPdesk ships for macOS and Windows only, and remote devices cover running work on another of the user's computers.

### MCP servers: why one is off, sign-in, every live session

- The user's report (21 Oct 2026): MCP servers an agent installed (Higgsfield, Mobbin: OAuth over http) were unusable after the sign-in in the session that asked (`No such tool: xd://mcp__…`), the agent drove a terminal `omp` to sign in, and Settings listed several servers as off with no reason. Bridge `extensions/mcp.ts` (rules `mcp-core.ts`, contract `mcp-contracts.ts`), main `mcp-live.ts`, card `panels/mcp/McpServerCard.tsx`.
- **Why off** (`RpcMcpServerInfo.offReason`, `offReasonOf`): stock's dashboard order (`resolveState` in pi-coding-agent `modes/components/extensions/state-manager.ts`): turned off by the person (denylist, or `enabled: false` in omp's own mcp.json) → `disabled`; the provider turned off as a whole (`isProviderEnabled`) → `toolOff`; only another tool's user config declares it and omp has not opted that tool's `~/` in (`isUserSourceEnabled`, stock's `FOREIGN_USER_PROVIDERS`: Codex, Claude Code, Cursor, Gemini, Windsurf, OpenCode, GitHub) → `otherTool`; the other tool's own file turns it off → `offInTool`; a project server while `mcp.enableProjectConfig` is off → `projectConfigOff`. The row shows one muted line and its fix inline (`otherTool`: "omp에서 쓰기"/"Use in omp"; `disabled`/`offInTool`: "켜기"/"Turn on"). A server omp's own config declares that another tool's does too reads "{tool} 설정에서 가져옴" (`alsoIn`). Pinned against stock's dashboard by `bridge-mcp-stock.test.ts`.
- **Use in omp** (`mcp_action` `adopt`, `adoptServer`): copies that one server's definition (as stock discovered it, without the other tool's `enabled: false`) into omp's user `~/.omp/agent/mcp.json` with stock's `addMCPServer`, then connects it. Not stock's provider-wide opt-in (`enabledProviders`: it would also bring the tool's other servers, skills and rules) and not `enabledServers` (it overrides an `enabled: false`, never a foreign source that is not opted in). A value the other tool forwards from the environment (Codex `env_vars`, `bearer_token_env_var`) goes in as `${NAME}`, never the secret (`adoptedEntry`).
- **Needs a sign-in** (`authState: required`): also when the server answered the last connect with an OAuth challenge (stock `analyzeAuthError`), though its config says nothing about auth (an http server added by url alone). The failure is stock's own (`MCPManager.waitForStartup(1)`'s `failed`, the TUI status line's source, kept from the first connect on; `lastError`), never a listener the bridge would have to attach before a fast 401. The row shows "로그인이 필요해요" with a Sign in button (the existing `mcp_reauth` flow).
- **A server that does not connect never fails the change that saved it**: stock's reload (`MCPCommandController.reloadServers`) presents every server that failed to connect as one warning; the bridge keeps that text (`mcp_add` and `mcp_reload` return it as `warning`) instead of rejecting, so an add of a 401 server, or a sign-in while another server still refuses, answers, and its broadcast goes out.
- **Every live session follows** (`mcp-live.ts`): a successful `mcp_add`, `mcp_action` (adopt, enable, disable, remove, reconnect), `mcp_reauth` and `mcp_reload` (local handlers in `local/extensions.ts`) call `noteMcpChange`; index.ts sends it as an extension apply plan (`ExtensionApplyPlan.mcp`) to every local tab and every tab this computer hosts: a ready tab at once, a tab in a turn when its turn ends. The tab's bridge runs `mcp_sync`: it clears stock's fs cache, reloads the credential store (another omp wrote the row), and per named server disconnects one no longer configured, connects one configured but not connected, reconnects one whose config changed, and leaves one connected with the same config alone (so the tab that made the change does nothing twice; stock's `/mcp reload` would drop every server). A paired host's tab runs its handlers on the host, so the host's sessions follow.
- **An MCP sign-in is no model account**: stock keeps a managed MCP OAuth token in the same credential store as model accounts, its provider column the credential id (`mcp_oauth:profile:<profile>:<server url>`, agent.db `auth_credentials`). Every place that lists stored providers leaves those rows out with stock's own `isManagedMCPOAuthCredentialId` (`mcp/oauth-flow`), never a string match: the Settings provider list (bridge `buildProviders`), the stock helper's `accounts.providers` (the tool CLIs of connected accounts) and `accounts.signedIn` (a first run holding only an MCP sign-in is not signed in). Per-provider reads (account rotation, model setup facts, usage) take a model provider's id and never see them. Pinned by `bridge-mcp-credentials-stock.test.ts` and `stock-helper-accounts.test.ts` (installed omp, a seeded MCP credential row).
- **Composer `/mcp`**: stock's headless `/mcp reauth` (omp ≥ 18.12.6) opens the browser on the computer omp runs on (a paired host's tab: the host's screen) and in RPC mode has no live manager to reconnect, telling the person to restart the session; its `reload` only refreshes commands. So a typed `/mcp <verb>` whose registry item is an action or window runs in the GUI (`composer-submit.ts`): `/mcp reauth <name>` signs in from the composer, `/mcp reload` is `mcp_reload` (this session, then every live one); `/mcp list` still goes to stock (`typedAsPrompt`).
- **The sign-in is stock's** (`mcp_reauth`, bridge `runReauth`): stock `mcp/oauth-reauth` `findMcpServerForAuth` + `reauthorizeMcpServer` (endpoints, client credentials, the browser flow, the credential row, the config write-back), with the GUI's presentation only: the browser URL as an `extension_ui_request` `open_url` frame (so a paired device opens it on its own screen), progress as `notify`, the code paste-back as the session's `input` dialog (dismissing it cancels, `MCPOAuthCancelledError` → `cancelled`), then the live session's servers reload. An earlier OMPdesk copy of that flow left a config's `Authorization` header in place, so the next omp kept sending the stale bearer; stock clears it. Pinned by "signs a server in over a stale bearer header" and "ends a sign-in the person dismisses or the GUI cancels" (`mcp_reauth_cancel` closes the open paste-back dialog) in `bridge-mcp-stock.test.ts`.
- **Agents** (`app-catalog.ts`): `extensions.mcp_servers` (read: state, tools, sign-in, why off; never a url or command), `extensions.mcp_sign_in` (sensitive, always: starts the OAuth sign-in in the caller's session, answers once it is under way), `extensions.mcp_use` (sensitive, always: the adopt), and `extensions.install_mcp` says whether the new server needs a sign-in and that open sessions pick it up. The skill (`references/app-control.md`) tells an agent never to start a terminal omp for MCP work.
- Pinned by `bridge-mcp-stock.test.ts` (installed omp: a 401 server lists as needing a sign-in; another omp stores its credential and `mcp_sync` connects it and its tool joins the live session; the real `mcp_add` of a server answering 401 at once, then the real `mcp_reauth` against a fake OAuth server (metadata, client registration, authorize, token; the `open_url` followed as a browser would) while another server still refuses; a temp HOME's Codex config with two servers: adopting one makes only it active, the other stays `otherTool`, no Codex skill appears), `bridge-mcp-core.test.ts`, `local/mcp-live-changes.test.ts`, `extension-reload.test.ts`, `app-control.test.ts`, `McpServerCard.test.tsx`, `composer-submit.test.ts`; captures `OMP_GUI_MCP_SETTINGS_SHOTS=<dir>` with `e2e/mcp-settings-captures.e2e.ts` (dev harness fixture `fixture-mcp.ts`; `OMP_GUI_MCP_SETTINGS_PREFIX` names the set).

### Built-in MCP (REA)

- `resources/omp-builtin/rea/.mcp.json` starts the dependency-free Node `rea-relay.mjs` via stock's `${OMP_PLUGIN_ROOT}` substitution, outside asar, as a directory `-e` on every eligible spawn (REA root = `resources/omp-builtin/rea/`; `resources/omp-builtin/` itself is OMPdesk's own root, see OMPdesk control tools). The relay runs pinned REA (`rea/rea-config.json` is the sole version pin), exposes 15 unchanged entry-tool schemas (`curatedTools`) plus `rea_catalog` / `rea_call`, and keeps the full catalog on demand instead of adding 207 xd:// mount-notice lines to each session.
- The same root carries REA's `reverse-engineer-anything` skill in `rea/skills/` (stock loads an extension root's `skills/`), so it comes and goes with the server. It is vendored from the pinned release plus an OMPdesk section (mounted vs `rea_call` tools, no `rea setup`) by `bun run sync:rea-skill`, which every version bump runs (`packaging-config.test.ts` fails on a stale copy). The pin moves by itself: see Built-in skill packs stay current (`skills-bump.yml`; a new release must still list every `curatedTools` entry in its catalog).
- Node/npx must satisfy REA's engines. Pref `builtinMcp.rea` (default on) governs tabs this machine spawns or hosts. stdin EOF kills the child process tree. Never write the user's MCP config.

### Built-in pstack engineering mode

- Settings › Agent behavior › Smart automation, pref `pstack` (`PSTACK_PREF`), default **on**; contract `src/shared/pstack.ts`, row `settings/PstackRow.tsx`. `resources/omp-builtin/pstack/` is a translated copy of [pstack](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/main/pstack) (MIT, Lauren Tan; pin `pstack-config.json`: upstream commit and pstack version, moved by `skills-bump.yml`) loaded as a directory `-e` beside the REA root only while the switch is on (`sidecar-factory.ts` `builtinMcp`, from the next spawn).
- Content: `skills/` (principles, playbooks under `poteto-mode/playbooks/`, workflow skills; every one `disable-model-invocation`, so hidden from the model's skill list and read as `skill://<name>`), `agents/` (`pstack-agent`, the wrapper every playbook delegate uses, and `comment-sicko`), the hand-written `adapter.md` (Cursor/Claude names → omp `task`/`ask`/`todo`, `ompdesk_session_open`/`compare`/`merge`, model roles `@task`/`@slow`/`@smol`, `~/.omp/agent/sessions` transcripts, the routing gate) and `pstack-manifest.json`.
- **Never add a `plugin.json` there**: omp would class the folder an Agent Plugins root and drop 54 of the skills for their frontmatter. `tdd`/`teach` are `pstack-tdd`/`pstack-teach`, so no stock or user skill is shadowed. Left out as Cursor-only: `setup-pstack`, `poteto-help`, `make-bot-ui`, Bugbot triage, the `orchestrate` playbook, the `watch-pr`/`orch` tools.
- Everything except `pstack-config.json`, `adapter.md`, `agents/pstack-agent.md` and `README.md` is generated by `bun run sync:pstack` (`scripts/sync-pstack.ts`: clone at the pin or `PSTACK_SRC=<clone>`, copy, a rule table in which every rule must match at least once, so a reworded upstream line fails the sync; `-- --check` compares the pin with the newest upstream past the 3-day hold) and pinned by `src/main/pstack/pack-drift.test.ts` (sha256 manifest, no `plugin.json`, names and links, no leftover Cursor term, section size cap, tool names). The pin moves by itself: see Built-in skill packs stay current (`skills-bump.yml`).
- The always-on part is one `registerPromptSection` section (`resources/gui-bridge/modes/pstack.ts` + `pstack-core.ts`; texts generated into `pstack-text.ts` from `adapter.md` and the translated `poteto-mode` skill: about 20 KB / 7.3k tokens): static text for the prompt cache, main agent only, opening with the precedence repo rules > user settings > a skill the user invoked > the pack, then a routing gate (a trivial request gets a plain answer; anything else reads the matching playbook first and copies its steps into `todo`).
- Off for subagents (they get `pstack-agent`), Plan mode, a chat session without a project (`OMP_GUI_PSTACK=0` at spawn) and the switch off. An Autopilot run gets the 2 KB `PSTACK_AUTOPILOT_SECTION` instead (its protocol and HARD LIMITS win; bug-fix, hillclimb, autonomous-run, session-pickup, pause-safely and prove-it-works, no shipping or PR playbooks). Solo delegation swaps the delegation paragraph.
- **Copied steps follow the todo language.** Every instruction in the pack to copy a playbook's steps into `todo` (the routing gate and adapter in `adapter.md`; `poteto-mode/SKILL.md` and each playbook's OMPdesk note through `sync-pstack.ts`'s rules `mode-todolist`, `mode-todolist-copy` and `insertPlaybookNote`) says: one item per step in order, written in the todo language the `<reply-language>` section sets, word for word only without one, skill, tool and file names kept. "Copied in verbatim" beat that section (real omp 18.12.3 on `smol`, Korean reply language, bridge and pack on `omp -p`, a bug prompt typed in Korean and an English agent brief, three runs each: 0 of 36 todo items in Korean before, 40 of 40 after, still one item per step in order). Pinned by `bridge-pstack.test.ts`, `pack-drift.test.ts` and the session-kind case of `bridge-reply-language.test.ts`; `e2e/real-pstack.e2e.ts` runs with reply language off, so its match of the playbook's English words still holds.
- **No ritual round trips, delegation only when faster** (20 Oct 2026, the user's rule: wall-clock speed matters, no feature switched off). The routing gate still reads the playbook first, but its `todo` init travels with the first step's own call, the list is kept current next to real work (never a response holding only `todo`), every finished step is marked done in the response with the last check (so stock's todo reminder has nothing to wake), and a failure the request already names is not reproduced again by a whole suite. Delegation sizes each item first: a few small edits are parallel tool calls in one response, subagents are for items of minutes each (`adapter.md` Delegation; `sync-pstack.ts` rules `feature-delegate-speed`, `swarm-size`, `mode-fanout-size`). Measured on six tasks (nanoid copies, 26 runs per build and model, real omp): GPT-6.1 todo-only responses 44 → 12 and their time 246 s → 77 s; Sonnet 5.5 todo-reminder turns 9 → 4; pass rate unchanged. **Known gap**: GPT-6.1 still fans three small edits out to three subagents when the request suggests subagents (11 of 11 runs, median 103 s against 50 s for Codex solo); Sonnet does it in 1 of 11.
  - **The playbook read goes out with the first look** (20 Oct 2026): the routing gate's first response reads the matching playbook and, as parallel calls beside it, lists the folder, reads the files the request names and runs the failing command it names; the `todo` list opens in the next response with the first step's own call. Measured on the clean harness (4 tasks × 3 runs, base vs this, all passing, answer time from prompt to final answer): GPT-6.1 381 → 341 s, Sonnet 5.5 103 → 93 s; requests that only read a skill or playbook per GPT run 1.2 → 0.2. A broader "fill each round trip" section (batch every read and edit, no lone skill read, one sentence between calls) helped GPT alone (339 s) but slowed Sonnet (t5 5 → 9 requests, 124 → 142 s with both), so it was dropped.
- Tests: `bridge-pstack.test.ts` (gating table and `install` on a fake `pi`), `e2e/pstack.e2e.ts` (fake omp whose `get_skills` and context inspector read the `-e` roots, real gating under `OMP_GUI_TEST_PSTACK=1`), opt-in `e2e/real-pstack.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`, `smol` role: a bug-fix prompt must read `bug-fix.md` and copy its steps into `todo`, a greeting stays plain, the switch-off control never reads the playbook; a small model sometimes skips the playbook on a one-line bug, so it asks one follow in three runs).

### Built-in ECC language and framework skills

- Settings › Agent behavior › Smart automation (right after pstack), pref `ecc` (`ECC_PREF`), default **on**; contract `src/shared/ecc.ts`, row `settings/EccRow.tsx`. `resources/omp-builtin/ecc/` is a copy of [ECC](https://github.com/affaan-m/ECC)'s omp/pi profile `pi/core` (MIT, Affaan Mustafa; pin `ecc-config.json`: upstream commit and ecc-pi-core version, moved by `skills-bump.yml`), loaded as a directory `-e` after the pstack root while the switch is on, every session kind (`sidecar-factory.ts`, from the next spawn). The root holds only `skills/` and `commands/`, which stock omp reads from an extension root; the one piece of bridge code is the project hint below (env `OMP_GUI_ECC`, `1` for a project session while the switch is on). Upstream's own exclusions (hooks, agent rosters, network/SaaS, Claude-Code-only mechanics) are its `UPSTREAM-CURATION.md`; ECC's hooks, rules, agents and full catalog are never shipped.
- **Cost**: every skill is `disable-model-invocation` (hidden, read as `skill://<name>`); the one listed entry is the generated index skill `ecc` (`skills/ecc/SKILL.md`, the skills by language and framework). Measured on omp 18.12.3 with stock's estimator in an isolated profile: the pack adds 71 prompt tokens (56 beside pstack); the same 132 skills listed unhidden add 3,912. `src/main/ecc/ecc-stock.test.ts` holds it under 150.
- **Project hint** (`resources/gui-bridge/modes/ecc-hint-core.ts`, wired by `modes/ecc-hint.ts`, capability `eccHint`; measured 20 Oct 2026 on real omp 18.12.3 with pstack on, 9 organic tasks in Rust, Python, Django, Swift, .NET, React, Java, a Flask security review and a Dockerfile, each on `smol` (Sonnet 5.5) and `default` (Opus 5.5)): with the pack on and only the `ecc` index listed, **0 of 18** sessions read the index or any ECC skill; a one-line pointer in the pstack routing gate made it 5 of 18 (and would misfire with ECC off), so it was not kept. Instead a session whose folder shows a language or framework the pack covers gets one `<ecc-skills>` section naming the skills that fit (`ECC_RULES`: top-level names such as `Cargo.toml`, `manage.py`, `*.slnx`, `Package.swift`, `src/main/java`, plus `package.json`/`pom.xml`/Gradle/`Gemfile`/`composer.json` dependencies; a more specific match stands in for a general one, Django for Python, Next.js for React). Read once per folder, the same bytes every turn (the prompt cache holds), every agent kind (subagents write the code), ≈ 65–90 tokens. Result: **10 of 18** sessions read the fitting skill before their first write (10 of 12 where the folder names the language; Python with only a CSV and the Dockerfile task have no marker, React was skipped both times); checks passed 18/18 either way, idiom checks 71/84 against 70/84, spend $7.22 against $7.43, and a blinded cross-family judge (GPT 6.1) scored the 10 pairs 186 against 184 (1 win each, 8 ties). Pinned by `src/main/bridge-ecc-hint.test.ts` (the table on fixed folders, every named skill shipped, `install` on real temp folders).
- **Curation, recorded in the pin's `curation`**: pstack's playbooks lead the workflow, ECC supplies language, framework, tool and domain specifics. 14 skills that restate a pstack workflow are left out (`tdd-workflow`, `verification-loop`, `change-impact`, `codebase-onboarding`, `benchmark-optimization-loop`, `blueprint`, `intent-driven-development`, `agentic-engineering`, `ai-first-engineering`, `agent-self-evaluation`, `parallel-execution-optimizer`, `ecc-council`, `dev-team`, `conventional-commit-review`), each with the pstack skill that `leads`; references to them in the 118 kept skills and the commands point there (`skill://pstack-tdd`, …). The 24 prompt commands stay (they run only when typed, and an invoked command outranks the pstack section); `plan` ships as `ecc-plan` because stock's `/plan` (TUI) and OMPdesk's Plan command take the name. No other skill or command name clashes with stock, pstack, REA, OMPdesk or this Mac's user skills. A few Claude Code names become omp's (`ask`, the `task` tool) and ECC's working files go under `.ecc/` instead of `.claude/`.
- Everything except `ecc-config.json` and `README.md` is generated by `bun run sync:ecc` (`scripts/sync-ecc.ts`: clone at the pin or `ECC_SRC=<clone>`, copy, a rule table in which every rule must match, an index group table every kept skill must sit in once; `-- --check` compares the pin with the newest upstream past the 3-day hold). **No `plugin.json` or `package.json` there** (omp reads the folders itself). Pinned by `src/main/ecc/pack-drift.test.ts` (sha256 manifest, hidden skills, index coverage, drops and their `leads`, names), `src/main/ecc/ecc-stock.test.ts` (installed omp through `stock-probe.ts`: cost, every skill loads and reads through `skill://`, every command offered as itself and none named like a stock command), the ECC case of `command-registry-coverage.test.ts` (no OMPdesk command name) and `packaging-config.test.ts`. Attribution: `LICENSES/MIT-ecc.txt` (written by the sync) and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` (`gen:licenses`). The pin moves by itself: see Built-in skill packs stay current (`skills-bump.yml`).

### Built-in skill packs stay current

- pstack, ECC and REA bump themselves (the user's rule, 20 Oct 2026). `scripts/skill-packs.ts` is the one table and rule: `check [<name>]` compares each pin with the newest upstream release that has been public for bunfig.toml's `minimumReleaseAge` (3 days, read from that file: one hold for dependencies and skill packs); `bump <name>` moves the pin's `commit`/`version` lines to it, runs the pack's sync, regenerates `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` (`gen:licenses`), checks REA's new catalog still lists every `curatedTools` entry (its real MCP server's `tools/list`), runs the pack's drift, stock and packaging tests and `licenses:check`. `sync:pstack -- --check` and `sync:ecc -- --check` call its `check`.
- **The hold**: a git pack takes the newest commit touching its folder (`pstack`, `pi/core`) whose committer date is at least 3 days old and descends from the pin; REA takes the highest stable `rea-agents` whose npm publish time is. Known gap: a commit pushed rather than merged on GitHub carries its author's own date, so a compromised upstream could backdate one; npm publish times are the registry's.
- **`.github/workflows/skills-bump.yml`** runs daily (05:41 UTC) and on dispatch (`gh workflow run skills-bump.yml`). `bump` (macOS, read-only token, no stored credentials, installs omp for ECC's stock test) tries each pack behind on top of the last one that passed and keeps each passing one as a commit `chore(skills): bump <name> to <version>`; `push` (ubuntu, `contents: write` and `actions: write`, runs no third-party code) applies them with `git am` onto main, pushes (fetch and rebase on a rejection, never force) and dispatches ci.yml on main (`-f win_platform=true`), because a push made with `GITHUB_TOKEN` starts no workflow. A pack whose sync rule stopped matching or whose test failed commits nothing: `report` (`issues: write`, waits for `bump` and `push`) opens or updates one issue "Skill pack bump failed" with the log's tail, also when a bump passed but `push` did not end `success` or `skipped` (git am, rebase or push failed: nothing reached main; or the ci.yml dispatch failed after the push: `push.outputs.pushed` tells them apart); only a run where every pack is current or bumped and pushed closes it. Fix the rule in the pack's sync script (adapt it, never silence it). The ordinary release cadence ships a bump; these commits never touch `.github/workflows`.

## Models, accounts and held stock defaults

### Desktop slot
- One session at a time drives the desktop. Files: contract `resources/gui-bridge/desktop-slot-contracts.ts`; bridge `desktop-slot.ts` + `desktop-slot-core.ts`; main `src/main/desktop-slot.ts` + `desktop-slot-host.ts` + `local/desktop-slot.ts`; renderer `stores/desktop-slot.ts`, `DesktopSlotIndicator.tsx`, the waiting line in `ToolCard.tsx`; capability `desktopSlot`.
- WHY: each omp has its own `ComputerSupervisor`; stock's native layer only refuses input while another operation owns focus ("no input was sent"). It neither queues nor spans a session's observe → act → verify, and nothing crosses processes. So **main** owns one slot per computer and the bridge patches `ComputerSupervisor.prototype.run` once (feature-detected; without `run`/`revokeControl` the slot is `unavailable` in capabilities/health and stock behaviour stays).
- A `snapshot.readOnly` run (screenshots, `capabilities`) never waits. The first acting run takes the slot (`desktop_slot_acquire` → `desktop_slot_bridge`) and keeps it until `agent_end`, `DESKTOP_SLOT_IDLE_MS` (20 s) without a computer run, Stop, abort, tab close/crash, or lease expiry (`DESKTOP_SLOT_LEASE_MS` 120 s without `desktop_slot_touch`).
- Waiters queue FIFO. An acting run that had to wait does **not** run once granted: it returns `DESKTOP_SLOT_STALE_NOTICE` so the agent re-screenshots and repeats.
- **Handoff**: abort the holder's in-flight input run, await stock `ComputerController.revokeControl()` on every controller the bridge saw, only then grant the next session.
- **Never handed on unconfirmed.** A `revokeControl()` that rejects (holder's acting runs get "input is blocked") or is silent for `DESKTOP_SLOT_REVOKE_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s) leaves the holder `revoke-failed` (`DesktopSlotState.holder.revokeFailed`): nobody is granted, main retries every `DESKTOP_SLOT_REVOKE_RETRY_MS` (5 s; a late answer counts), the indicator offers "다시 시도" (`desktop_slot_retry`) and "그 세션 중지" (`desktop_slot_kill`: abort + `restart()` of its omp, SIGTERM only).
- Freed only by a confirmed revoke, a confirmed exit of the holder's process (host re-reads `whenExited()` while the sidecar is ready; a failed grant is not a crash), or a kill whose process exited within `KILL_CONFIRM_MS` (5 s). A bridge `desktop_slot_release` follows only a confirmed revoke.
- UI: app-wide "화면 사용 중: <title> · 대기 N" with Stop (`desktop_slot_stop`: aborts the holder's turn, hands off); a waiting `eval` card says "화면 차례를 기다리는 중 (N번째)".
- Hosted tabs for a paired device join this slot; tabs on a remote host use that host's slot (main skips `sidecar.device` tabs). Autopilot never takes it (its `eval` is off). No "run marked done" release (stock has no marker).
- Tests: `desktop-slot.test.ts`, `desktop-slot-host.test.ts`, `bridge-desktop-slot.test.ts` (fail-first: rejecting revoke, silent revoke, holder death after failed revoke, kill), `e2e/desktop-slot.e2e.ts`.

### Account rotation
- Settings › Models & accounts pref `accountRotation` (`quota` default = stock's usage ranking | `even`; `src/shared/account-rotation.ts`, `providers/RotationModeControl.tsx`). Stock has no switch: each spawn passes `OMP_GUI_ACCOUNT_ROTATION` and the shared ledger `<userData>/account-rotation.json` (`OMP_GUI_ACCOUNT_ROTATION_STATE`); a change or bridge connect pushes `account_rotation_set {mode}` to every live local sidecar and every tab this machine hosts (`LocalMachine.setAccountRotation`, `src/main/account-rotation.ts`). A paired host's tabs follow the host's pref (`host.accountRotation.get|set`).
- **Even**: the bridge's `account-rotation.ts` (`before_agent_start`) gives an account-less session the unblocked account handed out least recently across all local omps, as an automatic warm pin (`authStorage.sessions.pin(…, {restoredAtMs})`). A session keeps it until blocked (idle pin renewed, not re-ranked); stock's 429 blocking/failover is untouched.
- **Quota re-ranks at turn start.** WHY: stock re-ranks an automatic pin only when cold (Claude: 60 min idle), blocked or spent, and `affinity.record` refreshes last-use on every request, so a session used without a 60-min gap stayed on its first account for days (subagents inherit the parent's pin via `sessions.inherit`). `rerankBeforePrompt` (`account-rotation-core.ts`) runs in the same hook for the main agent only (`ctx.agent.kind === "main"`; subagents keep the spawn copy), never mid-turn.
- It asks stock, not our own policy: the account stock's selector would give a pinless session now (`authStorage.keys.getWithCredential(provider, <stand-in id>, {…, recordAffinity: false})`). It moves the pin (automatic warm pin, `restoredAtMs: now`) only when ALL hold, else stock decides: >1 account; pin exists and is warm; pin is automatic (read via the `include(provider, explicit)` callback of `sessions.inherit` with a stand-in target returning `false`; no provenance = left alone); session unrestricted (`sessions.permits`) and no account has a stock policy (`oauth.policy`): pools/policies are the user's choice; pin not blocked; both accounts measured and stock's comparator (`orderUsageRankedCandidates` over `RankedOAuthCandidate` rows from both `usage.report`s, mirroring `#rankOAuthSelections`) still ranks the other first after the pin's required drain is multiplied by `RERANK_DRAIN_MARGIN` (2).
- The margin is OMPdesk's only addition (stock compares drain at 1e-6 and every switch rewrites the provider's per-account prompt cache). Only the plain drain order gets it; stock tiers (blocked, spent, 85% 5h demotion, unmeasured) move a pin at once; a round trip needs a 4x swing. Proper fix belongs in stock.
- Tests: `src/main/bridge-account-rotation-stock.test.ts` on the real omp (2.7x moves, 1.9x never, 2.1x does, no way back at 1.9x, hot 5h tier, explicit/cold/subagent, inheritance), rules in `bridge-account-rotation.test.ts`; internals in `capabilities.ts` (`accountRotation`) and `health.ts` (`auth/rank`).

### Provider sign-in
- Settings › Models & accounts and first run (`providers/ProviderLogin.tsx`) run stock `login` on the tab's omp. While a sign-in is in flight its `open_url`, `input` and info `notify` requests (`isSignInRequest` in `shared/rpc-types.ts`) belong to the panel (`loginRequestIds` in `stores/extension-ui.ts`) and **never render as chat cards**, even after the panel unmounts, the page reloads or a paired host's link returns.
- Main counts `login` commands in flight per tab (`SidecarPool.noteSignIn`); for a paired host's tab also the host's `signIn` tab event (sent as `login` starts/ends, on every `tab.open` incl. a new tab's `{active: []}`, and ahead of `openAsks` on re-attach), because the host's login runs on when this window's `login` call fails with the link. A host status (not the link) leaving `ready` drops the host's list. Main marks each such request (`IpcSignIn`: `active`, provider, browser page) on the live `EXTENSION_UI` frame, in `EXTENSION_UI_OPEN` and in a host's re-sent `openAsks`; all reach the page through `pushTabExtensionUiRequest`: a marked request is never a card.
- While `login` runs (`active`) the page adopts the sign-in (`adoptedLogin`, counted in flight) and the panel shows it when reopened ("Sign-in in progress…", browser link, paste fallback, Cancel) until main sends `SIGN_IN_ENDED` (`endTabSignIn`: answers the leftover; panel reloads providers or shows the failure; outcome is unseen if a host's login ended while the link was down). Once `login` answered, a request is a leftover and is answered `cancelled` at once.
- Browser sign-in finishes through stock's loopback callback; stock also races a code paste and never closes it, so the panel shows the paste as a collapsed fallback (open on a paired host's tab) and answers the leftover `cancelled` once `login` returns (an empty answer earlier only re-asks).
- Stock's RPC `login` has no cancel and holds omp's serial command queue (up to its 5-min callback timeout), so Cancel sends `login_cancel` (`src/main/local/settings.ts`), ending it via the callback's own denial (`error=access_denied` with the flow's `state`). A relaunched client has lost a host login's browser page (Cancel of its adopted panel then answers the paste).

### Featured providers
- One picker for both sign-in surfaces: first run's "which provider do you use" step and Settings › Models & accounts › Add a provider (`providers/ProviderPicker.tsx`; list `FEATURED_PROVIDERS` in `providers/provider-catalog.ts`; `STARTER_PROVIDERS` holds the featured ids too so cards show before omp answers).
- Popular ones lead as their own group ("Popular providers"/"주요 프로바이더"; cards `[data-featured-provider]`: `ProviderMark` + name + one line + action, `min-h-11` = 44 px, `auto-fill minmax(15rem)` so a phone gets one column); the rest stay under "Other providers"/"다른 프로바이더". A search is the flat list of every match, no group.
- Ids and login kinds are stock's (pi-catalog `auth/*.kdl`, `/login` roster), never invented: ChatGPT (Plus/Pro) = `openai-codex` OAuth; Claude (Pro/Max) = `anthropic` OAuth; OpenRouter = `openrouter` (browser sign-in or paste yields a durable API key); Google (Gemini) = `google-gemini-cli` (stock's plain `google` has no login); Grok (SuperGrok) = `xai-oauth` (device-code, primary) with `xai` API key as the card's quieter second action (`apiKeyId`; subscription first; the key action is a quiet row INSIDE the card under a hairline, never a loose row under the grid).
- Card names are fixed brand labels; a card whose logins stock's catalog doesn't list is skipped. First run (`showConnectedFeatured`) shows a connected featured provider as "Connected" in place; Settings lists connected ones above and leaves them out.
- First run (`onboarding/FirstRunSetup.tsx`, opened by `FirstRunGate`) is one step: brand mark, "Set up OMPdesk", one line, the picker, a one-line custom endpoint row (its explanation behind ⓘ) and Set up later. No step list and no GitHub step (GitHub sign-in lives in Settings › General and the PR center; an old saved `firstRunStep` of `github` is cleared). A browser sign-in the person starts on a local tab opens the browser by itself (stock sent a `launchUrl`; device-code and API-key pages wait for the click) and the login panel scrolls into view. Once a provider serves, the recommended model (`pickDefaultModel`, the connected provider first) is applied and saved as `modelRoles.default`, and setup ends with one toast naming it; the model step shows only when nothing can be picked or saving fails. The provider step ends setup only for a provider signed in on it or one that starts serving after it opened (`servingAtOpen`), so Back from the model step stays on the list. Cards are clickable while the omp still starts (`useProviderCatalog().loginReady`: status ready or starting): `useProviderLogin.start` waits for the tab itself (`tabReady`) and sends no `login` for an attempt cancelled meanwhile. The composer's "suggestions are off" line stays hidden while setup is open.
- The opt-in `e2e/first-run-walk.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_FIRST_RUN_WALK=<dir>`) walks it end to end: fake installer and fake omp in en/ko (install → Claude sign-in through the fake's callback → first answer), the real omp's provider step on an empty agent dir (no sign-in), and the phone page's provider step at 390 px; `walk-<lang>.json` / `real-<lang>.json` hold per-screen seconds, clicks, visible words and buttons.
- Tests: `ProviderPicker.test.tsx` (order, actions, connected, search), `e2e/first-run-no-omp.e2e.ts` (fake omp `OMP_GUI_TEST_FEATURED=1`, en/ko × light/dark), `e2e/real-core.e2e.ts` (stock's real catalog lists all five in order), `e2e/mobile-pages.e2e.ts` (`?firstrun=1`: one column, 44 px, no overflow).

### Held stock defaults
- Sessions OMPdesk spawns (local tabs and tabs a host runs) differ from stock in `GUI_THINKING_DEFAULTS` (`resources/gui-bridge/thinking-default-core.ts`, wired by `thinking-default.ts`) unless some layer (config.yml, project config, `--config` overlay, env) sets them.
- Each is a runtime override applied inside the extension factory (stock loads `-e` extensions after `Settings.init`, before createAgentSession builds tools/advisor and picks the first thinking level). **Never written to config.yml** (the omp CLI keeps stock); shown in Settings with `runtime` provenance; released by a Settings write (user's value persists and wins); taken up again by a reset; inherited by subagents via stock's `createSubagentSettings` snapshot. OMPdesk's own model roles and the security reviewer's tier follow the same rule (see Model roles › OMPdesk roles).
- Tests: `src/main/bridge-omp-defaults-stock.test.ts` and `bridge-goal-continuation-stock.test.ts` (real omp); rules in `bridge-thinking-default.test.ts`.
- The list:
  - `defaultThinkingLevel` = `auto` (stock `high`), `providers.autoThinkingMaxEffort` = `max` (stock `xhigh`). The advisor role is written `:auto` when picked without a level (`resources/gui-bridge/role-values.ts`), because stock runs an unsuffixed advisor at fixed `medium`; pickers hide the suffix.
  - `goal.continuationModes` = `["interactive", "rpc"]` (stock continues a goal over RPC only with `"rpc"` listed); reached by id through `config/registry` `lookup` (omp's bundle has no `goals/settings`; see Bridge, Only the running omp's modules).
  - `display.showTokenUsage`, `lsp.diagnosticsOnEdit`, `lsp.formatOnWrite` on (the GUI usage row follows the first unless a GUI display pref overrides).
  - `astGrep.enabled` on (`astEdit.enabled` already is in stock). Both are the `@oh-my-pi/pi-natives` addon; `applyGuiThinkingDefault` feature-detects it (`NATIVE_DEFAULTS`) and an omp without it keeps stock's value with a Settings note (`SettingEntry.note`, `settings.note.native`).
  - `computer.enabled` on (stock off): the `computer` eval prelude (screenshots, input, accessibility of the host desktop; `/computer on|off|status` toggles per session; a Settings write releases the default). An omp without the setting is skipped (`lookup` finds no handle).
    - **Cost/risk**: the prelude adds prompt and tool text to every session and rides on `eval`; **Autopilot removes `eval` (`EXCLUDED_TOOLS` in `modes/autopilot.ts`), so computer use stays off there by design.**
    - Stock's `computerApproval`: `capabilities`/`read_only` need `read` approval, every input action `exec`. Stock's `tools.approvalMode` default is `yolo` and OMPdesk keeps it, so **by default a session can click and type on the user's desktop with no approval card**; only `always-ask` (or a rule) puts a card before input.
    - The prelude runs in the omp process, so on macOS Screen Recording and Accessibility go to the app that launched it (OMPdesk, or the terminal for a CLI omp). Without them stock fails the call ("macOS Screen Recording permission is not granted for this process" / "macOS Accessibility permission is required for native input") and `computer.capabilities()` reports `capturePermission`/`inputPermission`/`axPermission`. macOS never prompts from stock's preflight and OMPdesk adds no hint. Windows is stock's and untested by OMPdesk.
    - Pinned by `bridge-omp-defaults-stock.test.ts` (resolves `true` as `runtime`, `eval` enabled, prelude active = `/computer status` "enabled · prelude: active") and opt-in `e2e/real-computer-use.e2e.ts` (read-only turn shows no card; an input turn under `always-ask` shows a card and is denied; nothing is ever approved).
  - `advisor.enabled` on: a second model (`advisor` role; unset follows `slow`, then stock's slow chain) reviews every turn, **one extra model call per turn**. Stock soft-pins it off for RPC hosts (`protocolDefault: ["rpc", "acp"]`, `applyProtocolDefaults` in `main.ts`); `PROTOCOL_PINNED` lets the override replace such a pin only while nothing but the pin sets the value (provenance `runtime`, value equals schema default, no persisted layer). Other advisor tuning (`advisor.syncBacklog`, …) stays stock's.
    - **Its spend is shown** (measured 19 Oct 2026: 21.8 % of 15 runs' spend, 3 advisories delivered). RPC `get_advisor_stats` (local handler `local/advisor-stats.ts` → bridge `advisor_stats`, `advisor-stats.ts`, pure mapping `advisor-stats-core.ts`, contract `AdvisorUsage` in `advisor-stats-contracts.ts`, capability `advisorStats`) maps stock `getAdvisorStats()` (cost = the session ledger stock restores from `__advisor*.jsonl`; calls = the advisors' assistant messages since their context last started, so compaction re-primes them) and `isAdvisorUsingSubscription()`, and counts the notes of the branch's `custom_message` `advisor` entries by severity. Free in `quota-commands.ts`; a paired host's tab answers on the host. Shown as one row of the Run popover/sheet (`composer/AdvisorUsageRow.tsx`, `data-advisor-usage`: Lightbulb, "Advisor", `$cost · N advisories`, calls, severities, model and billing behind (i); hidden while off with nothing spent or on an omp without the method; `RunChip` loads it with `lazyChunk`, because the phone's first-screen budget has no room) and Insights › This session's Advisor section (`insights/AdvisorInsights.tsx`, `data-advisor-insights`: cost with its share of session + advisor spend, calls, advisories by severity). `formatUsd` lives in `lib/format.ts` so a price never pulls in Chart.js. Pinned by `bridge-advisor-stats.test.ts`, `bridge-advisor-stats-stock.test.ts` (installed omp: shape, delivered notes, call overhead), `AdvisorUsageRow.test.tsx`, `InsightsPage.test.tsx`; captures `OMP_GUI_ADVISOR_SHOTS=<dir>` with `e2e/advisor-usage-captures.e2e.ts` (harness fixture `ADVISOR_USAGE`).
  - `title.icons` = `boring` (stock `nf+emoji`): plain-text titles, no icon/code card, no extra title-model request (`renameTitle` in stock `agent-session.ts`). Only new titles change; a stored carded title stays until ✨ or evolving makes a fresh one.
  - `async.widgets` off (stock on, omp ≥ 18.12.3): stock writes a live widget spec for each background job older than 15 s, one `widget`-role model call (resolved like `smol`; plus a retry on a rejected answer and re-authors on drift) as soon as anything views the job. Stock `inspectAsyncJob` always views, and the GUI's one jobs poll (`composer.jobs`, every job, every poll) calls it; stock has no read that does not, and the GUI never draws these widgets. Measured 20 Oct 2026: this Mac's journals held 11 such calls in 45 minutes, $0.134 ($0.012 each, Claude Sonnet 5.5 as `smol`); a 24 s background job read every second got 3 requests on a loopback provider, none unread and none with the default held. Pinned by `bridge-job-widgets-stock.test.ts` (installed omp: held → 0 requests under the poll; config.yml `async.widgets: true` → stock's requests; unread → 0) and `bridge-omp-defaults-stock.test.ts`.
  - **OTLP telemetry export off** (the user's decision; not a setting). `telemetry.otlpExportEnabled` is read by `initTelemetryExport` in `main.ts` before extensions load, so no override reaches it. Instead every omp OMPdesk runs gets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` (`OMP_TELEMETRY_OFF` in `omp-runtime.ts`, spread last over the user's env: local/hosted tabs via `SidecarManager`, scheduled runs, benchmarks, model setup, `omp` CLI calls via `runtime.run`), on which stock's only exporter (`telemetry-export.ts`) returns at once whatever `OTEL_*` the user sets. Stats server and stock helper never call it; `omp --version`/`update` exit before it. Pinned by `shell-env.test.ts`.
  - **Not held** (stock 18.11.1): `display.showTurnTime`, `read.toolResultPreview` (TUI renderer only, `modes/utils/ui-helpers.ts`; the GUI hides `display.showTurnTime`); `retry.usageAwareFallback`, `retry.waitForUsageReset` (account rotation and interrupted-turn resume cover them).
  - **Automatic session titles on** (not a stock setting: stock's `autoTitle` is a session option `main.ts` sets for the TUI only, and `main.ts` sets `PI_NO_TITLE` for `--mode rpc-ui`, so GUI sessions stayed "New session"). Prefs `autoTitle` + sub-switch `autoTitleEvolve`, both default on (Settings › Agent behavior › Session titles). Not a runtime override: the bridge (`modes/auto-title.ts`, `modes/auto-title-engine.ts`) runs stock's title machinery from live session events, so `PI_NO_TITLE` can't reach it. The user's own `PI_NO_TITLE` is respected: main reads it from the env it hands omp (`titleSuppressedByEnv`) and passes `suppressed` in `OMP_GUI_AUTO_TITLE`/`auto_title_set`. Pinned by `bridge-auto-title*.test.ts`, `shell-env.test.ts`.

### Model roles
