- Settings › Models & accounts › Model roles (`ModelRolesSection.tsx`, `ModelRoleRow.tsx`; contracts in dependency-free `resources/gui-bridge/model-role-contracts.ts`). Stock owns the roles (ids, names, tags, sections, `hidden`, `accepts`, empty-role fallbacks, `@role` resolution in `config/model-roles.ts`, `config/model-resolver.ts`); the bridge reads every one at runtime (`settings.ts` `roleMetadata`: each `MODEL_ROLE_IDS` id with `getRoleInfo`, `accepts` = the `modelKind`s of the catalog models its predicate takes, `defaults` = `rolePriorityDefaults`), so a role a newer omp adds is listed with no OMPdesk change. OMPdesk adds only an ordering preference (`ROLE_GROUPS`, `ROLE_ORDER`): Conversation (default, slow, smol, plan), Agent work (task, advisor, commit, tiny, widget), Input & output (vision, image, web, speech, dictation, judge); a role not in it goes by stock's section (kind → Input & output, chat → Agent work) after these, in stock's order. Hidden roles carry their reason (`HIDDEN_ROLES`): `memory` served only stock's memory backends, replaced by ai-memory.
- **No user-made custom roles** (the user's decision, 19 Oct 2026): `get_model_roles`/`get_model_role_metadata` list stock's built-in roles (`MODEL_ROLE_IDS`, `origin: "stock"`) then OMPdesk's own (`origin: "ompdesk"`, below); no add/rename/remove and no agent-usage lines. A `modelRoles.<id>`/`modelTags.<id>` the user set elsewhere is never read, listed or rewritten; stock still resolves `@id` for an agent that names it. The agent templates (`src/shared/agent-templates.ts`) name built-in roles and OMPdesk's own (`["@review", "@slow", "@task"]` for code-reviewer, `["@slow", "@task"]`, `["@smol", "@task"]`, `["@task"]`; pinned by `agent-templates.test.ts`); a template agent whose model line an earlier release wrote (`@debug, @slow, @task`, or code-reviewer's `@slow, @task`) is offered the current line by Definitions' "Update to role-based model" (`LEGACY_TEMPLATE_MODELS`, `agent-template-migration.ts`).
- **OMPdesk roles** (the user's decision: roles beyond stock are runtime-only; contracts `resources/gui-bridge/ompdesk-role-contracts.ts`, `OMPDESK_ROLES`; rules `ompdesk-roles.ts`): `review` (cross-family code review; chain `@review → @advisor → @slow → @task`). Each is stock's own `modelRoles.<id>` held as a runtime override: merged per key (`Settings.overrideModelRoles`, so `--smol`/`--plan` runtime values stay), value = the pick then the chain (`heldRoleValue`), never written to config.yml (the omp CLI stays stock), never over a config.yml/project value for the id (`getGlobalModelRole`/`getProjectModelRole`) or a runtime value OMPdesk did not write. Stock then lists it with its custom roles and resolves `@review` for every agent and subagent (overlay settings read the parent live).
  - Picks: `set_model_role` on an OMPdesk role writes `<userData>/ompdesk-roles.json` (`OMP_GUI_OMPDESK_ROLES`, passed by `sidecar-factory.ts` to every local and hosted omp; a single model selector per id, `parseRolePicks`), always, so config.yml is never written; while the user's config sets the id that value stays in force and the pick waits. Every omp re-reads the file at bridge start, before each turn (`before_agent_start`) and around the Settings reads and writes (`settleOmpdeskRoles`), so a pick on any tab reaches every omp of this computer from its next turn; a paired host's tab uses the host's file. Row: `model` = the pick, `effective` = `@review` through stock (`effectiveOmpdeskRole`: `configured` when the pick answers, else `fallback` with `via` the chain role that did).
  - Agent tiers (`AGENT_TIERS`): stock's bundled `security-reviewer` has no `model:` line and ran on the parent's model; OMPdesk holds `task.agentModelOverrides["security-reviewer"] = "@slow,@review"` the same way (Thinking first: on the P5 eval it scored 1.000 vs the review role's cross-family model 0.933, within noise; a cheap parent model must never run a security review), only while stock's bundled agent has no model line and no config sets that key. Overrides outrank an agent file's `model:`, so a user's own `security-reviewer.md` with a model line gets it back at spawn (`before_subagent_spawn`, stock `discoverAgents`).
  - Model presets: stock's `/modelpreset save` snapshots `getModelRoles()` (runtime layer included) and persists it through the `modelPresets` handle's `setEntry`, so the bridge wraps that handle's `setEntry` (`withoutHeldRoles`): every role OMPdesk holds in that Settings (same id, same value) is dropped from the entry before stock writes it. The guard is on the write, never a time window, so however long the command waits (other extensions' `input` handlers run first, one after another) and whichever path saves (RPC, TUI, ACP), a saved preset never holds `review`; a value the user set is not held and is saved as is. A `switch` clears every runtime role its preset does not list (stock `writePresetRoles`), which writes no `review` to config.yml; a `modelRoles` change re-holds the role right after (a `listen` per Settings, `settleOmpdeskRoles`).
  - Pinned by `src/main/bridge-ompdesk-roles-stock.test.ts` (installed omp: listed, `@review` follows the advisor, a pick held for agents and a subagent's overlay with config.yml bytes unchanged and a CLI runtime role kept, the user's config value wins and setting or clearing the role then leaves config.yml's bytes alone, the security reviewer resolves through `@slow` and not the parent's model, a user's own agent file keeps its model, `/modelpreset save` then `switch` over stock's RPC leave `review` out of the preset and config.yml and the pick resolves again afterwards), `src/main/model-roles-stock-coverage.test.ts` (the `review` row comes after stock's with its chain as `defaults`, words in en and ko) and `src/main/model-setup-review-stock.test.ts` (anthropic and anthropic + openai-codex OAuth fixtures: the model setup's full-auto plan, applied through `set_model_role`, makes `@review` resolve to the planned model with config.yml's bytes unchanged).
- Per role: a localized one-line "when it is used" (`modelRoles.use.<id>`, grounded in the stock call site, e.g. `commit/model-selection.ts`, `utils/image-question.ts`, `auto-thinking/classifier.ts`) and the empty-role fallback text (`modelRoles.fallback.<id>`). A role no locale knows gets generic sentences (`model-role-text.ts`: its stock name and the kinds it takes; the fallback from `effective.source`/`via` and whether it has `defaults`); a raw key never reaches the row. `src/main/model-roles-stock-coverage.test.ts` runs the installed omp: every `MODEL_ROLE_IDS` id is in `model_roles` with `accepts`/`defaults` or hidden with a reason, and every shown row, plus two synthetic roles (`future-chat`, `future-kind`), renders a purpose and an empty role's fallback in en and ko.
- `get_model_roles` adds `effective` per role (`role-effective.ts`: the model it really runs on via stock `resolveModelOverride` / `resolveRoleSelection` over the role's `roleCandidatePool`; `source` configured/fallback/session/none, `via` the answering role). Selector lists copy stock call sites, pinned by `src/main/bridge-model-roles-stock.test.ts` against `resolveRoleChain` and `resolveAdvisorRoleSelection` on the real omp.
- A row also shows the provider's account state and tightest live quota (`useUsageReports` in `hooks/use-usage-reports.ts`, the tab's one usage poll; `inUseReport`, `tightestLimit` in `lib/usage-reports.ts`), the thinking level the resolved selector names (else "follows session"), and a picker over stock's candidate pool.
- `effective.skipped` (`SkippedPreferred`, contracts file): an empty built-in role whose first candidate in stock's chain over the whole catalog (`resolveRoleChain` over `modelRegistry.getAll("all")` filtered by the role's `accepts`, providers in `disabledProviderIds` left out) has no credentials while the credentialed chain answers with another `modelKind`. Only the judge's native model (kind `judge`, e.g. Jev) differs in kind from the chat or tiny model that follows it, so only that row shows it: one muted line "{model} needs a key from {providers}", each provider name a link (`ui.focusProvider(id)`) that scrolls Providers & accounts to its card (`providerFocus`, opens the folded picker). Names and provider ids come from stock's catalog; the benefit sentence sits behind the (i) and says only "cheaper" for kind `judge` (stock's `workflow-notice.md`: cheaper than a completion for classification; no stock text claims speed). Pinned against stock's `hasNativeJudge` by `bridge-model-roles-stock.test.ts`.
- **Model setup** ("자동으로 구성", `settings/model-setup/`): two modes over every role `get_model_roles` lists (stock's and OMPdesk's alike, no role list in the code). **완전 자동 구성** opens straight onto its proposal: the pure planner `src/shared/role-plan.ts` `planRoles` (no omp import, no model call, deterministic; under 2 s including the reads) plans over subscription and free models only. **모델 선택 + 자동** plans over the models the person ticks, metered ones included. Rules: default keeps the model it runs on; slow/plan the strongest within 85 % of the top `int`; advisor the same from another vendor (`identity.class`) than the default when one qualifies; smol/tiny/commit the cheapest by output list price within 70 % of the top (commit ≥ 100k context, tiny ≥ 8k); task most `int` per price within 95 %; vision the default's model when it reads images; a role with no rule of its own takes the rule of the first `@role` its own `defaults` chain names that has one (OMPdesk's `review`, `@review → @advisor → …`, plans like the advisor: another family's strong model), else the default's model; a pick equal to what an unset role's chain already resolves to (`effective`) is left unwritten (`follows`), so the chain keeps deciding; a provider under 20 % headroom (usage reports) yields when another qualifies; model-kind roles (image, web, speech, dictation, judge) stay on stock's chain (nothing written).
  - **Billing** comes from bridge `model_setup_facts` (`resources/gui-bridge/model-setup-facts.ts`, contract `model-setup-contracts.ts`, RPC `get_model_setup_facts`, free in `quota-commands.ts`) from stock's auth data, never a host name or catalog `cost` (subscription models carry list prices): stock OAuth with no stored, env or config key and no models.yml entry = subscription; a catalog keyless local runtime on its stock endpoint = free; everything else = metered (API key, env, OpenRouter, OAuth plus a key, any models.yml provider or moved endpoint, loopback included, an unknown provider). Full auto never proposes a metered model; the judge exception (a stock judge-kind model from `rolePriorityDefaults("judge")`, never `jev-latest`, `judgePins`) applies only when picked. Pinned on the installed omp's real catalog by `src/main/model-setup-metering.test.ts` (OAuth, OpenRouter key, OAuth plus key, https and loopback models.yml providers, a moved and an unchanged local runtime); planner rules by `role-plan.test.ts`.
  - Proposal: one line per role `role → model`, the reason behind ⓘ; a role config.yml holds that OMPdesk's last apply did not write (`facts.roles[id].persisted`, never provenance) is "직접 지정됨 · 유지" and unticked. Apply writes ticked rows through `set_model_role` after storing `{applied: {at, values, before}, signature}` in prefs `modelSetup` per device; Undo (ModelSetupEntry) restores only roles still on the applied value. When `setupSignature` (roles, their candidates, providers' billing) differs from the stored one, ModelSetupEntry offers one quiet "다시 구성할까요?" line; nothing changes by itself. The old web research run (`model-setup-runner.ts`) is the optional "근거 더 찾기", which shows its estimated cost and time before it runs.
  - Free also covers stock's in-process `local` provider (`local://inference`: kokoro, parakeet; `IN_PROCESS_LOCAL` in `model-setup-billing-core.ts`); `web` stays metered (its `web://search` routes to search APIs that may bill). Proposal rows are one line, 40 px under touch (`e2e/responsive-audit.ts` surface `dialog-model-setup`: no issue at 390). Captures: `OMP_GUI_MODEL_SETUP_SHOTS=<dir>` with `e2e/model-setup-captures.e2e.ts` (dev harness; ko/en × light/dark × desktop/390: full auto, evidence confirm, picked, applied + Undo, the offer).
- `e2e/model-roles-real.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_ROLES_REAL=1`, private copy of the user's config via `e2e/real-omp.ts`, no model call) resolves every built-in role and template agent's model through stock and checks a custom role set outside OMPdesk stays unlisted yet resolved; `OMP_GUI_ROLES_CAPTURES=<dir>` adds light/dark × en/ko, wide/narrow captures.

### Idle compaction and recap
- **Idle compaction**: stock's `compaction.idleEnabled` / `idleThresholdTokens` / `idleTimeoutSeconds` (Settings › Agent behavior › Context, `/autoclear on [delay] | off`) had a timer only in the TUI, so `resources/gui-bridge/idle-compaction.ts` (rules `idle-compaction-core.ts`, a copy of `#scheduleIdleCompaction`) arms it after a terminal `agent_end` and calls stock `runIdleCompaction()`. It and the idle recap share one idle-window state machine (`timer-core.ts` `IdleWindowTimer`: window, superseded arms, GUI draft); each keeps its own settings, delay bounds and fire. The renderer (`hooks/use-idle-compaction-draft.ts`) reports each tab's draft empty ⇄ non-empty via `set_idle_compaction_draft`, because stock skips a session whose input holds text.
  - A session whose model's prompt cache never expires (logged-in OpenAI Codex: pi-catalog `isPromptCachePersistent`, omp ≥ 18.12.6) is never idle-compacted, checked at the arm and when the timer fires, as stock's `#promptCachePersistent` does: it would only throw a warm cache away. Pinned by `bridge-idle-compaction.test.ts`.
  - Timing follows stock (omp ≥ 18.12.2): with a long-tier (1 h) prompt cache entry still alive (`AgentSession.promptCacheEntry`), it compacts `IDLE_COMPACTION_CACHE_LEAD_MS` (5 s) before stock `getCacheWarmingDelayMs` (`session/cache-warmer`) would refresh it, so the summary still reads the cached prefix, and at once when the turn settled past that point; a 5-minute, expired or lifetime-less entry keeps the configured delay (60–3600 s). The idle recap has no such rule in stock (checked against 18.12.2's `#scheduleIdleRecap`). Pinned by `bridge-idle-compaction.test.ts` and `bridge-idle-compaction-stock.test.ts` (installed omp: feature available, `promptCacheEntry` present, stock's refresh points).
- **Idle recap**: stock's `recap.enabled` (default on) / `recap.idleSeconds` (240) had a TUI-only timer (`#scheduleIdleRecap`), so `resources/gui-bridge/session-recap.ts` (rules `session-recap-core.ts`) arms it after a terminal `agent_end` and recaps as `#runIdleRecap` does: stock's `prompts/system/recap-user.md` with the goal (or title) and next todo, one `runEphemeralTurn`, `previewLine(…, TRUNCATE_LENGTHS.RECAP)`, journaled with `sessionManager.recordRecap`.
  - Skipped while the composer holds a draft (same `set_idle_compaction_draft` report, also forwarded by `local/settings.ts` as `recap_draft`); cancelled by any new turn, compaction or typing; re-checked for idleness after the reply; follows `recap.*` writes (`recapSettingChanged`).
  - Delivery: stock's TUI notice is transient, so the GUI gets a displayed `recap` custom message (`custom_message` journal entry the transcript replays; `RecapCard` in `CustomMessageCard.tsx`, one quiet muted line, `chat.custom.recap`) and a `context` hook drops it from every model request.
  - Tests: `src/main/bridge-session-recap-stock.test.ts` (real omp), `bridge-session-recap.test.ts`; fake omp serves `recap_draft` and `OMP_GUI_TEST_RECAP` (`e2e/omp-defaults.e2e.ts`); `sessionRecap` in `capabilities.ts` / `health.ts`.

### Context inspector, prompt sections, prompt origins
- **Context inspector** (Insights › This session › Inspect, `components/insights/ContextInspector.tsx`): bridge `context.inspect` (`resources/gui-bridge/context-inspect.ts`, rules `context-inspect-core.ts`) keeps stock `getContextBreakdown()`'s anchored totals, splits tool schemas by source (built-in / MCP / extension, per tool via stock `estimateToolSchemaTokens`) and reads context files back out of the rendered `<repo-rules>` block. `context.entry` returns one entry's provider text (tool schema via `toolWireSchema`, messages via `convertToLlm`), capped at 32k characters.
- **Per-turn prompt sections**: stock drops what `before_agent_start` appended when a turn ends (and a base-prompt rebuild replaces it), so `session.systemPrompt` lacks OMPdesk's sections (reply language, profile, reply style, visuals) before the first turn and after a rebuild. Each mode registers its section via `resources/gui-bridge/prompt-sections.ts` (`registerPromptSection`: one "append if missing" step per id, hooked into `before_agent_start`); the inspector applies them in install order (`withTurnSections`) to preview the next prompt. **A new mode that appends to the system prompt registers there, never with a bare `pi.on("before_agent_start")`.**
- **Background checks** (`resources/gui-bridge/modes/background-checks.ts`, text and gate in `background-checks-core.ts`, capability `backgroundChecks`): one static section (`<background-checks>`, ~120 tokens) telling the agent to run tests, builds, lints and typechecks with bash `async: true` by default, keep working on independent steps, read the result before claiming done or before a dependent edit, and keep a run in the foreground only when the very next step needs it, it must be watched, or it would fight another run for a lock, port or build directory. Every session gets it, subagents included (stock's subagent rebind installs the hook), except while stock `async.enabled` is off (read by id through `config/registry` `lookup`) or an Autopilot run owns the session (`autopilotRunning`, `modes/autopilot-core.ts`): Autopilot's cycle report is judged from the turn's own foreground check results (`autopilot-verify.ts`). The GUI needs nothing new for these runs: the jobs tree, work board, dock island and composer cue already list running shells, the `async-result` card shows a failed job, the run that started it is charged with the failure (`chargeJobFailures`), and the work card takes its verdict (see Work card head). Pinned by `bridge-background-checks.test.ts`.
  - **Measured** (19 Oct 2026, real omp 18.12.2, `smol` = Claude Sonnet 5.5, a bug fix whose `bun test` takes 28 s plus independent writes, 38 runs, $2.22): told the suite takes ~30 s, stock already backgrounded it in 5 of 6 runs, so the section changed nothing (median work time 35 s stock, 34 s section, first tool call to the end). Not told, stock ran a baseline `bun test` in the foreground first in 3 of 6 runs (blocking 30 s), with the section in 2 of 8; median work time 55.5 s → 46 s, mean wall 61.3 s → 58.6 s, within noise at this sample. Every run of every variant read the test result before its final answer and left the suite passing.
  - **A run older than the last edit is never waited for** (20 Oct 2026): the section also says a run that started before the last edit to the code it covers says nothing about it, so the agent cancels it and runs the check once on the edited tree (a baseline suite started, then waited for after the edit, ran the slow suite twice).
  - **`bash.autoBackground.thresholdMs` is not held lower** (stock 60 s): 20 s gained nothing (chained runs 35–37 s of work against stock's 34–37 s), came with a redundant second suite run in 2 of 3 runs of the first scenario (wall 72–74 s against ~40 s), and turns every final verification run into a background job plus a `wait` call.
- **A todo list left from earlier work** (`resources/gui-bridge/todo-stale.ts`, rules and text `todo-stale-core.ts`, capability `todoStale`; the user's report, 21 Oct 2026: an orchestrator's work board showed a bug fix's 9/10 for hours while it took request after request). Stock reminds about a list only to finish it (completion reminder, mid-run nudge) and its eager prelude runs only on a session's first turn without one, so nothing told the agent to replace an old task's list. At the start of a turn a new request opens (`before_agent_start`, main agent only, `todo` active, not Plan mode, not an Autopilot run), `todoStaleness` reads the journal with stock's `tools/todo` `getLatestTodoPhasesFromEntries` and `getLatestTodoSnapshotIdentity`: stale when every item is done or dropped (at once), or items are open and a whole earlier request went by after the list's last change (`UNTOUCHED_REQUESTS` 1). It returns one hidden custom message (`ompdesk-todo-stale`, `display: false`, `<system-reminder>` naming the phases and done/total, "replace the list with its steps (op init) or mark the finished items done"), never a system-prompt section: a section that comes and goes changes the prompt in front of the whole conversation and the prompt cache would miss twice. A question or a short reply never gets it (`isNewRequest`: code rule, no model call; a trailing `?`, an English interrogative first word or a Korean question ending, unless a request word such as `줄래`, `고쳐`, `can you` is in it; under 8 characters; `/ ! $`). One list gets it at most twice (`MAX_REMINDERS`), the second only after 3 more requests left it alone; the reminder is journaled with the list's entry id, so a relaunch keeps the count. Whether the items name work the conversation moved past is not read (it needs a model); the untouched-request rule stands in. An orchestrator's list follows its children through the `ompdesk` skill (`references/orchestration.md`: one item per dispatched session, done on its `done` report), not the bridge.
  - **Measured** (21 Oct 2026, real omp 18.12.4, `smol` = Claude Sonnet 5.5, `TODO_STALE_BEFORE_REF=587ed16bd OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1 bunx vitest run src/main/bridge-todo-stale-stock.test.ts`, 2 runs a variant, 133 s): a session that finished a two-step listed task got two new requests and a question. Before: 0 of 4 new-request turns touched the list (it stayed the first task's 2/2). After: 4 of 4 opened a new list with `init` and ticked it, a reminder in each of those turns, none on the 2 question turns. Pinned by `bridge-todo-stale.test.ts` (rule) and the scripted half of `bridge-todo-stale-stock.test.ts` (installed omp: the line reaches the model after the person's prompt in the new request's turn, not for a question, not again two requests later; fails with the module unloaded).
- **Prompt origins** (`resources/gui-bridge/prompt-origin.ts`, contracts `prompt-origin-contracts.ts`): a host passes the device a tab runs for (and its own name) as `OMP_GUI_PROMPT_ORIGIN` at spawn; that omp journals one `ompdesk.prompt-origin` custom entry per user message (public `message_end` hook, keyed by message timestamp); `sessions.transcript` sets `promptFrom` on prompts typed on another device than the viewing one once a session holds prompts from two devices (MessageBubble: "from {device}"). Each device's tab is its own omp; the one other party that types into it or answers its asks is a window of the host itself (a hosted view, see Remote devices): its prompts are journaled as the host's (`prompt_origin_set` with `PROMPT_ORIGIN_HOST_ID`), and the first answer to an ask, the device's or the host window's, wins.

## Machine setup and updates

### Machine setup (`src/main/setup/`, contract `src/shared/setup.ts`)
- At launch main detects Bun, omp (through `resolveOmp`, so `MIN_OMP_VERSION` gates it), Git, GitHub CLI, Node.js (through the REA probe) and Tailscale, and installs what is missing **on its own**: no install button, no consent click. `SETUP_ITEMS` = bun, git, omp, gh, node, tailscale.
  - Required, in order: Bun (`curl -fsSL https://bun.sh/install | bash` / `irm bun.sh/install.ps1 | iex`, user-level); on Windows Git for Windows (an existing install with `bin\bash.exe` is reused, else the PortableGit pinned in `plans.ts` with its SHA-256, extracted without admin into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\ompdesk\tools\git`; never WSL); then omp (`<abs bun> install -g @oh-my-pi/pi-coding-agent`, or `omp update` below the minimum).
  - Optional, in the background once omp works: Git on macOS (`xcode-select --install`); gh and Node (Homebrew on macOS, else the official archive checked against its published SHA-256 into the managed tools dir `<userData>/tools`; `winget … --accept-source-agreements --accept-package-agreements` on Windows, else `unavailable`).
  - Tailscale (Mobile web) is never automatic (`setupItemAutomatic`): only `installForConnection` and an explicit Retry install it; the user's click is its consent.
  - Claude Code CLI and Codex CLI are never installed: omp signs in to Anthropic and OpenAI itself.
- `download.ts` fetches PortableGit and archives itself (streamed to `<tools>/.downloads`, SHA-256 checked, removed after the install) so an item's `progress` carries real bytes; running items also carry `step`/`steps` and `startedAt`. The view shows a determinate bar only for bytes, else an indeterminate one, plus "Step n of m" and the elapsed time.
- `paths.ts` appends the managed bin dirs to `process.env.PATH` and sets `GIT_INSTALL_ROOT` (stock `resolveWindowsShell` checks it after the Git for Windows roots) at startup and after each install. After an install it re-reads the login-shell PATH (macOS) or registry PATH (Windows), re-detects, and when omp became usable restarts every local tab in `error`/`exited` and the stats server. No app restart.
- Retry policy: each item once per launch automatically; after three automatic misses only once a day (`BACKOFF_MS` 24 h, `<userData>/setup-state.json`); the user's Retry always runs.
- Every install runs under `supervisor.ts` (the app binary as Node via `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`), which kills the installer's process tree when its stdin closes: Cancel (also aborts main's download), quit (`SetupService.dispose`) or a crash. POSIX ends the process group. **Windows has no group, so a PowerShell sweep ends the tree and repeats until two passes in a row find nothing new** (`TREE_SWEEP_SCRIPT`: Toolhelp's process snapshot through a dynamic assembly's P/Invoke, no WMI, no `Add-Type` compile; a process counts as a member only when born after its parent, the root only when born before the Stop; the supervisor exits after it). WHY (21 Oct 2026, `sessions-schedules` teardown `EBUSY rmdir …\project`, 1 of 40 runs under load): `taskkill /T` lists the tree and then ends it. A Stop 60 ms after the supervisor started reached the `cmd` shim before its `bun` (the fake omp's `-p` hang) existed; `bun` was born 200 ms before taskkill returned, taskkill exited 0 with the shim ended and `bun` alive, and nothing ended it. The folders of four such runs were still locked tens of minutes later, each by a live `bun.exe` whose working directory was the project (ending them freed the folders). Taskkill is now the fallback, used only when PowerShell cannot run the sweep. Pinned by `runner.test.ts` ("a child started in the gap between taskkill listing the tree and ending it": a slow `taskkill` that lists, waits 800 ms and ends its list; 3 of 3 runs failed with 7 to 13 survivors before, 3 of 3 passed after).
- IPC (`setup:get-state`, `setup:retry`, `setup:cancel`, push `setup:state`) takes item ids only; no handler accepts a command.
- Automatic installs run only in a packaged app outside `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=1`, or with `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER` (plus `OMP_GUI_TEST_DOWNLOAD`/`OMP_GUI_TEST_DOWNLOAD_SHA256`: a file every test plan downloads first). Dev and test launches otherwise only detect.
- Setup screen (`components/onboarding/SetupTakeover.tsx`, shown by `App.tsx` from `useMachineSetupNeeded()`): while a local tab's omp is unusable and a required item is missing or installing, one modal dialog covers the whole window (sidebar and title bar included; drag strip and OS window controls stay live). Everything beneath is `inert`; menu actions (except the language switch; the tray's "new session" included), omp:// links, shortcuts and drops are no-ops through `useMachineSetupStore.takeover`; Tab is trapped, and focus opens on the dialog itself, never on its first control. Each required item is one row with its phase and progress; the exact command, install location and output sit in one closed "Show details" disclosure (an OS prompt it will raise stays visible), and a failure adds the banner with the reason, manual steps and Retry. When required setup finishes it fades out (reduced motion skips the fade) into the first-run flow (`FirstRunGate` → provider step). Optional items and remote tabs never show it. Every item stays listed in Settings › Updates & about › This computer (`MachineSetupSection.tsx`).
- `.github/workflows/setup-assets-health.yml` downloads every pinned setup file weekly through `download.ts` (`scripts/setup-assets-health.ts`; `gh workflow run setup-assets-health.yml` runs it now) and keeps one issue open while any fails.

### Connect installs its tool
- `SetupService.installForConnection`: a connection the user starts that needs a missing optional item installs it right then, as the user's own action: past the daily back-off and the once-per-launch rule, queued behind any running install, only in a launch that installs on its own (else it only detects). The connection carries on when the tool lands. Callers: gh (GitHub sign-in), node (`LocalMachine.setBuiltinRea` turning the built-in REA on while Node is missing or too old), tailscale (`tailscale/service.ts`).
- Connect GitHub (Settings › General, the PR center's signed-out card; `onboarding/GithubSignIn.tsx`) calls `GithubAuthService.startLogin`: with gh missing the flow is `installing`, awaits `installGh`, then starts `gh auth login`. No gh after the install → `gh_install_failed`; nothing able to install it here → `gh_missing`; Cancel stops it (`cancelGhInstall`).
- `onboarding/SetupInstallInline.tsx` shows the item inline (step, bytes, clock; the reason plus Retry after a failure; the download page when nothing can install it), also for setup's own background gh install. A paired host's tab: the host's own UI and setup install on the host.
- git/gh lookups (`local/git/exec.ts` `resolveBin`, `execBin`) read the spawn PATH on every call (`spawnPath` shares the cached login-shell probe, which `refreshPath` drops after an install), so a fresh gh is found without a relaunch. Never keep a copy of PATH from the first git call.
- No stock omp provider login shells out to a CLI (github-copilot, gemini-cli, google-antigravity, cursor, anthropic, openai-codex are OAuth/device-code flows in pi-ai's `registry/oauth/`; perplexity only reads its macOS app's token), so no provider sign-in installs anything.
- `e2e/github-cli-install.e2e.ts` (fake installer) covers gh off PATH and in back-off, the click downloading (held at half), failing offline with Retry, installing into the managed tools dir and reaching the device code and signed-in account (en/ko captures).

### omp updates itself (`src/main/omp-updater.ts`, contract `src/shared/omp-update.ts`)
- OMPdesk's own update is `updater.ts`, installed by itself unless the user turns `appAutoUpdate` (default on) off.
- Main asks stock `omp update --check` (`SetupService.latestOmp`) at launch (after machine setup's first pass) and every 4 h, doubling the wait per failure in a row up to 24 h; while omp is missing nothing is checked. When the answer is newer than the installed omp (`compareVersions`) it runs stock `omp update` through `SetupService.updateOmp` (the omp item's `omp-update` plan under the setup supervisor): background, no click, queued behind any install, **never waiting for a turn to end** (the user's rule: a running omp keeps the code it loaded).
- Check and update share one environment (`ompUpdateEnv` in `omp-runtime.ts`): the detected omp's folder first on PATH (stock updates the first `omp` on PATH, `resolveOmpPath` = `$which("omp")`) and, for a `bun install -g` launcher, `BUN_INSTALL=<its Bun root>` (stock calls it a Bun install only inside `bun pm bin -g`). So both follow the user's registry (.npmrc, bunfig, `npm_config_registry`), channel and install method (Bun, npm, Homebrew, mise, standalone), agree, and land in the omp OMPdesk runs, never another `omp` earlier on PATH. `get_omp_update` (a paired host's check, `local/misc.ts`) uses it too.
- Why it is safe: `bun install -g` replaces package files by new inodes, and the bridge's literal `@oh-my-pi/*` imports resolve to the running omp's bundled singletons (`health.ts`). Processes that read omp's files from disk (stats server, stock helper: `import(variable)`) are stopped before the installer and started after it. Known limit: the old process's lazy hashed assets in `dist/` (HTML export template, highlight/marked chunks) vanish, so those can fail in a tab until it restarts. Windows locks files a running omp holds: stock's EBUSY/launcher repair applies; a failed install is kept in `OmpUpdateState.failure` and retried at the next check.
- After success main re-detects omp (`refreshSystemOmp`), starts the stats server on it, restarts every idle local tab and every idle tab it hosts for paired devices on their own session file, and restarts a busy tab one tick after its turn ends (`src/main/omp-restart.ts`; `SidecarPool.restartOnNewOmp`, `LocalHostServices.restartOnNewOmp`; `asleep` tabs need nothing). Composer drafts survive. The restarts re-ask each bridge's `capabilities` (`BridgeHealth` is keyed by omp version), so a feature the new omp lacks shows the existing `unsupported_omp` warning toast. Success shows one quiet "omp updated to X" toast in one window.
- One switch, "Keep tools up to date" (pref `toolsAutoUpdate`, `TOOLS_AUTO_UPDATE_PREF`; default on; a stored `false` turns it off), covers omp, the tool CLIs and extensions; it and omp's manual "Update now" live in Settings › Updates & about › This computer (`OmpUpdateRows.tsx`). IPC (`omp-update:get-state`, `omp-update:now`, push `omp-update:state`, `omp-update:updated`) carries no version, command or path.
- Each computer updates its own omp under its own pref, always through its own `OmpUpdater`. On a paired host's tab Settings › Updates & about shows the host's row (`HostOmpUpdateRow`); its button only asks the host to run its updater (`host.ompUpdate.now` / `host-omp-update:now`; state `host.ompUpdate.get` / `host-omp-update:get`; the host pushes every change as the `host.ompUpdate.changed` event, forwarded as `host-omp-update:state`), so the host's helpers, supervisor, re-detection and tab restarts run, and it never overlaps its own scheduled update. There is no `omp_update` RPC and no second `omp update` path.
- Scheduled checks run only in a packaged app outside `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=1`, or in a test launch with both `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER` and `OMP_GUI_TEST_OMP_UPDATES=1` (installer called `<installer> omp omp-update`).
- Tests: `e2e/omp-auto-update.e2e.ts` with `OMP_GUI_TEST_LATEST_OMP` (what `update --check` reports), `OMP_GUI_TEST_UPDATE_CHECK_LOG` (each check's first PATH entry), `OMP_GUI_TEST_VERSION_FILE`, `OMP_GUI_TEST_BOOT_LOG`, `OMP_GUI_TEST_UNAVAILABLE_AFTER_UPDATE`; `src/main/setup/omp-update-install.test.ts` runs the real `bun install -g` against a fake registry (`src/main/test-omp-registry.ts`) in a temp HOME and BUN_INSTALL, a second omp first on PATH staying untouched.

### `UpdateScheduler` (`src/main/update-scheduler.ts`)
- The omp, tool CLI and extension updaters share one loop: it ticks at start in a scheduled launch, re-arms its timer from the end of each tick (a rejected tick does not end the schedule), queues passes one at a time (`enqueue`, dropped once disposed) and owns the back-off (`nextCheckDelay`, per item `settleBackoff`; `CHECK_INTERVAL_MS` 4 h, `MAX_CHECK_INTERVAL_MS` 24 h).
- omp backs off its timer by its own failure count and joins a running check instead of queueing a second; CLI and extension updaters back off per tool or row (a cancel is no failure). `updater.ts` (its own 5-minute check) is not on it.

### Tool CLIs of connected accounts (`src/main/cli-tools/`, contract `src/shared/cli-update.ts`)
- Follows "Keep tools up to date" (`toolsAutoUpdate`, default on; a user may pin a vendor CLI; OMPdesk needs no minimum version of one). Runs at launch (after setup's first pass) and every 4 h with omp's per-tool back-off (`CliUpdater`).
- Main reads providers with a stored credential from stock's own store (stock-helper `accounts.providers` = `discoverAuthStorage().credentials.list()`, ids only; `sdk` is listed under `providers` in `capabilities.ts`) and maps them to CLIs (`catalog.ts`: anthropic → `claude`, openai-codex/openai → `codex`, google-gemini-cli/google → `gemini`, cursor → `cursor-agent`, github-copilot → `copilot`, `gh` and its `gh-copilot` extension), plus what setup installs (gh, and Node in the managed tools dir only).
- It **never installs** a vendor CLI: only an installed one is listed and updated, by the owner found from its resolved executable path (`detect.ts classifyInstall`): Homebrew (`brew upgrade [--cask] <name>`), a global npm (`npm install -g --prefix <that prefix>`) or Bun package (`bun install -g` under that `BUN_INSTALL`), winget (`winget upgrade --id`), the vendor's updater (`claude update`, which also owns a Claude Code in npm/Bun; `cursor-agent update`; `gh extension upgrade gh-copilot`), or setup's verified archive for the managed gh/Node. System (`/usr/bin`, Xcode CLT, `C:\Windows`), app-bundled (the Codex in ChatGPT.app) and admin-only installs are listed with their reason and never touched; no sudo.
- The check asks the owner first (`npm view`, `bun pm view`, `brew outdated --json=v2`, `winget list --upgrade-available`, the release feed), updates only when newer (`compareVersions`), runs `SetupService.runToolUpdate` under the setup supervisor (queued behind installs, never touching setup items' state) and detects again (`updated` or `not-updated`).
- Running-process rule: npm and Bun replace the whole package dir (a running process keeps its open files, but a later `require` reads the new version and a file only the old version had is gone); Homebrew removes the old keg. So on macOS an npm/Bun/Homebrew update of a Node CLI waits while a process of it runs (`ps`, `plans.ts waitsForIdle`/`runningIn`; result `deferred`, no back-off, retried next check). A native binary (gh), vendor updaters and managed copies update at once. On Windows a file a running tool holds just fails the update, which is recorded and retried. A failure backs off like omp's.
- IPC `cliUpdate.getState/now(id?)/onState` carries tool ids only (an unknown id throws; no command, path or version crosses it); opening Settings rescans. Each computer, and each paired host, updates its own tools.
- Homebrew prefixes (`/opt/homebrew/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`) are also searched when the login-shell PATH omits them (`resolveBin` for gh/git, setup's gh detection, `cli-tools/detect.ts`). The PR and GitHub centers show the connect card (install, then sign in) instead of a download link for a missing gh on this computer; a paired host's tab keeps the link (that gh is the host's).
- Tests: a test launch checks on its own only with `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER` plus `OMP_GUI_TEST_CLI_UPDATES=1` (runs `<installer> cli-<tool> <method> check|update`; `e2e/cli-update.e2e.ts`). `OMP_GUI_TEST_BREW_DIRS` (PATH-style list, empty = none) replaces Homebrew's prefixes so the developer's brew never answers a test.
- Windows: a tool is found only as `<bin>.exe` or `<bin>.cmd` (npm's bare `<bin>` and `npm` are POSIX scripts Windows cannot start). A `.cmd` runs through cmd.exe with the omp shim's quoting (`src/main/spawn.ts`: `launcherNames`, `findOnPath`, `spawnCommand`/`spawnArgs`/`needsShell`: the one PATH lookup and Windows spawn rule every external tool uses). npm writes a global bin there as a cmd-shim, so `detect.ts cmdShimTarget` reads the package file the shim runs (`"%dp0%\node_modules\<package>\<bin>"`) to find its npm prefix. A Bun global is a copy of Bun's shim `.exe` beside a `<bin>.bunx`, whose start names the target in UTF-16LE up to a `"`, relative to the folder above the shim's (`detect.ts bunxShimTarget`, bun's `BinLinkingShim.zig`; `install\global\node_modules\<package>\…` from `.bun\bin`), so Bun's install owns it.

### Extensions of connected omp (`src/main/ext-update/`, contract `src/shared/extension-update.ts`, helper contract `resources/gui-bridge/stock-helper/extensions-contracts.ts`)
- Follows "Keep tools up to date" (`toolsAutoUpdate`, default on); rows in Settings › Updates & about › This computer › Extensions. Marketplaces, plugins, skills and MCP servers, on `CliUpdater`'s schedule (after setup's first pass, every 4 h, per-row back-off `nextCheckDelay`, failures recorded, deferred rows retried; `omp` being installed defers without back-off). `ExtensionUpdater` reads the inventory in the extensions worker (local files, no network) and works marketplaces → plugins → skills → MCP, one row at a time.
- Marketplaces: stock `updateMarketplace` (what `marketplace_action update` runs).
- Plugins: marketplace installs by stock `checkForUpdates`/`upgradePlugin`, npm and git plugins by `PluginManager.upgrade`. Stock has no user pin for a marketplace plugin (the catalog's `ref`/`sha`/`version` is the author's; upgrade follows it).
  - `upgrade(name)` reinstalls an npm plugin at the registry's latest whatever its spec says, so OMPdesk reads `plugins/package.json` and calls upgrade only when the spec is what a bare install writes (bun 1.4.2: `pkg`/`pkg@latest` → `^x.y.z`, `pkg@beta` → `^<tagged>`, exact and range specs verbatim; `^<installed>`, `latest`, `*` follow latest) and latest is strictly newer.
  - An exact or ranged npm spec and a git commit/version-tag ref are pinned: checked, shown as "update available", never moved, not even by Update now. Linked plugins are skipped.
  - npm/git/linked plugins are user scope only (stock's `PluginManager` has no project scope); marketplace plugins also per project registry of the open local tabs' folders.
- Skills: stock records a source only for skills-registry installs (`skills.json`/`skills.lock.json`, `skillshare/manifest.ts`; `omp skill install|update|uninstall` take `@scope/name`; no git-source skill), so those update by `updateSkillPackages` (exact range = pinned; a version that ships scripts is never approved for the user: `needs-approval`). Plugin-provided skills follow their plugin (one grouped row); plain local files are one "local, not updated" row.
- MCP servers (the user's config is never written):
  - The built-in REA is listed as pinned to the OMPdesk release; URL servers are skipped (`remote`).
  - An unpinned `npx`/`bunx`/`uvx` package is "follows latest at start" and its cache is not touched (npm 11.17: a bare `npx -y pkg` after a new publish starts the new version; warming a cache would execute the server). A pinned `pkg@1.2.3` is checked against the registry and shown as an available update, config untouched.
  - A global binary is updated by its owner (`cli-tools/detect.ts classifyInstall` plus pipx `/pipx/venvs/<pkg>/`, uv tool `/uv/tools/<pkg>/`, pip via the shebang python's `importlib.metadata`) through `SetupService.runToolUpdate` under the supervisor (system, bundled and admin-only installs listed and skipped, no sudo).
  - Running rule: a script (shebang) whose owner replaces package files in place (npm/Bun, Homebrew, pipx/uv/pip) waits while a `ps`-visible process of it runs (`deferred running`, retried); a native binary updates at once; Windows has no scan, a held file fails and is retried.
- Applying to live tabs (`src/main/extension-reload.ts`, `SidecarPool.applyExtensionUpdate`, `LocalHostServices.applyExtensionUpdate`): one `ExtensionApplyPlan` per pass. An idle ready tab reloads now (bridge `extension_update.reload` = stock `reloadPluginState`, what `/reload-plugins` does); a plugin that ships extensions/tools/hooks (`activation: restart-required`, `extensions/activation-core.ts`) restarts the tab on its session file (`omp-restart.ts` pattern); an updated MCP package is reconnected (`extension_update.reconnect` = stock `MCPManager.reconnectServer`); a busy tab waits and applies one tick after its turn ends; `asleep` tabs need nothing.
- Every stock internal the worker uses is in `capabilities.ts` and `health.ts` `LOADERS`. IPC `extensionUpdate.getState/now(key?)/onState` carries row keys only. Each computer, and each paired host, updates its own.
- Tests: a test launch checks only with `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER` plus `OMP_GUI_TEST_EXT_UPDATES=1` (MCP binary checks/updates run `<installer> cli-mcp-<name> <method> check|update`; `e2e/extension-update.e2e.ts`).

### Disk care (`src/main/disk/`, contract `src/shared/disk-care.ts`)
- The user's rule (20 Oct 2026): OMPdesk keeps the disk it fills in check by itself, **zero settings, nothing lost**. `DiskCare` runs once 2 min after launch (skipped when a run ended within 20 h; the daily timer comes due instead), then daily, one at a time; the Settings button and the agent's `disk.clean` join a running one. Every action is one `disk-care` line in gui-runtime.jsonl (`run` summary, `artifacts`, `worktree-landed`, `worktree-saved` with its `git worktree add …` restore line, `kept` with the reason, `http-cache`, `bun-cache`, `failed`). State (last run, last measurement) in `<userData>/disk-care/state.json`.
- **Measured on the user's Mac (20 Oct 2026)**: `~/.omp/wt` 54 GB in 24 worktrees; read-only classification of its 18 orphans by the rules below: 41.8 GB, of which 97.3 % regenerable (Cargo `target/` with `CACHEDIR.TAG` 24.4 GB, lockfile `node_modules` 15.2 GB allocated, `.gitignore` build sections 1.1 GB), 14 MB for the archive; none was yet idle 3 days. `node_modules` is mostly copy-on-write clones of Bun's cache: deleting a fresh one freed 276 MB of its 925 MB. Sessions 1.9 GB, userData 1.3 GB (1.2 GB Chromium HTTP cache, 111 × ~100 MB update installers), Bun cache 1.1 GB, stats.db 110 MB, omp logs 45 MB.
- **Idle thresholds by evidence**: stock session files by cwd (Oct 7–20): a worktree was resumed at most 8.3 h after its last write; across every project p99 2.9 days, max 6.7 days. So build output goes after `DISK_CARE_ARTIFACT_IDLE_DAYS` 3 (the cost of a wrong guess is a reinstall or rebuild) and a worktree with unlanded work after `DISK_CARE_REMOVE_IDLE_DAYS` 14 (twice the longest gap seen). Idle = no open tab on any device (`openFolders`), no running process with its cwd inside (`lsof -d cwd` on macOS, `/proc` on Linux; Windows cannot tell, so these two steps keep everything there), and nothing changed for N days: HEAD commit, git dir HEAD/index/reflog, every tracked and untracked file, every ignored entry no tool makes again, and stock's newest session file for that cwd (`lastActivity`).
- **Every step keeps a worktree whole** (build output included) while an open tab on any device holds it, git lists it locked, a known process works in it, or a rebase, merge, cherry-pick, revert or bisect is half done (`inProgressOperation`; checked under the removal claim in the artifact step, in `archiveBeforeRemove` for the landed sweep and the person's Remove, and before a save).
- **A save never rewrites bytes**: a worktree whose restore could write other bytes than it holds is kept, not saved: `core.autocrlf` true/input (Git for Windows' default), `core.eol`, or any `text`/`eol`/`crlf`/`filter`/`working-tree-encoding`/`ident` attribute (`-text` excepted) on a file the save carries (`rewritesBytes` in `saved-worktrees.ts`: `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` with `:(attr:…)` pathspecs, so git itself reads tracked and untracked `.gitattributes`, the common dir's `info/attributes`, `core.attributesFile` and the system file; never a parsed or lower-cased path). Pinned by the attribute-source cases and the CRLF/LF/mixed save → restore sha256 round trip of `disk/disk-care.test.ts`, which run in `test:platform` on Windows too.
- **Regenerable by evidence, never by name** (`disk/regenerable.ts`, one rule table): a `CACHEDIR.TAG` with the standard signature; `node_modules/` beside a tracked `package.json` with a tracked lockfile at or above it; `__pycache__/` holding only `.pyc`; the output folder of a build tool the package depends on and **builds with** in a script (`vite build`, `electron-vite build`, `next build` → `.next/`, `next export` → `out/`, `astro build`, `parcel build`, `nuxt build|generate`, `tsc` without `--noEmit`; a bare `vite` or `next dev` proves nothing), or a `--outDir` a script passes; a tool's config file's literal `outDir`/`output` replaces the default, and one that sets it any other way (`outDir: resolve(…)`) leaves the output unknown, so nothing counts; an ignored path whose `.gitignore` line sits under a comment naming build output, artifacts, generated files, caches or dependencies. Anything else (a hand-made `dist/`, `tmp/`, `.env`, a local database) is data.
- Steps: (1) drop regenerable entries of idle orphans; (2) the landed sweep (Work tree › Swept by themselves) with the archive first; (3) an idle orphan with unlanded or uncommitted work, not locked, no half-done rebase/merge/cherry-pick/revert/bisect: what is staged becomes one commit (from a copy of the index) and the working tree's tracked changes and untracked files one more on top (scratch index), `commit-tree --no-gpg-sign`, no hook, on a new `ompdesk/saved/<folder>-<date>` branch (HEAD → staged → working; never its own branch, never main; a detached HEAD's commits get the branch too). **No secret enters a commit** (`secretIn`): a file whose name says secret (`.env*`, keys, `credentials*`), whose whole content holds a credential by the central redactor's rules (`resources/gui-bridge/redactor.ts`: a `<secret>` or `<token>` appears, so a `"password": "…"`, a Bearer value, a JWT or a vendor token; a lockfile's long hashes count too, which only archives it), or that cannot be read whole (over `SECRET_SCAN_MAX_BYTES` 16 MB, unreadable) goes to the archive instead; a staged one keeps the worktree. Everything git does not keep and no tool makes again plus those files is copied to `<userData>/disk-care/saved/<id>/` (0700/0600, copy-on-write, verified by a walk; never in git, never synced, never uploaded). Only when nothing moved since the save read the worktree (no process inside, no half-done operation, the same HEAD and status, and no tracked, untracked or archived ignored file, index, reflog or session written since: its `lastActivity` stamp, which the status alone misses for a second edit of an already modified file) is the record written, and only then does `git worktree remove --force` take the folder; its own branch stays. **Once removal starts nothing is rolled back**: a removal that stops half way leaves the record, branch and archive as the only copies (a landed worktree's archive is likewise kept unless the folder still holds every archived file); a record that cannot be written rolls back and removes nothing. Kept instead: a saved commit over 256 MB or an archive over 512 MB. (4) the app's HTTP cache past 256 MB is cleared (`session.clearCache`); update installers are now fetched `cache: "no-store"`. (5) Bun's cache past 2 GB is cleared with `bun pm cache rm` (Bun has no age option) while no install runs (`ps`; skipped on Windows, which shows no arguments).
- **Left alone, by measurement**: omp logs (stock `pi-utils` `pruneStaleProcessLogs` keeps one completed-process log per process and day over 5 days and never touches a live pid's; the 1,118 files are that window), sessions (never deleted; stock has no compressed form), `stats.db` (stock omp-stats has no retention), `~/.omp/puppeteer` (stock's browser).
- **Measuring is cheap** (21 Oct 2026, `disk/tree-sizes.ts`): every run ends with the Disk row's parts, and the HTTP and Bun cache checks measure too. Before, `du -sk` (macOS) and a serial `lstat` walk (Windows) read every file each time: this Mac's `~/.omp/wt` (924,219 files, 77,538 folders) 17.7 s, the Windows PC's Bun cache 10.1 s and `~/.omp/wt` 9.0 s (Defender on: ~93 µs an entry), and the Bun cache was walked twice a run, about 30 s of disk work there. `TreeSizes` keeps, per folder, its mtime, inode, the bytes of its direct files and its child folders in `<userData>/disk-care/sizes.json`, and reuses a folder while both are unchanged and its mtime is more than `RACY_MS` (2 s) older than the reading that saw it (git's racy rule: 1 to 2 s timestamps on HFS+, FAT and exFAT). Only the write-once parts reuse (`REUSED_PARTS`: worktrees, Bun cache); sessions, logs, stats and userData are appended to in place and are read in full (8,208 entries in 64 ms here). A full walk at least every 7 days (`FULL_WALK_EVERY_MS`) bounds what a reused folder cannot see: a file rewritten in place, or a second link given to a file in an unchanged folder. 16 calls at once; allocated blocks on macOS (equal to `du -sk` to the KiB on every part), sizes on Windows; a hard-linked file counts once per part, as `du` counts it (here 53,127 links held 8.25 GiB `du` counts once; on the Windows PC `~/.omp/wt` read 1.97 GB before and 1.36 GB with Bun's hard-linked `node_modules` counted once). Measured: this Mac 7.3 s full, 0.43 s reused; the Windows PC 5.6 s full, 0.40 s reused. Each run logs `usage` {ms, reused, lstat, readdir}. Pinned by `tree-sizes.test.ts` (each kind of change against a full walk, reads only the changed folders, hard links, the two misses bounded by the full walk, the racy rule, an unreadable file) and the first case of `disk-care.test.ts`.
- Surfaces: Settings › Updates & about › This computer one Disk row (`DiskCareRow.tsx`: total, last freed, breakdown in (i), Clean up); the Work tree's Saved worktrees with Restore; agent actions `disk.status` (read) and `disk.clean` (change, no card). IPC `disk-care:get-state`, `disk-care:run`, push `disk-care:state`. Each computer, and each paired host, cares for its own disk.
- **One listing per run** (21 Oct 2026): the artifact step walks the orphans' paths alone (`orphanWorktreePaths`, one `git worktree list`) and keeps what it read of each before any claim; after it, one `listAllOrphans` reading of every orphan's state serves the landed sweep (`sweepLandedWorktrees`' `listed`) and the save step (minus what the sweep removed), whose idle pre-filter reuses the artifact step's read (its idle days only when nothing was dropped: a drop writes the worktree's folder). A read of a worktree (`readWorktreeFiles`, `lastActivity`) runs `ls-files` once (`WorktreeFiles.tracked`), and the git dir comes from the worktree's `.git` (`gitDirOf`). The claimed reads and `stillSame` are unchanged. Git processes in one run: 99 → 58 for one idle unlanded worktree, 67 → 32 for one with build output, 115 → 49 for four kept worktrees.
- Tests: `disk/disk-care*.test.ts` (five files on one fixture, `disk-care-fixture.ts`, so they run side by side: each case runs real git dozens of times; real temp repos: lockfile `node_modules` and a build tool's `out/` dropped, a hand-made `dist/` and an edited `.env` kept; `.gitignore` sections both ways; a dev-only `vite`, `next dev` and a computed `outDir` never license deleting a hand-made folder; an idle unlanded worktree saved, removed and restored whole with no secret in git; a plain JSON password and a token past the first megabyte stay out of git and come back; staged A and working B each come back where they were; a staged secret keeps the worktree; an already modified file or an ignored `.env` edited again during the save keeps the worktree and its newest bytes; a new commit, an edited tracked file or a new untracked file after the shared listing, after the landed sweep or as the save step claims the folder, keeps the worktree (`disk-care-shared-listing.test.ts`, with the same worktree saved when nothing changes); an open tab, a process, a lock and a half-done merge each keep theirs, a half-done merge or rebase its build output too, landed or not; unknown processes keep everything; a landed worktree's `.env` archived and restored; a removal git stops half way keeps record, branch and archive; a record write that fails leaves the worktree untouched and no stray branch or archive), `DiskCareRow.test.tsx`, `WorkTreePanel.test.tsx`, `app-control.test.ts`.
- **Windows: Electron's `fs` holds the `.asar` files disk care walks** (measured 21 Oct 2026; Code conventions has the mechanism). After one run, Restart Manager named the app's main pid for the archive in a landed worktree's `node_modules`, in an archived `vendor/`, in a plain folder under the worktree root (only the usage measurement reaches it) and in the Bun cache, and for none outside what the run walked. So the sweep kept a landed worktree ("git refused to remove it"), failed with `EPERM unlink …\files\vendor\app.asar` (the archive could not remove its own copy), and a folder under the worktree root stayed undeletable while the app ran. Everything in `src/main/disk/` reads user folders through `original-fs`, `TreeSizes` (`tree-sizes.ts`, the measuring walk) included. `e2e/disk-care-asar.e2e.ts` (a real app, real git, those four archives, the Bun cache a private one; the run has no failure, both worktrees go, the other two folders delete while the app runs) failed before the switch and passes after it.

### Scheduled runs (Settings › Schedules; contract `src/shared/schedules.ts`, service `src/main/schedules.ts`)
- Main checks every 30 s (`SCHEDULE_TICK_MS`) and starts stock `omp -p [--model] [--yolo]` in the schedule's folder, only while OMPdesk is open. Times missed while it was closed are skipped; a start due while the previous run still goes is recorded as skipped. Stock omp has no scheduler.
- Each computer keeps and runs its own schedules. A tab on a paired host shows the host's in Settings › Schedules (`SECTION_SCOPE` `host`, "Selected host"): `SCHEDULES_COMMAND`/`SCHEDULES_LOG` take the focused tab's `device` and go through `machineFor` (`MachineServices.schedules`/`scheduleLog`/`createSchedule`; `RemoteMachine` → `host.schedules.command|log|create`), the folder picker browses the host, and the host pushes each change as `host.schedules.changed` (`LocalHostServices.schedulesChanged`), forwarded to the device's windows as `host-schedules:state` (`schedules.onHostState`). Pinned by `SchedulesSection.test.tsx`, `SettingsDeviceScope.test.tsx`, `machine-services.test.ts`, `schedules.test.ts` (concurrent creates).
- A schedule a paired device saves on a host (its Settings or its agent's `ompdesk_schedule`, `host.schedules.command` passes the peer as `origin`) is that device's: `Schedule.device`, cleared when someone saves it on the host itself. Every start of it, timed or run now from the device, takes one of that device's daily model requests through `ScheduleServiceOptions.reserve` (`RemoteDevices.reserveModelRequest` → `PeerQuota`): past the limit a timed start is recorded `skipped` with the reason and omp never launches, and a device's run now fails `remote_limit_reached`; a run that could not start gives the request back. The host's own run now of it is not charged. Pinned by `schedules.test.ts`, `host-services.test.ts`.
- Each run goes through the setup supervisor (`spawnSupervised`, the prompt file as omp's stdin), so a Stop, a quit or a crash kills omp's whole tree.
- Schedules and the last 200 runs (`SCHEDULE_RUNS_KEPT`) live in `<userData>/schedules.json`; run output in `<userData>/schedule-runs/<runId>.log`.

## Remote devices

### Tailscale only, transport, routing
- Code: `src/main/remote/`. OMPdesk on a host (B) runs tabs for client computers (A). **The user's rule (19 Oct 2026): computers share over Tailscale only, with no pairing code** — like Mobile web, any computer signed in to an allowed Tailscale account on the same tailnet sees this computer and can use it. No LAN listener, no UDP discovery, no code field; computers without Tailscale do not share (no fallback).
- Host: `RemoteHost` (`host-server.ts`) listens on this computer's tailnet addresses only (`status --json` `TailscaleIPs`, `bindHosts` in `devices.ts`; never 0.0.0.0, `::` or a LAN address), rebinding every 15 s and on a status change. Port: the first free one of `remotePortCandidates()` (`OMP_GUI_REMOTE_PORT`, default 47821, and its four successors, `REMOTE_PORT_SPAN`), the same port on every address. All five taken: it listens nowhere and `RemoteHostState.listenError` is `{code: "port_in_use", ports}` (Settings says which); no silent fallback, since a device only probes that list.
- Gate, per connection (`RemoteDevices.#gate`): the peer's address must be a tailnet address (100.64.0.0/10, fd7a:115c:a1e0::/48; `isTailnetAddress`) else `not_tailnet`; `tailscale whois` (`Tailnet`, `src/main/tailscale/tailnet.ts`, answers cached 60 s, status 3 s; works whether or not Mobile web is on) must name a node with a login (`no_identity`) on the allowed list (`not_allowed`; Mobile web's `mobileWeb.allowedUsers`, one list for both, default this computer's login) that Mobile web's blocks (`WebBlocks`, by node) do not hold (`blocked`). "Approve new devices on this computer" (Mobile web's pref `mobileWeb.confirmNewDevices`, default off: one switch for phones and computers, `ConfirmNewDevicesRow`, shown in sharing's section while sharing is on and in Mobile web's otherwise) asks through Mobile web's `PairingConfirmations` (`kind: "computer"`, no access tiers) before a new device is recorded (`declined`). Refusals travel as `auth-fail`/probe `error` codes (`REFUSAL_CODES`, `protocol.ts`) and reach the renderer as `remote_<code>`.
- Trust: handshake v2 (`HANDSHAKE_VERSION` 2; a v1 code-pairing peer gets `protocol`). Each side sends its static key; the session keys mix DH(eA,eB) ‖ DH(sA,eB) ‖ DH(eA,sB) and run AES-256-GCM frames over TCP, so only the pinned keys read the link. The host pins TOFU per Tailscale node (`PeerInfo.nodeId`/`login`, `#bindDevice`): a node holds one device and one key; another device id or key from that node, or that device from another node, is `key_mismatch` until the person removes the record (so a new key never earns a fresh PeerQuota record). The client pins the host's key per node (`PairedHost.nodeId`, `connectFirst`); a changed key is `key_mismatch`.
- Client: Settings watches the tailnet's computers (`remote:watch-tailnet`, every 5 s while shown; `TailnetComputer`): each online non-phone node of `status --json` is probed on the candidate ports (`probe` → `probe-ok {deviceId, name}` or the refusal; this computer itself is skipped). Connect (`remote:connect {nodeId}`) pins and links it. A `RemoteClient` dials only what `#hostAddresses` resolves on each attempt: the node's current tailnet address on the port it answered on in this run, then every candidate port. Stored addresses and ports are never dialed (a reboot moves the port; code pairing stored LAN addresses and old ephemeral ports, a real case that left a Mac unable to reconnect).
- **A link that went silent or dropped comes back on a signal, not on a timer** (20 Oct 2026, the Windows PC's report: "disconnected" and slow to return; its log had no line for any link event). A dead path (Wi-Fi off, the peer asleep, a NAT dropping the flow) is otherwise noticed only by the ping timer (`PING_INTERVAL_MS` 10 s, `DEAD_AFTER_MS` 30 s: 29–40 s) and then retried 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 30 s apart. `RemoteClient.wake(trigger)` (`WakeTrigger`: `resume`, `unlock`, `online`, `tailnet`, `focus`) is called by `RemoteDevices.wake` from `index.ts` for powerMonitor `resume` and `unlock-screen`, the `ConnectivityMonitor` going online, and `browser-window-focus`, and by the tailnet poll when a known host's node was offline in the last poll and is online now or Tailscale just became ready (`#wakeReturnedHosts`, `#poll`). Offline or waiting out a backoff: the backoff starts over at 1 s and an attempt runs now (`connect()` still retries now without resetting it). Online: `Link.check(WAKE_CHECK_MS)` pings and drops the link (`dead`) when no frame arrives within 3 s, then the client attempts at once; a frame in time leaves it alone. An attempt already running is the one trying now. `DEAD_AFTER_MS` and the backoff constants stay as the guard for the case no signal covers. Measured on real sockets through a relay that drops for 20 s (`RemoteDevices` on both sides): restored → online 10.0 s before (the 30 s dead timer: offline at 29.0 s, then the 1 s backoff), 3.0 s with the wake at the restore; first `offline` 29.0 s after the cut before, 23.0 s after (the wake's check, when the signal arrives at the restore).
- **A host whose app is restarting is dialed every second, not on a growing backoff** (20 Oct 2026: the Windows host auto-updated 5 times in 24 h, 21 s to app-ready and 26 s to the bridge each time, and the Mac's client sat in 1, 3, 7, 15, 31 s backoff). A dial that ends `ECONNREFUSED`/`ECONNRESET` on every address (`RemoteError.refused`, set in `dial`) means the computer answered and nothing listens: one cheap round trip, never a timeout or a missing route. `#scheduleRetry(refused)` then keeps the delay at `RECONNECT_MIN_MS` and the backoff at its first step, for `REFUSED_RETRY_WINDOW_MS` (60 s, over twice the longest update restart in the log) from the loss of the link; after that, and for any timeout, unknown host or refusal code, the backoff grows as before. The Windows firewall of the host does not turn this into a timeout (read on that PC, not on a Mac client: `Tailscale-In` allows the tailnet address on Private networks and the Tailscale adapter is Private). Measured with the relay refusing for 20 s: restored → online 11.0 s before, 0.2 s after. No quit-time event from the host: a crash, a kill and a shutdown would send none, while a refusal reaches the client whenever the computer is up.
- **Log** (`gui-runtime.jsonl`, source `remote-link`, transitions only apart from the host's `traffic` summary below, never a line per ping, no address, key or token): on the client `down` {deviceId, name, reason: `closed` | `dead` | `protocol` | `local` (`Link.closeReason`) or the failed attempt's code (`unreachable`, `key_mismatch`, … when a launch never got online), wasOnlineMs}, `attempt` {deviceId, n, delayMs, trigger: `start` | `connect` | `backoff` | a wake trigger} (backoff attempts past the 6th, 30 s apart, are not written) and `up` {deviceId, outageMs, attempts, appVersion: the host's build}; on the host `open` {deviceId, ms: accept to authenticated, appVersion: the device's build, slim: the slim frames it reads, `events,progress,autopilot,ends,reads` or `none`} and `closed` {deviceId, reason, ms: how long it was open} per device connection (`RemoteHostOptions.logLink`, `RemoteClientOptions.log`, both from `RemoteDevicesDeps.logLink`). Pinned by `transport.test.ts` ("a link that dropped or went silent"), `wire.test.ts` (`Link.check`, `closeReason`) and `devices.test.ts` ("a host link after the computer wakes").
- **What the link carries is logged, and a harness measures it** (the user's report, 20 Oct 2026: a constant 170–350 KB/s host → Mac while the Mac showed nothing; the samples were taken while this session's own subagents streamed over the same link, so they proved nothing about idle). `remote-link` `traffic` {deviceId, ms, tx, rx, top: JSON text of [{kind, frames, bytes} ×5], events: JSON text of {omp event type: count ×5}} (text because the runtime log keeps only primitive details: until 21 Oct 2026 both were dropped from every line): the host writes it once a minute for each device connection that moved at least `TRAFFIC_LOG_MIN_BYTES` (256 KiB; an idle link sends about 1 KiB a minute in pings) and once more when such a connection closes. `kind` is `tx`/`rx` plus the frame (`evt tab.event events`, `evt tab.event subagentFrame/subagent_progress`, `req tab.command/get_jobs` (a tab command with its RPC command's type: a link moved 3 MB a minute of `tab.command` answers on 21 Oct 2026 and the line could not say which), `res sessions.list` sized under its request's method, `ping`), in wire bytes (frame header, nonce and tag included); `events` counts the omp events inside `events` batches and `eventBytes` weighs each type by its own JSON (a batch's wire bytes are one figure for all its types, so a busy minute could not say whether its bytes were many small updates or a few whole messages). Never a payload, address or key. Code: `Link.onTraffic` (`wire.ts`) feeds a `LinkTraffic` per connection (`link-traffic.ts`, at most 128 kinds), started in `RemoteHost.#auth`. Pinned by `link-traffic.test.ts`, "the host's traffic line" in `transport.test.ts` (a minute passes: one line for a busy link, none for a link that only pinged, one on close inside the minute) and the real-pipeline case of `link-traffic-report.test.ts`.
- **Measured link traffic (20 Oct 2026; `OMP_GUI_LINK_TRAFFIC=1 bunx vitest run src/main/remote/link-traffic-report.test.ts`, idle seconds `OMP_GUI_LINK_TRAFFIC_IDLE_S`)**: two `RemoteDevices` over real loopback sockets, a real `LocalHostServices` and `SidecarManager` over the scripted fake omp `test-traffic-omp.ts` (stock frame shapes and the 150 ms subagent progress coalescing of omp's `task/executor.ts`). The harness counts every `Link.send` itself, independent of the production counter.
  - An idle open tab sends 17.6 B/s (ping and pong only, no `tab.event`, 120 s). A turn waiting 30 s on a silent bash tool sends one 18 KB burst at its start and nothing after (640 B/s over the 30 s). The link is quiet when nothing runs, so streaming was not changed (the requester's rule: only if idle is clearly above near-zero).
  - Running work is what costs. Before `slimEvents` the host forwarded each stock `message_update` whole, twice (`message` and `assistantMessageEvent.partial`; `message-update-slim.ts` slimmed it only on the local pool's way to a window), so a reply of K tokens cost O(K²): 450 tokens (150 thinking + 300 text) 1.88 MB at 324 KB/s, 900 tokens 6.66 MB at 573 KB/s, 1800 tokens 24.9 MB at 1.07 MB/s (twice the tokens, 3.5–3.7 times the bytes). An older device still gets that.
  - A task tool's subagent for 20 s: 21 KB/s with the `off` subscription (the parent's `tool_execution_update` carries every `AgentProgress` snapshot in `details.progress`, about 3 KB each at most every 150 ms), 49 KB/s with `progress` (the same snapshots again as `subagent_progress`), 284 KB/s with `events` (WorkMap open: each `subagent_event` `message_update` carries the subagent's whole message twice). The 170–350 KB/s of the report is that order.
  - **Done for `message_update` (20 Oct 2026, the same harness, `link-events.ts`)**: the device's hello carries `slimEvents: true` (`ClientHello`, `HostConnection.slim.events`; every capability is read from the hello in one place, `slimOf` in `protocol.ts`, a later one counting only with those it builds on); for such a connection the host sends `events` as the local pool sends a window's (`createMessageUpdateSlimmer`: the first update of each message whole, the rest reduced) and each subagent `message_update` through `slimSubagentFrame`, and drops a `usage` that repeats the last one of its message (`createLinkEventEncoder`, one per tab and connection, in `LocalHostServices.#emit`). The device (`RemoteSidecar.handleEvent`, `createLinkEventDecoder`) puts the usage back and rebuilds the text/thinking blocks and the tool call from the deltas, so the pool's per-window slimmer still gives a window that attaches mid-message the snapshot it needs and every window receives what a local tab's would; a whole update (an older host) passes through. **`PROTOCOL_VERSION` stays 1 on purpose**: host and device must agree on it exactly (`protocol` refusal), so a bump would lock every older device out. An older device sends no `slimEvents` and gets whole frames (pinned by `link-traffic-report.test.ts`, which strips the field from the hello); an older host ignores it. Never make the capability required, and never send a slim frame to a connection that did not ask.
  - **After**: 450 tokens 98 KB at 16.7 KB/s (was 1.88 MB at 324 KB/s, 19x less), 900 tokens 193 KB at 16.5 KB/s (was 6.66 MB at 573 KB/s, 35x), 1800 tokens 383 KB at 16.4 KB/s (was 24.9 MB at 1.07 MB/s, 65x): linear in the reply now. Subagent `events` subscription: `subagent_event/message_update` 225.5 → 16.4 KB/s (13.7x); the whole case 284 → 74.7 KB/s (3.8x), because the rest was never `message_update`: `subagent_progress` 27.7 KB/s, the parent's `tool_execution_update` progress copies 19.3 KB/s and one `tab.event` envelope per subagent frame (1,686 in 20 s, 9.1 KB/s). `off` (20.6 KB/s) and `progress` (49.3 KB/s) are unchanged.
  - **The page asks for a brief `get_state`** (21 Oct 2026): v0.52.2's per-command traffic line showed a paired device viewing one host tab got 3 MB a minute of `tab.command/get_state` answers, ~750 KB each: stock's `get_state` carries `systemPrompt` and every tool's wire schema (`dumpTools`), and the page asks for it every 15 s (the liveness probe) and at every turn's re-sync, reading neither. Every renderer `get_state`, and the OMPdesk control tools' reads of a session's state (a paired device's tab answers over the link), sends `brief: true`, and main's local handler (`briefState`, `local/composer.ts`) leaves the two out; a call without it (e2e specs, agent tools) gets the whole state. A host older than this ignores the flag. Pinned by `local/composer.test.ts`.
  - **A transcript page starts at the run it belongs to, not at the last typed prompt** (`transcriptRunStart`, `resources/gui-bridge/transcript-page.ts`): a page may also open right after an assistant message that stopped on anything but a tool call. Goal and Autopilot runs continue with custom messages, never a typed prompt, so the page snapped back to a prompt hours old: one tail page of this repository's Autopilot session was 1754 messages and 4.6 MB on every hydrate and every reconnect of a paired device (21 Oct 2026; 215 messages, 464 KB after). Pinned by `local/sessions.test.ts`. A page read from the end also weighs at most `BACKWARD_PAGE_BUDGET` (256 K JSON characters; `local/sessions.ts`): one heavier is read again with half as many messages, down to 20, and the rest comes as older pages on scroll; a single run longer than the budget comes whole, since the bridge snaps every shorter read back to its start (a read that starts where the last one did ends the loop) (the 200 messages a window asks for were 961 KB on this repository's Autopilot session's tail, sent to a phone on every open, 22 Oct 2026). A page also leaves out each thinking block's `thinkingSignature` (`transcriptPageMessages`; about a fifth of that page, read by stock from the journal, never from a page), and the renderer's live-to-page match ignores it (`message-identity.ts`).
  - **The model catalog leaves stock's `compat` block behind** (22 Oct 2026, the v0.53.0 traffic line: one `get_providers` to a paired device was 1.73 MB, `get_model_roles` 882 KB): `catalogSnapshot` (`resources/gui-bridge/settings.ts`, behind `providers`, `models_catalog` and `model_catalog_update`) sends each model without `compat` (the provider API's request quirks, about 1.9 KB of a 2.5 KB model on OpenAI's catalog; nothing in OMPdesk reads it). `get_model_roles` repeated each role's candidate list (most roles take the same chat models): the RPC client asks with `compact: true`, the bridge's `model_roles` then names each candidate once (`candidateModels`) and each role's candidates as indexes, and the client expands it back (`compactModelRoles`/`expandModelRoles` in `model-role-contracts.ts`; 78 KB → 19 KB on OpenAI's catalog); an older host ignores the flag and its plain answer passes through, an older device never asks. Pinned by `bridge-catalog-stock.test.ts` (installed omp: no model carries `compat`, under 1 KB a model, the fields the windows read stay; the compact roles expand to exactly the plain answer at under a third of its size) and `src/shared/model-roles-wire.test.ts`.
  - **Live frames carry no request bookkeeping** (`withoutFrameBookkeeping`, `src/main/frame-bookkeeping.ts`, applied as the sidecar takes each frame): stock records `inputTransformations`, `requestControls` and `contextSnapshot` on every assistant message, and in a long Anthropic session `inputTransformations` lists every earlier thinking block the API dropped (21 KB of a 26 KB message on 21 Oct 2026). `message_end`, `turn_end`, every message of `agent_end` and a `message_update`'s `partial` leave without them, to each window and over the link; nothing in main or the renderer reads them (transcript pages already drop them). A `turn_end` leaves with its type alone: its `message` and `toolResults` (each already sent by its own `message_end`, `message_start` and `tool_execution_end`) are read by nobody, and over this repository's Autopilot session they added up to about 30 MB per window and per device. A subagent's events (`subagent_event`, read only by its tools surface) leave the same way (`slimSubagentFrame`, `subagent-frames.ts`). Pinned by `frame-bookkeeping.test.ts` and `subagent-frames.test.ts`.
  - **A batch past the frame cap goes as several frames** (21 Oct 2026): an older device gets every `message_update` whole, so a burst of a long reply (an omp catching up, a fast provider) packed 24.9 MB into one 32 ms `events` batch, past the link's 16 MiB `MAX_FRAME_BYTES`; the host dropped it, `agent_end` included, and the device's tab never left "running". `Link.send` throws `FrameTooLargeError` (before sealing a message already past the cap) and the host's `sendTabEvent` halves such a batch until each part fits, in order; only a single event over the cap is still dropped and logged. The traffic report's slim-events tests stream unpaced, which reproduces it (the 1800-token case hung at its timeout without the split) and takes 0.4 s instead of 9.5 s.
  - **Done for progress snapshots and subagent frames (21 Oct 2026, the same harness, `link-events.ts`)**: a second hello capability, `slimProgress: true` (`ClientHello`, `HostConnection.slim.progress`; it counts only with `slimEvents`). It is its own flag because v0.46.0 devices already send `slimEvents` and can read neither a delta nor an array: they keep single, whole frames. For a connection that asked, the host sends a subagent's `AgentProgress` once and then as a delta against the last snapshot of that subagent id, whichever of the two copies brought it: the `subagent_progress` frame (`FieldDelta` `{"~delta": id, set, unset}`, by top-level field; the payload's fields beside the snapshot, where stock repeats the assignment, go the same way) or the parent's task `tool_execution_update` `details.progress` (also a nested subagent's, inside `subagent_event`). The device rebuilds the very frames and updates stock emitted. Encoder and decoder each keep the last snapshot per id for the tab's life (the renderer's roster does the same) and mirror each other because the link is ordered, which holds only if the encoder sees items in wire order: `createLinkEventSender` (one per tab and connection, `LocalHostServices.#emit`/`#sender`) holds the subagent frames of one `BATCH_INTERVAL_MS` (32 ms), encodes them when they leave and sends them as ONE `subagentFrame` tab event whose `arg` is an array; an `events` batch, any other tab event and the sidecar's `beforeResponse` (the pool's own rule for a response) send the frames held back first. A new connection gets a new encoder, which sends every id whole first, and a send that failed (over the frame cap) makes the next start over. A device with no snapshot behind a delta (not in normal running) drops that update or frame, never delivers half of one. The traffic line names the batch `evt tab.event subagentFrame/batch` and counts its frame types in `events`. Never send a delta or an array to a connection that did not ask, and never make the capability required.
