  - **After** (same rig, 20 s, B/s; before measured at `e5800d7b`, after re-measured on the tree rebased onto `eba4b2a1` with the same numbers): subscription `off` 20,589 → 5,545 (3.7x; each update's snapshot is a delta against the previous update); `progress` 48,952 → 7,028 (7.0x; `subagent_progress` 27.6 → 3.2 KB/s, 8.5x, and the parent's `tool_execution_update` 19.2 → 1.7 KB/s, 11.4x, now a reference to the frame's snapshot); `events` 75,106 → 26,554 (2.8x; leaving out the subagent's own `message_update` frames, 16.6 KB/s both times, the rest went 58.5 → 10.0 KB/s, 5.9x, and the 1,685 `tab.event` envelopes of 192 KB became 509 of 60 KB, 3.2x fewer). The streams are unchanged: 450 tokens 98,121 B (16.7 KB/s), 900 tokens 192,940 B (16.5 KB/s), 1800 tokens 382,793 B (16.4 KB/s). What is left of `events` is mostly the subagent's slim `message_update` frames, 16.6 KB/s. The scripted omp re-stamps `currentToolStartMs` and every `recentTools[].endMs` on each snapshot, while stock stamps them once (`task/executor.ts`, at the tool's start and end; read in the source, not measured on a real run), so part of the 0.5 KB a delta frame holds here (roughly 0.3 KB, an estimate from the size of the recent-tool list) is an artifact of the rig and stock's deltas should be smaller.
  - **A flush of subagent frames past the cap is halved like an `events` batch** (`sendTabEvent` splits `events` and `subagentFrame` arrays, in order; `#send` reports whether every part went out): the halves leave in the order they were encoded, so the device's decoder sees every delta after its base. A single frame over the cap is dropped, and the sender then starts a new encoder, so the next snapshot of each subagent goes whole and the device never sits on a delta without a base. Pinned by "tab events past the link's frame cap" in `host-services.test.ts` (a capped connection: 40 frames in several tab events rebuild equal; the frame after an oversized one arrives whole).
  - **An Autopilot status crosses as a delta** (21 Oct 2026; the v0.52.7 host's traffic lines: 10 `autopilot_update` frames a minute of 97 KB each, ~1 MB of the 1.3 MB minutes, because the status carries every cycle of the run and the board's run, ship and landing steps each re-send it). A third hello capability, `slimAutopilot` (with `slimEvents`; `HostConnection.slim.autopilot`): the host's encoder (`createStatusEncoder` in `link-events.ts`, one per tab and connection, reset with the rest after a send that failed) sends a tab's status whole once, then the top-level fields that changed, and of a list (the cycles) only its length and the items at an index that differ (`~items`). The device's decoder rebuilds the status the host's pool delivered; a delta with no status behind it is dropped, never delivered half. Measured on this repository's own run (87 cycles): a new cycle's update 96.8 KB → 1.4 KB, an update that changes one field 61 B. A device without it (v0.52.7 and older) gets whole statuses; never send a delta to a connection that did not ask. Pinned by "an Autopilot run's status" in `link-events.test.ts` and "an Autopilot status on the link" in `host-services.test.ts`.
  - **A message crosses whole once** (22 Oct 2026; v0.53.0's traffic lines on a paired host: `message_start`, `message_end` and `agent_end` were 36 % of a slim link's event bytes, because stock sends a prompt, tool result or custom message in its `message_start` and again in its `message_end`, and every message of a run once more in `agent_end`). A fourth hello capability, `slimEnds` (with `slimEvents`; `HostConnection.slim.ends`): the host's encoder (`createEndsEncoder` in `link-events.ts`, per tab and connection, reset with the rest after a send that failed) remembers each message it sent whole in a `message_start` or `message_end` (key: role, timestamp, tool call id; the newest 256, evicted in the same order on both ends) and sends a `message_end` equal to it, and each `agent_end` message equal to it but for the journal `entryId`, as `{"~sent": key, entryId?}`; both ends forget them at a terminal `agent_end`. A message that differs (the bridge's read-group decoration) goes whole. The device's decoder resolves the references; one it holds nothing for drops the event, never delivers half. Measured on the recorded real run (`e2e/fixtures/real-advisor-frames.json`): every `agent_end` message a reference, the three frame types 51 % smaller (the rest is each `message_start`). A device without it (v0.53.0 and older) gets every message whole; never send a reference to a connection that did not ask. Pinned by "messages a connection already carried" in `link-events.test.ts`.
  - **A reply that read files is a reference too** (22 Oct 2026, measured once both computers ran 0.54.0: `message_end` 56 → 12 KB a minute, `agent_end` only 55 → 38). The bridge adds `__ompReadGroup` to each read call of a committed `agent_end` message (`decorateMessages`, sessions.attach_entry_ids) and never to the live `message_end`, so every reply that read a file crossed whole. A fifth hello capability, `slimReadGroups` (with `slimEnds`): the encoder compares the message without those groups and sends them beside the reference (`{"~sent": key, entryId?, reads: {index: group}}`); the decoder puts each back last among its call's arguments. A 0.54.0 device parses a reference strictly (a `reads` key would make it a message), so it gets such replies whole; never send `reads` to a connection that did not ask. Pinned by "a committed reply whose read calls the bridge decorated" in `link-events.test.ts`.
  - **A tool result crosses once** (22 Oct 2026; v0.54.0's traffic lines on this Mac as host: `message_start` 18–95 KB a minute beside `tool_execution_end` 22–85 KB, because stock builds a tool result's `tool_execution_end` and its `toolResult` message from the same result, `content` and `details` included, `emitToolResult` in pi-agent-core's `agent-loop.ts`). A fifth hello capability, `slimToolResults` (with `slimEnds`; `LinkSlim.toolResults`, log name `tools`): the host's encoder (`createToolResultEncoder` in `link-events.ts`, per tab and connection) remembers each call's end (the newest 256, both ends forget them at a terminal `agent_end`) and sends a `toolResult` `message_start` whose `content` and `details` equal it as `{"~tool": toolCallId, ...the rest}`; its `message_end` was already a `~sent` reference. The device's decoder puts them back; a reference to a call it never saw end drops the event. A device without it (v0.54.0 and older) gets every result twice. Pinned by "a tool result its call's end already carried" in `link-events.test.ts` and, over the real pipeline, by `link-traffic-report.test.ts` (the scripted omp's tool call ends in stock's order: a 7.6 KB output's result message crosses as a reference, 9.3 KB of `message_start` for an older device; the renderer gets the same stream either way).
  - Pinned by `link-events.test.ts` (encode, JSON, decode gives a window that watched from the start, and one that attaches at any event, what a local tab's would; two messages in a row; whole updates pass through; a subagent frame slims to itself), `link-traffic-report.test.ts` (two runs of the same scripted reply, one as an older device: at most a tenth of the bytes for 450, 900 and 1800 tokens and for a subagent's `message_update` frames, whole `message_update`s for the older device, the same renderer view for both) and `e2e/remote-stream.e2e.ts` (a host's tab and a local tab each switched away from after line 10 of a streamed reply and back after line 20: same rows live and finished; with the decoder's snapshot rebuild removed it fails on the missing lines 11 to 20).
  - Pinned for progress by `link-events.test.ts` ("a subagent's progress snapshots": two parallel subagents, frames and task updates interleaved as stock reports them, a grandchild's task update included; the rebuilt frames and updates equal the raw ones, at most a fifth of the bytes (10.5x measured), an update after its frame's snapshot adds almost nothing, whole for a device that did not ask, rebuilt by a decoder that kept an earlier link's snapshots, a delta with no snapshot behind it dropped, an older host's whole snapshots passed through untouched; "a connection's event sender": one tab event a flush in order, frames before an events batch, `flush`, each frame alone for a device without `slimProgress`, starting over after a send that failed, `dispose`), `link-traffic.test.ts` (a batched tab event is one kind, its frame types counted) and `link-traffic-report.test.ts` ("a device that asks for slim progress": the rig as a v0.46.0 device, as the current one and as one older still; bytes a snapshot at most a half with the subscription off, a third with `progress`, a third for everything but the subagent's own messages with `events`; one tab event for several frames; the device's pool gets the progress frames, lifecycle frames and task updates the host's omp emitted, equal to them, tapped on the host's sidecar; an older device gets one tab event a frame and whole snapshots). The rig counts a device by what its hello says (`DeviceKind`).
- Migration from code pairing (`remote.tailnetMigratedAt`, once Tailscale is up): a host record without a node is matched to a node by, in order, a stored address among the node's `TailscaleIPs`, a stored address among its endpoints (`CurAddr`/`Addrs`, where Tailscale reports the LAN address code pairing stored), its announced name equal to the node's `HostName` (case aside). A match is kept with its stored addresses cleared and verified at first contact (the node pinned, a different host key refused `key_mismatch`); the rest are forgotten and named once (`RemoteState.forgotten`, pref `remote.forgottenHosts`, Settings notice). Until then an unmigrated record dials nothing. A host-side device record without a node binds on its first contact through the gate with its old key and keeps its limits; one not seen within 30 days is removed. Pinned by `devices.test.ts` "keeps a host code pairing stored by its LAN address and old ports".
- Keys and records live in `<userData>/remote-*.json` (`remote-identity.json`, `remote-peers.json`, `remote-hosts.json`; mode 0600, no safeStorage).
- The pool holds a `SidecarLike` per tab. A `RemoteSidecar` (A) proxies `tab.*` requests to B's `LocalHostServices`, which runs real SidecarManagers from `sidecar-factory.ts` and forwards their events.
- `ipc.ts` routes every handler that touches the omp machine (sessions, fs reads, launch profiles, project switches) by the tab's `device`. F-OWN keys are device + path.
- Settings, Usage and Stats follow the focused tab's `device`. Tab RPC already reaches the host. models.yml providers, the stats server, `omp bench` and scheduled runs go through `machine-services.ts` (`LocalMachine` here, `host.*` calls on B). App prefs, the updater and remote-devices stay this device's (`SECTION_SCOPE`, `components/remote/HostScope.tsx`).
- Host tool calls (`gui_open_url`, notify, clipboard) always run on A.
- Settings › Devices & connections (`RemoteSection.tsx`): Tailscale missing/stopped/signed out → one row with Install/Open Tailscale (Mobile web's own steps); the share switch; device name; keep-awake; tailnet addresses or the busy-ports line; approve new devices; devices using this computer (Limits, Rename, Remove, Block = block its node, shared with Mobile web; a connected one's line names the OMPdesk version its hello reported, `PeerView.appVersion`, as a paired host's row does, `HostView.appVersion`; read against this computer's `RemoteState.appVersion` as older or newer, `remote.versionOlder`/`remote.versionNewer`); allowed accounts and blocked devices (Mobile web's components); other devices; computers on the tailnet with Connect.
- Tests: `transport.test.ts` (gate codes, TOFU per node, key changes, ports), `devices.test.ts` and `devices-rename.test.ts` on `test-devices.ts` (two RemoteDevices with fake Tailscale on 127.0.0.1), `tailnet.test.ts`; e2e on `e2e/remote-harness.ts` (fake Tailscale per app; `OMP_GUI_TEST_REMOTE_LOOPBACK=1`, unpackaged only, lets 127.0.0.1 stand in for the tailnet), `remote-devices.e2e.ts` (connect with no code; not-allowed, block, next port, migration).

### Memory between computers
- Durable memory pages and session conclusions cross every remote link with no setting (`host.memory.pull`/`push`, `host.memory.conclusions.pull`/`push`, see Memory (ai-memory) › Shared between paired computers). The device that connected drives both directions; a blocked or revoked device gets nothing; a host with memory off answers `{memory: false}`.

### Device names
- **One name per device, the one it announces** (the user's rule, 19 Oct 2026; never a per-computer alias). This computer's is pref `remote.deviceName` (default its Tailscale node name, else the OS hostname; the OS computer name is never changed). Settings › Devices & connections shows the field always, sharing on or off (it was hidden while sharing was off, so a device that only connects to others could not rename itself).
- Renamable at any time from either side (`RemoteDevices`): `setDeviceName` saves and announces live, to every connected device as the event `host.name` (`RemoteHost.announceName`) and to every host this computer is connected to as the request `peer.name` (served by `RemoteHost` itself, it updates `remote-peers.json`); offline ones learn it from the next handshake (`auth-init`/`auth-resp` and the hello carry it). A probe answers the current name, so the tailnet list shows it.
- A host row's Rename (`REMOTE_RENAME_HOST` → request `host.rename`, served by `RemoteHost`, logged on the host) makes the host rename itself; a paired-device row's Rename (`REMOTE_RENAME_PEER`) sends that device the event `device.rename` and it renames itself; refused `remote_offline` while it is not connected (the button is disabled with the reason). Names are 1–64 characters (`DeviceNameSchema` in `app-protocol.ts`).
- A phone-page device's name is this computer's record of it (`devices-store.ts` `rename`, `WEB_DEVICES_RENAME`); Tailscale's node name is only the default.
- Agents rename through `ompdesk_app` `remote.devices` (read), `remote.rename_this_computer`, `remote.rename_host`, `remote.rename_device`, `mobile_web.rename_device` (change); `ompdesk_sessions` `device` resolves by id or current name.
- Inline field: `settings/DeviceNameField.tsx`, shared by the remote and phone-page rows. Pinned by `remote/devices-rename.test.ts` (two `RemoteDevices` over real sockets), `RemoteSection.test.tsx`, `e2e/device-rename.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_DEVICE_RENAME=<dir>` + `_THEME` captures in Korean).

### Per-device model limit
- A paired device's per-day model limit (`remote/peer-quota.ts`, prefs on the peer in `remote-peers.json`) covers every RPC command that calls a model on the host, plus `host.bench.run` (models × runs requests), `host.modelSetup.run` and every start of a schedule the device saved there (timed or run now; see Scheduled runs).
- `remote/quota-commands.ts` classifies **every** `RpcCommand` type as model, conditional (`switch_leaf` with `summarize`; `plan_approval` that runs or sends feedback; `set_goal` with an objective or `resume`) or free. A compile-time check and `quota-commands.test.ts` fail when a new command type is in none of the lists, and a type no list knows counts.
- `LocalHostServices` reserves the request (`PeerQuota.reserve`, no await between the check and the count; a weighted reservation that would take the day past its limit is refused whole) and refunds it when the work throws or fails.
- Terminals (`terminal.request`) and the agent's `bash`/`eval` stay outside the limit.

### Link loss: a tab never loses the user's answer
- `RemoteSidecar.sendSideChannel` throws `remote_offline` for an `extension_ui_response` while the link is down. Otherwise it returns its delivery (sent once a pending `tab.open` re-attach finished), which rejects with `remote_offline` when the link drops under it or with the host's error when the host refuses it.
- `routeSideChannel` and `EXTENSION_UI_RESPOND` await it, so the pool keeps the request route, the IPC call rejects, and the card or sign-in prompt stays open with an error toast.
- Replies to the host's tool/URI calls are queued and sent once the host tab re-attaches. A `tab.close` that could not be sent goes to `PendingTabCloses` (kept per host link in `devices.ts`, sent when the link is back).
- A remote tab's own link statuses (`error` on link loss, `starting` on reconnect; `statusFromLink`) never drop its open asks in the pool, so a reload still gets the card back. A status the host reports (its omp exited or restarted, or its refusal of the re-attach's `tab.open`/`tab.start` over a live link) does.
- Re-attach brings what changed while the link was down. Every `tab.open` of a tab the host still holds re-sends the asks its omp has open (the `openAsks` tab event; `LocalHostServices` keeps each ask's body until the device answers it or omp withdraws it). The pool replaces the tab's open set with that list:
  - a card whose ask is gone (omp restarted or withdrew it) closes through a synthesized `cancel`;
  - each open ask is forwarded again, since the reconnect's `starting` cleared the page (`pushTabExtensionUiRequest` never doubles a card still up);
  - an ask the tab already held goes out marked `resent` (`IpcExtensionUiPayload`), so the OS "needs you" notification is not repeated; an ask raised while the link was down notifies as new;
  - an ask whose answer is still on its way (`routeSideChannel`) is left as it is.
- **Host tool calls and URI reads the hosted omp waits on come back too** (21 Oct 2026, the v0.49.0 → v0.50.0 update restart: two agent-opened children on paired hosts called `ompdesk_report` 3–7 s after their device quit; the call reached no connection, nothing ever answered it, and stock's `RpcHostToolBridge` has no timeout, so both turns waited for good with their omps alive). `LocalHostServices` keeps each `hostToolCall`/`hostUriRequest` (`HostTab.hostCalls`) until the device's `host_tool_result`/`host_uri_result`, the omp's `host_tool_cancel`/`host_uri_cancel` frame, or a non-ready status (the process took them). A `tab.open` re-sends the ones no connection was handed; a relaunched device's (it adopts the tab under a new rtab, `#adopt`) gets every one, since its last process took the calls it held. A call a live device already has is not sent twice on a lost link, so no tool runs twice. Pinned by `host-services.test.ts` ("re-sends the host tool calls").
  - **A re-sent control call runs at most once.** The relaunched device may have carried a call out before it died (a session opened, a schedule saved) and never answered it. Every control-tool call that changes something (`mutates` in `agent-control/service.ts`: open, send, answer, stop, merge, `ompdesk_app`, schedule other than list, Autopilot other than status) is recorded in `<userData>/agent-control-calls.json` (`call-journal.ts`, atomic) under the caller's device, session file and the host call's id (stable across a relaunch, unlike tab ids): `begin` before the effect, `note` for each tab an open makes as it opens, `finish` with the result, `acknowledge` once the host took the result (a paired host's tab: `RemoteSidecar` emits `hostReplyDelivered` when `tab.sideChannel` returned, wired as `SidecarPool.hostReplyDelivered`; a local omp's at once, it never re-sends). Only acknowledged records are forgotten (after 3 days, then oldest past 500): a host waits on a call with no time limit and may re-send it days later, so a pending or unacknowledged one stays. The same call made again gets the recorded result. One left pending by a crash is reconciled, never run again (`#reconcile`): an open answers the sessions it made (by the noted tab ids through `formerIds`, else the caller's child opened for the brief's task), a schedule create answers the `[agent]` schedule of that name, and anything not found answers "its outcome is unknown after OMPdesk restarted" as an error. **Fail closed**: the host marks a call it re-sends after an earlier connection was handed it (`HostToolCallRequest.resent`; a call made while the device was away is new to it and unmarked), and a marked call this computer has no record of (forgotten, a new profile) is reconciled the same way instead of run. An older host marks nothing, so its re-sends count as new. Pinned by the relaunch cases of `service.test.ts` (died between the effect and the result: one session, one schedule; died before the effect: unknown, nothing opened; unacknowledged kept past 3 days and 500 newer calls; a re-sent call with no record found or unknown, never run) and "re-sends the host tool calls" in `host-services.test.ts` (only a call handed before is marked).
- `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts` cuts the link through a relay (`linkProxy` in `remote-harness.ts`) to cover both: the card comes back, and after the host's omp restarted none stays, not even after a reload.
- **A re-attach keeps the page** (the user's report, 20 Oct 2026: a long session went blank and loaded again from scratch each time a paired computer's link came back). Every host status names the omp process it comes from (`TabStatusArg.omp`, a new id each time the host's tab starts or restarts one, `HostTab.omp`). When the link is back, `RemoteSidecar` reports `starting` with `reconnecting: true` while it has seen an omp; the page (`use-rpc-events.ts` `handleStatus`) then keeps transcript rows (the same elements), draft, cards and state, and on the host's re-sent `ready` runs one `hydrateTabSession` (the web page's resync merge: state, transcript tail, queue, subagents, goal, plan; asks come back through `openAsks`) instead of the boot check plus a fresh load. A host that no longer held the tab (`attached: false`), re-sends another omp id, or names none (an older host) makes `RemoteSidecar` report a plain `starting` first, so the page starts over as for any new omp. The catch-up test of `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts` (100 ms each way through `linkProxy.setLatency`, 600-message session, a reply that finishes while the link is down) measured 0 ms blank and no row mounted again, against ~200 ms blank and every row mounted again before; link back → reply shown stays at the two round trips a re-open needs (~420 ms). The answer a reader wheeled up to stays within 2 px (it moved 23 px): the device's first prompt in a session the host typed makes every earlier prompt read "from <host>" (two devices now, `labelPromptOrigins`), the re-read adds that line to the prompts on screen, and TanStack compensates only rows entirely above the fold; so a commit that changes rows in view while the reader is away from the live edge holds the topmost unchanged row fully in view until those rows are measured (`content-anchor.ts`; ChatStream's measurement microtask and hold loop; rows compared by content, since a rebuild hands a split reply's rows new objects; a gesture lets go). Pinned by "a re-attach after a lost link" in `remote-sidecar.test.ts`, the omp-id case of `host-services.test.ts`, the re-attach case of `use-rpc-events.test.tsx` and `content-anchor.test.ts`.
- **A disconnect notice waits out a grace** (the user's report on the Windows host, 20 Oct 2026: the desktop said "disconnected" and seemed slow to recover; the log held no link outage at all, the notice showed on every app start, window reload and sub-second blip). One rule, one constant, `LINK_NOTICE_GRACE_MS` = 2.5 s (`src/shared/link-notice.ts`): a surface that tells the person the link to the computer is down shows only once it has stayed down that long, never before the device list has loaded, and it stays for as long as the outage does. 2.5 s is the web page's first retry (500 ms + 25 % jitter) or the desktop client's (`RECONNECT_MIN_MS` 1 s) plus the dial and handshake that follows it, so one routine drop (a phone page returning from the background, back in about 0.5 s) says nothing. Surfaces: the pane's `RemoteTabBanner` ("Reconnecting to {name}…", `useHostLinkLost` in `stores/remote.ts`, counted from when this window first saw the host not online, `downSince` set by `apply`; a tab opened in a long outage shows it at once) and the phone's bar (`web/connection-banner.ts`, `web.connection.lost` after the grace, `web.connection.connecting` after the same grace). A removed host (`remote.tab.removed`) and an unpaired phone (`web.connection.unpaired`) are final facts and show at once. **Left as they were, on purpose:** the title bar's status "Reconnecting" (`TitleBar`, a hollow `pending` dot: it is the tab's own state, which would otherwise read as the session's red `error` while the link is down; it only waits for the device list now), the device chip's dot and screen-reader state, the sidebar's "Offline" row and Settings' `HostScoped` notice (each replaces content that cannot be shown while the host is away, so they must follow the link).
- Pinned by `DeviceChip.test.tsx` (banner absent before the list loads, for a drop back inside the grace, present and staying after it, grace counted through offline → connecting, shown at once on a late-opened tab, removed host at once; chip waits for the list) and `web/page-chrome.test.ts` (bar absent for a reconnect inside the grace, present after it).

### A host tab never waits forever to come up
- Reported on v0.40.0: a phone opening a session of a paired Windows host showed the transcript skeleton forever, no toast, nothing logged. Whatever the host does, a tab on it now either comes up or says why.
- **Deadline**: every bring-up (`RemoteSidecar.#bringUp`: start, restart, the link coming back) has `REMOTE_START_TIMEOUT_MS` (60 s; env `OMP_GUI_REMOTE_START_TIMEOUT_MS` for tests) to see the host report the tab running. Past it a tab still `starting` (the link never connecting, `tab.open`/`tab.start` unanswered, or the host's omp never ready) turns `error` with `remote.tabStalled` ("{device} has not started this session after {seconds} s. Restart to try again."), shown by `SidecarBanner` with Restart; a link request that times out (`tab.open`'s 30 s) says the same instead of the raw timeout. It stays a link-derived status (`statusFromLink`), so open asks keep their routes, and a host that reports ready later still brings the tab up.
- **A tap on ☰ right after a phone page loads opens the drawer** (the "host never starts" e2e failed 3 of 10 under a locked parallel run with the drawer never showing; instrumented close reasons named two): the first tab list moving `activeTabId` from none to a tab closed it through `SidebarDrawer`'s leave-the-list rule, now `leftActiveSession` (a pick from one listed, non-ghost tab to another only); and the app surface's wrapper changed type with the first tab (`activeTabId ? <TabRuntimeProvider> : surface`), remounting the whole app, whose `useDrawerSidebarSync` put the drawer away again. `App.tsx` wraps the surface in `FocusedRuntimeProvider` (one element type, null runtime until the first tab). Pinned by `SidebarDrawer.test.ts` and `tab-runtime-provider.test.tsx`; 30 of 30 under the same load after.
- **Restart keeps the session**: `SIDECAR_RESTART` restarts on the session file the caller names, else on the tab's own (`SidecarPool.tabTarget().sessionFile`), never a fresh session; the banner's Restart names none. Before this, a host tab whose omp never reported its session forgot the stored session on Restart (`RemoteSidecar.restart` reset its resume path) and the host later started an empty one (the "host never starts" e2e failed whenever its Restart click landed: 7 of 15 runs under load, 3 of 3 with the click made certain).
- **Log**: each step goes to `gui-runtime.jsonl` as source `remote-tab` (`open` {attached, viewing}, `start`, `ready`, `stalled` {step: link | open | start | ready}, `failed` {step, error}), with the device, rtab and `ms` since the bring-up began; Copy diagnostics carries it.
- **The page never waits on a lost reply**: `WsConnection.#invoke` answers a result JSON cannot carry with an error `res` (it was dropped, and the page's call waited forever). A first transcript read that fails while nothing is loaded sets `pageError` (`commitHydration`): the transcript shows the failure with Retry (a fresh hydration), never an empty session's starters.
- Pinned by `remote-sidecar.test.ts` ("a start the host never finishes"), `ws-router.test.ts`, `use-rpc-events.test.tsx` ("a failed first transcript read") and `e2e/mobile-remote-session.e2e.ts` (phone → this computer → host with Windows-shaped paths: stored, large, link drop, agent-opened, desktop-held, host-held, asleep on a relaunched host, after this computer relaunches; and a host whose omp never starts: banner naming it, `remote-tab` log lines, up once the host can). `OMP_GUI_REMOTE_HOST_OUT=<built out/>` runs that host on another build (version skew; passes against v0.39.0).

### Host windows see the device's tabs
- **The user's rule (19 Oct 2026): the person at the host sees and can work in every session running on that computer, including one a paired device started** (e.g. with `ompdesk_session_open {device}`).
- Why: a hosted tab lives in `LocalHostServices`, not in the host's pool, so the host's sidebar had no tab for its session, and an agent-opened session (its first prompt an `<ompdesk-agent-message>` brief whose opener is on the device) folded away as a finished helper (`foldedRootOf` → `familyChildFor` `finished` → `isShownOpened` false); opening it spawned or switched a second omp onto the file.
- List: `LocalHostServices.hostedSessions()` (`IpcHostedSession`: path, id, title, cwd, kind, status with `running`, `awaitingInput`, the device's name; `onHostedSessionsChange` fires only on a real change) is `HOSTED_SESSIONS`, pushed as `HOSTED_SESSIONS_CHANGED` to every window (renderer `stores/hosted-sessions.ts`, synced in App, re-read on a web page's resync). The sidebar counts each as held by a tab (never folded, never archived out, its project expanded), gives it a live row before the index lists it, and takes its signal (running, needs input) from that state.
- Lanes and removed worktrees (the user's report, 20 Oct 2026: sessions the Mac opened on a Windows host were "missing" there and the Mac listed host rows the host did not show). One lane rule for both sides, `inChatLane` (`lib/session-title.ts`): a chat, or a session in that machine's no-project workspace (`~/.omp/work`, where `ompdesk_session_open {device}` without a folder starts one), sits in the Chat lane. A paired host's section and a focused tab on that host ask the host's own workspace (`useHostWorkspace` in `layout/sidebar/host-workspace.ts`: `sidecar.defaultWorkspace(device)` → `workspace.default`, its last answer kept in the snapshot cache), so a row never sits in Code on the device and in Chat on the host, and focusing such a tab opens the Chat lane where its row is. A session whose worktree was removed leaves the list unless a tab holds it, a paired device's tab here included (`heldByTab`). Pinned by `Sidebar.test.tsx` and `e2e/remote-host-lanes.e2e.ts`.
- Open: opening it views the device's tab. `spawnTabForWindow` (dep `hosted`) asks `viewSession(path)` for a `HostedTabView` (`remote/hosted-view.ts`, a `SidecarLike` over the hosted sidecar) and `SidecarPool.acquireHostedView` makes it the window's tab (`PoolEntry.hostedView`: listed `shared`, so the host decides steer/follow-up; owns the session for F-OWN). It is never in `resumeFacts`, `tabInventory`, the saved layout, `workspaceDirs`, `restartOnNewOmp` or `applyExtensionUpdate`, which are the device tab's. `GET_SESSION_OWNER` answers `{hosted: true}` and `switch_session` refuses `session_owned_elsewhere {hosted: true}`, both routed by the renderer to a tab (`routeToSessionOwner`).
- The view forwards the omp's stream but never `hostToolCall`/`hostUriRequest` (the device answers those), journals a prompt typed here as the host's and relabels a transcript page for the host (`labelForHost`). An ask's first answer, the device's or the window's, wins (`#answer`: the other side gets a `cancel` `answered:<id>`, which the pool marks `answeredElsewhere`, and its later answer fails `already_answered`).
- Lifetime: closing the view only leaves (`CLOSE_TAB` releases the entry; the omp runs on); a viewed tab outlives the device's grace period; the device's close, delete, revoke or the grace's end ends every view first (`VIEW_ENDED_EVENT`: the pool releases the entry and the window's list drops the shared tab, no error tab). A host-side delete of a session a device's tab runs is refused (`session_running`) only while that tab runs a turn or compacts; an idle one closes and its device hears `sessionDeleted` (see Interrupted turns › The host owns the turns).
- Tests: `host-services.test.ts` ("for this computer's own windows"), the hosted-view cases of `sidecar-pool.test.ts` and `tab-spawn.test.ts`, `Sidebar.test.tsx`, and `e2e/remote-host-view.e2e.ts` (device opens a session on the host → the host lists it once in its project → opens it as a view with no second omp launched → a prompt from each side reaches the one omp and shows on both → the device closes its tab → the host's view ends with no error tab).

### Devices see the host windows' tabs
- **The other way round (the user's duplicate-tabs bug, 19 Oct 2026): a device opening a session a window of the host already has open never gets a second omp.**
- Attach: `LocalHostServices.#open` asks `localTabs.viewForDevice(path, {deviceId, name}, hostName)` first; `SidecarPool.viewForDevice` hands out a `HostedTabView` of that window's tab (`PoolEntry.deviceViews`; the same view class, its `promptOrigin` the device, its transcript localized for it with `localizePromptOrigins`). The host tab holds it as its sidecar (`HostTab.viewsWindow`: never in `resumeFacts`, `hostedSessions`, `restartOnNewOmp` or `applyExtensionUpdate`; the window's open asks reach the device as `openAsks`).
- Device side: `tab.open` answers `{attached: true, viewing: true}` (`TabOpenResult.viewing`), so the device starts nothing and its `RemoteSidecar` reports `shared` (event `shared` → the pool re-lists the tab as shared: host decides steer/follow-up, closing it only leaves).
- Asks: the window's tab counts the device in `viewerCount`; the first answer, the device's (`#answerFromDevice`) or a window's (`routeSideChannel`), wins with the same `answered:<id>` cancel and `already_answered` refusal.
- Lifetime: the device's close only leaves; the window's close ends every device view first (`#releaseEntry` → `VIEW_ENDED_EVENT` → tab event `viewEnded` → the device's pool releases its tab quietly). The host's own delete of that session tells the device `sessionDeleted` first and closes the view, refused only while the window's tab runs a turn (see Interrupted turns › The host owns the turns); the device's delete is refused while a window here runs it live.
- Tests: the "device views" cases of `sidecar-pool.test.ts`, "a device opening a session a window of this computer runs" in `host-services.test.ts`, and the second half of `e2e/remote-host-view.e2e.ts` (host opens first → device opens it, no new omp launched → prompts from both reach the one omp and show on both → the host closes → the device's view ends, no error tab).

## Interrupted work, quit and diagnostics

### Interrupted turns continue by themselves
- `src/main/turn-resume.ts`, `src/main/connectivity.ts`. Settings › General › Continue interrupted work after relaunch, pref `resumeInterrupted`, **default on** (main reads `!== false`).
- **Two paths, one coordinator (`TurnResume`), driven by `SidecarPool.onRunningChange` (`resumeFacts()`).**
- **① Relaunch.** Eligibility is a marker file `<userData>/interrupted-turns.json` ({window, tab, peer, sessionPath, cwd, kind, startedAt, reason}) rewritten atomically on every run-state transition. NEVER write it in a quit hook: SIGKILL/crash runs none. On Windows the rename over the file fails (`EPERM`) while any process has it open for reading (a poll, a virus scan, a backup), and a running turn has no later transition that writes it again: `InterruptedTurnStore` writes through `writeFileAtomicDeferred` (one attempt now, the retries on a timer) and logs `markers not saved` (source `turn-resume`) only when every try failed. Measured: a second process reading the file in a loop failed 2918 of 4000 renames in a bare Node loop, and 11 of 16 e2e runs lost the `running` marker (0 of 16 with the retry).
  - Present while a turn runs (`running`); cleared on normal completion and on the user's Stop (`SidecarPool.noteStopped`, called by the RPC dispatcher for `STOP_COMMANDS` `abort`/`abort_and_prompt`/`abort_and_restore_queue`, so a kill before the run's end event leaves none); kept as `asking` while blocked on an ask/approval (the relaunch re-runs the cut-off call, the card reopens; not "continuing"). An omp that exits or restarts takes its open asks with it (the pool forgets owners on any non-ready status).
  - A quit (`quitApproved()`) freezes writes: tearing tabs down must not read as turns ending. Closing the last shown window on Windows with close-to-tray off is such a quit, asked for before the window goes (`lastWindowCloseAction`, `quit-guard.ts`).
  - A marker whose session the user deleted (`deletedSessions()` tombstone) is dropped at launch, never continued, not even into a fresh tab. A never-written session has no tombstone and still continues.
  - `TurnResume.launch()` (after saved windows exist): each marker finds its tab by session path (deferred tab: `SidecarPool.startTab`; missing from the layout: opened) and continues through stock: `restore_interrupted` (local handler → bridge `composer.restore_interrupted` → stock `restoreSession`, what `omp --restore` runs) for a cut-off turn, `retry` (`/retry`, the quota-resume path) for a failed one. A killed OMPdesk's omp usually closes the turn itself on stdin EOF (pending ask fails "RPC client disconnected before extension UI response completed", run journaled aborted), so `restoreSession` finds nothing; the relaunch then runs stock `retry`, which replays the failed tool batch or aborted reply and does nothing for a turn that really ended.
  - **A turn cut before its next model call** (v0.41.0 update restart: `restored: nothing, retried: false`): stock records a closed stdin as a `normal` `session_exit` with `pendingToolCalls` only while a tool runs, and the abort record that would follow never reaches the journal when the cut lands before the model call (the agent loop stops there with no message) or omp's graceful EPIPE quit ends the process first. Stock's `findUnfinishedTurn` then reads the turn as ended and `/retry` finds no failed reply. The bridge's restore (only ever called for a marker of a running turn) checks `requestCutAtExit` (`restart-core.ts`: the replaced process's clean exit record, nothing after it, a prompt or tool result before it that nothing answered) and appends stock's own interrupted-turn record (`session/exit-diagnostics` `createInterruptedTurnAbortMessage` over the branch without that exit) before stock `restoreSession`, which then continues from the recorded tail: no tool reruns, no prompt sent twice. A prompt stock journals only as its process ends lands after the exit record and needs nothing. Pinned by `bridge-restart.test.ts`, `src/main/bridge-restore-request-stock.test.ts` (installed omp: a `context` handler holds the request preparation) and `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts` (the fake omp records stock's exit record on stdin EOF).
  - **An aborted turn with a note after it** (v0.48.0 → v0.49.0 update restart, 21 Oct 2026: two running sessions logged `restored: nothing, retried: false, journal "assistant/aborted, …"` and sat idle): the quit aborted them mid-tool and stock journaled the aborted result and reply, then a custom message landed after the reply (a report the bridge handed the session once the abort left it idle; a follow-up stock had queued since an earlier relaunch, journaled at the abort with its old timestamp). Stock's `retry` reads the conversation's end past eval snapshots only (`turn-recovery.ts` `conversationEnd`), so the note hid the aborted reply. The bridge's restore (`turnCutBehindNotes` in `restart-core.ts`: last message an aborted or failed reply of the replaced process, a non-eval custom message after it, nothing typed since) continues it with the relaunch's one follow-up (`ompdesk-restart-turn`, or folded into the jobs/subagents one; details `turn: true`; the transcript's muted line "Continuing the work the restart cut off", `data-restart-turn`) and reports `followUp`/`turnCut`; `TurnResume` counts `followUp` as continuing and sends no `retry` on top (log `restored: "turn-behind-notes"`). It reads the journal only, so state v0.49.0 wrote at its quit is continued too. Pinned by `bridge-restart.test.ts`, `turn-resume.test.ts`, `MessageBubble.test.tsx` and the note case of `bridge-restore-request-stock.test.ts` (installed omp: a bash cut by the quit, an `ompdesk.mail` line appended as v0.49.0 wrote it, the relaunch continues).
  - A continuation waits for its tab's omp (`RESUME_READY_TIMEOUT_MS`, 5 min; an omp that already failed drops the marker at once). The resuming row's Stop, the waiting row's Cancel and a new prompt on a held tab call it off even during the wait (a per-tab generation checked before every command).
  - `restore_interrupted` and `retry` get `RESUME_COMMAND_TIMEOUT_MS` (2 min), not the RPC client's 8 s default: stock's restore reads every subagent transcript first (v0.37.0, 577 transcripts: reply cut, logged `failed`, stock kept reviving).
  - No toast: each resuming tab shows its own calm row ("Continuing where it left off…", `data-resuming`, one per tab, `setResuming` → tab `resuming` → `TurnStatusRow`; not for a reopened card) with the Stop on it (`resume.stop(tabId)` → `TurnResume.stopResumed(tabId)`: that tab only, noted as the user's Stop so its marker goes and Autopilot's goal resume ends `cancelled`). The only resume indicator.
  - Rank order: focused tab, other panes of its split, then background tabs (`ResumePool.resumeRank` ← `SidecarPool.resumeRank`).
  - Setting on: a quit (⌘Q, tray, last window on Windows) and "Restart to update" ask nothing (`quitNeedsConfirmation(risk, resumes)` in `quit-guard.ts`, `confirmQuit()` in `app-quit.ts`, the updater's `UPDATER_APPLY`); off, they ask before stopping running work.
- **One conversation stays one session across a relaunch**: a tab whose session file exists starts on it (`--session`), the continuation answers into that file, the sidebar lists it once. Pinned by `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts` (no second file, one indexed row, after kill and normal quit), `e2e/real-update-restart.e2e.ts` (a)/(a2) and the SIGKILL test of `e2e/real-resume.e2e.ts`. Two rows once seen in the README resume clip were the fake omp writing a second journal (see the fake omp under Build, test), never OMPdesk.
- **The host owns the turns it runs for a paired device.**
  - On the device (A) a remote tab (`device`) is skipped entirely: no marker, no judging, no takeover by A's connectivity.
  - On the host (B) `index.ts` composes `TurnResume`'s pool from `SidecarPool` and `LocalHostServices` (`resumeFacts`/`startTab`/`setAwaitingConnection`/`noteStopped`/`tabForSession`, ids `peer:<deviceId>:<rtab>` kept when re-keyed; run bookkeeping via `trackRunEvent` in `sidecar-run-state.ts`; asks from the tab's `extensionUi` and the device's `extension_ui_response`; the device's Stop through `tab.command`). B writes the marker (with `peer`), continues after its own crash or quit (the marker revives the hosted tab, unstarted, waiting for its device in the grace period: `RemoteDevices.reviveHostTab`, only while hosting is on) and judges network failure by B's connectivity.
  - Grace (`HOST_TAB_GRACE_MS`, 10 min from link loss, `LocalHostServices.#startGrace`) closes only an idle hosted tab; one that runs out while the tab runs a turn (revived included) or holds it for B's connection starts another. A turn blocked on an ask waiting for the device keeps its omp until the device answers or B quits; no overall cap.
  - A only mirrors the wait (`awaitingConnection` tab event, emitted ahead of the failing events and re-sent on every `tab.open`; "Waiting for {device}'s internet connection…") and sends its Cancel to B (`RemoteSidecar.cancelHostWait` → `tab.cancelWait`).
  - A relaunched device reopens tabs under new rtabs: `tab.open` with a session path takes over the tab an earlier connection left (`#adopt`), so no second omp starts on the session.
  - **A device's quit leaves its host tabs running** (v0.46.0's update restart journaled the host's turn `aborted` 146 ms after the swap helper started: every disposed `RemoteSidecar` sent `tab.close`). During an approved quit with Continue interrupted work on, `RemoteSidecar.dispose` sends no `tab.close` and defers none (`keepOnHost`, `RemoteDevicesDeps.keepHostTabsOnQuit` = `quitApproved()` and the pref, wired in `index.ts`), so B keeps the tab through its grace and the relaunch adopts it. With the setting off the quit dialog counted those turns and promised to stop them, so they close as before; a user's own tab close always closes. Pinned by `remote-sidecar.test.ts` and the quit test of `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts`.
  - **A device whose saved layout lost a running host tab gets it back** (21 Oct 2026, the v0.48.0 → v0.49.0 update restart: the first quit tore the services down and stalled, a Dock click restored the layout again on a disposed `RemoteDevices`, the host's tab got no tab there, and the layout rewrite without it was what the relaunch read; the host's turn sat in its grace until reopened by hand). Every time a host's link comes up (`RemoteDevices.onHostOnline`) the device sends the request `tabs.kept` (`KEPT_TABS_METHOD`, `app-protocol.ts`). The host's `LocalHostServices.#keptTabs` answers, for the asking device, the tabs in their grace period (no live connection) with a session file that run a turn, settle (background work), wait on an ask, or are held for the host's connection (`state`: `running` | `settling` | `asking` | `waiting`), each with `rtab`, `formerRtabs` (the rtabs it had before an adoption re-keyed it, `HostTab.formerRtabs`, ≤ `MAX_FORMER_TAB_IDS`), `sessionPath`, `cwd`, `kind`, `title`. An idle grace tab is left to its grace. `readoptHostTabs` (`remote/readopt.ts`) leaves a session a local tab already holds to that tab (`SidecarPool.sessionOwner`, which a deferred layout tab holds too; next bullet) and opens the rest in the front window through `SidecarPool.acquire(…, formerIds)` (wired in `index.ts`): the tab's omp is the host's own, adopted by session path (`#adopt`), and `[rtab, ...formerRtabs]` are its `former` ids, so an id an agent was given before the relaunch resolves (`ControlTab.formerIds`). One runtime-log line per tab, source `remote-tab`, message `readopt` {device, rtab, formerRtabs, session, state, tab}; `readopt-failed` {device, error} when the host's answer fails for any reason but being away. A host that answers `unsupported` opens nothing. **A v0.49.0 host is not covered**: none of its existing routes names the orphaned tab (`sessions.list` rows carry no live state and no rtab, `HOSTED_SESSIONS` goes only to the host's own windows, `tab.open` needs the path first), so a device updated before its host is protected only once the host updates too. Pinned by `host-services.test.ts` ("lists the working tabs a device's last connection left"), `readopt.test.ts`, `devices.test.ts` ("the tabs a host keeps for this device", the `unsupported` host), the former-ids case of `sidecar-pool.test.ts` and `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts` ("a host's running tab the saved layout lost").
  - **A host tab the saved layout kept comes back attached, not deferred.** The same `tabs.kept` answer starts the tab here that holds a kept session (`readoptHostTabs` → `holder`/`start` → `SidecarPool.startTab` on an `asleep` tab, whose `tab.open` adopts the host's tab; its `readopt` log line adds `held: true`). Before, a background host tab came back "not started" and attached only when shown, so the host's running turn was invisible on the device (the v0.49.0 → v0.50.0 restart of the same day). Pinned by `readopt.test.ts` and the background test of `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts`.
  - One session file = one host tab = one omp. An open of a file this connection shows under another rtab, or another device's tab holds, is refused `session_open_in_tab`; `#open` re-checks the holder after its awaits; a tab whose omp moves onto a file another host tab holds (`#dedupe`, on its `session` identity) stops the other (keeping one that runs a turn) and tells its device `exited` (`host-services.test.ts`, three fail-first cases).
  - A host's session closes and deletes like a local one: its sidebar row (`SidebarDevices.tsx`) has Close (the tab; session stays on the host) and Delete (confirm names the host, `remote.sidebar.deleteMessage`; tab closes first, then `sessions.delete` on the host), both behind the row's one ⋯ (`data-sidebar-row-menu`; an action sheet on touch) like a local row's. `#deleteSession` takes the host's own delete path (`session-mutations.ts` `deleteSession` with `LocalHostServices` as `deviceTabs`): every idle tab on the file closes (the asking device's own, including one left from an earlier connection; another device's, which hears `sessionDeleted` once the file is gone; a window's tab on the host, as closing a tab does), the omps are waited for, and it is refused (`session_running`) only while one of them runs a turn or compacts. Until 21 Oct 2026 a host window or another device holding the session idle refused it for good ("Session is open on the host"), and the device had no tab there to close (`host-services.test.ts`, `Sidebar.test.tsx`, the delete tests of `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts`: gone on the host and after both apps relaunch, and a session the host's own window holds; the touch ⋯ in `e2e/mobile-shell.e2e.ts`).
  - **The host's own delete closes the devices' tabs on the session** as another window's tab (`deleteSession`'s `deviceTabs`, `LocalHostServices.closeSessionTabs`): refused (`session_running`) only while one of them, or the window's own tab (`SidecarPool.sessionRunning`), runs a turn or compacts; otherwise their tabs end (`exited`, "closed on the host"), their omps exit, the file goes, and only then (the notice `closeSessionTabs` resolves to, run after the stored delete succeeded and the tombstone is written) each device hears the tab event `sessionDeleted` (its pool records the tombstone, drops the tab and its saved layout entry). A failed stored delete tells no device: a tombstone for a file still there would refuse it forever. An older device drops the event name and keeps an exited tab. A device viewing a window's session (`viewsWindow`) is told the same. A device off the link learns it at its next `tab.open` (`session_deleted`). Pinned by `session-mutations.test.ts`, `host-services.test.ts` and the host-delete test of `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts`.
  - **Stop and delete** (21 Oct 2026, the user's call: one action, no second confirmation). A delete a running turn refused, here (`Sidebar.tsx`: this window's tab, another window's, a paired device's tab this computer runs) or on a host (`SidebarDevices.tsx`), is a toast "This session is running…" with one action, "Stop and delete" / "멈추고 삭제" (`sidebar.stopAndDelete`). It calls `sessions:stop` (`window.omp.sessions.stop(path, device)`): main stops every turn on the file (`LocalHostServices.stopSessionTurns`: `SidecarPool.stopSession` for a window's tab in any window, and the device tabs it runs; with `device`, the host's `sessions.stop` does the same there) with `abort_and_restore_queue` (logged by `session-delete`), and answers once each of those runs has ended (the tab's `idle` activity / run events, never a timer; `session_running` after `STOP_SESSION_WAIT_MS` if one does not). Then the same delete runs again. Pinned by "Stop and delete" in `sidecar-pool.test.ts` and `host-services.test.ts`, `Sidebar.test.tsx` ("a delete refused because a turn runs offers Stop and delete") and the host-window delete test of `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts`. An older host has no `sessions.stop`: the action then fails with its message.
  - Every delete, here or asked by a device, writes one `gui-runtime.jsonl` line (source `session-delete`, `recordSessionDelete` in `session-mutations.ts`): `device` (the machine the file is on, `local`), `by` (the asking device), the file's name, and `deleted`, `refused` with the code or `failed` with the message.
- **② Connectivity.** `ConnectivityMonitor` (net.isOnline() sampled by edge, windows' `online`/`offline` via `resume.reportOnline`, `powerMonitor` suspend/resume with a DNS probe until the wake is real, then `RESUME_WINDOW_MS` = 60 s) decides `shouldWait()`. A turn ending in a network-class error (`isNetworkError`, subset of stock's retryable pattern) while `shouldWait()` holds is not failed: the tab shows `awaitingConnection` (`TurnStatusRow`'s "Waiting for the internet connection…", Cancel → `resume.cancelWait`; `TurnErrorNotice` hidden), keeps a `network` marker and runs stock `retry` once online. A new run on the held tab (a prompt sent meanwhile, not the taken-over retry saga still ending) replaces the wait.
- **Stock's own retry comes first.** A dropped connection (Bun's "The socket connection was closed unexpectedly before the response completed", which both patterns already match) starts stock's auto-retry saga; the saga is one run, so its failed attempts are live messages (`liveMessages`) until its terminal end. While it retries (`retryInfo`) the latest failure shows no card, only the status row's "Retrying (n/m)…" with its Stop (`TurnErrorNotice` reads the live tail for `latest` and `retried`); the card comes once stock gives up, with main's countdown. Why (20 Oct 2026, this Mac, a socket error at 21:35 KST; the Wi-Fi drops airportd logged came after, 21:37–21:45): stock had already scheduled its retry (omp log `agent.continue scheduled source automatic-retry`) while a red "Response failed" card with Try again sat beside the retry row; 4 s into the attempt an abort of the user-interrupt class (errorId `UserInterrupt`, which stock gives RPC `abort`/`abort_and_restore_queue`/`abort_and_prompt`) ended it, and a turn ended by a stop is never continued. Who sent that abort was not recorded anywhere; that the card prompted it is an inference (hence the stop log under Relaunch log). Other sessions that day rode the same drops out on stock's retries alone. The fake omp's `fixture socket drop` plays stock's saga (`auto_retry_start`, backoff, non-terminal `agent_end`, `agent_start`; `abort_retry` → "Retry cancelled"). Pinned by `TurnErrorNotice.test.tsx`, the socket tests of `e2e/scheduled-retry.e2e.ts` (online all along: no card during the saga, then main's countdown and the turn going on) and `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts` (offline: the wait takes the saga over).
- **Tests**: `connectivity.test.ts`, `turn-resume.test.ts` (marker transitions incl. crash, skip rules, rank order), `remote/host-services.test.ts` (hosted facts, revive, adoption, revived turn outliving the grace, open asks re-sent on re-attach), `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts` (SIGKILL and normal quit, Stop, setting off, offline wait/Cancel/online; en/ko × light/dark), `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts` (host/device crash, host offline wait across a lost link and Cancel, a question across a cut link with and without host omp restart; two apps), `e2e/real-resume.e2e.ts` (opt-in, real omp). The fake omp implements `composer.restore_interrupted`, `fixture network error`, `/retry`.

### What a relaunch continues beyond the cut-off turn
Measured on the real omp by `e2e/real-update-restart.e2e.ts` (opt-in `OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`; "Restart to update" = an approved quit while work runs).
- Stock: a foreground bash call is journaled "outcome unknown" and runs again; subagents (awaited or background) are revived by `restoreSession` (the parent gets the result, a waiting `wait` included, when stock continues its turn); an open ask/approval reopens.
- OMPdesk handles four things stock leaves behind:
  - **Background bash jobs** (`async: true`) started by the last process and never settled are named to the agent in one follow-up (`ompdesk-restart-jobs`; bridge `composer.restore_interrupted` → `resources/gui-bridge/restart-core.ts` `interruptedJobs`, reported as `jobs`). The model gets `restartFollowUp`'s text (each job `- <id>: <intent> — \`command\``, intent = the call's own `i` argument, then "start again only the jobs the work still needs, say nothing about the restart or this notice, carry on"). The transcript gets one muted line ("Re-running N background task(s) the restart stopped", `RestartNote`, `data-restart-jobs`, intents as hover title): `details` hold only job ids and intents, never a user bubble, never a raw command (a hidden `display: false` message left that to chance). Jobs are read from entries journaled before this process started, from after the second-to-last `session_exit`, never cut at a `session_exit` alone; a job an earlier relaunch already named counts as settled. Pinned on stock by the SIGKILL test of `bridge-autopilot-stock.test.ts` (no `session_exit` in its journal).
  - **Subagents revived for a session whose own turn did not go on**: same follow-up (next subsection).
  - **A goal active until the last process ended** (`goalActiveAtExit`: the last `mode_change` journaled before this process started, `performance.timeOrigin`, is `goal`; NEVER read it from a `session_exit`, which stock does not journal for SIGKILL/crash nor always when stdin closes mid-turn: omp 18.11.3 journaled only the aborted turn and an Autopilot run came back paused `app_closed`) that stock paused at startup comes back as `goalPausedByExit`. `TurnResume` resumes it once after the restore (`resumeGoal`, injectable; default local `set_goal` `resume`; `goalSettled` tells Autopilot `resumed`, `none` or `cancelled`).
  - **A new session's first turn cut off before stock wrote its file** (at the first assistant message): the pool keeps the prompt (`unsavedPrompt`, marker `prompt`) and the relaunch sends it again when restore and retry find nothing; it leaves the marker before sending, so once. The saved layout drops the missing path (`tab-layout.ts`), so the tab returns as a fresh session in the same folder and `TurnResume.launch` takes it for the marker (`#unwrittenSessionTab`: a local tab in the marker's folder with no session file, one per marker), never a hidden second one; only without such a tab does `openTab` open one. A spawn whose `--session` file does not exist starts fresh in its folder (`#spawn` in `sidecar.ts`, fresh-launch config so no older session auto-resumes; stock exits "Session not found" on it; also covers an omp restart of a never-used tab).
- Pinned by `turn-resume.test.ts`, `sidecar.test.ts`, `bridge-restart.test.ts` (intent, notice wording, details without a command), `MessageBubble.test.tsx` (muted line, not a bubble), `ChatStream.turn-status.test.tsx` (row's Stop), `quit-guard.test.ts`, `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts` (background-job relaunch: no toast, one resuming row, one muted line, no bubble, transcript at the bottom; idle session with a working background subagent: one muted subagents line and a turn; setting off: the quit asks, "Quit anyway" leaves no marker). Real omp (`(b2)`, 18 Oct 2026) records `ui` and `saidAfterNotice`: no restart narration from the model.

### Revived subagents
- **Seen**: the bridge's `composer.restore_interrupted` hands stock `restoreSession` the buses a live `task` run reports on (the task tool's `ToolSession` `eventBus`/`subagentEventBus`; TaskTool keeps it in a TS-private `session` field, `getToolByName("task")` in `capabilities.ts`), as `omp --restore` passes main.ts's own. Without them a revived subagent delivered its result but omp's RPC registry (`get_subagents`, the `subagent_*` frames the work board, dock and composer cue read) never heard of it (v0.35.1). Pinned by `src/main/bridge-restore-subagents-stock.test.ts` (installed omp, scripted loopback provider, no spend: a SIGKILL-cut background subagent is listed `running` after restore).
- **Told, when the session is idle**: if stock revived subagents but `turn` is false, the session would sit silent until an async result arrives (maybe hours). Causes (18 Oct 2026): the turn had ended and only background subagents kept the marker, or stock declined to continue (omp 18.12.1: parent in `wait`, session had used `eval`, so stock's `eval-state-context` message follows the interrupted-turn record and `restoreInterruptedTurn` and `/retry` answer "Nothing to retry"; oh-my-pi#16195, fixed in omp 18.12.2 whose `conversationEnd` looks past trailing eval-state-context messages; `MIN_OMP_VERSION` still admits older omp, where the follow-up is what wakes it).
  - The bridge adds them to the relaunch's one follow-up (`restartFollowUp(jobs, outcome.turn ? [] : revivedSubagents(branch, outcome.subagents))`; customType `ompdesk-restart-subagents`, or `ompdesk-restart-jobs` when jobs come too: one message, one turn). The model gets `- <id>: <intent>` (intent = the `task` assignment's `Title:` line, else its first line, `assignmentHeadline`, cut at 120 chars; a subagent's child has its id alone) and is told to check them, tell the user in a sentence or two what is in progress, keep supervising, and say nothing about the restart.
  - The transcript shows one muted line ("Continuing N background task(s) after the restart" / "재시작 뒤 백그라운드 작업 N개를 이어서 진행해요", `RestartNote`, `data-restart-subagents`, intents as hover title) from `details.subagents`. A restored turn gets none (its cut-off `wait` reruns and finds the revived job).
  - The bridge reports `followUp: true`; `TurnResume` counts revived subagents as continuing, so nothing is retried on top; a launch restores once per marker, so the follow-up comes once per relaunch.
  - Pinned by `bridge-restore-subagents-stock.test.ts` (an ended turn gets one follow-up; a cut-off `wait` in an eval journal is restored by stock ≥ 18.12.2 with none and gets one on older omp, the test reading the installed `omp --version`; a `wait` stock continues gets none), `bridge-restart.test.ts`, `turn-resume.test.ts`, `MessageBubble.test.tsx`, `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts`.

### Relaunch log
- Every relaunch decision goes to `<userData>/logs/gui-runtime.jsonl` (source `turn-resume`): `launch` {markers, enabled}; one `marker` per marker {session, reason, tab, via: layout | unwritten | opened | none, peer}; one `continue` per continuation {tab, action, restored: turn | subagents:N,jobs:N | nothing | failed | skipped, retried, prompt, goal, started, followUp, cutBeforeRequest, journal, cancelled, restoreError/restoreCode, retryError/retryCode} or {ready: false} when its omp never came up. `journal` (only when nothing started) is the journal end the restore judged (`journalEnd`: e.g. `assistant/stop, exit normal`, `toolResult, exit normal+pending`, `user, no exit`).
- `retried: false` means no retry continued anything, not that none was sent: a failed restore always falls back to stock `retry`, both errors land on the line. A tab that "did not continue" is explained from the user's own files. Pinned by `turn-resume.test.ts`.
- **Every Stop says who sent it** (`src/main/turn-stop-log.ts`, source `turn-stop`; the 21:35 abort above was the gap): each `abort` / `abort_and_restore_queue` / `abort_and_prompt` that reaches an omp writes one line: the command, `tab`, the session file's name, and `by` (`message` too) with what names the asker. The renderer tags its Stops with `StopOrigin` (`surface`: `stop-button`, `send-menu` (the send slot's right-click / long-press Stop), `esc`, `status-row`, `palette`, `agents-page`, `handoff`, `auto-mode`, `retry`), carried on the RPC command and taken off by main before omp sees it. The dispatcher (`dispatchedCommand`, called by `dispatchRpcCommand` in ipc.ts) adds `window` (routing id) and `web-device` (a paired phone's device id), else names the IPC `channel`; an OMPdesk control tool's Stop is `agent-control` with its `caller` tab. A paired host's tab gets the command with its origin and the device's line names the `host`; the host logs it again as `desktop-device` with the device id (`LocalHostServices.#command`). Parts of main that stop a turn themselves call `recordTurnStop`: `autopilot` (`stop-now`, `run-stopped` with the run as `caller`, `run-ended`), `desktop-slot` (`screen-stop`, `screen-kill`), `quit` (`worktree-finalize`), `turn-resume` (`resuming-row`). No message text. Pinned by `turn-stop-log.test.ts`, the stop-log case of `remote/host-services.test.ts` and the Stop test of `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts` (the line names `window` and `stop-button`).

### Branch from here (fork)
- `sessions.fork_from` (message row's Branch, tree's "Open in new window"; rules in dependency-free `resources/gui-bridge/session-fork-core.ts` `forkCut`, used by the bridge and the fake omp) NEVER ends the copy on something nothing will answer: stock reads that as a cut-off turn (`findUnfinishedTurn`: the next omp journals "Previous OMP process exited before completing the turn." and a restore would run it).
  - From a user prompt the copy ends before it and the prompt returns as `draft` (stock `branch` semantics): `forkSessionFromEntryInNewTab` fills the new tab's composer (text and pictures); `fileBranchDraft` files the text as the new session's saved draft (`draft-persistence.ts`) for the tree's new window and a branch whose tab could not open.
  - Inside a tool batch the copy runs through the recorded results (stock `fork(entryId)` `#resolveForkLeaf`); a batch with a call still awaiting its result is cut before its assistant message.
  - On touch (`useShellInput() === "touch"`) Branch sits in the message row's ⋯ `MenuButton`, never a direct button a scroll-stop tap can hit (v0.35.1: a phone Branch under a just-sent prompt made a same-titled session that looked like a turn the restart never continued).
  - Pinned by `bridge-session-fork-stock.test.ts`, `messages.test.ts`, `MessageBubble.test.tsx` (touch), `e2e/branch-from-prompt.e2e.ts`.
- **A fork shows where it came from**, so one keeping its parent's title never reads as a second copy (the user's two "추천 프롬프트 중복" rows): stock's `parentSession` header (`SessionInfo.parentSessionPath`, a path or, after a move, the parent's id) → `sessionForkOrigin` (`lib/session-fork.ts`) gives the parent's title, screen-reader words and one tooltip line "분기 · <parent> · <time>" / "Branch · <parent> · <time>" (`session.fork.*`). The sidebar row draws ⑂ (`GitFork`, `data-sidebar-fork`) and a muted "← <parent>" (`layout/ForkParent.tsx`, `data-fork-parent`); the title bar puts the same pair after the session name (`data-titlebar-fork`). A parent the index no longer lists leaves ⑂ and "Branch · <time>". Pinned by `Sidebar.test.tsx` ("marks a fork") and `TitleBar.test.tsx`.

### Auto panels (both off by default)
- `src/renderer/lib/panel-auto-open.ts`, fed per tab event batch by `use-rpc-events.ts`; Settings › Agent behavior › Advanced (`AutoPanelRows.tsx`, first group "Smart automation"). Prefs `autoPreviewDevServer` and `autoOpenChanges` default **off** since 19 Oct 2026 (the user found panels opening by themselves disruptive); only a stored `true` turns them on; read from main prefs at event time. **A phone never auto-opens whatever the prefs** (the rule sits in `openPanel`). A run's start opens no panel at all: its home is the work board.
- **Dev server → Preview**: a bash result holding a loopback address **with a port**, from a server-looking command or banner (`devServerUrl`; never a `curl`/`wget` client), opens the Preview when on. Otherwise (and always) the bash card shows a quiet "Open Preview"/"미리보기 열기" link under its output (`data-bash-open-preview`, `BashRenderer.tsx`) that moves no layout. Guards (`panel-auto-open.test.ts`, `e2e/panel-auto-open.e2e.ts`): only the focused tab on the session page (`activeTabId`; a split's other pane never); never a paired host's tab (the Preview blocks its loopback); never over unsaved Files edits (`fileEditDirty`/`pendingFileSwitch`); never moves focus; a URL is offered once per session at first detection (re-detection and a preview the user closed stay closed); an open panel is replaced only when it shows a passive view left alone for `PANEL_IDLE_MS` (30 s; terminal, files, preview, subagent views never).
- **Edits → Changes**: with it off the work card's "Open Changes" chip is the way in. A turn that edited files (an `EDIT_TOOLS` call ended without error, or `get_git_fingerprint` moved against the baseline read at `agent_start`; the tool-only case also needs `get_git_status` to show changes) opens Changes at its terminal `agent_end`, never mid-stream. Guards: only the focused tab, only while the side panel is closed, not over unsaved Files edits, once per turn, and after the user closes the panel it opened, not again for that session until they open it themselves.

### Batched notifications
- `src/main/notification-batch.ts`, always on, no Settings row (the `batchNotifications` pref is retired, `src/main/retired-prefs.ts`; it also governs Web Push). `SYSTEM_NOTIFY` carries `kind` (`completion`|`error`|`ask`, from `maybeNotify`); main batches per window: the first event shows at once and opens a fixed 4 s window (`BATCH_WINDOW_MS`), followers are recorded one per tab (ask > error > completion), and at the end with followers the first banner is closed and one summary ("3 tasks finished · 1 needs you", `batch.*` in `i18n-text.ts`) replaces it; a click focuses needs-you first, then failed, then the latest finished.
- Never batched: a notify without `kind` or tab. The 1.5 s dedupe (`ipc.ts`) and the renderer's `resent` ask rule run before it. Timers are `unref`'d and cleared with the window and at quit.

### Scheduled retry
- `TurnResume`, follows stock's `retry.enabled` (Settings › Agent behavior › Retry; the `scheduledRetry` pref is retired, `src/main/retired-prefs.ts`). Once stock's own auto-retries are spent and a turn ends in a transient error (`isTransientError`: stock's retryable pattern with word-bounded 5xx codes, minus auth, quota/rate limit, bad request, context overflow, certificates), main arms stock `retry` after 30 s, 2 min, 10 min (`SCHEDULED_RETRY_DELAYS_MS`, three attempts; `OMP_GUI_RETRY_DELAYS_MS`, comma-separated ms, shortens them for e2e). `TurnErrorNotice` shows it ("Retrying in 0:30…", Cancel through `RESUME_CANCEL_WAIT`, Retry now) from `scheduledRetry` on the pool entry (`IpcTabInfo`, so a renderer reload re-reads it).
- Guards: never a hosted/peer tab, a tab without a session file, after Stop, unless stock's `retry.enabled` reads `true` (`get_settings`), or for a network error the connection wait already holds. Called off by any run that is not its own retry, Cancel, a session change or a `TOUCH_COMMANDS` edit (`touched` on the pool entry), stock's `retry.enabled` reading off at fire, tab close, quit. A retry due while offline goes to the connection wait. The attempt count resets when a retry ends in anything but a transient error or the user runs something new.
- Pinned by `turn-resume.test.ts`, `TurnErrorNotice.test.tsx`, `e2e/scheduled-retry.e2e.ts`.

### Close to tray
- Closing the last shown window hides it instead of ending the app (`lastWindowCloseAction` in `quit-guard.ts`, the `close` handler in `window.ts`, pref `closeToTray`, default **on**: only an explicit `false` keeps the Windows quit, via `closeToTrayEnabled`).
  - Windows needs a tray (`hasTray()`; headless runs have none and quit) and shows a one-time "OMPdesk keeps running in the tray" Notification (pref `closeToTrayNoticeShown`).
  - macOS hides regardless of the pref (it has no Settings row): window, tabs and omp keep running; `app.on("activate")` shows the same hidden window (spawns one only if none exists).
  - **After `quitApproved()` nothing rebuilds a window**: `activate` (`activateAction`, `quit-guard.ts`) and every `spawnWindow`/`spawnWindowWithLayout` path (tray, menu, deep link, second instance) do nothing, and the saved desktop session is never rewritten (`layoutsToSave`, `saved-layouts.ts`, used by `persistTabLayouts` for both the tab-change and the window-closed hook; the web layouts and the resume markers were already frozen). The teardown closes tabs and windows one by one, and a quit that stalls afterwards (the first quit of the update restart above) would otherwise save the session smaller, or rebuild it on disposed services and lose the tabs they cannot open. A window the user closes before any quit still leaves the saved set. Pinned by `quit-guard.test.ts`, `saved-layouts.test.ts`.
  - A non-last window closes as before and ends its tabs: hiding it would leave no Dock or tray way back.
  - A hidden window stays in `getAllWindows()`, so tab inventory, notifications (a click shows it), keep-awake, badge and tray counts keep working.
  - Real quits (menu, ⌘Q, tray, logout/update restart) still go through the quit guard; once `quitApproved()` every window closes.
- Tests: `quit-guard.test.ts`, the macOS `desktop-tabs` e2e (close during a fake turn, then `activate`), the `@smoke` tray test in `desktop-surfaces` (default pref).

### Keep awake, lid closed while charging
- The user's rule (21 Oct 2026): closing the lid of a laptop that is charging never stops OMPdesk's running work; on battery the OS sleeps on a lid close as usual. No setting of its own: it is part of "Keep this computer awake while working" (pref `keepAwakeWhileWorking`, `KEEP_AWAKE_WORKING_PREF`, default **on** since this change: only a stored `false` turns it off) and of the host role's `remote.keepAwake` (default on).
- Electron's `powerSaveBlocker` `prevent-app-suspension` (`KeepAwakePolicy`, `remote/keep-awake.ts`) is an idle-sleep assertion a lid close ignores. So `LidAwake` (`src/main/power/lid-awake.ts`, pure, injected backend) wraps both keep-awake blockers (`wrap(blocker, "work" | "host")` in `index.ts`) and, while any is held, holds a platform `LidBackend` as long as the computer is a laptop (has a battery, asked once) on AC (`powerMonitor.isOnBatteryPower()` at start, `on-ac`/`on-battery` flip it at once). It converges one step at a time, logs one `gui-runtime.jsonl` line per change (source `keep-awake`: `held`/`released` with `reason` `work` | `charging` | `work-ended` | `on-battery` | `quit`, plus `skipped` no battery, `recovered`, `failed`), retries a failed step only on the next input change, and releases synchronously at quit (`lid-awake` teardown step). Never in a test launch (`OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=1`: a killed test app would leave the setting changed) or a second instance.
- **macOS** (`lid-backend-mac.ts`): stock `/usr/bin/caffeinate -s -w <main pid>`, a PreventSystemSleep assertion macOS honours on AC only; `-w` ends it with the app, so a crash holds nothing. No admin, no `pmset disablesleep`. Measured on the user's MacBook (21 Oct 2026, AC 95 %, no external display): with only that assertion held, the lid closed at 07:32:37 local (`ioreg -r -k AppleClamshellState`: `AppleClamshellState = Yes`, `AppleClamshellCausesSleep = Yes`) and the Mac stayed awake: a 1 s ping to the router had no gap and lost nothing while closed, and the user kept talking to this Mac's session from a phone. On battery the same assertion is ignored and the lid sleeps the Mac as usual.
- **Windows** (`lid-backend-windows.ts`): the active scheme's AC "lid close action" (`SUB_BUTTONS`/`LIDACTION` by GUID) goes to 0 (do nothing) with `powercfg /setacvalueindex` + `/setactive SCHEME_CURRENT` (no admin), and back to the user's value when work stops. The DC (battery) value is never touched; a user who already chose "do nothing" is left alone; a value the user changed while it was held stays theirs. The original is written to `<userData>/lid-awake.json` before the change and put back at the next launch (`recover`), so a crash or kill is undone. `powercfg /query` labels are localized, so the AC value is the first of its two `0x` lines. A desktop (no `Win32_Battery`) does nothing.
- **A powercfg failure never loses the user's value**: the backend keeps the original (`#held` and the marker) until a restore succeeded, so a failed release is retried on the next change, at quit (`dispose` always asks the backend) or at the next launch; a hold made while a restore is still owed reuses that original instead of reading the 0 it left; a hold that fails after changing the value rolls itself back.
- Pinned by `power/lid-awake.test.ts` and `power/lid-backend-windows.test.ts` (fake powercfg, English and Korean output, crash recovery, injected failures after `setacvalueindex`, during rollback and during release); `e2e/desktop-tabs.e2e.ts` (default on: the blocker is held during a turn, the switch turns it off).

### Watchdogs
- **Quit watchdog** (`src/main/quit-watchdog.ts`, electron-free, injected clock/log/exit; wired in `app-quit.ts`): once a quit is committed (`before-quit` lets the teardown run, after `finalize`, which may still be asking the user; or `before-quit-for-update` fires) the process force-exits (`app.exit(0)`, a runtime-log line `source: "background-task"`, `details.watchdog: "quit"`) if still alive after `QUIT_EXIT_TIMEOUT_MS` (30 s), because a wedged teardown otherwise strands the updater's swap helper or installer waiting for this pid. `app.exit` skips `will-quit`, so the relaunch swap registers `onQuitForcedExit` and starts through one once-only call (`updater.ts` `launchStagedSwap`). A failed `quitAndInstall()` (autoUpdater `error`) disarms the update countdown (`disarmQuitWatchdog("update")`). Idea: Orca's `update-install-exit-watchdog.ts` (MIT), reimplemented. Pinned by `quit-watchdog.test.ts`.
- **Main-thread hang watchdog** (`src/main/hang-watchdog.ts` wiring, `hang-watchdog-core.ts` pure rules, `hang-watchdog-worker.ts` the worker, its own `electron.vite.config.ts` main input `out/main/hang-watchdog-worker.js`; wired in `index.ts` right after `app-ready`; packaged builds or `OMP_GUI_HANG_WATCHDOG=1`, never under vitest or in a second instance): main posts a heartbeat to an in-process, unref'd `worker_threads` Worker every `HANG_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS` (1 s); the worker checks every `HANG_CHECK_INTERVAL_MS` (1 s) and records a stall after more than `HANG_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 s) without a heartbeat, in `<userData>/logs/main-thread-hang.json` ({detectedAt, pid, unresponsiveMs, selfRecovered, phase, steps}), rewritten with `selfRecovered: true` when heartbeats resume (`detectedAt` is then the moment main answered again, so the stall began `unresponsiveMs` earlier). A tick gap over `SLEEP_GAP_FACTOR` (3×) is a system suspend, not a hang (the wait restarts); `powerMonitor` `resume` resets it too. **Observation only**: nothing kills or restarts main. `markMainStep(phase, step)` names what main is about to run (startup marks, quit teardown steps, the update's quit); the worker keeps the last `HANG_STEP_HISTORY` (8). The next launch consumes the marker and writes one `gui-runtime.jsonl` line (`source: "main-thread-hang"`, `details` {unresponsiveMs, selfRecovered, detectedAt, previousPid, phase, steps}); `steps` is text, oldest first joined with ` > `, because the runtime log keeps only primitive details and drops an array (the stalls logged on 19-20 Oct 2026 read `phase: update` and nothing else). Idea: Orca's `src/main/hang-watchdog/` (MIT), reimplemented. Pinned by `hang-watchdog-core.test.ts` and `hang-watchdog.test.ts`.
