- **GPU fallback and crash-loop guard** (the user's report, 20 Oct 2026: on a new Windows laptop with an integrated GPU, v0.44.0's window opened and closed by itself over and over). `src/main/gpu-fallback.ts` relaunches into software rendering after a GPU process crash (`gpu-fallback.json` marker, read before ready), but **never from a launch that already renders in software** (marker, `--disable-gpu`, safe mode), **never from a test launch** (`OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=1`: the process the test drives exits, its page reads "Target page, context or browser has been closed", and the relaunched app runs on unwatched; Electron's GPU helper segfaulted under parallel e2e load, which closed `mobile-work-strip`'s desktop page 1 in about 40; Chromium restarts the GPU process itself, logged `no relaunch (test-launch)`) and at most once per `GPU_RELAUNCH_COOLDOWN_MS` (10 min) across processes. WHY: with hardware acceleration off Chromium still runs a GPU process (SwiftShader, WARP on Windows), restarts it itself when it dies and falls back further on its own (measured on Electron 36: four crashes in a row, hardware or software, the window stayed up); the per-process guard let each software launch relaunch again, forever. Rules in `launch-guard-core.ts` (`gpuRelaunchDecision`, `crashLoopDetected`), history in `launch-guard.ts` (`<userData>/launch-history.json`, newest 10). Every launch holding the instance lock is recorded before ready and marked stable after `STABLE_AFTER_MS` (20 s) or a clean quit (`will-quit`); a crash, a kill or the GPU relaunch's `app.exit` leave it unstable. When the last `CRASH_LOOP_LAUNCHES` (3) were all unstable within 10 minutes, the launch starts in **safe mode**: software rendering whatever the switches say, no automatic GPU relaunch, machine setup and memory setup paused (`automatic: false`; Retry still installs), one `gui-runtime.jsonl` line `source: "launch-guard"`. Packaged runs outside `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=1` only (a dev run restarts main on every edit, e2e kills apps on purpose); `OMP_GUI_LAUNCH_GUARD=1` forces it. **Hardware comes back by itself** (`gpu-fallback-core.ts`): the marker records the GPU the crash happened on (`app.getGPUInfo("basic")`'s active adapter: vendor and device ids, driver version and date; `complete` names SwiftShader in a software launch, `basic` the real adapter), a software launch records the GPU it sees (`seen`), and a launch under the marker tries hardware once when the GPU or its driver changed or `HARDWARE_RETRY_AFTER_MS` (7 days) passed since the crash or the last trial. The trial is written into the marker before it runs (`markerForTrial`: `trialAt`, the tried GPU becomes the crash GPU), so it happens once; after 20 s without a GPU crash it clears the marker (`confirmHardwareTrial`); a crash in it writes a fresh marker and relaunches only within the 10-minute cap. Each step is a `gpu-fallback` log line. `--enable-gpu` still clears the marker by hand. Pinned by `gpu-fallback-loop.test.ts`, `launch-guard.test.ts` and ci.yml's `installer_check` (`windows-installer-check.ps1` `Assert-StaysUp`: one main process for 60 s, no relaunch or safe mode in the log; `Assert-NoGpuRelaunchLoop`: the GPU process ended with 0xC0000005 relaunches a hardware launch once, and the software launch survives further GPU crashes).
- **omp ended by the Windows session is no crash** (20 Oct 2026, the user's log: both OS shutdowns on 19 Oct logged `Exit code 1073807364`, then respawns that died with `3221225794` or hung 16 s in startup). Logoff and shutdown end omp's tree with DBG_TERMINATE_PROCESS (`WINDOWS_SESSION_END_EXIT_CODE` 0x40010004, `restart-policy.ts`) while main lives on for seconds. `SidecarManager`'s `exit` handler treats that code like exit 0: status `exited` ("The Windows session ended"), no `restarting` banner, no `reportFailure`, no respawn (the 1/2/4 s ladder's respawns ran into STATUS_DLL_INIT_FAILED 0xC0000142, the session being gone). The status carries `sessionEnded: true` and nothing else about the turn changes: `SidecarPool` and `LocalHostServices` skip their usual "a non-ready status ends the run and its asks" reset for it, because that reset made the next `TurnResume.sync()` drop the cut-off turn's marker (a restart or a crash did the same before this), so the reboot would not have continued it. A marker already in `interrupted-turns.json` stays, as after a killed main. The exit code is the signal, not BrowserWindow's `query-session-end`/`session-end` (Windows, but only while a window exists, and nothing orders them against omp's death) or `powerMonitor` `shutdown` (linux/darwin only). `taskkill /f` (`terminate`) ends a process with 1 and a POSIX exit code is ≤ 255, so a user kill or a real crash still restarts. Pinned by `sidecar-os-shutdown.test.ts` (a fake child: only Windows reports the code), and in `sidecar-pool.test.ts` and `remote/host-services.test.ts` (resume facts keep the running turn and its ask after that status, and lose them after `restarting`). The stats server (`stats-server.ts`) shares the ladder and does not check the code.
- **Settle diagnostic** (`src/main/settle-watch.ts`, installed on the pool by `index.ts`; log only, changes nothing): a local tab whose turn ended but that still reads running (settling: stock reported background work, no `session_settled` since) for `SETTLE_DIAGNOSTIC_MS` (60 s) writes one `gui-runtime.jsonl` line (source `settle-watch`, "still settling") with stock's `get_state` (`isSettled`, `isStreaming`, `isCompacting`, `queuedMessageCount`, `hasPendingAsyncWork`, queue lengths, `messageCount`), main's run flags and the last `prompt_result`/`session_settled` main applied with its age. Why: 2 of 95 real GPT runs on 20 Oct 2026 stayed settling for good after the answer, and 414 later attempts (74 instrumented app runs, 340 scripted stock runs) never reproduced it; the line tells the next one apart (stock unsettled vs. a settle frame main missed). Pinned by `settle-watch.test.ts`.
- **A refused todo call no longer leaves a turn running** (`resources/gui-bridge/todo-reminder.ts`, rule `todo-reminder-core.ts`, capability `todoReminder`): the cause the settle line's first catches named (stock `queuedMessageCount: 1`, both queues empty, `hasPendingAsyncWork: false`, `isSettled: false`; 11 of 11 stuck GPT runs followed a refused `todo` call such as `op: "append"` without a phase). Stock answers that refusal with a hidden `todo-error-reminder` sent `deliverAs: "nextTurn"`; during a turn it waits for the next explicit prompt and its count keeps `isRpcSessionSettled` false, so `session_settled` never comes. The bridge wraps the session class's `sendCustomMessage` once and sends only that reminder, only while a turn streams and never one with `triggerTurn`, as stock's `aside`: the model reads it at the next step of the same turn and the session settles when it ends. Pinned by `bridge-todo-reminder-stock.test.ts` (installed omp, scripted provider: fails on stock, the reminder never in that turn and no `session_settled`; passes with the bridge) and `bridge-todo-reminder.test.ts` (what is left alone). The proper fix is stock's: count only messages that can wake the session.

### Copy diagnostics
- Settings › Updates & about, `settings/DiagnosticsRow.tsx`; channel `diagnostics:build` = `window.omp.runtime.diagnostics()`, `host-admin` in `ipc-policy.ts`; builder `src/main/diagnostics.ts`, registered in `index.ts` via `registerDiagnosticsIpc`, electron-free like `setup/ipc.ts`. The row's Preview shows one redacted text built in main on every open (OMPdesk/omp/Electron/Chrome/Node/OS versions, machine setup items (phase, detected version and path, failure), the OMPdesk versions of the paired hosts and devices connected now (never their names; `DiagnosticsDeps.pairedVersions`), how the window draws (`Display:` hardware or software rendering from `softwareCompositing()`, and the OS's Reduce motion from `systemPreferences.getAnimationSettings()`; `DiagnosticsDeps.display`), the last `DIAGNOSTICS_LOG_LINES` = 100 entries of `<userData>/logs/gui-runtime.jsonl`, topped up from `.1`, each line cut at 3,000 chars; a run of lines that differ only in their timestamp folds into the newest plus one "… the line above N times" note, and the 5-minute update check's `no-update` line folds so across the lines between too (it sat between the link's traffic lines: folding only adjacent ones left 25 of 100); any other line that comes back after others stays where each one was, so a sequence such as ready, failed, ready reads whole, `foldRepeats` in `diagnostics.ts`); Copy puts exactly that text on the clipboard. **Nothing is uploaded or written**; no network. `runtime:log-tail` and the diagnostics share `tailLogLines` (`runtime-log-core.ts`).
- **A runtime-log source is a compile-time contract** (`gui-runtime.jsonl`; 21 Oct 2026: `setup`, `keep-awake`, `remote`, `memory-sync`, `settle-watch`, `sidecar-restart`, `goal-time-limit` and `slow-bridge-call` lines had all been written as `unknown`, so Copy diagnostics lost their area). `writeRuntimeLog` / `appendRuntimeLogAtPath` take a `RuntimeLogReport` whose `source` is `RuntimeErrorSource` (`src/shared/ipc-types.ts`), so a new source is added there with a doc comment and as a row of `RUNTIME_ERROR_SOURCE_TABLE` (`Record<RuntimeErrorSource, true>`, `runtime-log-core.ts`; tsc fails without it). Only the renderer's IPC payload is untrusted: `ipc.ts` runs it through `normalizeRuntimeErrorReport`, which turns a name outside the union into `unknown`. There is no top-level `level`; a severity is `details.level`.
- `resources/gui-bridge/redactor.ts` (`redactDiagnosticsText`, pure, import-free: the memory extension embeds it for turn conclusions, see Memory quality) hides, in order: URL userinfo, PEM blocks, JWTs, vendor tokens (`gh*_`, `github_pat_`, `sk-`, `xox*-`, `AKIA…`, `AIza…`), `Bearer` values, `key=value` whose key names a secret (`tokens` counts and `true/false/null/numbers` stay), hex runs ≥ 32 and mixed-case base64-ish runs ≥ 32, emails (`git@host` stays), tailnet addresses (100.64.0.0/10, `fd7a:115c:a1e0::`, `*.ts.net`), LAN addresses (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, `fe80::`, `fc00::/7`), home folders (`/Users/<name>`, `/home/<name>`, `C:\Users\<name>`, also JSON-escaped, spaces in a Windows name) → `~`. Versions (semver, `10.0.22631.4890`), UUIDs, short hashes, loopback and public addresses, ordinary words stay; `redactor.test.ts` pins both sides. A new secret or address kind a log can hold gets a rule and a test there. Idea (five families): Orca's `observability/redactor.ts` (MIT), reimplemented.

### Diagnostic bundle
- "Save diagnostic bundle" ("진단 묶음 저장") in a session row's context menu and touch action sheet (a paired host's rows too) and in Settings › Updates & about beside the diagnostics Preview (focused tab). The plain item is stock `/dump all`; holding Option/Alt or the "(anonymized)" item is stock `/dump anon`. A stored session no tab holds has the item disabled (stock's dump runs inside the tab's omp).
- Over stock RPC `/dump all|anon` only prints the archive path in a `command_output` prose frame and rides the prompt queue, so bridge `session_dump` (`resources/gui-bridge/session-dump.ts`, feature `sessionDump`) calls the same `dumpSessionArchiveToTmpDir()` / `dumpAnonymizedArchiveToTmpDir()` and returns stock's `SessionDumpArchive`; local handlers `dump_session_archive` / `discard_session_archive` (`src/main/local/session-dump.ts`, both quota-free; discard deletes only archives this process produced).
- **Every member is redacted, both variants** (the UI promises it). Stock's `/dump all` zip holds the raw transcript (`session.md`), the LLM request (`llm-request.json`) and `subagents/**`; stock says it "may contain raw context/secrets". `dump_session_archive` never hands stock's file on: it repacks it into `ompdesk-dump-<uuid>.zip` beside it, every UTF-8 member through `redactDiagnosticsFile` (`redactor.ts`: `.json`/`.jsonl` value by value so they still parse, a field naming a secret blanked; anything else as `redactDiagnosticsText`), a member that is not text dropped and named in `dropped`, then deletes stock's file whatever happened. `/dump anon` keeps stock's anonymizer and then gets the same pass. The handler runs on the machine whose omp wrote the archive, so a host redacts before a paired device reads a byte; the answer carries `redacted: true` and main refuses an archive without it (an older host: `remote_protocol`, never saved, still discarded). A new redaction need goes into `redactor.ts`, never a second pass elsewhere.
- Main's `sessions:dump-bundle` (`src/main/diagnostic-bundle.ts`): save dialog first (cancel makes nothing), then the dump on the tab's omp, then `ompdesk-diagnostics.txt` (the Copy diagnostics text) added as a zip member (jszip is in `MAIN_BUNDLED_DEPS`), written, and the temp archive deleted even on failure. A paired host's tab dumps on the host through `tab.command` and reads the bytes back with `host.fs.readBinary` (`FS_BINARY_REMOTE_MAX_BYTES` 11 MB, else `file_too_large`); the host's temp zip is discarded. A web page picks the path on the computer through the host browser's save mode (`destination: {kind: "path"}`).
- Agents: `ompdesk_app` `sessions.dump_bundle {session?, anonymized? = true, path?}` (tier `change`, no card): anonymized unless asked otherwise, written to the absolute `path` or Downloads (never overwriting), refused for a session hidden from agents.
- Pinned by `diagnostic-bundle.test.ts` (main, shared, renderer; main's paired-host cases: fake secrets in every member never cross the link nor land in either saved variant, a binary member is dropped, an unredacted archive is refused), `local/session-dump.test.ts`, `redactor.test.ts` (`redactDiagnosticsFile`), `bridge-dump-stock.test.ts` (installed omp: both archives' members, `/dump all` over RPC yields only `command_output`, and a session, a subagent transcript and the LLM request seeded with a gh token, `sk-` key, Bearer value, email, tailnet and LAN address and home path leave none of them in any member of either bundle), `app-control.test.ts`, `shim.test.ts`, `e2e/diagnostic-bundle.e2e.ts` (fake omp writes a real zip).

## Build, test, CI

### Checks

- Typecheck `bun run check:types`. Lint/format `bunx biome check src e2e resources scripts`. Surface policy (the SURFACE POLICY block in `src/renderer/styles/global.css`) `node scripts/lint-surfaces.mjs`.
- The same script fails a renderer `.ts`/`.tsx` function that tests a key for `"Enter"` without the shared IME guard (`isImeKeyEvent`, `src/renderer/lib/ime.ts`), so the Enter that commits a Korean composition never submits. Rule in `scripts/lint-ime.mjs`, tested by `scripts/lint-ime.test.mjs`. A handler that can never see a text field says why with `// ime-ok: <reason>`.
- The same script fails a renderer `void window.omp.…(…)` or `void tabRpc.…(…)` call whose rejection nothing handles (no `.catch(…)` or two-argument `.then` at the end of its chain): on a paired phone page every such call is a WebSocket call a dropped link fails, and an unhandled one is a page error and a click that silently does nothing. Links go through `lib/open-external.ts`, update buttons through `stores/updater.ts` `runUpdaterAction`. Rule in `scripts/lint-unhandled.mjs`, tested by `scripts/lint-unhandled.test.mjs`; a deliberate exception says why with `unhandled-ok: <reason>`.
- The same script fails a renderer number or date formatted in the OS locale (`toLocaleString()`, `toLocaleDateString()` or `toLocaleTimeString()` with no locale): a German Windows grouped "20.000" in the English UI. Pass `uiLocale()` (`lib/format.ts`). Rule in `scripts/lint-locale.mjs`, tested by `scripts/lint-locale.test.mjs`; an exception says why with `locale-ok: <reason>`.
- Supply chain: `bunfig.toml` sets `[install] minimumReleaseAge = 259200` (Bun skips a version published less than 3 days ago when it resolves; `bun.lock` pins are untouched; a fresher `bun add`/`bun update` waits or needs `minimumReleaseAgeExcludes`). Every workflow's `oven-sh/setup-bun` pins one exact `bun-version` (never `latest`; bump all together). `src/main/supply-chain.test.ts` pins both.
- Unit/component tests: `bunx vitest run`. The full suite must stay green.
  - `vitest.setup.ts` pins `navigator.language` to en-US and `navigator.platform` to MacIntel, so keymap defaults and shortcut labels are the macOS ones on every runner. Windows behavior is tested by passing `"windows"` to the `lib/keymap.ts` functions. `vitest.renderer-setup.ts` (renderer project only) gives linkedom's documents standards mode (`compatMode`), as the app's doctype does: KaTeX checks it as it loads and otherwise warned about quirks mode in every test file that renders Markdown.
  - `scripts/vitest-real-home-guard.ts` makes any fs write under the **real** home (from `os.userInfo().homedir`, so a faked `HOME` does not hide it; only this checkout and the OS temp dir are exempt, never another `~/.omp/wt` worktree) throw and fail the test even when the code swallows the error. Give the test a temp HOME, or set `OMP_GUI_ALLOW_REAL_HOME=1` in a suite that runs a real omp on purpose. Child processes are outside the guard. Reads are outside it too: on Windows `os.homedir()` reads `USERPROFILE`, not `HOME`, so a test that fakes the home sets both, and one whose code measures bun's cache also points `BUN_INSTALL` at it and drops CI's `BUN_INSTALL_CACHE_DIR` (disk care's fixture walked the runner's real cache: ~20 s of every Windows case).
  - `scripts/vitest-orphan-guard.ts` (from `vitest.setup.ts`, every worker of both projects) ends a test worker whose parent is gone, checked every 2 s (`ppid` changed, or the parent no longer runs, which is what Windows shows). A `forks` worker otherwise outlives a killed or crashed run and keeps its test file and memory going under launchd: seven at 2.7–3.5 GB each were alive on a 16 GB Mac when its WindowServer stalled 158 s and the machine rebooted (22 Oct 2026). Never cut a test run off with a pipe into `head`; stop it through its own process (`bun run locked` stops the whole tree). Pinned by `scripts/vitest-orphan-guard.test.ts` (a real orphan ends; the same worker without the guard runs on).
  - Two projects (`vitest.config.ts`): `renderer` (`src/renderer/**`) runs on worker threads with `lucide-react`, `linkedom` and `react-dom` pre-bundled by the deps optimizer (it stays: it saves seconds per run); `node` (everything else) keeps a child process per file, since those tests spawn processes, open sockets and edit `PATH`/env. They run one after the other (`sequence.groupOrder`; side by side each would take every worker). Workers: as many as cores on GitHub's runners (`GITHUB_ACTIONS`; never key on `CI`, which agent shells on this Mac export too), locally `OMP_VITEST_WORKERS` or at most 8 (8 is as fast as 14 here). Both keep per-file isolation; never turn `isolate` off.
  - A test waits on an event, a promise or fake timers, never on wall-clock time. The `*-stock` files (`bridge-autopilot-stock`, `stock-helper-extensions`, `bridge-accounts-stock`) run real stock omp and stay ungated; they are the ones that time out under load.
  - A test that needs a git repository takes it from `initRepo` / `initRepoWithRemote` (`src/main/local/git/test-repos.ts`): a copy of a template each test process builds once (in the run's folder from `scripts/vitest-test-repos.ts`, deleted when the run ends), with `maintenance.auto` and the remote's `receive.autogc` off. Every git process costs about 30 ms on an idle windows-latest runner and past a second on a saturated one, and the Windows suite is bound by its git-heavy files: a file whose cases each run git dozens of times is split so its cases run side by side, its shared world in a `*-test-rig.ts` (or `*-fixture.ts`) the parts import (`disk/disk-care*.test.ts`, `autopilot*.test.ts`, `autopilot-ship*.test.ts`, `agent-control/branches*.test.ts`, `worktree-orphans*.test.ts`: on 21 Oct 2026 this took the Windows platform job from 6 min 13 s to 4 min 47 s, its longest file from 203 s to 65 s; by 22 Oct 2026 the next tail was 68-100 s, the branch choice, cycle-end landing and orphan sweep groups, split off in turn). Find the next one in that job's log: each file's line ends with its milliseconds. On Windows the node project's hooks get the tests' 20 s, since they run git too.
- Build: `bun run build` (`scripts/build-cache.ts`: electron-vite → `out/`, then `scripts/check-main-bundle.ts`).
  - It reuses `out/` when it was built from identical inputs: a SHA-256 over `src/` and `resources/` (test and bench files outside `src/renderer` excepted; Tailwind scans renderer tests for class names), the electron-vite config, `tsconfig*.json`, `package.json`, `bun.lock`, `.gitignore`, `.env*`, the build's own scripts and the build env (`NODE_ENV`, `MODE`, `NODE_PATH`, browserslist, the `*VITE_*` vars). The hash is stored with the size and mtime of every `out/` file in `out/.build-stamp.json`, written only after the build and the bundle check passed (a check costs ~20 ms against ~8 s to build).
  - A missing `out/` or stamp, a different hash, or a removed, added or rewritten `out/` file rebuilds; `OMPDESK_FORCE_BUILD=1` always does. One `[build] reusing …` / `[build] building out/: <reason>` line says which. CI and packaging start from a fresh checkout (no stamp), so they always build; a local `package:*` reuses only a byte-identical-input `out/`, and electron-builder leaves the stamp out of the package.
- Dev: `bun run dev` (HMR against the system omp; opens a visible window).
- Fresh worktrees install through the repo's `.ompdesk/worktree-setup` (`.cmd` on Windows; OMPdesk runs it after `worktree add`, run it yourself after a plain `git worktree add`): `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, then `bun scripts/ensure-electron.ts` (the `postinstall` script runs it on every `bun install`). Why: bun runs electron's `install.js` with the `node` on PATH, and on Node 26 `extract-zip@2` never settles, so `install.js` exits 0 after unpacking only `dist/LICENSES.chromium.html` (no `path.txt`, no binary, "Electron failed to install correctly"; not the lockfile path, `minimumReleaseAge`, trust or `ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD`). `ensure-electron.ts` re-runs the installer under bun's runtime from the `~/Library/Caches/electron` zip. Never copy `node_modules` or `dist` from another checkout.
- Default agent loop: `bun run check:changed [--base <ref>] [--no-e2e] [--list]` (`scripts/check-changed.ts`), not the gate.
  - It holds no lock as a whole. In a `vitest` slot it runs typecheck (whole project), biome on the changed files, the surface policy and `vitest related` (every test that imports a changed file, transitively) side by side; then `bun run build` (`build` slot; reused when `out/` matches); then, in a `playwright` slot, the Playwright specs that touch the change.
  - A spec is picked when it is changed itself, or its file name (for a hyphenated or camel-case name, its text) mentions a changed source file's name (`layout/work-board/WorkBoard.tsx` → `e2e/work-board.e2e.ts`; `auto-title-core.ts` → `auto-title`; a plain word such as `window.ts` matches file names only). "Changed" = working tree against HEAD plus untracked files under `src/ e2e/ resources/ scripts/`.
  - It is a name match, not a dependency graph: a change whose specs are named otherwise, or one in e2e support files, needs `bun run locked -- bunx playwright test <spec>` by hand. `--list` shows the pick without taking a slot. Playwright runs with `--pass-with-no-tests`: an opt-in spec (the responsive audit, a capture run) registers nothing without its env. It picks from the default run only (no variants). About 10 s of checks plus the matched specs, against 39 s for full vitest alone and ~4.5 min for the gate. Neither the full vitest nor the full Playwright suite is needed between edits.
  - Before the build it asks Playwright to start each browser a picked spec imports from `@playwright/test` (`chromium`, `webkit`; `scripts/e2e-browsers.ts`): a Playwright upgrade leaves its versioned browsers to `bunx playwright install`, and without them every phone and web spec fails at its launch with the same "Executable doesn't exist" box. A missing one gets one warning line naming it, the specs that import it and the install command, before the specs and again after a failed run. Only a warning: an import does not mean the spec runs (an opt-in spec such as `web-perf` skips with its gate off), so Playwright's result stays the verdict (pinned by `scripts/e2e-browsers.test.ts`).
- Never drive the app with synthetic OS input (osascript keystrokes etc.); use Playwright.

### E2E

- Run: `bunx playwright test`. `playwright.config.ts` sets `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=1`: windows are transparent, click-through, off the Dock/taskbar and never focused, and the app creates no menu-bar tray item and never registers the `omp://` handler (both belong to the user's installed OMPdesk; a test's tray once stalled the main thread before the first window reached Playwright). It posts no OS notification either (`holdTestNotifications`, `src/main/app-icon.ts`: `Notification.prototype.show` is a no-op, so a spec that wraps it still sees each one): macOS filed a test's notification under node_modules' Electron.app and the user's Notification Center showed e2e's "Autopilot ended" with the Electron logo. Tests never cover the user's screen or rewrite their LaunchServices state. Set `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=0` only when you must watch.
- Fake vs real omp: `e2e/real-core.e2e.ts` and `e2e/deep-audit.e2e.ts` run against the real system omp (isolated agent dir, no credentials). Every other suite uses the fake omp in `e2e/sidecar-fixture.ts`, installed per test by `fixtureOmp()` (`e2e/harness.ts`; an `omp.cmd` shim on Windows) and passed as `OMP_GUI_OMP`. It speaks stock `--mode rpc-ui` and serves the gui-bridge methods those suites reach on `OMP_GUI_BRIDGE`.
- Tear apps down with `exitApp()` (same file; it kills a main process that does not act on the exit within 10 s). On Windows it then waits (`e2e/process-tree.ts`) for every process the app started (Chromium's helpers, omp, its MCP servers, git) and kills one still running after 10 s: a process holds the folder it works in, and the launcher exits ~0.1 s before they do, so a spec's `fs.rm(profile)` right after found `project` busy (`real-core.e2e.ts`: `EBUSY` in 5 of 24 runs, the omp and its `npx` chain alive when `exitApp` returned; 0 of 30 after). Shortcuts in tests use `ControlOrMeta`. A path inside a CSS attribute selector goes through `cssString()` (`e2e/harness.ts`): a raw Windows path's backslashes read as CSS escapes and the locator matches nothing.
- **A test app never outlives its worker, and a run's temp files never outlive the run** (21 Oct 2026: a Windows PC held 108 test Electron processes, their fake omps and fake ai-memory servers, and 289 test profiles plus 405 WebKit profiles after a day of agent runs). Playwright's exit handler is the only thing that ends an app it launched, and a worker that dies without running it (Node 24.15's 0xC0000409, an outer `taskkill /F`) left the app running under the `cmd.exe` Playwright starts it with; stdin cannot tell, since Electron's main process on Windows has an empty `process.stdin`. `playwright.config.ts` sets `OMP_GUI_TEST_LAUNCHER_PID` to each runner's and worker's own pid and `appLaunchOptions` (`e2e/harness.ts`) puts it in every `launchApp` env, and main (`src/main/test-launcher-exit.ts`) exits once a second's check finds that pid gone (taken out of its environment, so nothing it spawns inherits it); a spec that builds its own env keeps the variable by spreading `process.env`. On Windows every run also gets its own temp folder (`e2e/run-temp.ts`: TEMP/TMP point at `<temp>/ompdesk-e2e-XXXXXX` with an `owner.json`), so every `mkdtemp` profile, Playwright's own and whatever the apps and fakes write land there; `globalTeardown` ends every process whose command line names it (the detached fake ai-memory server included) and removes it, and `globalSetup` does the same for a folder whose runner died first. What must outlive a run (the compiled fake `gh` launcher, opt-in evidence folders) uses `baseTemp()`. Pinned by `e2e/app-lifetime.e2e.ts` (launches with `appLaunchOptions`' env, whose launcher dies running no handler: the app, its helpers and its omp are gone within seconds; it fails with that wiring removed) and `e2e/run-temp.test.ts`.
- Fake omp layout: `e2e/sidecar-fixture.ts` only reports the version (`fixture/version.ts`) and dispatches in stock's order: `--version`, `stats` (`fixture/stats.ts`), `update --check` (`update-check.ts`), `worktree … --json` (`worktree.ts`), `-p` (`print.ts`), then `--mode rpc-ui` (`rpc-ui.ts`: stock RPC plus every gui-bridge method the suites reach; one closure, because its fake session state is shared by every command). A new fake RPC command or bridge method goes into `rpc-ui.ts`; a new CLI invocation into its own `e2e/fixture/` module and the entry's dispatch. A fixture branch no spec uses any more goes with the spec that used it.
- With `OMP_GUI_TEST_SESSION_FILES=1` the fake's journal is a stock-shaped session file: a new session writes `sessions/fixture/fixture-<pid>.jsonl`, and a stored file that `--session` or `switch_session` opened keeps its header and takes every later entry, as stock's `SessionManager.open` does (`e2e/fixture/rpc-ui-session-file.test.ts`). Writing it elsewhere makes a relaunch's continued turn a second session the sidebar lists beside the first.
- **No build, no run.** Electron with a missing entry opens a native "Error launching app: Unable to find Electron app at …/main/index.js" dialog on the user's screen for every test, and `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS` does not stop it. `playwright.config.ts`'s `globalSetup` (`e2e/global-setup.ts`) resolves `<OMP_GUI_E2E_OUT ?? out>/main/index.js` once (`scripts/e2e-app-entry.ts`) and fails the whole run before any worker starts ("No build: … Run `bun run build` or `bun run check:changed`"). `launchApp` (`e2e/harness.ts`) asserts the same before `electron.launch` (covers a spec run with a custom config); a spec that calls `electron.launch` itself uses `assertAppBuilt()`. Pinned by `scripts/e2e-app-entry.test.ts` (Playwright on an empty `OMP_GUI_E2E_OUT`: fast non-zero exit, no process on that path). Never run Playwright in a fresh worktree before `bun run build`.
- Specs launch `path.resolve(process.env.OMP_GUI_E2E_OUT ?? "out", "main/index.js")` (a new spec does the same, or uses `launchApp`); see Lock and gate for why the path is a snapshot.
- Env-gated specs and helpers:
  - `e2e/performance.e2e.ts` runs only with `OMP_GUI_PERFORMANCE=1` (optional `OMP_GUI_BASELINE_DIR` compares another built checkout); CPU and compositing budgets run only with it.
  - `e2e/event-replay.e2e.ts` runs the fake omp with `OMP_GUI_TEST_REPLAY_FRAMES=1` (every message and tool frame sent again behind the next one) and main with `OMP_GUI_TEST_BATCH_INTERVAL_MS=400`, so snapshot loads overtake held frames. `src/renderer/hooks/event-replay.test.tsx` is its seeded unit counterpart. Renderer stores apply a frame for a message or call they already hold (by stock timestamp and call id) as a no-op.
  - `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER=<executable>` turns on automatic machine setup in a test launch and replaces every install plan with `<executable> <item> <method>`. `e2e/machine-setup.e2e.ts` uses it with `OMP_GUI_OMP` naming a not-yet-existing fake omp (missing omp → install fails → Retry → detected → the tab restarts ready); `OMP_GUI_SETUP_CAPTURES=<dir>` adds its captures. Real installs never run in a test against the user's profile: `scripts/setup-install-check.ts` runs the real Bun installer, PortableGit (Windows) and `bun install -g` omp in a child with a temp HOME/USERPROFILE and BUN_INSTALL, checks `omp --version` against the minimum, and deletes the temp dir.
  - Opt-in model audits (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`, they spend tokens): `e2e/real-work-map.e2e.ts`, `e2e/real-work-scenarios.e2e.ts` and `e2e/real-advisor.e2e.ts` (asserts each custom message an `agent_end` commits, advisor card or background job result, carries its journal `entryId`, which the sidecar's `sessions.attach_entry_ids` stamps, and that its card is in the live transcript; `e2e/fixtures/real-advisor-frames.json` is a trimmed recording). All copy `config.yml` and `agent.db` into a private 0700 agent dir through `e2e/real-omp.ts` and run the main model on the user's `smol` role; `OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK_ARTIFACTS` picks the capture directory.
- **Tiers.** `@smoke` (the tag in a test's title) runs on every release's `mac` and `win` packaging jobs against the build they just made; it is the only e2e a release waits for. The default run (`bunx playwright test`) is the full suite: locally on demand (`bun run gate`; `check:changed` picks specs from it), on hosted runners nightly and on demand (`e2e-nightly.yml` and ci.yml's `e2e=true`, both through `e2e-shards.yml`; informational, never a release block).
- **Variants and captures are opt-in.** `variants(all, keep?)` in `e2e/harness.ts` keeps one variant (en light; for the mobile matrix one engine × device per shell mode) and does not register the rest unless `OMP_GUI_VARIANTS=1`, so they neither run nor show as skipped. `OMP_GUI_*=<dir>` capture tests register only with their dir set.
- **Rules for a new e2e.** No theme, language or size variant matrix as blocking tests (en light runs, the rest goes behind `variants()`). Prefer a unit or component test (vitest with linkedom) for anything that needs no real Electron window, Chromium layout, real processes or the wire between them; an e2e asserts a flow across those, and its header names the component tests that cover the rest. To move an e2e down, write the unit case first, confirm it fails when the behaviour breaks (break the guard, run it, put the guard back), then delete the e2e and any fixture branch only it used.
- **Parallelism.** Locally Playwright runs 4 workers (`OMP_GUI_WORKERS` overrides; 1 on GitHub's runners, `GITHUB_ACTIONS`). Every test launches its own app on `mkdtemp` userData, agent dir and fake omp, with ephemeral ports (a fresh bridge socket per spawn; a remote spec's host and client share one free port from `makeRemoteProfile`, which a parallel test may hold a successor of, so never assert the exact successor), so a new spec never uses a fixed path, port or shared profile. That remote port comes from `freePortRun` (`src/main/remote/test-ports.ts`), which walks a band of 500 ports per parallel worker (Playwright `TEST_PARALLEL_INDEX`, Vitest `VITEST_POOL_ID`) run by run, never at random from one shared range: a run is picked seconds before its app listens on it, and every app on the machine sees the others on 127.0.0.1, so two random picks that touched sent a device to another test's host, and the second device to reach it failed `[remote_key_mismatch]` in `remote.connect` (`connectHost`). Measured with the range cut to 45 ports: 2 of 24 `remote-resume` runs failed that way, each beside a pair of touching picks in the pick log (`test-ports.test.ts` pins the bands).
  - The suite is bound by its total work, not its slowest test: all workers stay busy to the end and apps running at once slow each other down. A launch on the fake omp costs about 1 s here and 2 s on a hosted runner, so sharing an app between tests saves little; fewer tests is the lever. Split a long test into tests that run side by side only while it ends the run.
  - `e2e/deep-audit.e2e.ts` (`mode: "parallel"`) splits its settings rows by measured cost over `SETTINGS_SHARDS = 3` apps (6 shards added work and left wall time unchanged). 5 or 6 workers measured only ~10 % faster than 4 at more load, so 4 stays.
  - Reference (18 Oct 2026, 15-core Mac): full default run ≈ 4 min; `MEASURED_SECONDS` in `scripts/e2e-shard.ts` holds per-spec costs for the shard dealer.
- **Responsive audit.** `e2e/responsive-audit.ts` (engine), `e2e/responsive-surfaces.ts` (named surfaces), `e2e/responsive-audit.e2e.ts` (the matrix), `e2e/responsive-smoke.e2e.ts` (the default run). It visits the dev harness (`src/renderer/harness.html`; `?stress=1` overflow-prone turns, `?host=1` a fake web host so host sheets mount, `?pwa=push|ios`, `?platform=darwin|win32` paints the page as that OS's Electron window: desktop layout, no mobile shell; `window.__stores` exposes the ui/toast/extension-ui/plan/updater/host-ui stores so a surface opens the way a click would) at 48 looks: touch phones 320/360/390/412, tablets 768/834/1024 (portrait and landscape) and iPads sideways 1133/1180/1366, then desktop windows 1100/1280/1440/1920 with a mouse, once as the web client (column `W`) and once as the Electron window (`E`); en and ko, light (dark at 390 and 1280), with simulated safe areas (`--safe-*`) on touch looks. A surface that shows a panel's error boundary (`PanelErrorBoundary`, `data-panel-crashed`) or the root crash screen fails with its message (the harness once answered `getLaunchProfile` with nothing, and Settings › Projects & launch was measured as "This panel crashed" for weeks). Each surface reports page overflow, escaping elements, truncated controls, clipped text, off-screen popovers, hit boxes under 44 px (a list row under the shell's `--sc-row-touch`, 36 on a phone; 36 on a field or a cluster that declares `--hit-gap-*`), text under 11.5 px, overlaps, stolen taps and content under the insets; hit areas, stolen taps and insets only on touch looks. Anything covered by a layer (judged at the centre of its on-screen part) or scrolled out of its scroller is not measured, and a popover or toast inside a scroller is judged by what the scroller lets through on each axis it scrolls (a fixed box, or an absolute one whose containing block sits above the scroller, escapes it; pinned by "a scroller lets through only what it scrolls" in `responsive-smoke.e2e.ts`); a menu, popover, sheet or toast over a control covers it rather than stealing or colliding; the drawer's edge strip is looked through (it hands taps on); a control drawn inside a field's box is the field's adornment; a list row's own actions over its title, whose ::after stretches over the row (ListRow's ⋯ the title runs on under), are layered, not an overlap; a text editor (CodeMirror's contenteditable) is a field, its hit box its own; a checkbox or radio inside its label is measured by the label's box and reach (it carries the hit area); a point outside its clipping ancestors (a tab half scrolled out of its strip) is no tap of the control's; a control with `pointer-events: none` is no target (a tree row's chevron on touch, `TreeView`: a row with children and no action of its own toggles them, so the whole row is the target); a point past the curve of a clipping ancestor's rounded corner (a half-scrolled segmented tab; per ancestor and corner, `e2e/rounded-outline.ts`) taps nothing drawn and counts for no one, while a point inside the curve still counts. Soft-keyboard surfaces (`composer-keyboard`, `composer-keyboard-slash`, `dialog-rename-keyboard`, portrait touch looks) shrink the layout viewport as Chrome on Android does, and the home bar's inset is dropped while `html[data-kb="open"]`.
  - `OMP_GUI_RESPONSIVE=<dir>` (plus `_WIDTHS`, `_LANGS`, `_THEMES`, `_ORIENT`, `_DEVICE=touch|web|electron`, `_AREA`, `_ONLY=<regex>`, `_SHOTS=all|none`) writes `report.json`, `summary.md` and `shots/`. Run it one look per `bun run locked` hold (`-g "responsive 390P-light-en$"`, ≈ 1.5–3 min) with `--trace off --output <private dir>`: a Playwright locator action has no default timeout (the audit sets 8 s) and a shared `test-results/` is wiped by other agents' runs.
  - The default smoke (390 + 834, en light) refuses page overflow, an escaping element, an off-screen popover and, at 390, an app chrome above 19.4 % of the screen. It also pins what the pass of 20 Oct 2026 fixed: at 390 and 834 no stolen tap, overlap or inset finding on fourteen key surfaces (the feedback and worktree dialogs' checkbox and radio rows, a question card and a figure header among them) and two host sheets, a question card's step tabs and a figure header's buttons as 36 px targets, whole summary figures on a work card and 36 px controls in a docked tablet sidebar; a tap on the drawer's edge strip reaches the PR row under it; a 1100 px Electron window keeps Settings › Memory's tabs clear of the project picker and cuts no Run popover choice; an Autopilot run's board (1133 docked, 390 in the strip) never scrolls sideways, its controls are 36 px targets and the goal bar's Pause, Edit and Drop keep their taps; more toasts than a 1280×480 window holds scroll inside it (`Toast.tsx` `data-toast-scroll`, top from `--omp-toast-top`), a wheel over a toast reaching the newest. Every look emulates Reduce motion, and under it a mermaid diagram still lays out: `global.css`'s reduced-motion rule ends animations at 0.01 ms but runs no transition at all (`transition-duration: 0s`), since at 0.01 ms every element began one on any style change and code that styled and measured in one task (mermaid's labels) read the old size.

### Lock and gate

- The machine-wide lock is one counting semaphore per kind of heavy work, because too much at once overloads this Mac until load-sensitive tests fail (`scripts/lock-slots.ts`, `scripts/build-lock.ts`). Slots (`DEFAULT_SLOTS`; `OMPDESK_LOCK_SLOTS=playwright=3,vitest=2` overrides for measuring): `build` 1 (builds into `out/` never overlap), `playwright` 4, `vitest` 1 (a full run with the static checks), `other` 1.
- Why those numbers (measured 18 Oct 2026, loaded 15-core Mac). Playwright: 4 runs at once add no flakes beyond the solo baseline and 4 runs × 2 workers is the fastest per run, so 4 slots share 8 workers. Full vitest: two runs at once lost `*-stock` tests (real omp subprocesses that time out under load) in half or more of the runs and fewer workers did not cure it, so 1 slot. With one lock for everything, agents waited 11 h in 6 h and one responsive-audit held it 93 min. A test that failed in every concurrent run was a peer's edit, not load.
- A slot is the directory `/tmp/ompdesk-locks/<kind>.<i>` (`os.tmpdir()` on Windows) with an `owner` file (first line `name HH:MM:SS pid N`; refreshed every minute; one older than 15 minutes is stale and the next waiter removes it). Waiters keep a ticket in `<kind>.queue/` and are served first come, first served; a late short job never overtakes an earlier one. A non-gate hold past 15 minutes is warned about and flagged by `lock-stats`.
- A Playwright run never reads `out/`: every `bun run build` (which takes the `build` slot itself) also publishes an immutable copy `out-<first 12 hex of the input hash>/` (an APFS clone; gitignored; deleted 30 minutes after a run last touched it; `scripts/out-snapshot.ts`), and `playwright.config.ts` pins `OMP_GUI_E2E_OUT` to the current one once per run, so a build another agent starts mid-run never changes the app under it.
- A Playwright run that finds others holding slots gets fewer workers: `OMP_GUI_WORKERS` = min(4, ⌊8 ÷ runs holding a slot⌋), so 4 each for one or two runs and 2 each from three (set `OMP_GUI_WORKERS` or pass `--workers` to choose). Vitest has the matching `OMP_VITEST_WORKERS`.
- `bun run gate` (`scripts/gate.ts`) is the full local check and holds no lock as a whole: typecheck, biome, the surface policy and the full vitest suite side by side in one `vitest` slot while the build takes the `build` slot, then, only if all passed, the full Playwright suite in a `playwright` slot on that build; its holds are exempt from the max hold. It is optional: the release gate is release.yml (see CI).
- Run any other build, Playwright run or full vitest run as `bun run locked -- <command>` (e.g. `bun run locked -- bunx playwright test e2e/smoke.e2e.ts --output /tmp/<you>-results`); the kind comes from the command, `--kind <kind>` overrides it, `--chunk <n>` runs a playwright/vitest command as `--shard=1/n … n/n`, one hold per shard, so a long job lets waiters in between.
- `locked` waits for a slot (printing `[lock] waited 42.3s for <holder>` past 1 s), stops the command's whole process tree and releases the slot when interrupted; a nested locked command of a kind an ancestor holds runs inside it (`OMPDESK_BUILD_LOCK_HELD` lists the kinds held). The old single lock directory `/tmp/ompdesk-build.lock`, while it exists, counts as holding every slot (old `until mkdir` users keep working). Each release appends one JSON line (owner = `OMPDESK_LOCK_OWNER` or `user@checkout`, command, kind, `waitedMs`, `slot`, `via`, `heldMs`, exit code) to `/tmp/ompdesk-build.log` (`scripts/lock-log.ts`, trimmed to its newest 4000 lines past 5000); `bun run lock-stats [--since 2h]` summarizes it. `OMPDESK_LOCK_ROOT` and `OMPDESK_LOCK_POLL_MS` exist for tests, never for daily use.
- Parallel agents: the lock lets six heavy holds run at once (4 Playwright, 1 full vitest or check run, 1 build), so run up to six agents at once, each on one disjoint slice; a seventh only queues. Iterate with targeted vitest outside the lock, run builds and e2e as short `bun run locked -- …` holds (several specs in one command, a long sweep split with `--chunk`), commit verified hunks locally as you go, and leave the full gate to CI. Waits come from full suites in front of short holds, so iterate with `check:changed`. `bun run lock-stats` shows whether agents wait more than they work (avg wait over a minute: too many at once or a hold that should be split) and which slot each run used.

### CI

- Workflows: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `release.yml`, `e2e-shards.yml`, `e2e-nightly.yml` (plus `setup-assets-health.yml` and `skills-bump.yml`, see Built-in skill packs stay current). The repo is public, so every job runs on a GitHub-hosted runner at no cost and the layout follows wall time, not billing.
- **Pre-flight, every push to `main`** (ci.yml; not a `release: vX.Y.Z` push, whose checks release.yml runs; docs-only pushes are skipped via `paths-ignore`: Markdown, `docs/`, `LICENSE`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt`, issue templates, the health-check workflow): the release's blocking checks side by side, so a release finds nothing red that a push did not already show. Jobs: `static` (types, biome, surface policy), `check` (vitest as three jobs: `node`, `renderer 1/2`, `renderer 2/2`), `build`, `win-platform` (`bun run test:platform` on Windows) and `mac-smoke` (`@smoke` on macOS against a fresh build). A pull request runs only the Linux jobs (`static`, `check`, `build`).
- Failures: a failed job writes its failing step and the first error lines to its job summary at once (`scripts/ci-failure.sh`, last step `if: failure()`, read-only `actions: read` token). A failure never cancels independent jobs: `publish`'s `needs` already blocks the release, and `gh run rerun <id> --failed` reruns only what failed. ci.yml cancels a superseded run of the same ref; release.yml never cancels a running run (`cancel-in-progress: false`): a later push or dispatch waits for it, and a newer waiting run replaces an older waiting one.
- **release.yml is the release gate.** Nothing is run on this Mac first. Every job starts right after `prepare`: `build` (only `bun run build` and the `out/` upload; its end is what `win` and `mac` wait for), `static`, `check` (the three vitest jobs), `win-platform`, `mac-real` (`e2e/real-core.e2e.ts` against the latest omp, on build's `out/`), `mac` (package build's `out/`, verify, `@smoke`) and `win` (package build's `out/`, `@smoke`). `publish` needs prepare, build, static, check, `win-platform`, `mac`, `mac-real` and `win`.
  - `mac`, `mac-real` and `win` install their dependencies meanwhile and wait for build's `out/` artifact with `scripts/ci-fetch-out.sh` (polls this run's artifacts, `gh run download`, fails if build ends without one) instead of `needs: build`, which would start them only after build's job is over. None builds a second time, so the packaging tests exercise the one shared build (`out/` is byte-identical on every OS).
  - Why `win-platform` is its own job: inside `win`, `test:platform` held packaging back by ~2 min. The macOS path (package + verify + `@smoke`) is the floor for the time to `publish`; levers left are fewer or faster macOS `@smoke` specs and running `Verify bundle` beside `@smoke`.
  - vitest: the renderer project is most of the work; two renderer shards beside the node job cut the wall time from ~3 min to ~1:10, a third would save little against ~25 s of setup.
- **Full Playwright suite is not part of a release.** `e2e-nightly.yml` (03:17 UTC and on demand) and ci.yml's `e2e=true` run it in 4 `macos-latest` shards from `e2e-shards.yml`. `scripts/e2e-shard.ts` deals whole spec files by estimated cost, because `playwright --shard` counts tests that skip inside their body and leaves a shard nearly empty; `deep-audit` keeps its own `SETTINGS_SHARDS` split inside its shard; each shard installs omp with `bun install -g` (the runner is ephemeral) and Playwright `chromium webkit`. Informational; the local `bun run gate` is the full-suite proof. A public repo runs at most 5 macOS jobs at once; raise the shard count only together with that cap.
- Manual checks (`workflow_dispatch` inputs of ci.yml):
  - `install_check=true`: the `install-check` job on `windows-latest` runs `bun scripts/setup-install-check.ts --hide-system-git` (the runner's preinstalled Git for Windows hidden, so PortableGit installs) against a temp USERPROFILE and asserts omp is detected at the minimum, the PortableGit download reported byte progress, the registry PATH refresh found Bun, and stock omp's shell resolves to that Git Bash, not cmd.exe.
  - `installer_check=true`: the Windows setup is one click, per user (`electron-builder.win.yml`: `oneClick`, no `perMachine`; `resources/installer.nsh` removes an all-users copy the old assisted installer left, after one question and one UAC prompt, the only case that asks anything). The `installer-check` job packages it and runs `scripts/windows-installer-check.ps1` silently, as a double-click (it must finish with only a progress bar and start the app), and over `legacy_tag`'s (default `v0.26.0`) all-users install, checking the folder, the Start-menu and desktop shortcuts, the HKCU uninstall entry, the app's window and the uninstall.
  - `win_platform=true` runs `test:platform` on Windows (main's pushes cancel each other); `win_e2e=true` see Windows.
- **Public-repo trust rules.** No workflow uses `pull_request_target`. A pull request (fork or not) runs only ci.yml's Linux jobs with a read-only `GITHUB_TOKEN` and no secrets. release.yml starts only from a push to `main` that touches `package.json` or from a manual dispatch (write access). `MAC_SIGNING_P12_BASE64` and `MAC_SIGNING_PASSWORD` are read only by `mac`'s Package step and only for `main` (a dry run from another ref signs ad hoc). Every job has `permissions: contents: read` (plus `actions: read` where it lists the run's artifacts or jobs, for `ci-fetch-out.sh` and `ci-failure.sh`) except `publish` (`contents: write`), skills-bump.yml's `push` (`contents: write`, `actions: write` to dispatch ci.yml; it runs no third-party code) and the health and bump issue jobs (`issues: write`). Every action is pinned to a commit SHA. Repo settings behind this: "Require approval for all outside collaborators" and read-only workflow permissions. The former self-hosted runner (`ompdesk-mac`) is retired; its secret `RUNNER_STATUS_TOKEN` is unused.
- **Why no bun cache.** `actions/cache` keyed on `bun.lock` saves nothing: a cold `bun install` takes 2–10 s on Linux and ~10 s on macOS (a restore costs about as much), and on Windows restore + install measured no faster than the 28–41 s cold install, with a 42 s upload per lockfile change. So no job caches bun's install. The two caches that pay are the memory build's (`Swatinem/rust-cache` and the built binary, see "Memory (ai-memory)"). A pull request's cache entries are scoped to its own ref, so a fork's run can never put a binary in front of `main` or a release.
- **Windows packaging.** electron-builder packs the NSIS payload at `-mx=9` whatever `compression:` says (61 s of one 262 s `win` run), so `win`'s Package step sets `ELECTRON_BUILDER_COMPRESSION_LEVEL=4` (~27 s for a setup about a quarter larger, ~110 MB instead of 88 MB; the Mac DMG is untouched; never below 4). `ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD` on Windows fails (gpu-fallback and sidecar tests import electron) and saves nothing. `win`'s lever is fewer `@smoke` specs, since every one guards a Windows-only failure.

### Windows

- **Test scope.** release.yml's `win-platform` job runs `bun run test:platform` (`vitest run --exclude "src/renderer/**"`), the suite minus the renderer. Anything that can behave differently on Windows (paths, PATH lookup, shells and `.cmd` shims, spawn, sockets and named pipes, file modes) lives in main, shared, the bridge or scripts, so its tests run there by default. Renderer tests never read the host platform (pass `"windows"`/`win32` explicitly); `src/main/platform-test-scope.test.ts` fails on a renderer test that checks `process.platform` and pins the script and the workflow step.
- **Windows e2e** (`@smoke`, never run by a human). release.yml's `win` job runs every `@smoke` spec after packaging (~105 s; two `--shard` halves would save ~30 s of wall time but pay a second checkout and install, so they don't). Tag a new spec `@smoke` only when it is worth ~10–25 s there and guards a Windows-only failure. Current set: `machine-setup`, `split-select`, `interrupted-resume`, Model roles, `desktop-surfaces`, `window-theme`, `first-launch-window`, `source-control`, Preview, `steer-send`, `remote-devices`, `oauth-login`, `session-delete`, `autopilot-cycles`, `mobile-web`, `smoke`; macOS only: `terminal` (the Windows e2e jobs install no stock omp, whose PTY natives it runs). Pitfalls each one pins:
  - `interrupted-resume`: kill the app's own main pid; on Windows Playwright's `app.process()` is a launcher one level above it and killing that leaves the app running.
  - `desktop-surfaces`: the tray test runs without `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS` on a real desktop and returns the window with a second launch.
  - `window-theme`: `nativeTheme.themeSource`, pre-paint and the surface colors of both windows, with Playwright's color-scheme emulation off.
  - `first-launch-window`: `dist/win-unpacked/OMPdesk.exe` (or `OMP_GUI_E2E_EXE`), no `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS`, empty profile and saved maximized window; the screen pixels under the window must be one flat color. A Mica window shown maximized stayed blank while its renderer had drawn the app, so `WindowManager.createWindow` gives a window that opens maximized no Mica. A hidden window with `titleBarOverlay` never gets `ready-to-show` once its first frame is slow (electron/electron#54025, reproduced on 36.9.5: a restored window never appeared), so `showOnFirstPaint` (`window-show.ts`) also shows it at `did-finish-load`.
  - `source-control`: the smoke case uses no `gh`; the full flow's stand-in comes from `writeShellGh` (`e2e/fixture/shell-gh.ts`), see Windows e2e pitfalls in docs/windows-dev.md.
  - `steer-send`: Enter, the Send button and the platform's send chord (⌘↵, Ctrl+Enter on Windows) reach the fake omp as `steer`; only `composerFollowUpChord` in `lib/keymap.ts` (⌃↵ macOS, ⌥↵ Windows, because ⌃↵ is ⌘↵ there) sends `follow_up`. Ctrl+Enter once read as the follow-up chord on Windows, so a steer waited for the turn to end.
  - `mobile-web` (~17 s on a developer PC): the fake Tailscale is a `.cmd` shim calling bun in a folder with a space, started through cmd.exe like the fake omp, so the CLI's path and argv quoting are exercised.
- **Fake tools on Windows** (the phone, GitHub sign-in, update and remote specs run on a Windows PC). A fake is a file Windows can start the way the product looks the tool up: omp, the Tailscale CLI, the test installer, Claude Code and the bundled Codex are `.cmd` shims (`writeFakeOmp`, `fixtureTailscale`; the shim calls `bun <script>`); `gh` is a bun-compiled `gh.exe` (`e2e/fixture/fake-gh-install.ts`: `resolveBin` starts only `gh.exe` there, and bun bundles an entry with no extension as data instead of running it); a `gh` stand-in written as a shell script (PR Center, Autopilot release) is `writeShellGh` (`e2e/fixture/shell-gh.ts`): one launcher `gh.exe`, compiled once into `%TEMP%\omp-e2e-fake-gh`, runs the script with Git for Windows' `bin\sh.exe`; an npm global is `<prefix>\node_modules\<pkg>` with npm's `.cmd` shim in the prefix (`extension-update`); the bundled Codex sits under a `WindowsApps` folder, which `classifyInstall` reads as another app's copy (`cli-update`); no login-shell stand-in is written, Windows has no login-shell probe. `mobile-remote-session` plays a Windows host on POSIX with a folder named `C:\Users\admin\.omp\agent`, which cannot exist on Windows, so there the host's agent dir is an ordinary folder. Still macOS only: `github-cli-install` (Windows installs gh with winget only, `selectMethod` in `setup/plans.ts`: no archive download and no managed `tools/gh/bin`, the install it drives).
- Manual run: `gh workflow run ci.yml --ref <branch> -f win_e2e=true [-f win_e2e_grep=<regex>]` runs them, preceded by `scripts/omp-update-lock-check.ts`, on windows-latest with captures in the `e2e-windows-captures` artifact (`OMP_GUI_DESKTOP_CAPTURES`, `OMP_GUI_SETUP_CAPTURES`, `OMP_GUI_SPLIT_SELECT_CAPTURES`, `OMP_GUI_SCM_CAPTURES`, `OMP_GUI_PREVIEW_SCREENSHOTS`). Dispatch from a branch nobody pushes to (ci.yml cancels a superseded run per ref, so a push to `main` cancels a dispatch on `main`).
- The omp update lock check (manual only, 18 s, so it stays out of `win`) exercises omp's auto-update while omp runs: a fake npm registry, the real `bun install -g` into a temp BUN_INSTALL, one process holding the package's files open with a DLL loaded from it, then `SetupService.updateOmp`. On windows-latest the update goes through (Bun replaces the files; the running omp keeps its loaded code and lazy assets), so the app needs no EBUSY handling beyond the recorded failure.
- **Playwright's own Chromium.** A spec that launches it (`chromium.launch()`: `device-prefs`, `pwa-install`) is not `@smoke`. The `@smoke` jobs (ci.yml `mac-smoke`, release.yml `mac` and `win`) and `win-e2e` run `bunx playwright install chromium` (a headless shell is ~196 MB unpacked); `e2e-shards.yml` installs `chromium webkit`. A job that runs such a spec without that step fails with "chromium_headless_shell … Executable doesn't exist".
- **Playwright's WebKit.** `mobile-attach`, `mobile-composer`, `mobile-enter`, `mobile-notification-open`, `mobile-pages`, `mobile-panels`, `mobile-shell`, `mobile-work-strip`, `phone-day`, `phone-run-sheet`, `web-no-reload` and `web-perf` launch `webkit` as an iPhone. None is `@smoke`, and no Windows job installs WebKit (`win` and `win-e2e` run `bunx playwright install chromium`), so they run on macOS (`e2e-shards.yml` installs `chromium webkit`) and on a Windows dev PC, where `bunx playwright install webkit` (60 MB) comes first: without it each WebKit case fails with `browserType.launch: Executable doesn't exist at …\ms-playwright\webkit-<n>\Playwright.exe`, and a `beforeAll` launch (`mobile-shell`) fails its first case and does not run the rest of the describe. That build is not Safari. Measured on it: no `OffscreenCanvas`, no HEIC decoder, `navigator.platform` `Win32`, Korean in Malgun Gothic, and a `resize` that comes after `html[data-kb]` is set by long enough for a read to land between them (`mobile-composer`'s slash menu measures its room on `resize`, so that test waits for the menu to fit). A phone spec asks the page what the browser can do (`typeof OffscreenCanvas`, a decode) or the measured width, never the engine's name, and never skips WebKit on `win32`: everything else it asserts holds there.
- **Stopping an omp needs WMI** (`terminate`, `src/main/sidecar.ts`). `taskkill /T /F` finds the tree through WMI. During an EBUSY hunt on the user's PC WMI wedged (`Get-CimInstance` and `tasklist` failed with 0x80041032 "call cancelled"; it recovered once the leaked test apps were killed): all 94 taskkills then exited 1 and killed nothing, so a closed tab's omp ran until the app exited and every `agent-control-across` run failed `EBUSY` (before: 94 of 96 exited 0). A taskkill that exits non-zero while the child still runs now falls back to ending its stdin (omp exits when it ends) and `child.kill()`; pinned by `sidecar.test.ts` with a failing `taskkill.exe` first on PATH. Never ask WMI from tests or probes: `e2e/process-tree.ts` lists processes with `e2e/win-processes.ts` (run by bun: Toolhelp32 for pid, parent and name, `NtQueryInformationProcess` for the command line, `GetProcessTimes` for the creation time; no WMI, no PowerShell). Beside a running 4-worker suite, 223 of 542 `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` listings failed with 0x80041032 (1069 ms each); the same 542 asked of the helper at the same moments all passed (167 ms each). A process this user may not open (system services) has no command line there; WMI gave none for them either, and every process an app or spec starts is listed in full.

### Dev rules

- Never reinstall `/Applications/OMPdesk.app` from inside the app (you may be running *inside* OMPdesk; quitting it kills this session). `scripts/reinstall-mac.sh` (`bun run package:mac -- --publish never && bash scripts/reinstall-mac.sh`) runs **only when the user explicitly asks for a local reinstall**. **Never** schedule a swap with `launchctl submit` (launchd keeps submitted jobs alive and reruns them, so the app quits and relaunches forever) or any `KeepAlive` job.
- Child processes the app spawns must not outlive it: they get an open stdin pipe and exit when it closes (see `resources/gui-bridge/stats-host.ts`), because a crash or force-kill skips the quit handlers. The one process meant to outlive the app is ai-memory's own per-user service (launchd LaunchAgent / Windows logon entry, serving the terminal omp too): it is not OMPdesk's child but the service `ai-memory setup` installs. That `setup` is supervised like any installer and dies with the app; it runs isolated (`SpawnSpec.isolated`) so the service holds no handle of the app's (see "Memory (ai-memory)"). A new command whose descendant outlives the app by design runs isolated the same way.
- A spawned omp never inherits another OMPdesk session's plumbing: `SESSION_OWNED_ENV` (`src/main/sidecar.ts`) lists every `OMP_GUI_*` variable a spawn sets from its own options (bridge socket, reply language, profile, judge assist, delegation, account rotation, roles file, REA root, pstack, memory), and those are removed from the app's and the login shell's environment before the spawn adds its own. Without it a dev or test run started from an OMPdesk session's shell handed its tabs the parent's bridge socket, delegation and memory file. A new per-spawn variable joins that list (pinned by the "never passes on the session variables" case of `shell-env.test.ts`); test hooks (`OMP_GUI_TEST_*`) still pass through.
- No git command, the app's or a test's, follows a repository selector it did not set: `git bisect run` from a linked worktree, a hook or a shell inside a git operation exports `GIT_DIR` and its kin (`GIT_REPO_SELECTOR_ENV`, `src/shared/git-env.ts`), and a test's temp-repo `git init`/`git config` once set the shared checkout's `.git/config` to `core.bare = true` with a test identity. `execBin` (`local/git/exec.ts`) drops the ambient ones before the caller's own `env` (disk care's scratch `GIT_INDEX_FILE` still applies), as do `gitSync`/`initRepo` (`local/git/test-repos.ts`), both test runners' configs, `vitest.setup.ts` and e2e `launchApp`. A new spawn of git outside those builds its env with `withoutGitSelectors`. Pinned by `local/git/git-env.test.ts` (a sacrificial repository the selectors point at comes out byte-identical).

## Change workflow

Commit and push each change as it lands; release in small batches without waiting to be asked (the repo is public, CI and releases cost nothing).

1. Each finished fix or feature: run `bun run check:changed`, commit with a normal descriptive message (no version bump, no CHANGELOG release section), push to `main`. ci.yml's `check` runs on the push; a red `check` is fixed before anything else lands.
2. Release by what landed since the last release, never by the clock: three user-visible changes (`feat`, `fix`, `perf`), one urgent fix (a defect that breaks the released app, a regression of the last release) on its own, or a user-visible change that has waited a day. Docs, tests, refactors and CI alone never make a release (the user's call, 22 Oct 2026: a "once or twice a day" rule kept a batch of fixes back for hours). Every release prompts every user to update and restart, so a lone small change waits for company. No local gate: ci.yml already ran the blocking checks per push, and release.yml is the gate (types, biome, surface policy, vitest, Windows platform tests, `mac`/`win` packaging with `@smoke`, real-core). The full Playwright suite runs nightly and on demand (`e2e-nightly.yml`), not in a release.
   - A release is a cut of `main`, not a list of work to finish. It ships what `main` holds when it is cut; work still running in other sessions, a hunt for an intermittent test, a red nightly or a Windows full run ships in the next release. On 21 Oct 2026 a release held until every running session finished was the whole delay: the lock queue had waited 6.5 minutes in those 6 hours (`bun run lock-stats --since 6h`).
   - Other sessions keep pushing while a release runs, `package.json` changes included. The tag lands on the run's own commit; a later push that starts a release run waits for the running one and then finds the tag cut (it ends after `prepare`) or releases a newer version. No session holds its pushes; a release commit that finds `main` moved rebases like any push.
3. Release per the steps under Release: one dated bilingual `CHANGELOG.md` section covering every commit since the last release, one version bump, commit `release: vX.Y.Z`, push, watch the run to the end (rerun only failed jobs), tell the user it went out.

- A run takes about 6 minutes to `publish`, bound by `mac`, when the memory binary comes from the cache, and about 15 when one of its inputs (native/memory, scripts/build-memory.ts, resources/gui-bridge/memory-prompt.ts, resources/gui-bridge/redactor.ts) changed (dry runs 39240205382 and 39238195669); every blocking job starts right after `prepare`.
- Never leave an `Unreleased` section on `main`.
- Do not reinstall the local app unless asked; the in-app updater picks the release up.

## Release

- Semver `X.Y.Z`; tag `vX.Y.Z`; commit `release: vX.Y.Z`. No AI attribution trailers in commit messages.

### Steps

1. Pick the version from commits since the last tag (features → minor, fixes only → patch). Bump `package.json` `version`; add `## [X.Y.Z] - YYYY-MM-DD` to `CHANGELOG.md`: Korean summary, a line holding only `<!-- en -->`, then the English summary. release.yml turns the marker into `---`, so the GitHub notes carry both, Korean first; the What's new and changelog windows show the UI language's part and fall back to Korean (`changelogSection`/`changelogReleases` in `src/renderer/lib/whats-new.ts`).
   - **Open each section with one plain sentence.** `layout/WhatsNewCard.tsx` (one in-flow row under the title bar, mounted by `AppShell`; never over a composer, transcript or side panel; once per new version; never during the setup takeover, first run or tour) shows that first paragraph (`changelogSummary`); "자세히 보기" opens the section in the dialog. After skipped versions one card names the newest and the dialog lists every release since the last seen (`changelogSince`). The card stays until dismissed or opened, and hides after 60 s of focused, unhovered time (`WHATS_NEW_AUTO_HIDE_MS`).
   - Update README version mentions. Confirm `MIN_OMP_VERSION` (`src/main/omp-runtime.ts`) is current.
2. `git commit -am "release: vX.Y.Z" && git push origin main`. Do not tag. The tag lands on the commit the run starts from, and that commit's diff must not touch `.github/workflows/*`: `publish` tags with the run's `GITHUB_TOKEN`, which never has the `workflows` permission, so GitHub refuses with HTTP 403 "Resource not accessible by integration" (v0.35.1). Ship a workflow change in its own commit, then an ordinary commit (the release commit, or an empty `chore: release point …`) before dispatching the release.
3. `release.yml` does the rest; every job starts right after `prepare`:
   - `prepare` reads version and notes; `build` builds `out/` once and uploads it (byte-identical on any OS); `static` runs types, biome, surface policy; `check` runs vitest as three jobs (node project + two renderer shards); `win-platform` runs `bun run test:platform`; `mac-real` runs `e2e/real-core.e2e.ts` on `out/`.
   - `mac` and `win` install while `build` runs, wait for `out/` (`scripts/ci-fetch-out.sh` polls the run's artifacts; `needs: build` would wait for the whole job), package it and run `@smoke` on the fake omp. `mac`: arm64 DMG, signed with the pinned certificate on `main`, codesign + designated requirement + `gui-bridge` verified. `win`: x64 NSIS setup (`makensis` cannot run on Apple Silicon).
   - Only if all passed, `publish` creates tag `vX.Y.Z` and the Release (title `vX.Y.Z`) with exactly two version-free assets, `ompdesk-mac-arm64.dmg` and `ompdesk-windows-setup.exe` (so `/releases/latest/download/<name>` stays stable). Notes = changelog section + minimum omp version + `<!-- ompdesk-update {"version","releaseDate","files":{"mac"|"windows":{"name","size","sha512"}}} -->` (sha512 base64), appended by the "Append update metadata" step; GitHub does not render it.
   - No `latest*.yml`, ZIP, blockmap or portable exe. An existing tag or an `Unreleased` heading makes the run a no-op. Package artifacts expire after 1 day (7 on a dry run); the Release keeps the assets.
   - **Legacy feed switch**: `LEGACY_UPDATE_FEED` at the top of release.yml, `"false"` since v0.18.1 (v0.18.0 carried the bridge). Installs up to v0.17.x updated from `latest-mac.yml`/`latest.yml`, fetching `ompdesk-X.Y.Z-arm64-mac.zip`, `ompdesk-X.Y.Z-arm64.dmg` and `ompdesk-X.Y.Z-setup.exe` by exact name. `"true"`: the mac job also builds the ZIP (`electron-builder --mac dmg zip`) and both jobs add the yml plus every file it lists. While `"false"`, `publish` refuses any release without exactly the two assets.
4. Watch: `gh run watch <run-id> --exit-status`. A failure the code didn't cause (runner, network, flaky test): `gh run rerun <run-id> --failed`, never a whole new run. Otherwise fix on `main` and re-run with `gh workflow run release.yml`. Rehearse from any branch: `gh workflow run release.yml --ref <branch> -f dry_run=true` (artifacts kept 7 days).

- Manual fallback: `bun run package:mac -- --publish never` on a Mac, `bun run package:win -- --publish never` on Windows; copy `dist/ompdesk-X.Y.Z-arm64.dmg` → `ompdesk-mac-arm64.dmg` and `dist/ompdesk-X.Y.Z-setup.exe` → `ompdesk-windows-setup.exe`; append the metadata comment to the notes as that step does (a release without it is not offered as an update); `gh release create vX.Y.Z --target main --title vX.Y.Z --notes-file <notes> ompdesk-mac-arm64.dmg ompdesk-windows-setup.exe`.

### Updater

- **Auth** (`src/main/update-auth.ts`). The repo is public: the release lookup and asset download carry no Authorization header. A token is optional (`GH_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN` → `gh auth token` → a safeStorage-encrypted token from Settings › Updates & about, whose row shows only while the updater is `auth-required` or a token is stored); it raises the rate limit (anonymous: 60 requests/hour per address; a check is one request per 5 min) and keeps updates working if the repo goes private. Never bundle a token.
  - A token answered 401 (expired `GH_TOKEN`, stale `gh` login) is dropped and the check retried anonymously (401 fails even public reads). Only an anonymous "not found" (repo private again) ends in `auth-required`; a 403 with `x-ratelimit-remaining: 0` is a failed request, not missing access.
- **Release lookup.** Each check reads `/releases/latest` (or `/releases` with pre-releases on) through the API. `src/main/update-release.ts` validates the metadata comment with zod against the release (tag = version, asset present with that size), identical with or without a token. A missing, malformed or contradicting comment means no update and the error "no valid update information"; a release without this platform's file reports a missing installer. `src/main/update-provider.ts` gives electron-updater a custom provider (`setFeedURL({ provider: "custom", updateProvider })`) pointing at the API asset endpoint (`githubApiHeaders` in `updater-state.ts`); differential download is off.
- **One check at a time** (`runCheck`/`checkOnce` in `updater.ts`). A Check for Updates (menu, Settings, a paired device) that arrives while the 5-minute background check runs joins it, and that check settles as asked: its failure or "no result" reaches the banner. Two checks used to run side by side on the one shared status: the background one settled "checking" as a quiet idle and the person's then found nothing to settle, so the sidebar card spun on "Checking for updates…" (desktop.e2e "Check for Updates…", 2 of 16 runs beside 4 workers and 6 busy loops on Windows; 32 of 32 after). Pinned by `updater-check.test.ts`.
- **Dev seams** (unpackaged only; launch Electron on the repo root so the version is `package.json`'s). `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_CHECK=1 OMP_DEV_UPDATE_MODE=manual` (+ `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_PRERELEASE=1` for a throwaway pre-release). `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_MODE=relaunch OMP_DEV_UPDATE_BUNDLE=<writable copy of OMPdesk.app>` runs the relaunch path against that stand-in and swaps it on quit.
- **Install modes** (`src/main/updater.ts`). Windows: electron-updater's NSIS flow (background download, SHA-512 checked, applied on restart or quit). On a restart the installer starts on a worker thread (`OmpNsisUpdater` in `src/main/nsis-updater.ts`, over `spawnDetachedOffThread` in `installer-spawn.ts`) and the quit follows once that attempt ends, started or not. `CreateProcess` on the freshly downloaded, unsigned 113 MB setup waits for Defender's scan, and electron-updater's own `quitAndInstall()` makes that call on the main thread: the window froze 5.6 s on the user's PC (hang watchdog, 19-20 Oct 2026). Measured here under Electron 36 with a fresh unsigned 96 MB exe: the call blocked main 4.5 to 5.3 s; off the thread it returns in 3 ms, main's loop gap is 36 ms at most, and the process exists after the same 4.5 to 6 s. The scan is the OS's and stays; only the thread that waits for it changed. The install at app quit (`autoInstallOnAppQuit`) still spawns in place, because the process may end right after it. macOS, ad-hoc or signed, avoids Squirrel (it would need the ZIP the release no longer ships):
  - **relaunch** (writable bundle): download `ompdesk-mac-arm64.dmg` into `<userData>/updates/ompdesk-X.Y.Z-arm64.dmg`, verify SHA-512, `hdiutil attach -nobrowse -readonly`, copy the `.app` out with `ditto`, detach, check `codesign --verify --deep --strict`, bundle id and version (`stageBundleFromDmg` in `updater-state.ts`). On "Restart & install" or any approved quit a detached `/bin/bash` helper (`SWAP_SCRIPT`) waits for exit, swaps with rollback, and reopens on restart.
  - **manual** (read-only: DMG, App Translocation): the same DMG, verified and opened in Finder from Downloads.
- **Install updates automatically** (Settings › Updates & about; pref `appAutoUpdate`, `APP_AUTO_UPDATE_PREF`, **default on** since 21 Oct 2026, the user's call so an unattended Mac running Autopilot updates itself: only a stored `false` turns it off; `AppAutoUpdateRow.tsx`). Off: check, background download, "Restart & install", applied on the click or any approved quit.
  - On, once a verified update is downloaded and `restartableUpdate()` holds (relaunch or NSIS; never the manual DMG, whose switch is disabled with the reason, `updater:installMode`), `AutoInstallScheduler` (`src/main/app-auto-update.ts`, electron-free, injected clock, ticked every second by `updater.ts`) decides when.
  - **With Continue interrupted work (`resumeInterrupted`) on: the next safe point.** No ask, approval or plan-proposal card open (`asking`: the pool's and hosted tabs' open asks, `SidecarPool.restartHolds().planWaiting`) and no compaction under way (`compacting`: `restartHolds().compacting`, `LocalHostServices.compacting()`; a cut compaction does not resume). A running turn, a focused window and typing hold nothing back: the relaunch continues the turn (see Interrupted turns), Autopilot runs and hosted tabs included. The countdown's end waits for a safe point again (log `held` with the reason) instead of installing over a card. Log `safe {waitedMs, running, resume}`.
  - **With it off, or after the user's "Later" that round:** a quiet moment: no tab running a turn or compacting, no ask open (`resumeFacts`/`tabInventory`, this computer's tabs and hosted ones), and every window unfocused or no input for 2 min (`AUTO_INSTALL_IDLE_MS`, `powerMonitor.getSystemIdleTime`). Not quiet after 30 min (`AUTO_INSTALL_MAX_WAIT_MS`): with resume on it proceeds anyway; with it off it never forces the restart and keeps waiting for idle.
  - Then it announces: `UpdateStatus.downloaded.restartAt` (epoch ms, 60 s ahead, `AUTO_INSTALL_COUNTDOWN_MS`) shows one quiet line with a countdown and "Now / Later" in the sidebar update card and Settings › Updates & about, never a modal. "Later" (`updater:postpone`) postpones to the quiet-moment rule and restarts the 30 min wait.
  - At the deadline `restartIntoUpdate()` runs the existing quit path (`requestQuit`/`quitAndInstall`, so drafts, layout and resume markers survive); with resume off and work started during the countdown it withdraws the notice instead of asking. It first sets `UpdateStatus.downloaded.installing` (an optional flag, also in the remote host schema): the sidebar card and the Settings row read "Installing update X…" with no button, even for a version put off with Later, and `current.installing` guards `restartIntoUpdate` and `updater:apply`, so a second click, "Now" after "Now" or the deadline meanwhile starts nothing. The scheduler is done once `install` ran; it announces and installs again only after the update stops being installable (the install failed) or the pref goes off. Before this, a blocked main thread let the next tick come 3.5 to 5.5 s late and log `quiet` + `countdown` again after `install`, and a second click 5 s after the first ran the install twice (19 Oct 2026).
  - Dev seams: `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_STAGED` (+ `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_STAGED_VERSION`) treats a bundle as the verified staged update (with `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_MODE=relaunch`, `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_BUNDLE`; `e2e/fixture/stand-in-bundle.ts`); `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_AUTO_COUNTDOWN_MS` shortens the countdown. `e2e/app-auto-update.e2e.ts` covers the default (installs unclicked) and a stored off (waits); the second test of `e2e/autopilot-restart.e2e.ts` turns it on while an Autopilot cycle runs, the update installs at once (`safe … running: true`) and the relaunch continues the run. Rules pinned by `app-auto-update.test.ts`.
- **Update log.** Every decision goes to `<userData>/logs/gui-runtime.jsonl` (source `app-update`, `logUpdate` in `updater.ts`, `details.level: "info"`), so an unclicked restart is explained from the log (Copy diagnostics carries the last 100 entries, repeats folded, redacted). Never the token or a URL: failure reasons pass `scrubUpdateText` (`update-log-core.ts`: drops URLs and the token, runs the diagnostics redactor). `message` is the area, `details.event` the step:
